  `8ac63f3` (`xsd: decide cos-equiv-derived-ok-rec clause 2.3 exactly,
  collapsing the two substitution-group predicates into one (#249)`, 8 files,
  +499/−243) and `764304b` (`xsd: repair #249 round 1 — fix clause 2.3
  simple-type walk abandonment, correct mis-charged c-vs-sg comment`, 4 files,
  +159/−51) — plus `ba90bde`, a clean merge of `origin/main` (`7159aed`, #264)
  taken during the repair. Branch total against `7159aed`: 8 files,
  +619/−255 — `xsd/substitutiongroup.go`, `xsd/substitutiongroup_test.go`,
  `xsd/particleattribution.go`, `xsd/particleattribution_test.go`,
  `xsd/contentrestricts.go`, `xsd/elementconsistent.go`,
  `xsd/wildcardadmit.go`, `xsd/resolve.go`. No new files. **REJECT on round 1,
  ACCEPT on round 2 — one repair round consumed of the two allowed.**
- **Ratchet: unchanged — `datatypes 1129 / schema 15432 / instance 26426`; the
  ratchet run rewrote every lane file byte-identical, zero regressions, zero
  new banks.** The arbiter's figures, copied not recomputed. Round 1 measured
  the same and said so explicitly: *"movement is exactly zero on all six lanes
  — `schema`, `datatypes`, `instance`, `xpath`, `json`, `ber`"*, with
  `git status` empty after `GOXSD_RATCHET=1`. Plain conformance: 41858 cases
  across 6 lanes, both rounds.
- **Zero movement is the PREDICTED outcome, not a shortfall, and the prediction
  was written down before the work started.** `parser` still passes `nil` for
  `{substitution group affiliations}` (that is **#281**, the producer half), so
  every consumer of this predicate is reasoning over a substitution-group graph
  with **no edges in it** — an exactly-decided membership relation over a
  universally-empty set is still universally false. That correction was made on
  the thread by the #263 post-land pass, and #229's unblock comment moved it
  into the issue body's `## Acceptance` as *"`unchanged` is the expected and
  legal outcome unless #281 has already landed"*. Legal per "scores only move
  up", not "must move".
- **What landed.** `(*Schema).inSubstitutionGroupOf(member, head QName) bool`
  in `xsd/substitutiongroup.go` decides `cos-equiv-derived-ok-rec` (§3.3.6.3)
  clause 1 (`member == head`, folded in at the top), clause 2.1
  (`H.{disallowed substitutions}` does not contain `substitution`), clause 2.2
  (the `{substitution group affiliations}` chain) and clause 2.3. Clause 2.3 is
  the new helper `derivationAdmitsSubstitution`: walk `{base type definition}`
  from `M.{type definition}` toward `H.{type definition}`, collect the
  `{derivation method}`s involved, and test non-intersection against the union
  of (1) `H.{disallowed substitutions}`, (2) `H.{type definition}.{prohibited
  substitutions}` when that type is complex, and (3) the `{prohibited
  substitutions}` of every **strictly**-intermediate type. **Both sets are
  collected in full before either is read** — an intermediate's `{prohibited
  substitutions}` must be able to block a `{derivation method}` contributed
  further DOWN the chain, so no incremental per-step test is equivalent to the
  spec's set formulation; the arbiter re-derived that independently and
  `TestSubstitutionGroupClause23IntermediateProhibitedSubstitutions` pins it
  with the extension step *below* the prohibiting intermediate.
- **What was deleted, which is the actual point of the issue.**
  `mayBeInSubstitutionGroupOf` (over-approximating) and
  `certainlyInSubstitutionGroupOf` (under-approximating), `inSubstitutionGroup`,
  `type substitutionFacts` together with its `resolve()` computation, and **both
  `// GAP(subst)` markers**. ~12 call sites across `xsd/wildcardadmit.go`,
  `xsd/contentrestricts.go`, `xsd/particleattribution.go` and
  `xsd/elementconsistent.go` were repointed at the single predicate. This is
  PRINCIPLES 4 / STYLE D3-D4 discharged directly: the split existed **because**
  clause 2.3 was undecided, and two predicates for one relation is two
  encodings of one fact. `anyAffiliation` was a cache, not a fact, and the
  arbiter confirmed dropping it costs nothing measurable. **Exported surface:
  identical across `xsd`/`parser`/`conformance`, `origin/main` vs HEAD** — every
  identifier added or removed is unexported.
- **Arbiter round 1 — REJECT, and the defect is a live false-REJECT of a valid
  schema, not a latent one.** The clause-2.3 walk bailed at the first operand
  that was not a `ComplexType` (`if !ok { return true }`), **discarding every
  `{derivation method}` already collected from the complex prefix**. But
  `key-derived` (§2.2.1.1) is kind-blind — *"if a type definition D can reach a
  type definition B by following its base type definition chain, then D is said
  to be derived from B"* — and a `<simpleContent>` complex type has a **simple**
  `{base type definition}` (§3.4.2.2), so the chain crosses kinds and the set
  must survive the crossing. Minimal shape, all ordinary schema: simple `A`,
  simple `B` restricting `A`, complex `C` = `simpleContent extension of B`,
  `<element head type="A" block="extension"/>`, `<element member type="C"
  substitutionGroup="head"/>`. Spec says clause 2.3 FAILS (involved =
  `{extension}`, union member (1) = `{extension}`); the code answered `true`.
  The arbiter then reproduced the consequence end-to-end: put the two into an
  `<xs:choice>` and the branch charges `cos-nonambig` against a schema the spec
  accepts — **exactly the two-sided-safety hazard the deleted
  under-approximation existed to prevent**, and the reason this was
  rejection-grade rather than a nit.
- **Three secondary round-1 findings, all repaired.** (a) A comment charged
  chain-existence to *"e-props-correct clause 4 (c-vs-sg)"* as if this repo
  enforced it — **it does not**: no check function implements clause 4, and
  `{substitution group exclusions}`, the property clause 4 reads, was stored and
  exposed but read by no constraint. The diff's own prose pointed at an
  enforcement that does not exist. (b) Both `GAP(subst)` markers were deleted
  while that real incompleteness went untracked, against STYLE P3. (c) Three
  minors: an over-long reflowed comment in `contentrestricts.go`, two
  now-**dead** name-equality guards left over from the old split
  (`wildcardadmit.go:195`, `contentrestricts.go:479` — `inSubstitutionGroupOf`
  folds clause 1 in ahead of any index lookup), and `blocked :=
  h.disallowedSubstitutions` aliasing the declaration's unexported slice.
- **The repair, bounded to what the reject specified.** The `*SimpleType` arm
  now **contributes nothing to either set and CONTINUES the walk** via
  `(*SimpleType).Base()` — correct because §3.16.1 gives a Simple Type
  Definition neither `{derivation method}` nor `{prohibited substitutions}`, and
  clause 2.3's union member (3) is explicitly *"(respectively the empty set)"*.
  The comment now states honestly that the predicate is **silent** when no
  ·derivation· exists (a blocking clause blocks nothing over an empty set;
  requiring the chain would be legislating a different rule — the arbiter
  affirmed this reading in both rounds and asked only that the *justification*
  change) and **claims no enforcement elsewhere**. In its place a new
  `// GAP(xsd)` quotes `c-vs-sg` verbatim (`xmlschema11-1.md:1521`) and says
  plainly that the rule is enforced **nowhere in this repo**, that a member
  whose `{type definition}` does not ·derive· from its head's is accepted today,
  and that the finding is recorded in the #249 arbiter review. Two new tests
  pin the fix — `TestSubstitutionGroupClause23SimpleTypeOnDerivationChain` and
  `TestSubstitutionGroupClause23SimpleTypeChainNotReachingHead` — and the
  arbiter mutation-verified **both**: reintroducing `case *SimpleType: return
  true` fails the first; flipping the nil-base exit to `false` fails the second,
  so the second is not redundant even though it also passes under the old bug.
  `break walk` is labelled, not bare — a bare `break` would now leave the
  `switch` and loop forever, and the arbiter checked that specifically.
- **`origin/main` advanced mid-repair (#264, `cos-ct-extends`) and the merge was
  clean** — `xsd/resolve.go` auto-merged with no conflict, carrying the branch's
  clause-2.3 acyclicity note and `facts`-parameter removal alongside main's
  Phase D `cos-ct-extends` prose. The arbiter verified `git diff 764304b
  ba90bde` contains **only** main's #264 work, and that all `docs/` content on
  this branch arrives with the merge rather than from either mason commit.
- **Round 2 — ACCEPT.** Full gate green in **both** rounds: `go build ./...`,
  `go test ./...`, `go vet ./...`, `golangci-lint run` (0 issues), and
  `go test ./conformance -run TestConformance -count=1`. Two **non-blocking**
  comment-staleness findings were recorded rather than fixed, and are item 1 of
  Next below: (a) `xsd/substitutiongroup.go:163-171`'s "anonymous H" paragraph
  has one sentence stale after the repair — *"every step past the first is a
  named `{base type definition}` and so a `Schema.Type` point lookup"* is no
  longer literally true now that the `*SimpleType` arm steps by `Base()` pointer
  deref; **the paragraph's conclusion still holds** via its other clause
  (`sameTypeDefinition` rejects anonymity), so this is a comment-accuracy
  defect, not a behaviour defect; (b) `:156-158`'s GAP text *"consulted by no
  constraint"* went **marginally** stale under the merge, because #264's
  `elementDeclarationsIdentical` (`complexextension.go:705`) now compares
  `{substitution group exclusions}` for property identity — the substantive
  claim (clause 4 is never enforced as a **blocking** keyword check) is intact.
- **Friction, and it is a pattern worth the retro's attention rather than a
  one-off.** The round-1 defect is the `if !ok { return true }` reflex on a type
  assertion — and **the issue body's own Notes named that exact trap in advance**:
  *"Write it so #340 needs no re-derivation: switch the sum, don't special-case
  'simple'."* The oracle's GROUNDING said it a second time, in its EDGE CASES:
  *"don't panic/zero-value crash on a simple intermediate, just contribute
  nothing to either set."* Two independent written warnings, one repair round
  spent anyway. The distinguishing process fact against the immediately
  preceding session: **#249 had no design pre-flight and no warden pre-flight**
  — the thread runs grounding → implementation → verdict — whereas #264, which
  accepted on round 1, had both, and its entry banks three of the warden's five
  findings as *"defects in a plan, caught before a line was written"*. One
  contrast is not a law, but the two sessions are adjacent, comparable in size,
  and differ in exactly that step. Recorded for `/retro` to weigh, **not**
  proposed here as a rule change.
- **Trailer accuracy: `Ratchet: unchanged` is accurate, the NINTH consecutive
  accurate trailer** since the #231/#232 pattern (#235, #344, #331, #240, #276,
  #241, #246, #264, now #249). Tenth carry of Next item 3.
- Next:

## 2026-08-02 — #251 — cmd: `-h`/`-help`/`--help` in ANY argument position, and the bare invocation, stop hitting the not-implemented stub and print the usage contract to stdout at exit 0; the stub path is carved around rather than rewritten, `doc.go` was reconciled in the SAME commit under T6, and the arbiter accepted on round 1 with the ratchet proved flat by md5 rather than by argument

- Shipped on `wip/issue-251` as one mason commit on top of `2421d17` —
  `87c2adc` (`cmd: help must never be gated behind unimplemented subcommands
  (#251)`), 3 files, +151/−2: `cmd/goxsd8/main.go`, `cmd/goxsd8/doc.go`, and
  the new `cmd/goxsd8/main_test.go` (86 lines, the package's first test file).
  **Arbiter ACCEPT on the first round, no repair round consumed** —
  `FINDINGS: [none blocking]`, three non-blocking observations, no workflow
  note owed beyond this entry.
- **Ratchet: unchanged — an OBSERVED no-op, not an assumed one.** The
  arbiter's figures, copied not recomputed:
  `md5sum conformance/testdata/expectations/* | md5sum` is
  `322e118c96766c993487c3f38cc8af2b` **both before and after** the
  `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` run, and `git status --porcelain` is empty afterwards. The
  ratchet wrote nothing; no lane moved in either direction. Plain conformance
  ok in 3.13s, the ratchet run ok in 3.61s. Diff touches exactly three
  `cmd/goxsd8/*.go` files: **no `conformance/testdata/expectations/**` edit and
  no `docs/LOG/**` touch** (the chronicler's alone, per #195) — the arbiter
  checked both explicitly.
- **Spec: n/a — a CLI-behaviour bug, not an XSD/XPath/F&O rule.** The
  GROUNDING says so in its first line and cites no rule ID, which is the right
  answer here; `cmd/goxsd8` implements no spec clause. Gate otherwise green:
  build, test, vet, `golangci-lint run` → `0 issues.`
- **Session shape: this was a scheduled `/develop` iteration that spent its
  first step ruling out resumption.** The WIP survey found three pushed
  branches — `wip/issue-195`, `wip/issue-226`, `wip/issue-368` — and **all
  three are discharged, none resumable**: #195 was closed `needs-replan` and
  replanned as #304, #226 was closed `not_planned` as superseded by #344, #368
  is closed completed. That is consistent with the 2026-08-01 entry that
  already recorded them as stale, so the survey re-derived a known fact rather
  than discovering one (see Friction). Work then went to the **oldest open
  `ready` issue**, #251.
- **The defect, stated exactly.** `main` printed the not-implemented stub to
  stderr and `os.Exit(2)` for **every** invocation, unconditionally and before
  any argument inspection — so `goxsd8 -help` reported that goxsd8 is not
  implemented and exited non-zero. Help was gated behind subcommands that do
  not exist yet, which inverts the dependency: the usage contract is the one
  thing a not-yet-implemented CLI *can* honour, and it is precisely what a user
  meeting the stub needs next.
- **The fix is a seam plus a predicate, and it carves AROUND the stub instead
  of rewriting it.** `main` became
  `os.Exit(run(os.Args[1:], os.Stdout, os.Stderr))`, with
  `run(args []string, stdout, stderr io.Writer) int` holding the decision.
  `wantsHelp(args)` is true for zero args or for `-h`/`-help`/`--help` **in any
  position** — justified in a comment by a constraint rather than a
  restatement: no subcommand is implemented, so nothing downstream can claim
  the flag first. On help, a new `const usage` (doc.go's contract rendered as
  plain CLI text) goes to **stdout** and `run` returns 0. Every other input,
  real subcommand or unknown word, keeps the pre-existing stderr stub and exit
  2 **verbatim** — the arbiter's binary check confirms the non-help path is
  byte-for-byte what it was.
- **The stdout write error is consumed; the stderr one is discarded with a
  proof, not a shrug (STYLE S3).** A truncated `Fprint` to stdout returns exit
  2 — the contract's usage/IO code — because help the user never received is
  not a success. The `_, _ = fmt.Fprintln(stderr, notImplemented)` is the only
  dropped error in the diff and its comment establishes that it *cannot*
  matter: the exit code is 2 either way, and stderr is the only channel left on
  which a stderr failure could be reported.
- **`doc.go` was reconciled in the SAME commit, and the arbiter named that as
  the primary reason this landed rather than bounced (STYLE T6).** One new
  paragraph — "Implemented today: …" — states what `go doc` now truthfully
  renders, while the forward-looking contract prose is **relabelled in place
  rather than deleted**. Verified against rendered `go doc -all` output, not
  against the source comment. The counterfactual is worth recording: a
  behaviour change that leaves package doc describing the old behaviour is a
  T6 finding even when the code is right.
- **Exported surface: nothing to justify.** `package main` publishes no library
  surface; `go doc -all ./cmd/goxsd8` renders only the package doc, and the
  only new top-level identifiers are the four `Test*` functions the framework
  requires. `usage`, `notImplemented`, `run` and `wantsHelp` are all
  unexported.
- **The arbiter mutation-tested the new tests rather than reading them, and all
  five mutations died.** Applied to `main.go`, each run against the committed
  test file, tree restored byte-identical afterwards: `len(args) == 0` →
  `return false` (bare invocation stops being help) — caught by `TestRunHelp`;
  dropping `--help` from the recognized set — `TestRunHelp`; `range args` →
  `range args[:1]`, i.e. help only in first position — `TestRunHelp` via the
  `{"validate", "-help"}` case; a failing stdout write returning 0 instead of 2
  — `TestRunHelpWriteFailure`; usage printed to stderr instead of stdout —
  `TestRunHelp`. **The `{"validate", "-help"}` case is the one that pins the
  issue's actual title requirement** — help behind an unimplemented
  subcommand — and it is the mutation a position-naive implementation would
  survive.
- **The real binary was exercised end to end, not just the `run` seam**:
  `[]`, `[-h]`, `[--help]`, `[-help]` all exit 0 with 1142 bytes on stdout and
  empty stderr; `[parse x.xsd]` exits 2 with empty stdout and the stub on
  stderr. Testing through an injected-writer seam is the right design, but it
  can only prove `run` — that `main` wires `os.Args[1:]`/`os.Stdout`/`os.Stderr`
  to it correctly is a claim only the binary can settle.
- **`README.md` was checked and is not stale** — it makes no CLI help or
  exit-code claims, so nothing there needed to move with this change. Recorded
  because "checked, not assumed" is the difference between a doc that happens
  to be right and one known to be.
- **Trailer accuracy: `Ratchet: unchanged` on `87c2adc` is accurate, the TENTH
  consecutive accurate trailer** since the #231/#232 pattern (#235, #344, #331,
  #240, #276, #241, #246, #264, #249, now #251). Eleventh carry of the
  standing `/retro` decision, below.
- Friction — **none charged against the change; one round, one commit, no gate
  churn. The one real friction is upstream of the issue and is now a
  cross-session pattern, not a one-off.** The WIP survey re-derived, from
  scratch, that `wip/issue-195`, `wip/issue-226` and `wip/issue-368` are all
  discharged — three `git ls-remote` hits, three issue lookups, three
  closed-state confirmations — to reach a conclusion the **2026-08-01 entry had
  already written down**. The branches are dead but still pushed, so the
  fixed-name scheme keeps offering them to every subsequent session, and the
  cost recurs per session rather than per branch. That is at least the second
  session paying it. **Recorded for `/retro` to weigh, not fixed here**, and
  noted as plausibly a `kind/tooling` item (PRINCIPLES 27) rather than a doc
  edit: the durable fix is deleting a retired branch at the moment it is
  retired, or teaching the survey to skip branches whose issue is closed.

- Thread record (do not duplicate) —
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/251>: the GROUNDING at

## 2026-08-02 — #401 — xsd/finalize: §3.4.2.4 clause 3's inherited attribute uses become a MATERIALISED property computed once in base-before-derived order, and the lazy per-name `foldedAttributeUse` chain walk is deleted; the arbiter rejected round 1 because the unapplied clause 3.2.2 was a false-REJECT regression rather than the fail-open gap mason argued for, and the repair put the one mapping fact the component model drops — the prohibited names — on the component itself

- Shipped on `wip/issue-401` as two mason commits on top of `3b98af7` (#251) —
  `0628c2d` (`xsd: materialise §3.4.2.4 clause 3's inherited attribute uses at
  finalize (#401)`, 9 files, +523/−145) and `4a66daa` (`xsd: apply §3.4.2.4
  clause 3.2.2 so a prohibited attribute is not inherited past a restriction
  (#401)`, 19 files, +576/−76) — plus this log commit. Branch total against
  `3b98af7`: **23 files, +899/−221**, two of them new
  (`xsd/attributeusefold.go`, `xsd/attributeusefold_test.go`), the rest edits
  to `xsd/complextype.go`, `xsd/complexderivation.go`, `xsd/complexextension.go`,
  `xsd/defaultbinding.go`, `xsd/resolve.go`, `xsd/valueconstraintvalid.go`,
  `parser/produce_complex.go` and eleven test files. **REJECT on round 1,
  ACCEPT on round 2 — one repair round consumed of the two allowed.**
- **Ratchet: unchanged — zero expectation files moved, in either direction, and
  `conformance/testdata/expectations/` was byte-identical to `origin/main`
  before AND after the `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` run, with `git status` still empty
  afterwards.** The arbiter's figures and its verification method, copied not
  recomputed; it measured the same on both rounds. Plain conformance `ok` in
  3.05s. **The arbiter's posted verdict states the movement, not the lane
  totals** — see Next item 1, because that distinction is exactly the standing
  trailer-accuracy question and this is the first landing where it has a
  concrete edge.
- **The issue is the split of #265 section 2 and inherits a hard constraint from
  #262's accept verdict: the replacement must be strictly STRONGER than
  `foldedAttributeUse`, not merely different.** That function was added in
  #262's *repair* round, not its design, to stop `checkRestrictionAttributes`
  false-rejecting the valid chain `A(@x) <- B(inherits, re-declares nothing) <-
  C(re-declares @x)` — a real bug the full gate did not catch. Predecessors
  #262, #263 and #264 were already closed; sections 1 (`cos-ns-subset` for the
  wildcard half) and 3 stay with #265 and do not gate this.
- **What landed, and why it is a property completion rather than a cache.**
  `(*Schema).foldAttributeUses` in the new `xsd/attributeusefold.go` walks the
  finalized type definitions and OVERWRITES each Complex Type Definition's
  `{attribute uses}` with the spec's set: the type's own uses (clauses 1 and 2,
  which the producer can and does apply from one `<complexType>` element)
  unioned with those inherited from the resolved `{base type definition}`, less
  what clauses 3.2.1 and 3.2.2 exclude. It is **the one mutation the finalize
  pass performs**, and the doc argues the distinction explicitly: a cache would
  hold state derivable from a QName plus an index (STYLE D3), whereas this
  replaces a partial value with its correct one, leaving the spec's set with
  exactly ONE encoding and the producer's under-approximation gone rather than
  kept beside it. The recursion is memoised through `attributeUseFold`
  (`position`/`uses`/`folded`, a parameter object discarded when the pass
  returns) and **carries no visited set** — Phase B has already established
  acyclicity, and ·xs:anyType·'s licensed self-derivation (§3.4.7,
  `any-type-itself`) is excluded by position (`j != i`) rather than by a guard
  (PRINCIPLES 5, STYLE 5). Phase order is load-bearing and says so: after Phase
  A so a base-name miss in `position` is a simple or absent base and never a
  dangling reference, after Phase B for the acyclicity, and before Phase D,
  which is the first phase to READ the property.
- **What was deleted, which is the actual point of the issue.**
  `foldedAttributeUse` — #262/#264's lazy, single-name, search-order-dependent
  reconstruction at each read site — together with its call sites. Four
  consumers were repointed at the stored property:
  `checkRestrictionRequiredAttributes` (`derivation-ok-restriction` c-ran),
  `checkAttributeUseNamesUnique` (`ct-props-correct` clause 4),
  `checkExtensionAttributeUses` (`cos-ct-extends` c-cte), and
  `attributeDefaultBinding` case 2 (`xsd/defaultbinding.go`). The T4/D3 charge
  is discharged directly: one relation had two encodings, and the lazy one
  under-approximated for **every consumer that quantifies over the whole set**
  rather than looking up a single name.
- **Arbiter round 1 — REJECT, and finding 1 is the instructive one: mason's
  fail-open argument failed on its own terms, in the categorically worse
  direction.** The fold shipped without clause 3.2.2 (the
  `<attribute use="prohibited">` block) and defended the gap with the claim that
  *"every consumer of the set charges on a member that is MISSING"*. That is
  false for `checkAttributeUseNamesUnique`, which charges on an **extra** member
  — and not applying 3.2.2 **adds** members. The two compose into a false
  reject, which the arbiter reproduced through the real producer rather than
  arguing: `A(@x)` <- `B` restricting `A` with `<attribute name="x"
  use="prohibited"/>` <- `E` extending `B` and declaring its own `@x`. Per
  clause 3.2.2 `B.{attribute uses}` is empty, so clause 3.1 gives `E` exactly
  one use for `x` and the schema is **valid**; `origin/main` accepted it,
  `0628c2d` charged `ct-props-correct` clause 4 on a duplicate the source never
  wrote. So this was a **behavioural regression introduced by the branch**, and
  `origin/main` had been right here only by accident of walking a type's own
  uses alone. The arbiter's framing is worth keeping: *"the corpus does not
  happen to contain the shape (which is why the ratchet did not move) … but
  'ratchet unchanged' is luck, not evidence of soundness"*, and the invariant
  the repair had to restore is **an unapplied clause 3.2.2 may cost a
  rejection, never fabricate one**.
- **Round-1 finding 2 is a different failure mode and the more subtle one: new
  prose that CERTIFIES a pre-existing gap as safe.** The rewritten paragraph in
  `xsd/defaultbinding.go` and its mirror in `parser/produce_complex.go` claimed
  the unfolded extension `{attribute wildcard}` *"can only withhold a binding
  the caller would have compared, never invent one: it is FAIL-OPEN, never a
  false reject"*. `checkRestrictionAttributes` charges
  `derivation-ok-restriction` **precisely when `attributeDefaultBinding` returns
  `!ok`** — a withheld binding on the B side *is* the rejection. Reproduced on
  `origin/main` and on `0628c2d` alike with
  `A(<anyAttribute ##any lax>)` <- `B`(extension adding nothing) <-
  `C`(restriction declaring `@foo`). The *behaviour* is pre-existing and out of
  scope (#265 section 3); the *claim* was new, replaced text that was
  stale-but-true, and repeated the same reasoning error as finding 1. The
  arbiter's instruction was to restate honestly or file the follow-up — **not to
  fix the behaviour**.
- **The repair, bounded to what the reject specified.** `parser` now records the
  expanded names its own `<attribute>` children give `use="prohibited"` via a
  new unexported `prohibitedAttributeNames` helper, and threads them onto the
  component through `NewComplexType`. The fact is consulted **once, at that
  type's own fold step**, and never walked up a chain — which is precisely what
  the oracle's grounding predicted, since clause 3.2/3.2.2 is defined per single
  derivation step against the immediate base's already-computed property, and
  each level's stored set is itself exclusion-correct. The extension branch
  ignores the names, as §3.4.2.4's Note directs. The two false "fail-open"
  paragraphs became a `GAP(xsd)` marker naming the charging site and pointing at
  #265 section 3, and the two non-blocking findings were fixed in the same
  round: `ComplexType.AttributeUses`' doc stopped saying "the FULL clause 3
  property" (it now says NAMED type, names the 3.2.1/3.2.2 exclusions, and
  carries a `GAP(xsd)` for the nested-anonymous case reachable through
  `SchemaBuilder` but not through the parser), and `xsd/valueconstraintvalid.go`
  documents that Phase E's quantification WIDENED — an inherited use's
  `au-props-correct` clause 3 violation is now re-checked once per derived type
  and charged against the derived type's `Loc`/owner phrase.
- **`prohibitedAttributeNames` is a retained mapping INPUT, not a second
  encoding — and that is the whole justification for the one exported-surface
  delta.** Structures line 843: an `<attribute use='prohibited'>` *"does not map
  to, or correspond to, any schema component at all"*, so no property records it
  and absence from `{attribute uses}` **cannot distinguish "never declared" from
  "explicitly prohibited"** — exactly the distinction clause 3.2.2 turns on. The
  warden's API review (APPROVE, no blocking findings) confirmed this is D3-clean
  rather than a duplicate fact, and ruled out all four alternatives on the
  record: `produceAttributeUse` returns before building anything, so no trace
  survives for a post-hoc fold; `SchemaBuilder` exposes only
  `AddType(TypeDefinition)`; a QName-keyed side table dies on anonymous types;
  and a `WithProhibitedAttributeNames` copier would open a **second construction
  path** to a value documented as single-path-immutable (T1/T7).
- **Exported surface: the delta is exactly ONE signature — `NewComplexType`'s
  new `prohibitedAttributeNames []QName` parameter — plus doc prose on
  `ComplexType`, `NewComplexType` and `AttributeUses`.** Verified twice
  independently: the warden over `go doc -all ./xsd ./parser`, the arbiter over
  `go doc -all -short` across all nine packages (`./xsd ./parser ./loader
  ./validate ./value ./xsderr ./builtin ./codec ./codegen`). `./parser`'s
  exported doc is **byte-identical**. Everything in `xsd/attributeusefold.go`
  and the new producer helper is unexported. The warden also cleared the
  positional-parameter hazard specifically: 13 parameters is at the legibility
  limit, but the mis-order risk is same-typed adjacency and there is none —
  `[]AttributeUse`, `[]QName`, `*Wildcard` are mutually distinct, so any
  transposition is a compile error, and placement immediately after
  `attributeUses` reads as the pair it is. It was explicit that this is **the
  last positional slot to wave through**.
- **Round 2 — ACCEPT, and the arbiter re-verified by REPRODUCTION across three
  worktree states rather than by reading the repair.** The finding-1 schema was
  driven through `parser.Parse` at `origin/main` (ACCEPT), `0628c2d` (false
  reject), and `4a66daa` (ACCEPT); the finding-2 schema was confirmed identical
  on `origin/main`, establishing that restating rather than fixing was the right
  call. The four new tests were **mutation-tested** — reverting
  `slices.Contains(prohibited, name)` out of `inheritAttributeUses` fails
  `TestAttributeUsesFoldProhibited`,
  `TestCTPropsCorrectExtensionOverProhibitingRestriction`,
  `TestDerivationOKRestrictionProhibitedRequired` and
  `parser.TestProhibitedAttributeNotInheritedPastRestriction` — and, the part
  that matters, **each test's control rows still REJECT under the mutation**, so
  the new accept was not bought by weakening `ct-props-correct` clause 4. That
  was the exact hazard the first verdict named. Full gate green both rounds:
  build/test/vet, `golangci-lint run` `0 issues.`, conformance `ok`.
- **Spec fidelity was checked against `docs/specs/md/xmlschema11-1.md`, not
  against the diff, by both reviewers, and it turned on §3.4.2.4's scoping
  Note**: *"references to 'the [children]' refer to the [children] of the
  `<extension>` or `<restriction>` element … otherwise to the [children] of the
  `<complexType>` source declaration itself"*. `prohibitedAttributeNames(parent)`
  walks direct children only — no descent through `<attributeGroup ref>`, no
  base — and the three call sites pass exactly the elements the Note names. Name
  computation goes through the same `localTargetNS(el, "attributeFormDefault")`
  as `produceLocalAttribute`, so blocked names cannot drift from
  `attributeUseName`'s `form=`/`targetNamespace=` collation; verified end to end
  for both the `ref=` and the qualified `name=` form.
- **Surprises.**
  1. **The oracle's grounding predicted the no-marker design and the repair
     needed one anyway — and both are right.** The grounding argued (correctly)
     that clause 3.2.2 needs no fact surviving *past the level that applied it*,
     because each level's stored set is already exclusion-correct. What it did
     not say, because it was answering a different question, is that the level
     applying it still needs to KNOW the name at fold time, and the fold runs
     after the producer is gone. The marker that landed is the narrow one the
     grounding's reasoning permits — per-type, consulted once, never propagated
     — not the chain-walked one it warned against.
  2. **A dead check got LESS dead, and another stayed dead, in the same diff.**
     `checkRestrictionRequiredAttributes`' second arm was documented-unreachable
     in round 1's judgment-call table; the repair makes it **reachable** (if B
     requires `@x` and T prohibits it, an element valid against T omits `x` and
     is invalid against B) and the doc was updated to say so.
     `checkExtensionAttributeUses` remains unreachable — clause 3.1 copies B's
     entire materialised set into T, and a re-declaring extension is charged by
     `checkCTPropsCorrectResolved` first, an ordering the arbiter verified
     directly in `checkComplexDerivations` — and is kept per S3/P3 as the
     executable statement of c-cte clause 1.2.
  3. **A rejection got narrowly WIDER as a side effect and it is correct.** A
     prohibited `<attribute ref="undeclared:x"/>` now charges `src-resolve`
     where it was previously ignored, because `prohibitedAttributeNames` calls
     `resolveQName`. That fails only on a prefix with no in-scope binding — a
     genuine XML namespace error, never a "target does not exist" check — so the
     widening is narrow, documented on the helper, and was cleared explicitly.
  4. **All 14 mechanical test edits are a `nil` at the new parameter position**
     and the arbiter inspected every one: no assertion weakened, no case
     dropped. Worth recording because a signature change touching eleven test
     files is exactly where a quiet weakening would hide.
- Friction — **one repair round, and the cost is attributable to a single
  reasoning habit, not to the design.** Both blocking findings are the SAME
  error: asserting a gap is fail-open from the shape of one consumer instead of
  quantifying over all of them. Finding 1 checked the consumers that charge on a
  *missing* member and missed the one that charges on an *extra* one; finding 2
  certified a withheld binding as harmless at a site whose rejection condition
  **is** the withheld binding. Both were caught by reproduction in seconds once
  someone thought to try, and neither the full gate nor the ratchet could catch
  either — the corpus does not contain the shapes. **This is the second
  consecutive session whose repair round was a fail-open claim that was actually
  fail-closed** (#249 round 1 was a clause-2.3 walk that ABANDONED and returned
  `true`, likewise argued safe), which makes it a candidate pattern rather than
  a one-off, and it is handed to `/retro` in Next item 2 rather than fixed here.
  No gate churn, no branch races, `origin/main` did not advance mid-session, and
  nothing was re-derived that a prior entry had already written down.
- Next:
  1. **[NEW — for `/retro`, and it sharpens the ELEVENTH-carry item below rather
     than replacing it] The arbiter's verdict verified `unchanged` by
     BYTE-IDENTITY; mason's trailer on `4a66daa` states per-lane pass counts
     (`schema 4257/15432, datatypes 1107/1129`) that the posted verdict does not
     restate.** The claim that matters — no lane moved — is independently
     verified and the trailer is accurate, making this the **eleventh
     consecutive accurate trailer** (#235, #344, #331, #240, #276, #241, #246,
     #264, #249, #251, now #401). But this is the first landing where the
     trailer carries a figure the verdict does not, which is the structural
     blind spot #232's entry enumerated, in the concrete. Either the verdict
     format should restate the totals it verified, or trailers should carry only
     what the arbiter states.
  2. **[NEW — for `/retro`; a PATTERN, not a finding against this diff] Two
     consecutive sessions burned their one repair round on a "fail-open" claim
     that was fail-CLOSED** (#249's clause-2.3 abandonment, #401's clause 3.2.2
     and wildcard prose). The smallest durable fix probably lives in STYLE 9 or
     in the mason prompt: a fail-open marker must name **every** consumer of the
     value and state which direction each charges in, because a gap is fail-open
     only against the whole consumer set. Both sessions' claims would have died
     immediately under that requirement.
  3. **[NEW — candidate for filing at the post-land pass] A stale
     `foldedAttributeUse` reference survives in
     `xsd/complexextension.go:606`**, inside
     `typeDefinitionSlotsIdentical`'s fail-open rationale (*"c-cte routinely
     compares a base's own attribute use with itself — foldedAttributeUse
     returns exactly that whenever T does not re-declare the name"*). The
     function is deleted; the mechanism is now the materialised fold, so the
     paragraph's **conclusion still holds** and this is a comment-accuracy
     defect, not a behaviour defect. Found by the chronicler at landing, caught
     by neither reviewer, and NOT fixed here — the tree the arbiter accepted is
     `4a66daa` and editing it now would land something unjudged. Same shape as
     #249's own Next item 1, which is still unfiled.
  4. **[NEW — warden non-blocking, PRE-EXISTING and surfaced by this work; wants
     its own issue at the next backlog pass] `produceAttributeUse` returns
     before `valueConstraintOf`, so `src-attribute` clause 5 (`fixed` +
     `use="prohibited"` forbidden) and clause 3 are NEVER charged for a
     prohibited `<attribute>`.** The new helper deliberately does not re-charge
     them — the right call for this diff — but the hole is real. Not to be
     confused with the two gaps below, which are already tracked.
  5. **[NEW — warden non-blocking, minor] Two shape limits recorded as
     tripwires**: `produceAttributeUses` is now a 4-tuple and a **fifth** result
     should become a small unexported struct; a **fourteenth** `NewComplexType`
     parameter should trigger a params struct rather than another positional
     slot. Also `TestComplexTypeSlicesDoNotAlias` was not extended to the new
     slice parameter — the defensive copy is present and correct and, with no
     getter, the aliasing is unobservable from outside the package, so the
     arbiter recorded it as a coverage note rather than a gap.
  6. **[ALREADY TRACKED — flag as still open, do NOT re-file as new]** (a) The
     **§3.4.2.5 clause 2.2 extension-base-wildcard fold** is **#265 section 3**
     and is the source of the pre-existing false reject in round-1 finding 2;
     the `GAP(xsd)` markers now name it. (b) The **§3.4.2.1 clause 1
     `{assertions}` fold** has the same shape as what landed here and is
     **#346**. Whichever of #265's remaining sections lands next re-greps the
     markers in `xsd/complexderivation.go`, per the issue body's own
     instruction.
  7. **[carried, still unowned — TWELFTH carry] `/retro` must DECIDE on the
     trailer-accuracy fix enumerated in #232's entry** — file it or dismiss it
     on the record. Item 1 above is the first concrete instance in eleven
     sessions and should settle it either way; a twelfth undecided carry is the
     only outcome that is certainly wrong.
  8. **[carried, still unowned] The standing leak list**, repeated verbatim so
     the follow-up audit sees it and not only the entries that created it:
     #246's pre-merge-CI advisory, #241's unmet "stated rule" half, #238's two
     follow-ups, #309's three non-blocking findings, #277's `STYLE T7`/`D6`
     citation-drift decision, #240's foreign-namespace-host prescan advisory,
     #276's scan-scoped `closureScan.unresolved` coarseness note, #249's two
     comment-staleness corrections and its `e-props-correct` clause 4 /
     `c-vs-sg` `GAP(xsd)`, and #251's two items (`TestUsageCoversContract`'s
     four-substring drift guard, and the `notImplemented` stub as untracked P3
     debt). **None has a tracking issue. Neither #249 nor #251 has a post-land
     retirement commit on `main` as of this landing**, so both sessions'
     post-land decisions are still owed on top of this one's.

## 2026-08-02 — /retro (weekly, fourth) — the twelfth-carry trailer question DECIDED and fixed at the squash step; the fail-open-direction pattern fixed as STYLE P3a; 2 ledger leaks filed as #404/#405; repair rate up 1.5×, schema lane +1260

- Trigger: weekly `/retro` over `ef77dda..b5b5598` (the window since the third
  retro, which itself landed folded into `c3a8eb8` rather than as its own
  commit). Evidence: **42 in-window LOG entries** (a 43rd, #182's, documents a
  landing at or before `ef77dda` and belongs to the previous window), `git log`
  (**50 commits = 38 issue landings + 12 `meta:`/`docs: retire` bookkeeping**),
  the `needs-replan` label query (**empty, 0 issues** — verified, not assumed),
  the full `ready` list (73), and the verdict/post-land comment threads on
  #354, #396, #400, #401, #403 — which is where this window's real evidence
  lives, exactly as the duty text warns. Chronicler Part 1 only; the steward's
  Part 2 architecture audit is delegated separately.

- **Recurring friction #1 (DECIDED and FIXED here) — a `Ratchet:` trailer
  written by anyone but the arbiter is an unfalsifiable prediction, and the LOG
  had carried the undecided question TWELVE consecutive times.** This is the
  longest-lived open item in the project's history and the retro's first duty
  was to end it either way.
  - **The record, both directions.** Two false trailers, both on 2026-07-31 and
    both corrected at land: #231 shipped `Ratchet: unchanged` against a real
    schema `+7`, #232 the same against a real `+2`. Since then **eleven
    consecutive accurate trailers** (#235, #344, #331, #240, #276, #241, #246,
    #264, #249, #251, #401), including #276's adversarial case — a trailer
    written across a reject/repair boundary that predicted a **downward** move
    and named the four cases it expected to lose, which is what handed the
    arbiter its own REJECT evidence.
  - **Eleven correct guesses do not make the twelfth verifiable, and #401 gave
    the question its first concrete edge.** Mason's `4a66daa` carried
    `Ratchet: unchanged (schema 4257/15432, datatypes 1107/1129 …)`; the
    arbiter's ACCEPT verified `unchanged` by **byte-identity** and stated **no
    per-lane totals at all**. So the branch trailer carried a figure the verdict
    does not — accurate, and unfalsifiable by anything its writer could run.
  - **Entry point = the squash step (WORKFLOW step 6.2), not mason.** Any fix
    shaped as a diligence requirement on mason is the wrong fix: the instrument
    does not exist for mason **by design**, and eleven-for-eleven is evidence of
    care, which is not a mechanism. #354's `## Notes` says this in terms and the
    evidence never contradicted it.
  - **Smallest durable fix applied in this `meta: retro 2026-08-02` commit** —
    two bullets in WORKFLOW.md's "The ratchet" section plus one cross-reference
    where a mason actually looks: (a) *a read-only conformance PASS is evidence
    of NO REGRESSION only, never of unchanged counts*, naming the
    `Improved`-passes-silently mechanism (#354 candidate **(b)**); (b) *the
    landed `Ratchet:` line carries only what the arbiter's accept verdict
    states, in the verdict's own terms* — branch commits may and should predict,
    but at squash time the prediction is **replaced**, not confirmed, and any
    figure the verdict does not state is dropped (the narrower **fourth shape**
    the #401 post-land pass proposed, which is strictly smaller than (a) or (c)
    and needs no change on the arbiter's side); (c) one line under "Commit
    format" pointing at it. **#354 closed by this landing.**
  - Deliberately NOT done: candidate (a)'s `Ratchet: predicted … (unverified)`
    syntax, which would have taxed every branch commit to fix a defect that
    surfaces once per twelve landings at the squash; and candidate (c)'s issue-
    template change, which is #315's territory and would have widened the same
    prose from two directions at once — the #195 failure mode verbatim.
  - Scope check: WORKFLOW.md's ratchet **section** is ordinary process text.
    CLAUDE.md's "one rule" and `arbiter.md`'s ratchet-integrity section were
    **not touched**, and the fix is deliberately one-sided so that it never
    needs to be — constitutional guardrail respected.

- **Recurring friction #2 (FIXED here) — a gap's error direction is asserted
  from the shape of the consumers the author happened to have in view.** Two
  consecutive sessions, both burning their single repair round on it, and the
  gate and the ratchet are structurally blind to both.
  - **#401 (08-02), arbiter REJECT round 1, two independent instances in one
    diff.** Finding 1: the new fold's marker claimed *"every consumer of the set
    charges on a member that is MISSING"* — false for
    `checkAttributeUseNamesUnique`, which charges on an **extra** member, and
    leaving clause 3.2.2 unapplied **adds** members. Reproduced as a real false
    reject through `parser.Parse`. Finding 2: new prose certified an unfolded
    extension `{attribute wildcard}` as *"FAIL-OPEN, never a false reject"* at a
    site — `checkRestrictionAttributes`, charging on `!ok` — **whose rejection
    condition IS the withheld binding**.
  - **#249 (08-02), arbiter REJECT round 1.** A `cos-equiv-derived-ok-rec`
    clause-2.3 walk that ABANDONED at a simple type and returned `true`, argued
    safe on the identical reasoning.
  - **The arbiter's framing is the one worth keeping**: *"the corpus does not
    happen to contain the shape (which is why the ratchet did not move) … but
    'ratchet unchanged' is luck, not evidence of soundness."* Both claims died
    in seconds under reproduction once someone thought to try.
  - **Entry point = mason's implementation, at the moment the marker text is
    written** — caught at the verdict, at the cost of the round. Already filed
    as **#403** by the #401 post-land pass, with three candidate fixes and an
    explicit *"pick one; do not do all three."*
  - **Fix applied: candidate (a), a new STYLE.md `P3a`.** A marker or comment
    asserting a direction must list the readers of the value it withholds or
    adds, **by their actual identifiers**, and state which direction each
    charges in — because a gap is fail-open only against the WHOLE consumer set,
    and the unenumerated "every consumer of X …" is precisely the sentence
    produced by checking only what was in view. Chose (a) over (b) (a mason
    pre-handoff check: same content, but reachable only by an agent reading its
    own prompt, and STYLE is what the arbiter cites) and over (c) (an arbiter
    checklist row, which the issue itself calls the weakest — it does not stop
    the claim being written). **#403 closed by this landing.**
  - CLAUDE.md's style headline 9 is a mirror of STYLE 9, not of P3, and stays
    correct unedited — no second encoding minted (T4).

- **Observed, real, and deliberately NOT fixed — the post-land pass's
  completion signal.** The 07-26 retro promoted "a second skipped post-land
  pass" to the next retro's durable-fix slot. What this window produced instead
  is sharper and already better diagnosed than a retro clause could make it:
  #249's pass **ran in full** on GitHub and left no commit on `main`, and the
  next session — reasoning correctly from `git log` — recorded it as skipped
  **in a committed LOG entry**, then appended two already-tracked items (#395,
  #396) to a standing leak list on that basis. That is a memory-corruption mode,
  it is filed as **#400** with three mutually exclusive mechanisms and a
  demanded decision between them, and it needs a session with a gate, not a
  retro clause. **Not re-filed, not duplicated; flagged as the leading candidate
  for a develop iteration.**

- **Follow-up ledger audit — 2 confirmed leaks, filed as #404 and #405; the
  rest of the standing list is DISCHARGED and the list itself was the problem.**
  - **The headline finding is that the leak list was mostly false.** Every
    2026-08 entry from #241 onward repeats a verbatim "still unowned" block, and
    #400's own reconciliation shows nearly all of it was tracked before it was
    copied forward: #246's pre-merge-CI advisory → **#389**; #241's unmet
    "stated rule" half → **#387**; #277's `STYLE T7`/`D6` citation drift →
    **#382**; #240's foreign-namespace-host prescan → **#384**; #309's three
    non-blocking findings → **#374**/**#375**; #238's follow-ups → **#377**;
    #249's items 1 and 2 → **#396**/**#395**; #251's two items → **#398**;
    #401's items 3 and 4 → **#396** (scope addendum) and **#358**. **A leak list
    that is mostly discharged but copied forward unrevised trains sessions to
    skim it** — which is exactly how #249's items got onto it. Confirmed against

  - **Leak 1 → #404** (`ready`/`kind/gap`/`area/conformance`).
    `closureScan.unresolved` is scan-scoped: it records that SOME directive

    close it on a measurement.
  - **Leak 2 → #405** (`ready`/`kind/refactor`/`area/xsd`+`area/parser`). #401's
    `Next` item 5 — the warden's two positional-shape tripwires
    (`NewComplexType`'s 13 parameters, "the last positional slot to wave
    through"; `produceAttributeUses`' 4-tuple) plus the
    `TestComplexTypeSlicesDoNotAlias` coverage note. Items 1–4 of that harvest
    were dispositioned; item 5 was the only one left living solely in a closed
    issue's review comment, which is #270's premise verbatim.
  - **Dismissed on the record, not filed:** #249's `Next` item 6, *"the
    `PARTIAL` marker question, for `/retro`"* — already settled by the 07-26

  - **Verified NOT leaks** (checked against issue bodies, not entries): #238's

    §3 and #346, both open and both named in the markers.
  - **The 07-26 retro's own five "Next" items, discharged:** (a) the
    `origin/main` base rule ended the stale-ref recurrences — **zero instances
    this window**; (b) #195 was NOT promoted, hit the two-reject cap and parked,
    and is replanned as **#304** — the manual toil it was filed against ended
    anyway when the mason-boundary held for all 38 landings; (c) the repair rate
    did NOT fall back (see metrics) but the reason is legible; (d) STYLE P3's
    broadened marker rule is visibly in use — #396, #398 and #401's own repair
    all cite it, and no new `PARTIAL`/bare-`GAP:` debt appeared; (e) **#270 is
    still open and undischarged** — flagged for the cartographer, not re-filed.

- **Metrics (window `ef77dda..b5b5598`, 2026-07-27..08-02, vs the third retro's
  2026-07-19..-26):**
  - Sessions per landing commit: 42 entries / 38 landings ≈ **1.11** (was 1.19,
    1.26, 1.35) — a fourth consecutive tightening, and the tightest yet.
  - Repair rounds per accept: **11 / 38 ≈ 0.29** (was 0.19) — **up 1.5×**. The

    (#236's repair round was a **warden** round, not an arbiter reject, and is

    07-26 retro earned: the window ran the M4 parser/finalize frontier at
    unprecedented volume — 38 landings against 26 — and **two** of the eleven

    contains its own diagnosis.
  - Rejects per accept: 11 single rejects + **2 issues at the two-reject park

    denominator growing 55 rows); `instance` untouched at 0 / 26426 (M5

    surrendered **69 wrong-reason schema passes** exposed by its own multi-

    lane, all window.** All figures copied from the arbiter verdicts and the

    rather than acted on**: #347 is now the second-oldest undecided process
