    cartographer's instrument, not the chronicler's.

- Ratchet: **unchanged** (two process docs; no Go file, no expectations file
  touched, no `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` run — the chronicler cannot and did not run it).
- Decisions: **decided #354 by acting on it**, on the narrowest of the four
  candidate shapes, because a thirteenth undecided carry was the only outcome
  certainly wrong and because #401 finally supplied a concrete instance to size
  the fix against. Took #403's candidate (a) over (b) and (c) for reach: STYLE
  is what the arbiter cites in a verdict, an agent prompt is not. Did NOT fix
  the post-land-signal pattern — #400 already specifies it better than a retro
  clause could, and picking between its three mechanisms needs a gate. Filed the
  two ledger leaks separately rather than as one issue (unlike #270's bundling),
  because they share no file, no area and no reviewer. Landed both doc fixes in
  ONE `meta: retro` commit per the trigger's own convention, and opened the PR
  against both tracking issues rather than minting a third.
- Surprises: (1) **the leak list was largely fiction.** Nine of its eleven
  standing items already had trackers; the list was copied forward verbatim for
  six entries and grew two *false* items along the way. The audit's value this
  window was subtraction, not addition. (2) The friction that looked worst by
  the numbers — repair rounds up 1.5× — turned out to contain the pattern being
  fixed: two of the eleven rounds are #403's two instances, so the metric partly
  measures the defect the same retro closed. (3) The 07-26 retro's predicted
  durable-fix slot (a second skipped post-land pass) was claimed by something
  adjacent and stranger — a pass that ran and left no trace, which is worse than
  one that was skipped, because it produced a confidently wrong committed claim.
- Friction (this run): none material. `origin/main` was fresh on first fetch
  (the 07-26 fix holding), the `testdata/xsdtests` submodule was absent as
  always and initialized before any gate run — #309's hard failure now makes
  that impossible to miss, which is the second 07-26-era fix visibly working.
- Next:
  1. **`/retro` Part 2 (steward architecture audit)** — delegated separately. It
     should weigh the `xsd` finalize surface after #401 added a **mutating**
     phase (`foldAttributeUses`, the one write the finalize pass performs) and
     the `parser` producer's now-13-parameter component constructors (#405).
  2. **#400 wants a develop iteration, not a retro** — it is the leading
     process defect on the board and it decides between three mechanisms.
  3. **#347 (the `ready` band) is the next twelve-carry candidate.** Six
     backlogs out of band, two of them explicitly deferring the decision.
  4. **#270 is still open and undischarged** from the 07-26 retro; the
     cartographer should split or close it.
  5. Next chronicler retro: (a) confirm the squash-trailer rule actually shows
     up — the observable signal is a landed `Ratchet:` line that quotes the
     verdict's formulation rather than a per-lane figure the verdict never
     stated; (b) confirm STYLE P3a shows up in a new marker's text, or in a
     verdict citing it; (c) watch whether the repair rate falls back once the
     fail-open-direction pattern is closed; (d) watch for a **third** authorized
     downward ratchet correction — two in one window is either a healthy harness
     finally telling the truth or the beginning of a habit, and one more makes
     it worth deciding which.

## 2026-08-02 — /retro Part 2 (steward architecture audit)

- Scope: `origin/main` at `06951ee` (the Part 1 retro commit, #403/#354),
  the window since the 2026-07-26 audit — 38 landings, M4's
  `<import>`/`<override>` composition seam (#182/#183), the finalize
  phases D/E in `xsd` (#228, #263, #264, #249, #401), and the schema lane's
  multi-document gating (#238, #276). Nothing re-flagged that PR #406 fixed.
- Full gate green on the audit commit (build/test/vet/lint/conformance,
  `0 issues.` from golangci-lint); docs-only change. Ratchet untouched and
  unread — the steward never runs it.
- **Pre-1.0 confirmed** (docs/PLAN.md §"v1.0 — the stability line": 1.0 is
  human-declared, expected after M12; M4 is in progress). Mobility policy
  applies: movement is cheap, spend it.

### Per-package verdict

| package | verdict |
| --- | --- |
| `xsderr` | **drift noted** — pure leaf; doc.go's enforcement claim still false (#273) |
| `xsd` | **refactor filed (S-A)**; #271 still open and now wider |
| `parser/xmltree` | **sound** |
| `parser` | **refactor filed (S-A)**; #259/#272 open and growing |
| `loader` | **sound** |
| `value` | **refactor filed (S-D)** |
| `value/backendtest` | **sound** |
| `builtin`, `builtin/strict` | **sound** (but #273 touches `builtin/strict`) |
| `builtin/native` | **sound** (doc-only, correctly marked planned) |
| `regex` | **sound** |
| `conformance` | **refactor filed (S-B)** |
| `validate*`, `xpath`, `codec`, `codegen` | **drift noted → S-C** (doc.go present tense over empty packages) |
| `cmd/goxsd8` | **sound** (contract stub, correctly marked) |

Import graph re-derived with `go list`: **the DAG holds.** `xsderr` imports
nothing; `xsd` imports only `xsderr`; **nothing imports `conformance`**;
`encoding/xml` is imported only by `parser/xmltree` and the (test-only)
harness. No new package, no new edge, no cycle.

### Findings filed (hand-off — the steward has no GitHub tools)

- **S-A, rank 1 — the annotation subsystem is modeled in three packages and
  produced by none.** `xsd/annotation.go` exports `Annotation`/`AppInfo`/
  `Documentation`/`Attr` + constructors + accessors (18 exported
  identifiers); `SchemaBuilder.AddAnnotation`; a trailing
  `annotations []Annotation` parameter on **13** component constructors;
  and `parser/tree.go`'s `Node`/`Text` character-data retention, justified
  in its own comment as existing so `<xs:documentation>` text "round-trips
  through the tree". Evidence: `NewAnnotation`/`NewAppInfo`/
  `NewDocumentation`/`NewAttr` are called **only** from `xsd`'s own tests;
  all 36 `xsd.New*` call sites in `parser` pass `nil`; no accessor is read
  module-wide; `Text.Data` has no caller; docs/PLAN.md contains no
  milestone for populating `{annotations}`. Cost of delay: the slot is
  positional and trailing on 13 constructors — #405's "last positional slot
  to wave through" tripwire, once per constructor — and M5 adds more
  component constructors that will copy the shape. Demands a decision, not
  a cleanup: either file the work to populate §3.15 `{annotations}` in
  `parser`, or unexport the subsystem and drop the 13 slots until a
  milestone claims it. Pre-1.0 mobility.
- **S-B, rank 2 — `builtin.FacetName` is manufactured by string cast at the
  conformance boundary, against the owning package's written policy.**
  `builtin/restriction.go`'s `facetNames` table calls itself "THE bridge
  between the two spellings of one closed set" and states it is
  "deliberately NOT a `FacetKind.String()` round-trip", naming the past bug
  ("the old `facetKind` silently omitted maxScale/minScale, making the pair
  disagree on two of sixteen entries"). `conformance/datatypes.go` does
  exactly that round-trip in both directions — `facetKindOf` matches on
  `k.String()`, and `buildOwnFacets` calls
  `spec.Applies(builtin.FacetName(kind.String()))` — with a comment
  asserting the opposite policy ("never a hand-typed name table; the token
  is `FacetKind.String`'s own output"). Two packages, one conversion,
  contradictory stated rules. Failure mode is silent and lands in the
  ratchet's own currency: a spelling that ever diverges makes `Applies`
  answer false and the cohort `return nil, false` — a silent DECLINE, an
  invisible lane loss, where `builtin`'s table would have shown a gap.
  (The 13-of-16 subset in `conformance.facetKinds` is deliberate and
  documented — **not** part of this finding.) Direction: export the bridge
  from `builtin` and delete the harness copies. Pre-1.0 mobility.
- **S-C, rank 3 — the STYLE T6 sweep is incomplete: five package docs state
  a contract in present tense over an empty package.** `validate`,
  `validate/bersrc`, `xpath`, `codec`, `codegen` export **nothing** (`go
  doc` renders no identifier) yet open with "Package validate assesses…",
  "The engine consumes marker interfaces…", "Package codec is the dataset
  serializer…", "Package codegen emits Go source…". T6 requires the heading
  to say so, and the sibling packages already comply —
  `validate/xmlsrc` ("Planned contract (M5 — not yet implemented)"),
  `validate/jsonsrc`, `builtin/native`, `cmd/goxsd8`. So this is
  inconsistency with an established in-repo convention, not a new rule.
  Cheap now, staler every milestone; a libuser reading godoc is told four
  packages do work they do not do. (Fixed in ARCHITECTURE.md this commit;
  the `doc.go` half is Go code and must be filed.)
- **S-D, rank 4 — `value.LexicalFacet`/`value.ValueFacet` are exported with
  no consumer and no way for one to exist.** Both interfaces are
  implemented only by unexported types inside `value`; the only function
  taking them (`compile`) is unexported; no exported API accepts or returns
  a stage. Meanwhile `value/doc.go` and ARCHITECTURE.md presented them as a
  composition seam ("each stage is a value users can compose for their own
  types"). STYLE T5's exact shape. Either grow the seam or unexport;
  ARCHITECTURE.md is corrected in this commit either way.

### Evidence added to existing issues (not re-filed)

- **#271** (F1, the two-homed circularity rejection) — **the split widened
  exactly as F1 predicted.** `parser/produce.go`'s `buildComplexType` now
  also charges `ct-props-correct` cl. 3, alongside `xsd/resolve.go`'s
  `checkComplexBaseAcyclic`; the code argues it well (two construction
  paths, demand-driven recursion must terminate) but the reason a second
  entry point is needed at all is the eager-pointer representation #271
  removes. Two rules now charged from both packages, not one. And **half
  the ordering window has closed**: F1 said "land before
  `<import>`/`<redefine>`/`<override>`", and #182/#183 landed 2026-07-27
  with the refactor undone. `<list>`/`<union>` in the producer are still
  ahead (`parser/produce.go` declines them) — that is the last cheap moment.
- **#259 / #272** (F2, the duplicated closure walk) — **growing on
  schedule.** `conformance/schema_closure.go` went 315 → 396 lines (+26%)
  between `c3a8eb8` (07-27) and `0b459d1` (08-01) as the composition
  features landed. The two `resolveSchemaLocation` bodies were re-diffed
  and are still byte-identical: the cost so far is the declared coupling,
  not yet divergence.
- **#273** (F3, the unenforced typed-`Rule` currency) — unchanged and
  slightly worse: bare-string rule-ID sites are now **63** (was 60), split
  `builtin/strict` 50 / `value` 13; `IsValidRule` still has zero non-test
  consumers; `xsderr/doc.go`'s "Tests assert that every Rule constructed in
  this module is either in the catalog or a sentinel" is still false. No
  struct-literal bypass of `New`/`Wrap` exists anywhere — that half of the
  invariant does hold.

### Confirmed sound — do not re-flag

- **`xsd`'s ~6,700 zero-export finalize lines are correctly placed.** Thirteen
  files export nothing at all, which reads like a missing `xsd/finalize`
  sub-package. It is not: the machinery reads and writes the components'
  unexported fields (`attributeusefold.go` reads
  `ComplexType.prohibitedAttributeNames` and writes `attributeUses` back),
  so the split would force those onto the exported surface or mint a
  mutator API — a permanently wider public surface and a representable
  illegal state (STYLE T1) traded for a smaller file tree. Recorded as a
  ruling in ARCHITECTURE.md so future audits stop rediscovering it.
- **The one finalize mutation (`foldAttributeUses`, #401) is sound.** It
  OVERWRITES `{attribute uses}` with its correct value rather than caching
  a derivable one, and the producer's partial value is gone afterwards
  rather than kept beside it — one fact, one encoding (STYLE D3). Phase
  order is documented as load-bearing and is.
- **The three-way split of `cos-st-restricts`** (`xsd` counts/tokens,
  `builtin` applicability, `value` value-space) is a DAG consequence, not
  drift; each half states the other's boundary in its own godoc.
- **`xmltree.Name` vs `xsd.QName`** and **`xmltree.Namespace` vs
  `xsd.NamespaceBinding`** — still exactly two each, still the raw-XML /
  component-model boundary. **No third name+namespace type appeared this
  window**, and `xsderr.Loc` is still the module's only location type.
- **`regex.FlavorFO`** (no consumer until M6) and **`loader.FS`/`HTTP`/
  `Chain`** (no consumer, but declared library surface in `loader/doc.go`)
  are the "documented contract it fulfills" half of T5. Justified.
- The 2026-07-19 `conformance/datatypes.go` cohort-isolation ruling stands;
  `schema_closure.go`'s growth is the F2 seam gap, not cohort accretion.

### Doc corrections landed here

ARCHITECTURE.md, seven:
1. "Ships with two backends" → one ships; `builtin/native` is an M12
   doc-only contract that exports nothing.
2. The facet-pipeline "each stage is a value users can compose for their
   own types" → there is no composition seam (S-D).
3. The `xsd` phases paragraph → records the widened split, names #271,
   and replaces the stale ordering claim with what actually happened.
4. The `parser` seam gap → names #259/#272 and records the +26% growth.
5. Codegen & codec → a status banner (neither package ships anything;
   `value.Emitter`, `AppendCanonical`/`ParseBytes` and the differential
   tests do not exist), and the three present-tense claims requalified.
6. Query & walk → `Walker` (M9) and `Matcher` (M5) marked as absent, matching
   `xsd/doc.go`, which already got this right via #203.
7. New sections: the `xsd` single-package ruling, the finalize-mutation
   note, and a standing **"Exported surface without a consumer"** list so
   each audit re-checks one set instead of rediscovering it.

WORKFLOW.md, one: Part 2 now states that the steward cannot file its own
issues or merge its own PR — its tool set is Read/Grep/Glob/Bash by design
and GitHub was unreachable from that context on **both** audits run so far.
The hand-off to the orchestrating session is the intended seam; the
2026-07-26 entry asked for this line "if it recurs", and it recurred.

### Not fixed here, reported instead

**docs/PLAN.md violates PRINCIPLES 32** ("Ratchet numbers live in
expectations files only. Never copy baseline counts into prose docs;
duplicated numbers drift"). The M3 heading carries "`datatypes` lane 1043
pass / 31 fail (1074)"; the committed lane file says **1107 pass / 22
fail**. The drift is exactly what the principle predicts. Left alone
deliberately: PLAN.md is the cartographer's instrument, the remedy is a
policy call (strip the counts vs. date-stamp them), and it recurs
throughout a 91 KB file. Cartographer's to decide, not the steward's to
edit mid-audit.

- Ratchet: **unchanged** (docs only; no Go file and no expectations file
  touched, no `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` run — the steward cannot and did not).
- Next: (1) the orchestrating session files S-A…S-D as `kind/refactor` and
  posts the evidence comments on #271, #259/#272 and #273; (2) S-A is a
  **decision**, not a task — route it to the cartographer before a mason
  sees it; (3) next audit re-checks the standing exported-surface list in
  ARCHITECTURE.md rather than rebuilding it, and watches whether #271's
  last ordering gate (`<list>`/`<union>` in the producer) closes too.

## 2026-08-02 — #314 — parser: `restrictionFacets`' document-order fold guarantee under interleaving gets its first committed guard, a 5-case table proved failure-capable by mutation; the oracle's key finding was that the cross-KIND ordering is a codebase determinism choice and NOT a spec rule, and a pre-existing APFS case-insensitivity conformance artifact surfaced mid-session and was filed as #417 rather than allowed to block the land

- Shipped on `wip/issue-314` as one mason commit on top of `bddad48` —
  `652ec31` (`parser: pin restrictionFacets' document-order fold guarantee under
  interleaving (#314)`), **1 file, +95/−0, test-only**:
  `parser/produce_test.go` gains `TestProduceRestrictionFacetInterleaving` in
  the external `parser_test` package. No production file touched; the diff adds
  no exported identifier and no behaviour. **Arbiter ACCEPT on the first round,
  no repair round consumed** — two non-blocking advisory findings, neither
  charged as repair work.
- **Ratchet: unchanged.** The arbiter's figure, copied verbatim from the verdict
  and not recomputed: **unchanged**. A test-only diff with no expectations-file
  edit; the arbiter ran the ratchet as part of its accept and it wrote nothing.
  The one wrinkle worth recording is that this run happened *while* two schema-
  lane cases were failing for an environmental reason (see #417 below) and **the
  ratchet tool still refused to write anything** — all six expectation files
  byte-identical after the run, `git status` empty afterwards. Ratchet integrity
  was never at risk, and the tool's downward-refusal behaviour was incidentally
  demonstrated under exactly the condition it exists for.
- **Spec: `xr-pattern` (§4.3.4.2) and Datatypes §4.3.13 (assertions facet)** —
  and the grounding's most useful contribution was a NEGATIVE finding. The two
  positive citations are what the fold implements: pattern children collapse
  into one pattern facet with their values concatenated **"in order"**, and
  assertion children collapse into one assertions facet **"in document order"**.
  But `{facets}` is spec-defined as an **unordered SET** ("A set of Constraining
  Facet components", std-facets), and §4.3 is silent on where a folded facet
  sits among facets of other kinds. So the *cross-kind* list ordering this test
  pins — each fold landing at its kind's FIRST-occurrence position — is a
  **determinism choice of this codebase (STYLE D2), not a spec-mandated rule**.
  That distinction went into the test's own doc comment, so a future reader
  cannot mistake a house invariant for a conformance requirement. Grounding:
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/314#issuecomment-5158992835>.
- **The gap #314 named, stated exactly.** #214 landed the two folds and shipped
  three tests, none of which interleaves the fold kinds with each other or with
  plain one-to-one facets. The ordering guarantee was therefore real in the code
  and unguarded in the suite. The five cases are copied **verbatim from the #214
  arbiter verdict's own interleaving table** rather than re-invented — the
  verdict had already enumerated the shapes it reasoned about; this issue's work
  was to make that reasoning executable.
- **Failure capability was PROVED, not asserted.** Mason temporarily reworked
  `restrictionFacets`' pattern fold into the design **#214 deliberately
  rejected** — a second post-loop `slices.Insert` mirroring the assertions fold,
  instead of the in-loop `facets[patternAt] = folded` rewrite — and **2 of the 5
  subtests fail** under that mutation (`assertion_pattern_pattern` and
  `minLength_assertion_pattern_pattern`): two independent post-loop inserts
  invalidate each other's recorded index, so the second inserts against a slice
  the first already shifted. `produce.go` was then reverted to a state
  byte-identical to `main`. The mechanism is written into the test's
  `FAILURE CAPABILITY:` comment so the proof survives the session.
- **The arbiter reproduced the mutation independently AND went one step
  further.** In its own throwaway worktree it re-derived the 2-of-5 failure, and
  then asked the question the issue did not: is the new test the only guard? It
  applied the mutation **and removed the new test file**, and `go test ./parser`
  **stayed fully green** — #214's original three tests miss this regression
  entirely. That converts "adds coverage" into a measured claim: this file is
  the sole guard against that specific reintroduction. Verdict:
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/314#issuecomment-5159067963>.
- **Surprise — a PRE-EXISTING conformance failure surfaced mid-session and is
  NOT this issue's, NOT this branch's, and NOT a ratchet event.** Two W3C
  schema-lane cases (`MS-Schema2006-07-15/schZ012_b` and `schZ012_c`) fail on
  this session's host. Root cause, found by mason and independently re-derived
  by the arbiter with inode and `git blame` evidence: `schZ012_b2.xsd` imports
  `schemaLocation="Schz012_b.xsd"` — **wrong case** — which on an
  **APFS case-insensitive** filesystem resolves to the same inode as
  `schZ012_b.xsd`, producing a spurious double-import and a false
  `sch-props-correct` clause 2 rejection. On a case-SENSITIVE filesystem the
  import simply fails to resolve (permitted by §4.2.3 clause 2.4) and the case
  passes exactly as committed. It reproduces on a pristine `origin/main`
  worktree, so it predates `wip/issue-314` by every measure. The arbiter filed
  it as **#417** (`kind/bug`, `area/conformance`, `area/loader`) with the full
  evidence, and correctly did **not** let it block this land — an environmental
  artifact of the developer host is not a defect in a 95-line test diff. **It is
  already filed; this entry records its existence, it does not request it.**
- Decisions:
  1. **Test-only, production-code-untouched, deliberately.** #314 asked for a
     guard on an existing guarantee, not a change to it. The mutation was a
     temporary instrument and `produce.go` returned byte-identical to `main`;
     the arbiter verified that rather than taking it on trust.
  2. **The determinism-vs-spec distinction was written into the test, not just
     the issue thread.** A reader who finds this test in two years should not
     have to fetch a GitHub comment to learn that the cross-kind ordering is
     STYLE D2 and not `xr-pattern`.
  3. **#417 was filed as a separate issue instead of folded into #314 or fixed
     inline.** Different area, different cause, unrelated to the diff under
     judgement; folding it in would have made a clean test-only land carry an
     unreviewed loader change.
- Friction: **none material to the change itself** — one round, one commit, no
  repair. The time cost of the session was the #417 detour: a red conformance
  run on an unrelated lane had to be fully root-caused before anyone could say
  it was not caused by this branch, and that took a filesystem-level inode
  investigation plus a pristine-`main` reproduction. That is the correct amount
  of work to spend before dismissing a red gate, but it is worth noting that
  **the suite's behaviour is host-filesystem-dependent and nothing in the repo
  says so** — which is precisely what #417 now tracks.
- Next:
  1. **[filed — no action owed here] #417**, the APFS case-insensitivity
     double-import artifact. Recorded so a later session meeting the same two
     red `schZ012_*` cases recognizes it in seconds instead of re-deriving the
     inode evidence.
  2. **[arbiter non-blocking advisory 1 — candidate for the post-land pass, NOT
     charged as repair] a duplicated facet-kinds-loop pattern.** The
     kinds-collecting loop in the new test repeats a shape already present
     elsewhere in `produce_test.go`; a small helper would carry it. Below the
     bar for a repair round on a 95-line test diff; fold into a nearby parser
     test-hygiene issue or dismiss on the record.
  3. **[arbiter non-blocking advisory 2 — same disposition] one unreachable
     guard in the new test.** The `if at < 0` check after
     `slices.Index(kinds, xsd.FacetPattern)` cannot fire: the preceding
     `slices.Equal(kinds, tc.kinds)` assertion has already established that a
     pattern facet is present in every case. Harmless, but it is dead code in a
     file whose whole purpose is precision about what is and is not guaranteed.
  4. **[carried from the 2026-08-02 retro, still open]** #400 wants a develop
     iteration rather than a retro slot; #347 (the `ready` band) is the next
     twelve-carry candidate; #270 is still undischarged from the 07-26 retro.
     None of these is this session's to close — listed so the count of sessions
     they have survived stays visible.
- Thread record (do not duplicate) —
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/314>: the GROUNDING at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/314#issuecomment-5158992835> (the two
  positive citations plus the std-facets unordered-set finding that reclassified
  the cross-kind ordering as STYLE D2) and the arbiter's ACCEPT verdict at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/314#issuecomment-5159067963> (the
  independently reproduced mutation, the sole-guard experiment, the
  ratchet-wrote-nothing proof, and the two non-blocking advisories). Prior art
  referenced and not re-derived: **#214**, which landed both folds, rejected the
  two-insert design this test's mutation resurrects, and supplied the
  interleaving table the five cases are copied from. Filed out of this session
  and tracked elsewhere: **#417**. This entry is WORKFLOW's "Land" step, written
  on the WIP branch after the verdict and before the PR is opened so it rides in
  the squash; the branch's commits squash into one whose message carries
  `Ratchet: unchanged`.

## 2026-08-02 — #252 — xsd: `*Schema`'s eight §3.17.1 document-order enumeration accessors ship as one closed set, each returning a COPY of its source-of-truth slice; the warden was run BEFORE code and returned `revise` on the planned surface, which turned every one of its eight findings into a doc-contract requirement rather than a repair round, and the arbiter accepted on round 1 after mutation-proving all eight tests

- Shipped on `wip/issue-252` as one mason commit on top of `bdd750b` —
  `a1d2c3a` (`xsd: add document-order enumeration accessors to Schema (#252)`),
  **4 files, +460/−8**: `xsd/schema.go` (+137, the eight methods),
  `xsd/schema_test.go` (+187), `xsd/example_test.go` (+124), `xsd/doc.go`
  (+20/−8, the Query API section reconciled in the SAME commit under T6). The
  eight methods are `Types`, `Elements`, `Attributes`, `AttributeGroups`,
  `ModelGroups`, `Notations`, `IdentityConstraints`, `Annotations`, each a
  one-line `cloneSlice(s.<field>)`. **Arbiter ACCEPT on the first round, no
  repair round consumed** — four non-blocking advisory findings, none charged
  as repair work.
- **Ratchet: unchanged.** The arbiter's figures, copied verbatim from the
  verdict and not recomputed: **schema 4257/15432, datatypes 1107/1129,
  instance 0/26426, ber/json/xpath 0 cases — every lane unmoved.**
  `GOXSD_RATCHET=1 go test ./conformance` was RUN, not assumed, and **wrote
  nothing**: `git status --porcelain` empty afterward, no expectation file
  touched in either direction. Expected for a purely additive query API that
  touches no parse or validation path, but the arbiter's note is the right one
  — a flat ratchet on an additive diff is a measurement, not an inference.
- **Spec: Structures §3.17.1 (the Schema component tableau)** — and the
  grounding's decisive contribution was a distinction, not a citation. The
  Schema component has eight properties, and **seven of them are worded "A set
  of … components"** (type definitions, attribute declarations, element
  declarations, attribute group definitions, model group definitions, notation
  declarations, identity-constraint definitions). **Only `{annotations}` is "A
  sequence of Annotation components."** So for seven of the eight accessors the
  document order this repo returns is a **goxsd8 determinism choice (STYLE
  D2/D3), not a spec mandate**; for `Annotations()` alone the order is
  spec-significant. That split is written into the method docs themselves —
  seven say the order is guaranteed by *this contract* "even though §3.17.1
  words the property as an unordered set," and `Annotations()` says the order
  is spec-significant *as well as* stable. A reader two years out cannot
  mistake a house invariant for a conformance requirement. Grounding:
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/252#issuecomment-5159118180>.
- **The warden ran BEFORE any code was written, and that is the process fact
  worth carrying forward.** Pre-flight verdict was `revise` — but explicitly
  *"Shape is right — do not redesign the method set."* All eight findings were
  doc-contract and disclosure fixes on a design already correct: the
  `cloneSlice` copy is REQUIRED not optional (T1 — an aliasing handle onto a
  source-of-truth slice is a representable illegal state, since the by-QName
  indexes are DERIVED from those slices); `xsd/doc.go`'s "views … never copy
  it" claim becomes false the moment copying enumerators ship (T5);
  `Types()` must disclose the seeded builtins; `Annotations()` must disclose
  that nothing populates it; the seven-sets/one-sequence wording (D2);
  `Types()` must not overpromise "mutating the result does not affect s" for
  the `*SimpleType` variant (D3); two cross-type name echoes need one
  disambiguating clause each; no capability interfaces; ship all 8 at once.
  **Every one of those became a requirement mason implemented rather than a
  finding mason had to repair.** The diff review then closed all eight against
  the diff (not the summary) and returned `approve`:
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/252#issuecomment-5159146860> (pre-flight)
  and <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/252#issuecomment-5159206438> (diff review).
- **Exported surface, measured not argued: exactly 8 additions, 276 → 284
  symbols.** The arbiter rendered `go doc -all ./xsd` on both trees and diffed
  them. All eight are methods on `*Schema` (3 → 11 exported methods); **no new
  exported types, interfaces, functions, fields, or
  `var _ X = (*Schema)(nil)` assertions**, and no change to `Schema`'s field
  set. Matches the warden's predicted surface exactly.
- **Test load-bearingness was PROVED by mutation, on all eight at once.** In a
  scratch copy of the tree the arbiter rewrote every one of the eight bodies to
  `return s.<field>` — the aliasing implementation.
  `TestSchemaEnumeratorsReturnCopies` **fails on all eight labels**
  (`Types: got "<nil>", want "t1"` … `Annotations: got "<none>", want
  "first"`), and `Example_schemaEnumeration`'s final output line flips too.
  `TestSchemaEnumeratorsEmptyAreNil` additionally fails an
  `append([]T{}, …)` implementation, so empty-is-nil is genuinely pinned rather
  than incidentally true, and `TestSchemaTypesIncludesAnonymous` fails any
  named-only filter. The copy contract cannot silently rot.
- **Surprise 1 — `Types()` returns roughly fifty components no user ever
  wrote, and they come FIRST.** `parser/produce.go`'s `newSymbols`
  (lines 253-267) calls `AddType` for every seeded builtin simple type and for
  the synthesized `xs:anyType` **before** `p.run()` reaches any document type.
  The tempting fix — filter by target namespace — is a heuristic that breaks
  immediately (`xs:anyType` is in the XSD namespace too), so the warden
  correctly ruled it out and the order was **disclosed instead of hidden**:
  "builtins first, then each document's top-level types in document order."
  The arbiter fact-checked the stability of that prefix independently —
  `builtin.Seed` walks the generated `Types` table and never ranges a map
  (`builtin/seed.go:171-181`) — so builtins-first is deterministic, not
  incidental.
- **Surprise 2 — `Annotations()` exposes a property that NOTHING populates.**
  `SchemaBuilder.AddAnnotation` exists and is correct, but grep finds no call
  site outside tests: `parser` never wires a `<xs:schema>` element's own
  `<xs:annotation>` children (§3.17.2). So `Annotations()` returns nil for
  **every** schema `parser.Parse` produces. The method shipped anyway — a
  write-only builder property is strictly worse than an accessor that honestly
  documents the gap — with the nil-for-parsed-schemas fact stated in its own
  doc comment, and **#420 filed for the parser side**. Closing #420 means
  deleting that disclosure.
- Decisions:
  1. **All eight ship together, or the arbitrary subset becomes the contract.**
     §3.17.1 is a closed, spec-fixed tableau. Shipping the three or four the
     hypothetical consumer wanted first would publish a boundary the spec does
     not have and that a later addition could not un-publish.
  2. **The SLICE is copied; the COMPONENTS are not.** `cloneSlice` copies
     interface values, so `*SimpleType` pointer identity is preserved — which
     is load-bearing per `Finalize`. The docs say exactly that rather than the
     easier and false "mutating the result does not affect s."
  3. **No capability interfaces, deliberately (T3/T5).** The by-QName lookups
     have `ElementResolver`/`AttributeResolver`/`TypeResolver` because real
     consumers wanted exactly one method each. Nothing enumerates yet, so
     extraction waits for a second consumer; one fat bundling interface would
     be the wrong answer in any case. Recorded in `xsd/doc.go` so the absence
     reads as a decision, not an oversight.
  4. **The Example is the in-repo consumer of record.** The named consumer,
     `goxsd8 parse`'s summary, is M4 and unbuilt, so T5's consumer pressure was
     discharged by `Example_schemaEnumeration` — which exercises all eight and
     demonstrates the copy semantics in shipped godoc — rather than by waiting.
  5. **#420 was filed rather than folded in.** Exposing a property and
     populating it from the parser are different changes in different packages;
     folding the parser work in would have made a 460-line additive query-API
     diff carry an unreviewed producer change.
- Friction: **none material — one round, one commit, no repair.** The item
  worth naming is not friction inside this session but a **note that has now
  been raised twice and fixed zero times**: `xsd/doc.go:68-69` says the
  enumerators report "in document order" without hedge, while `Types()` on a
  parsed schema leads with add-order builtins that no document declares. The
  warden flagged it as a minor note, the arbiter independently re-derived it as
  a `[D2/doc]` finding, and both deferred it to "the next time this section is
  touched." The method doc gets it right; only `doc.go`'s summary sentence is
  loose. Two independent reviewers spending effort to re-find the same
  known-and-deferred wording defect is the cost of deferring it — flagged for
  the retro as a candidate pattern, not as this issue's debt.
- Next:
  1. **[filed — no action owed here] #420** (`kind/gap`, `area/parser`) —
     wire schema-level `<xs:annotation>` into `Schema.{annotations}` so
     `Annotations()` stops returning nil for parsed schemas. Its acceptance
     should ABSORB the arbiter's `[T5]` finding below; the three unpinned
     claims are all parser-level and fit one cheap parser test.
  2. **[arbiter non-blocking finding 1 — `[P3]`, for the post-land pass]**
     `xsd/schema.go:536-541` — `Annotations()`'s disclosure describes a
     deliberate incompleteness in shipped surface but carries **no greppable
     `GAP(` token and does not name #420 in the prose**, so
     `grep -rn "GAP("` does not surface it. The marker itself is owed at the
     *parser* site when #420 lands (this diff has no parser call site to mark);
     adding "(#420)" to the prose costs nothing and should ride the next touch.
  3. **[arbiter non-blocking finding 2 — `[T5]`, same disposition]** three
     published contract claims are **unpinned by any test above package
     `xsd`**: the builtins-first prefix (`schema.go:428-433`), the
     top-level-only scope (`schema.go:435-438`), and
     `Annotations()`-is-nil-for-parsed-schemas. All three are true today (the
     arbiter fact-checked each against source) and all three are parser-level
     assertions. The warden asked for the builtins-prefix test at pre-flight
     and **waived it at diff review**; the arbiter declined to override a
     waived requirement absent independent harm. Belongs on #420's acceptance.
  4. **[arbiter non-blocking finding 3 — `[D2/doc]`, same disposition]**
     `xsd/doc.go:68-69`'s unhedged "in document order" for `Types()` — the
     defect described under Friction above. Raised by warden AND arbiter.
  5. **[arbiter non-blocking finding 4 — `[T4]`, same disposition]**
     `xsd/example_test.go:209-253` adds a second family of
     component-construction helpers alongside `schema_test.go`'s `*Named`
     helpers in the same `xsd_test` package. **The split is justified** — the
     `*Named` helpers take `*testing.T` and call `t.Fatalf`, unusable from an
     Example, and the new ones match the file's existing panic-on-err
     convention — but T4 asks that the reason ride in the commit message, and
     it does not. Either a thread comment recording the reason, or a
     test-hygiene issue, or an explicit dismissal on the record.
  6. **[carried, still open]** #400 wants a develop iteration rather than a
     retro slot; #347 is the next twelve-carry candidate; #270 is still
     undischarged from the 07-26 retro; #417 (APFS case-insensitivity
     conformance artifact) is filed and unowned. None is this session's to
     close — listed so the count of sessions they have survived stays visible.
- Thread record (do not duplicate) —
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/252>: the GROUNDING at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/252#issuecomment-5159118180> (the
  eight-property tableau and the seven-sets/one-sequence finding that
  reclassified document order as STYLE D2/D3 for seven of them), the warden
  PRE-FLIGHT at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/252#issuecomment-5159146860> (the
  eight disclosure requirements, issued before code existed), the warden DIFF
  REVIEW at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/252#issuecomment-5159206438> (all
  eight closed against the diff, plus the two minor notes), and the arbiter's
  ACCEPT verdict at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/252#issuecomment-5159244423> (the
  eight-way mutation proof, the 276→284 surface diff, the fact-check of every

  and the unfiled M9 epic (Walker), and the 2026-07-31 `/backlog` libuser

## 2026-08-02 — #417 — loader: `Dir`'s resolved-location identity is respelled to the on-disk case, so two differently-cased `schemaLocation` hints for one file dedupe to one document instead of composing it twice and fabricating a `sch-props-correct` clause 2 reject; the oracle's decisive finding was that cross-spelling identity is spec-ENCOURAGED and never spec-mandated, and the warden's post-implementation diff review — not the arbiter — caught a doc-contract overreach that one repair round closed before the arbiter ever saw the diff

- Shipped on `wip/issue-417` as two mason commits on top of `bdd750b` —
  `63fcf57` (`loader: canonicalize Dir's resolved-location identity to on-disk
  case (#417)`) and `0dd002c` (`loader: gate Resolver's canonical-identity
  contract on capability, not absolute (#417)`), **6 files, +209/−4, all under
  `loader/`**: `loader/dir.go` (+75/−1, the two new unexported helpers and one
  changed return statement), `loader/loader_test.go` (+103),
  `loader/resolver.go` (+13), `loader/doc.go` (+10/−3), `loader/fs.go` (+7),
  `loader/http.go` (+5). `conformance/` untouched;
  `git diff origin/main..HEAD -- conformance/testdata/expectations/` empty.
  **Arbiter ACCEPT on the first round it judged** — the one repair round this
  session consumed was spent on the *warden's* diff review, not the arbiter's.
- **Ratchet: unchanged — and precisely NOT a lane advance.** The arbiter's
  figure, copied verbatim from the verdict and not recomputed: **unchanged —
  the ratchet run wrote NOTHING.** `GOXSD_RATCHET=1 go test ./conformance -run
  TestConformance -count=1` was RUN (`ok … 13.185s`) and `git status
  --porcelain` immediately afterwards was **empty**; expectations files
  byte-identical to `origin/main`, no file touched in either direction. The
  distinction that matters for the record: `conformance/testdata/expectations/
  schema.txt:9840-9841` **already read `pass`** for both cases on `origin/main`.
  This is an **un-regression restoring already-committed `pass` entries** on a
  host that experiences the environmental bug — there is nothing above the
  committed baseline to record. It is not upward lane movement and must not be
  described as one.
- **What #417 actually was: a real, currently-gating conformance regression on
  case-insensitive filesystems (macOS APFS).** `loader.Dir`'s `Resolve`
  returned the caller's possibly-wrong-cased location string
  (`filepath.Join(root, location)`) as its second return value — the
  dedup-identity key. On APFS the misspelled hint **opens the same file**, so
  two differently-cased `schemaLocation` hints for one document produced two
  distinct keys, the document composed twice, its single global
  `<element name="root"/>` was contributed twice, and a schema-valid input was
  false-rejected under `sch-props-correct` clause 2. Two W3C cases hit it:
  `MS-Schema2006-07-15/schZ012_b` and `schZ012_c`
  (`schZ012_b2.xsd` imports `Schz012_b.xsd`; `schZ012_c2.xsd` imports
  `Schz012_C.xsd`). It was **filed by the arbiter while judging #314** — see
  this file's immediately preceding `#314` entry, where the inode evidence and
  the pristine-`main` reproduction were first recorded.
- **Spec: `sch-props-correct` clause 2 (§3.17.6.1) is the ONLY rule ID this
  change may cite — and the grounding's decisive contribution was a negative
  finding.** §4.2.3 (`src-include`) and §4.2.6.2 (`src-import`) make **lexical**
  location-string identity (after relative-URI resolution) a **mandatory**
  same-document signal. Recognizing that two *different* strings name the same
  resource is a different matter entirely: §4.2.3's escape valve is
  capability-gated — *"unless the implementation is able to determine that the
  two URIs are references to the same resource"* — and the Notes to both
  sections say applications are *"allowed, indeed encouraged"* to avoid
  composing the same document twice. **Encouraged, never required.** A full-file
  grep of `xmlschema11-1.md` for `case-fold|case sensitiv|equivalence class`
  returns **zero matches**; §4.3.2 (`schema-loc`) says location strategy is
  *"processor and application dependent."* So canonicalizing by on-disk case is
  **spec-permitted engineering, not a numbered spec rule** — it carries no rule
  ID of its own, and the code comments and commit messages fabricate none. Only
  `sch-props-correct` clause 2 names the *outcome the fix prevents*. The
  oracle's over-fix guard was also honored: lexically identical locations still
  produce identical `full` → identical `rel` → identical output, so §4.2.3's
  forward direction is untouched. Grounding:
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/417#issuecomment-5159152418>.
- **The fix, exactly.** Two **unexported** helpers in `loader/dir.go`, wired
  into `dirResolver.Resolve`'s **second return value only** (`dir.go:62`):
  1. `canonicalCase(root, rel string) string` (`dir.go:85`) — **total, no error
     return, best-effort**. It respells each already-confined relative segment
     as the parent directory lists it. Two degrade paths, both documented and
     both covered: an **unlistable directory** (`os.ReadDir` error — a
     search-only `0111` directory is openable-but-unlistable) and **a fold
     `open(2)` accepted that `strings.EqualFold` does not** (macOS folds Unicode
     differently — Turkish dotless i, HFS+ normalisation). Both degrade to the
     caller's casing, which is the previously-correct answer — never a new
     failure mode. The warden demanded the error return be dropped at
     pre-flight, precisely so an unlistable directory could not turn a working
     read into a hard error.
  2. `matchEntryName(entries []os.DirEntry, want string) string` (`dir.go:109`)
     — **deterministic**: an exact-match pass first, then the FIRST `EqualFold`
     match in `os.ReadDir`'s own documented sorted order. **No fold-keyed
     `map[string]string`** — on a case-sensitive filesystem two entries can
     fold-equal, and a map would make the pick nondeterministic (STYLE D1/D2).
  It runs **after** the `..`-traversal rejection, over the **already-confined
  `rel` reused rather than recomputed**, and only ever refines a SPELLING —
  substituted names come from the parent's own listing, `ReadDir` never yields
  `.`/`..`, and segment count is invariant — so confinement cannot be reopened
  and no second differently-derived path exists to disagree with the checked
  one. Doc-only updates rode alongside: `loader/resolver.go` (the `Resolver`
  interface's dedup-key contract, since `ResolverFunc` is exported and third
  parties owe it), `loader/doc.go` (T6 sync), and one-line carve-outs in
  `loader/fs.go` and `loader/http.go` — neither canonicalizes; `Dir` alone does.
- **The process fact worth carrying: the DIFF REVIEW caught it, not the
  arbiter, and one repair round closed it before the arbiter saw the diff.**
  The session ran the full pipeline in order — oracle grounding → warden design
  pre-flight (`approve with changes`) → mason implementation → **warden
  post-implementation diff review (`revise`)** → mason repair (a **new commit,
  `0dd002c`, not an amend**) → arbiter judgment (`accept`). The warden's diff
  finding was a single doc-contract overreach: `resolver.go`'s first draft said
  the canonical-identity obligation is one *"every implementation … owes,"*
  unconditionally — while **two of this package's own three resolvers do not
  meet it** (`FS` says so explicitly; `HTTP` returns `location` verbatim). A
  third party reading the interface would have concluded the package ships
  non-conformant resolvers. The repair gated the obligation on **capability, in
  §4.2.3's own "unless the implementation is able to determine…" terms**, and
  made `doc.go`, `fs.go` and `http.go` say the same thing. This is a working
  instance of the multi-persona pipeline, not merely a landing: the design
  pre-flight fixed the shape before code existed (the dropped error return, the
  no-fold-map rule, the `filepath.Separator` split, the ordering constraint),
  and the diff review caught the one thing implementation got wrong — so the
  arbiter's round was spent on judgment, not on rework.
- **Failure capability was PROVED by mutation, and the mutation reproduced the
  bug exactly.** The arbiter replaced `canonicalCase`'s body with `return
  filepath.Join(root, rel)` — precisely `origin/main`'s behaviour — then
  reverted via `git checkout --` and verified a zero diff against the branch
  tip. Under the mutation: `TestCanonicalCase` **FAIL**,
  `TestDirResolveCanonicalIdentity` **FAIL** (`resolved identities differ`), and
  `go test ./conformance` **FAIL** with `lane schema: 2 regressed case(s):
  [MS-Schema2006-07-15/schZ012_b/schema/schZ012_b
  MS-Schema2006-07-15/schZ012_c/schema/schZ012_c]` — **exactly the two reported
  cases and no others**. `TestDirResolveCanonicalIdentity` **passed (not
  skipped)** on this host, so the capability probe fired and the
  case-insensitive-open path was genuinely exercised;
  `TestMatchEntryNameExactWins` skipped by design here, gated on a capability
  probe and never on `runtime.GOOS`, as the warden specified. Verdict:
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/417#issuecomment-5159293598>.
- **Blast radius, confirmed by reading every consumer — the resolved string is
  an identity key and a debug field, nothing else.** It is read in exactly three
  places: `parser/parse.go:505` (`docKey{resolved: …}`) and `:220` (the assembly
  seed), `parser/parse.go:508` (a `Debug` log field), and
  `conformance/schema_closure.go:252,326` (`scanKey{resolved: …}`, compared via
  `visited()`). Decisively, `parser/parse.go:513` builds the Document with
  `ReadDocument(requested, rc)` and **every** error message at `:499` and
  `:519-520` interpolates `requested` — the caller's original hint; same at
  `readRootDocument` (`:601-611`), which passes `location`. So **no
  `Document.URI()` and no user-visible error text can change** — only the dedup
  key does, and no other test's output could have been affected. None was. The
  conformance closure scan stays in sync precisely *because* canonicalization
  lives in the resolver both sides share, which is the argument for fixing it
  there rather than in `parser`. The arbiter added the monotonicity argument
  that makes the degrade paths safe: the function is deterministic in
  `(root, rel, fs state)`, so any two hints main deduped still dedupe and some
  main did not now do — the fix can never *un*-deduplicate.
- **Concurrent-session note.** Before the PR was opened this branch merged
  `origin/main` at `dcbd24c`, bringing in **#252**'s unrelated landing
  (`4fd77dd`, the `*Schema` enumeration accessors). **Clean, no conflicts** —
  the two changes touch disjoint files (`loader/*` here versus `xsd/*` and this
  log file's tail there). Recorded because the merge commit sits between the
  issue's two commits and this one, and a later reader should not mistake it for
  part of #417's diff.
- Decisions:
  1. **Fixed in the resolver, not the parser.** Identity belongs to whatever
     turns a location hint into a resource; the warden confirmed the shape at
     pre-flight by reading the consumers. Fixing it in `parser` would have left
     `conformance/schema_closure.go`'s parallel resolved-keyed index out of
     sync, since both sides key off the same resolver's output.
  2. **`Dir` alone canonicalizes, and the other resolvers say so out loud.**
     `FS` cannot (`fs.FS` guarantees no listing); `HTTP` does not (host case,
     default port, percent-encoding are not normalized). `Map` is *vacuously*
     conformant — exact-key lookup cannot serve one resource under two
     spellings — and `Chain` is pure pass-through, so the arbiter confirmed
     omitting both from the "cannot determine" list is correct, not an
     oversight.
  3. **The contract is capability-gated, not absolute** — §4.2.3's own wording,
     adopted after the warden flagged the first draft's overreach. A resolver
     that cannot determine cross-spelling identity returns the location as
     given, the loader composes the document twice, and §4.2.3/§4.2.6.2 permit
     exactly that.
  4. **No cache.** Per-resolve `os.ReadDir` is O(entries) and unmeasured; STYLE
     D4 says leave it. The warden ruled on this at pre-flight and the arbiter
     confirmed it in the diff.
  5. **The repair was a NEW commit, not an amend** — the round is visible in the
     branch history rather than erased from it.
  6. **The squash message must be written from the FINAL state.** `63fcf57`'s
     body carries the absolute *"contract every implementation owes"* phrasing
     that `0dd002c` superseded; a concatenated squash message would re-assert
     the exact overreach round two removed.
- Friction:
  1. **The documented lint command does not work as written, and BOTH mason and
     the arbiter hit it.** CLAUDE.md documents `golangci-lint run` as gate part
     2, but `which golangci-lint` → not found, and `go.mod`'s `tool (…)` block
     lists only the six in-repo tools, so neither `golangci-lint run` nor
     `go tool golangci-lint run` runs in a fresh shell. Both personas had to
     discover the installed binary and invoke it by absolute path
     (`/Users/kud360/go/bin/golangci-lint` → `0 issues.`). The arbiter flagged
     it explicitly as an **environment seam, not a defect in this diff**, and
     declined to gate on it. Two personas paying the same tax in one session is
     the shape of a pattern, not a one-off — see Next.
  2. **The doc overreach cost a full repair round on a diff whose CODE was
     clean on the first pass.** Worth naming honestly: the warden's diff review
     found nothing wrong with `dir.go`; every pre-flight item was discharged as
     specified. The round went entirely to one sentence in an interface doc
     comment. Cheap, correct to spend, and an argument for the pre-flight
     covering doc-contract wording as explicitly as it covers signatures.
- Next:
  1. **[arbiter non-blocking finding 1 — `[P2]`, for a later touch, NOT charged
     as repair]** `loader/dir.go:77-78` — *"It degrades to `rel`'s own casing,
     unchanged"* is literally true only for a **level-0** degrade; a **mid-path**
     degrade returns canonical-prefix + as-given-suffix (the local comment at
     `dir.go:94` is accurate; the doc sentence generalizes past it). The
     load-bearing safety claim at `dir.go:82-84` remains TRUE by the
     monotonicity argument, so this is precision, not correctness.
  2. **[arbiter non-blocking finding 2 — `[P1]`, same disposition]**
     `loader/resolver.go:30-31` — *"the loader composes that document more than
     once, which §4.2.3 and §4.2.6.2 permit"* states the spec permission but
     stops one clause short of the observable consequence **in THIS processor**:
     composing twice is what produced the spurious `sch-props-correct` clause 2
     reject #417 is about. A reader of `FS`'s carve-out could reasonably infer
     double composition is benign. Half a sentence naming the consequence would
     make the carve-out actionable.
  3. **[unfiled — the lint-command environment seam]** The friction item above:
     CLAUDE.md's documented `golangci-lint run` is not reproducible in a fresh
     shell. Two personas hit it in this session alone and the arbiter named it
     a seam worth a separate issue. Candidate `kind/tooling` (add it to
     `go.mod`'s tool block, or amend CLAUDE.md to state the real invocation).
     **Not filed by this session** — listed here so the retro's follow-up ledger
     audit catches it if nobody does.
  4. **[deliberate omission, recorded so it is not later read as an oversight]**
     the `os.ReadDir`-error degrade branch in `canonicalCase` is **untested**.
     Exercising it needs a search-only (`0111`) directory, and mason judged the
     portability cost of constructing one in the suite above the value of
     covering a branch whose whole contract is "return what the caller gave
     us." A conscious trade, not a gap.
  5. **[carried, still open]** #400 wants a develop iteration rather than a
     retro slot; #347 is the next twelve-carry candidate; #270 is still
     undischarged from the 07-26 retro; #420 (schema-level `<xs:annotation>`
     never wired into `Schema.{annotations}`) is filed and unowned. None is
     this session's to close — listed so the count of sessions they have
     survived stays visible.
- Thread record (do not duplicate) —
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/417>: the GROUNDING at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/417#issuecomment-5159152418> (the
  mandatory/encouraged split across §4.2.3 and §4.2.6.2, the zero-match
  case-folding grep, and the instruction to fabricate no rule ID for the
  canonicalization itself), the warden PRE-FLIGHT at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/417#issuecomment-5159173949>
  (`approve with changes` — drop the error return, no fold-keyed map, split on
  `filepath.Separator`, canonicalize after the `Rel` check, probe-gated tests,
  no cache), the warden DIFF REVIEW at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/417#issuecomment-5159229599>
  (`revise` — all six pre-flight items discharged, one doc-contract overreach
  in `resolver.go` plus the matching `HTTP` carve-out), and the arbiter's

  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/417#issuecomment-5159293598> (the

  `pass` lines under a condition — the import being unresolved — that holds only
  on a case-sensitive host. Merged in before landing and NOT part of this diff:

## 2026-08-02 — #253 — parser: the last silently-skipped composition directive, a child `<xs:redefine>`, becomes a debug record on `WithLogger` carrying the `src-redefine` rule ID and its `Loc`; two thirds of the issue as filed were STALE — `<xs:import>` (#182) and `<xs:override>` (#183) are actually followed now — so the scope narrowed to the one real remaining gap, and the arbiter rejected round 1 over an overstated spec claim (`src-redefine` clause 1 is UNCONDITIONAL, not resolution-conditioned) plus two `GAP(` markers missing from the canonical incompleteness site

- Shipped on `wip/issue-253` as two mason commits on top of `9315ce1` (#417) —
  `235ea45` (`parser: log the child <xs:redefine> element skip via WithLogger
  (#253)`) and `1e73a93` (`parser: address arbiter repair round 1 on #253 — GAP
  markers, rule ID, accurate clause-1 note`), **5 files, +56/−4, all under
  `parser/`**: `parser/parse_test.go` (+33, the one new test),
  `parser/parse.go` (+17/−1, the new switch arm and `Parse`'s doc paragraph),
  `parser/doc.go` (+6/−2), `parser/override.go` (+3/−1),
  `parser/produce.go` (+1). `conformance/` untouched; `git diff
  origin/main...HEAD -- conformance/` is 0 lines. **One arbiter repair round,
  ACCEPT on round 2.**
- **Ratchet: unchanged.** The arbiter's figure, copied verbatim from the FINAL
  verdict and not recomputed: **unchanged (ran `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` on accept — no
  lane moved, no expectation file rewritten)**. `GOXSD_RATCHET=1 go test
  ./conformance -run TestConformance -count=1` was RUN (`ok … 13.3s`) and `git
  status --porcelain` immediately afterwards was **empty** — the ratchet rewrote
  no expectation file, so no case flipped in either direction. The change adds
  one `slog.Debug` call on a switch arm that previously had **no statement at
  all**; it cannot reach a schema-validity verdict, and the suite confirms it
  does not.
- **The issue was two-thirds stale, and finding that out was the session's
  first real decision.** #253 was filed 2026-07-25 (libuser-harvested, their P1
  and the highest-priority item of that `/backlog`) against
  `<xs:import>`/`<xs:redefine>`/`<xs:override>` — all three then silently
  skipped. A codebase reality-check run alongside the grounding found `<import>`
  (#182) and `<override>` (#183, in part) have **landed since**: `parse.go`'s
  `(a *assembly) discover` directive switch already had `include`, `override`
  and `import` arms. Only `redefine` had no arm at all, so a `<redefine>` child
  fell through the switch and was ignored with **zero trace anywhere** — the
  arbiter's round-1 negative control independently confirmed the issue's
  premise, the only remaining debug record with the new arm deleted being
  `msg="producing schema document"`. Implementation was narrowed to that single
  gap rather than re-litigating two constructs that no longer skip. The
  reality-check is recorded as a session addendum appended to the grounding
  comment, so a later reader sees why the landed diff is a third of the filed
  scope.
- **Option 1 was taken outright: logging only, no exported surface.** The issue
  offered (1) a `WithLogger` record or (2) an exported "known-incomplete" signal
  on the result, and made (2) contingent on a warden pre-flight because it is
  exactly the kind of surface #182/#183 would later want to delete. (1) needs no
  pre-flight and nothing to retire, and the arbiter's T5 check — a mechanical
  `go doc -all -short ./parser` diff between a throwaway `origin/main` worktree
  and `HEAD` — found the **only** differences are prose inside two existing doc
  comments. **Zero new exported identifiers**; `ruleSrcRedefine` is unexported
  and `TestParseRedefineIsLoggedNotFollowed` lives in package `parser_test`. No
  new export means no justification owed and no warden pre-flight, so the warden
  was correctly never run this session.
- **Spec: `src-redefine` (§4.2.4), and the grounding's decisive contribution was
  again a NEGATIVE finding.** None of `src-import`/`src-redefine`/`src-override`
  mentions "skipping" — all three are Schema Representation Constraints that
  bite *when the element is present*, and their resolution clauses open with
  "If the ·actual value· of the `schemaLocation` attribute successfully
  resolves". There is **no clause anywhere licensing a processor to decline to
  attempt resolution**: the skip is a goxsd8 gap, not spec-sanctioned behaviour,
  which is why the marker is `GAP(xsd)` and not a documented option. The oracle
  also fixed the vocabulary the code now uses — "schema document" (§3.1.2,
  `key-schemaDoc`), "child `<xs:redefine>` element" and **not** "top-level"
  (the spec reserves *top-level definition item* for named declarations, per
  `src-expredef` clause 1.1), and `schemaLocation` **hint** (§4.2.6.2) — plus
  the trap the log message is written around: do not conflate "the referenced
  document did not resolve" (tolerated as a non-error by all three rules) with
  "resolution was never attempted", which is what actually happens here.
  Grounding: <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/253#issuecomment-5159371930>.
- **The change, exactly.** A `case isXSD(el, "redefine"):` arm in `discover`'s
  directive switch (`parser/parse.go:263`) whose body is a single
  `a.log.Debug("composition skipped: <xs:redefine> is not followed, its
  schemaLocation hint is never resolved", "rule", string(ruleSrcRedefine),
  "location", attrOr(el, "schemaLocation"), "at", el.Loc().String())` — rule
  first, location last, byte-for-byte the shape of both sibling records
  (`parse.go:389` `ruleSrcImport`, `parse.go:509`). `ruleSrcRedefine
  xsderr.Rule = "src-redefine"` was added to `parser/produce.go:21` in the
  sibling const block between `ruleSrcOverride` and `ruleSrcElement`, reusing a
  **real catalog entry** (`xsderr/catalog.go:210`), not an invented ID. The arm
  carries a bare `GAP(xsd):` marker, `Parse`'s and `doc.go`'s `<redefine>`
  paragraphs now name `WithLogger` as the observation mechanism and its level,
  and `override.go`'s redefine paragraph gained a one-clause pointer to the same.
  The test, `TestParseRedefineIsLoggedNotFollowed`, drives a `slog.NewTextHandler`
  over a `bytes.Buffer` at `LevelDebug`, asserts a `<redefine
  schemaLocation="absent.xsd"/>` is **not** an error (the sibling
  `<element name="root"/>` still lands, which is precisely why the record has to
  exist), and pins `level=DEBUG`, `<xs:redefine>`, `rule=src-redefine`,
  `location=absent.xsd` and `at=main.xsd:`.
- **The repair round: four findings, none of them about behaviour.** Round 1
  (<https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/253#issuecomment-5159419435>)
  rejected with all-green gate and all four findings on *honesty of the record*:
  1. **`[P3]`** the new switch arm — the canonical deliberate-incompleteness
     site — carried **no `GAP(` token**, so the documented debt sweep
     `grep -rn "GAP("` did not see the redefine gap at all.
  2. **`[P3]`** `parser/doc.go:97`'s redefine bullet was the **only one of the
     five** in "Planned composition" without `GAP(xsd):`; its four neighbours
     (`doc.go:105, 111, 120, 132`) all carried it.
  3. **`[L1]`** the debug record omitted the rule ID, which L1 requires in every
     message and which both sibling records carry — one of them (`parse.go:386`)
     on a non-error informational path exactly like this one.
  4. **`[P1]`** the comment claimed "src-redefine's clauses all presuppose a
     successful resolution", which §4.2.4 does not say. **Clause 1 is
     unconditional**: *"If there are any element information items among the
     [children] other than `<annotation>` then the ·actual value· of the
     `schemaLocation` [attribute] must successfully resolve."* Only clause 2
     opens with the "if it resolves" antecedent, and 3 and 4 are reachable only
     through clause 2's D1/D2 bindings. So the skip does not merely decline
     conditional clauses — it silently accepts a **genuine `src-redefine`
     violation**, and the landed comment now states that as fact.
  All four were addressed in `1e73a93` as a **new commit, not an amend**, so the
  round stays visible in the branch history. FINAL verdict:
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/253#issuecomment-5159450478>.
- **Failure capability was proved twice, at both rounds and on the repair
  itself.** Round 1: deleting the whole `case` arm makes the new test fail with
  no redefine record present at all. Round 2: deleting **only** the `"rule",
  string(ruleSrcRedefine),` pair makes it fail with `log output does not mention
  "rule=src-redefine"` — so the assertion added for finding 3 is load-bearing,
  not decorative. The tree was restored with `git checkout --` and verified
  clean after each.
- Decisions:
  1. **Narrow to `<redefine>` alone rather than "fixing" import and override
     too.** They no longer skip; a log record for a followed directive would be
     a lie, and re-opening them would have widened an issue whose own body says
     *"Do not widen this issue into implementing the constructs."*
  2. **The `GAP(xsd)` markers are BARE — no issue number — deliberately.** P3
     only requires a marker to name an owning issue *if it names one at all*,
     and that issue must still be open. **#183 covered redefine and is CLOSED
     with redefine undelivered**, so there is no open issue to name; citing it
     would point the sweep at a closed thread. Recorded here because a bare
     marker otherwise looks like an omission.
  3. **No second `WithLogger` clause in `doc.go`'s "Current coverage" list.**
     Mason declined it and referred the call to the arbiter, who agreed
     explicitly and told the repair round **not** to add it: "Current coverage"
     is a signature summary, the "Planned composition" bullet already states the
     mechanism *at the point where the reader has just been told the schema
     assembles short*, and `WithLogger`'s own godoc carries the level —
     repeating it would be a second encoding of one fact (D3).
  4. **Debug, not Warn.** The record is a fact about the input, not a
     recommendation; STYLE 10's silent-by-default logger and both sibling
     composition records set the level.
- Surprises:
  1. **A P1 issue can rot in nine days.** #253 was the top item of the 2026-07-25
     `/backlog` and by 2026-08-02 two of its three constructs had been closed out
     from under it by #182/#183. Nothing in the issue body could have said so —
     it was written before those landed. The cheap defence is the one that
     worked here: a codebase reality-check run **alongside** the grounding,
     before any code, with its result posted to the thread.
  2. **`GAP(` markers get lost exactly when a gap is FORMALIZED.** The
     pre-existing redefine skip was an absent switch case — nothing to annotate.
     This landing is the first time the gap has a code site, which makes it the
     first time it *could* carry a marker, and it took an arbiter finding to
     notice that the site which most needs one was created without one.
- Friction:
  1. **`golangci-lint` is still not on `PATH` in fish, and the arbiter paid the
     tax on both rounds.** It runs clean from `~/go/bin/golangci-lint` (`0
     issues` both times). This is the seam recorded as unfiled in the #417 entry
     immediately above; it is now **#426**, and the arbiter cited it by number
     in both verdicts while correctly declining to gate on it. Left here so the
     retro can see the same tax charged in two consecutive sessions.
  2. **The repair round cost nothing on behaviour and everything on record
     hygiene** — one missing key-value pair, two missing markers, one overstated
     spec sentence. Cheap and correct to spend, but the same shape as #417's
     round: a diff whose *code* was right on the first pass, rejected on what it
     said about itself.
- Next:

  2. **[arbiter non-blocking observation 2, unfiled — same disposition]** **#183

  3. **[recorded, not a defect]** the landed comment says "clauses 2-4" and does
