## 2026-08-03 — #256 — parked (needs-replan): the doc-only fix to `AddIdentityConstraint`'s "top-level" claim was verified correct on round 2, but the branch had turned a consistently-wrong pair into a self-contradiction — `Schema.IdentityConstraints()`, the reader of the same slice, still said "top-level"

- Attempted: `wip/issue-256` @ `c56d9f7` — **not merged, retired in place** after
  two arbiter rejections (the hard cap, docs/WORKFLOW.md / PRINCIPLES 30). Issue
  relabeled `needs-replan` (was `ready`); labels now `needs-replan, kind/bug,
  area/xsd`. This entry lands separately off `origin/main` @ `8913fe7`, since a
  park leaves the WIP branch untouched.
- Ratchet: **unchanged on `main`** — nothing landed, and nothing was ever
  forecast to move. #256 is a doc-comment + test issue over paths the producer
  already honours; both commit messages read `Ratchet: unchanged`, and the
  arbiter ran no ratchet in either round (a reject does not bank movement, and
  there was none to bank). Ratchet integrity clean both rounds — the diff never
  touched `conformance/`.
- What was attempted: `xsd.SchemaBuilder.AddIdentityConstraint`'s doc comment
  claimed a **top-level** identity-constraint registration contract, and the
  spec requires every identity-constraint definition to be registered at ANY
  nesting depth. Scope as filed: the doc comment plus one new parser test for the
  cross-declaration shape (a `<keyref>` under one local `<element>` resolving to
  a `<key>` declared under a different one), no behaviour change. Two commits:
  `3c0c918` (doc rewrite + `TestProduceKeyrefOnLocalElementResolvesAcrossDeclarations`)
  and `c56d9f7` (the round-1 repair), 2 files, +89/−3.
- Grounding held up under both rounds. The oracle confirmed the issue's spec
  claim and the arbiter re-derived it independently from `docs/specs/md/`: the
  "anywhere within the [[children]]" wording is deliberately contrasted, **in
  the same tableau**, with `{element declarations}`'s "(top-level)" scoping;
  `§3.11.2` resolves a keyref's `refer` against that document-wide set,
  `§3.17.6.2 src-resolve` cl. 1.7 is the resolution it feeds, and
  `§3.11.6.1 c-props-correct` cl. 1–2 are the checks that never run if the
  target is unfindable.
- Decisions:
  - **The section number the ISSUE asserted was wrong, and round 1 caught it.**
    Both quoted phrases live in §3.17.2's *XML Mapping Summary for Schema*
    tableau; §3.17.1 carries only the abstract declaration
    ("`{identity-constraint definitions}` A set of Identity-Constraint Definition
    components") and no such wording. The repo's own convention already had this
    right at `parser/produce.go:446` and `:187`. `c56d9f7` reattributed the
    tableau to §3.17.2 in the doc, in the new test's comments, and in the `Spec:`
    trailer.
  - **The corrected contract had to exclude the `ref=` reuse form.** Round 1's
    second finding: "register EVERY constraint" is wrong in the opposite
    direction — `ref=` defines nothing and must NOT be re-registered
    (`parser/produce_xpath.go:128-133`), or a second registration under the same
    name fabricates a `sch-props-correct` (§3.17.6.1) clause 2 collision against
    the very definition it reuses. The repair restated the contract as every
    identity-constraint **DEFINITION** exactly once, with the carve-out as its own
    paragraph.
  - **Round 2 confirmed both round-1 findings genuinely fixed** — citations
    re-derived rather than taken on trust, `ref=` semantics re-checked against
    §3.11.2's mapping, gate green (`golangci-lint run` → 0 issues, conformance
    ok), and the new test mutation-verified load-bearing a second time (dropping
    `AddIdentityConstraint` makes both the `key` and `unique` subtests fail with
    exactly the predicted `src-resolve` cl. 1.7 false reject).
  - **The blocking finding is one the BRANCH created.** `xsd/schema.go:536-537`
    — `Schema.IdentityConstraints()`, the reader of the exact slice
    `AddIdentityConstraint` writes — still says "the top-level identity-constraint
    definitions", proven false by execution (a `<key>` nested in a local
    `<element>` inside a named complexType comes back from it). Before this branch
    both sides said "top-level": wrong, but ONE story. `3c0c918` made
    `AddIdentityConstraint` say "'Top-level' is NOT the contract here" and left the
    paired accessor asserting the opposite — a self-contradiction 440 lines apart
    in one file, on the exported surface, about the exact fact the commit exists
    to establish, visible to anyone running `go doc ./xsd`. The arbiter ruled it
    in scope rather than deferrable **because the docs ARE the deliverable of a
    doc-only commit** (STYLE T5 directly; T6 as the nearest norm, its letter being
    scoped to package `doc.go` prose).
- Surprises:
  - **A doc-only issue reached the park cap.** Nothing here was a design
    question, a false accept, or a ratchet hazard; all three findings across two
    rounds were about the accuracy of English sentences carrying spec citations.
    That is the risk profile of a commit whose product IS prose — the usual
    green-gate safety net proves nothing at all about it.
  - **Fixing one half of an inconsistent pair is a way to make things worse
    before making them better.** The pre-branch state was uniformly wrong and
    self-consistent; the branch's improvement to one side was strictly more
    accurate and yet left the file in a state no reader can act on. The unit that
    had to move was the PAIR, not the writer.
  - **The issue body itself carried the wrong section number** (`§3.17.1`
    throughout, including in its Spec block), and both the oracle grounding and
    mason's first commit propagated it before the arbiter grepped the spec. CLAUDE.md's
    "do not guess spec behavior from memory — grep the spec" applies to citations
    inherited from an issue too.
- Friction:
  - **Round 2's finding was reachable from round 1's own material and was not
    reached.** Round 1 read `xsd/schema.go` closely enough to quote lines 99-105
    twice and to certify T5 exported-surface parity, but did not read 440 lines
    down to the paired accessor that the same diff had just falsified. A finding
    that is one `grep 'top-level' xsd/schema.go` away from the site already under
    review cost the second round and the park.
  - **The repair scope in round 1's verdict was enumerated line-by-line and
    mason followed it exactly** — which is what a good repair round looks like,
    and is also why nothing in it prompted a sweep for collateral contradictions.
    A repair round scoped to "the flagged lines only" cannot discover a finding
    outside the flagged lines.
  - No gate leg could have caught any of this: build, test, vet, lint and
    conformance were fully green in both rounds. Doc-contradiction findings are
    produced by an agent reading the file, or not at all.
- Next: the replan is **one sentence wide**. The arbiter's own words — "the diff
  is one sentence away from correct". Carry forward as-is, all three
  independently verified: the §3.17.2 citation (with §3.17.1 kept only for where
  the property is *declared*), the `ref=` carve-out paragraph and its
  `sch-props-correct` cl. 2 rationale, and
  `TestProduceKeyrefOnLocalElementResolvesAcrossDeclarations` (mutation-checked
  twice; "it should survive any replan"). The single addition: reword
  `xsd/schema.go:536-537` off "top-level" — e.g. "every identity-constraint
  definition in the schema, at any nesting depth" — while PRESERVING its existing
  SCHEMA-level vs `ElementDeclaration.IdentityConstraints` §3.3.1 scope contrast.
  No test asserts top-level-only semantics for that accessor, so the fix is prose
  only. Grounding is still valid and reusable (`.agent/grounding-issue-256.md` on
  `wip/issue-256`, summarized in the GROUNDING comment), so a replan does not
  need to re-ground. `wip/issue-256` **stays retired at `c56d9f7` and is never
  resumed**; the cartographer closes #256 as superseded and files the replacement,
  and the fresh attempt starts as `wip/issue-<M>` off `origin/main`.
- Thread record (do not duplicate) — grounding
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/256#issuecomment-5161048579>; round 1
  verdict (reject — the §3.17.2 misattribution and the `ref=` overbreadth, with
  the mutation transcript and the line-by-line repair scope)
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/256#issuecomment-5161117289>; round 2
  verdict (reject, hard cap — both round-1 findings certified fixed by
  independent re-derivation, the `Schema.IdentityConstraints()` contradiction
  proved by execution, and the replan guidance)
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/256#issuecomment-5161167733>.

## 2026-08-02 — #257 — conformance/loader: the closure walk's inability to tell a `loader.Dir` confinement REFUSAL from a genuinely absent D2 is settled as correct-by-two-independent-arguments rather than papered over with a new resolver sentinel; the issue's own option (b) won because §4.2.6.2 makes resolution an *application*-defined "strategy" whose failure reasons are out of spec scope, and because `execSchemaCase` hands ONE `loader.Dir` to the walk, `extraDocsInClosure` AND `parser.Parse` — so the symmetry is structural, not a property of today's suite; the arbiter accepted on round 1 after mutation-killing all three failure modes

- Shipped on `wip/issue-257` as one mason commit — `fbf18a3` (`conformance,loader:
  pin a confinement-refused schemaLocation to the same non-fabricated verdict a
  genuinely absent D2 gets (#257)`) — merged with a pulled-in `origin/main` @
  `dd87b9e` (which had advanced with #256's park and #377's post-land pass) at
  `813ccad`, the tree the arbiter judged. **3 files, +149/−1**: `loader/dir.go`
  and `conformance/schema_closure.go` gain doc-comment paragraphs, and
  `conformance/schema_closure_test.go` gains two regression tests plus their
  fixture helpers. **No production statement changed, no behaviour changed, zero
  new exported identifiers.** The single deleted line is the tail of a reflowed
  comment bullet. **Arbiter ACCEPT on round 1, no repair round consumed** — one
  non-blocking prose imprecision, not charged as repair.
- **Ratchet: unchanged.** The arbiter's figure, copied verbatim and not
  recomputed: **`RATCHET: unchanged`**. Proof mechanism, also verbatim: it ran
  `GOXSD_RATCHET=1 go test ./conformance -run TestConformance -count=1` — ok
  (23.4s) — and *"`git status --porcelain` empty afterward, so **no expectations
  file moved in either direction**."* No file under
  `conformance/testdata/expectations/` appears in the diff, and no lane could
  have moved: the diff is comments plus unit tests over synthetic
  `writeSchemaTree` trees and decides no suite fixture at all.
- **The hazard as filed, and why it dissolved.** `conformance/schema_closure.go`'s
  discovery walk (landed with #242, reshaped by #276) and `loader.Dir` both map a
  `..`-traversal-confinement refusal onto the same `loader.ErrNotFound` a
  genuinely-missing document yields, so the walk cannot distinguish "D2 does not
  exist" (§4.2.3 clause 2.4's spec-sanctioned non-error) from "`loader.Dir`
  declined to even try, for an engineering-only defense **no XSD text governs**".
  The filed worry was a latent false-accept: a suite bump introducing a `../`
  `schemaLocation` pointing at a REAL document. The issue offered (a) a new
  distinguishable resolver-level sentinel or (b) document + test that the current
  collapse is intentional and safe. **(b) was chosen, and the session's job was
  to make (b) provable rather than merely asserted** — which took two independent
  arguments, both verified by the arbiter against primary sources rather than
  taken from the grounding summary.
- **Argument 1 — BY SPEC (oracle-grounded, arbiter re-derived from
  `docs/specs/md/xmlschema11-1.md`).** The spec draws no distinction between
  "genuinely does not resolve" and "processor-local policy declines to attempt
  resolution", and this is affirmative text, not mere silence:
  - `src-include` §4.2.3 clause 2.4 reads *"**D2** does not exist (e.g. because
    the ·actual value· of the `schemaLocation` [attribute] does not resolve
    successfully)"* — the **`e.g.` is load-bearing**: the clause offers one
    illustrative cause, enumerates none exhaustively, distinguishes none. Its
    trailing prose: *"It is not an error for the ·actual value· of the
    `schemaLocation` [attribute] to fail to resolve at all, in which case the
    corresponding inclusion must not be performed."*
  - `src-import` §4.2.6.2 is the stronger one: *"It is **not** an error for **the
    application schema component reference strategy** to fail."* The spec's own
    term for resolution names it as **application-defined**, putting its internal
    failure reasons out of scope by construction. `loader.Dir`'s confined-to-root
    serving IS that strategy for its callers.
  - Corroboration the oracle supplied and this entry keeps because it forecloses
    the counter-argument: §C.2.5 `vsc_failure` discusses only a processor's
    *reaction* to failure, never the *cause*; §4.3.2 `schema-loc` clause 3
    explicitly contemplates a processor that never attempts dereferencing **as a
    matter of policy**; and §4.2.6.2 states that anything stricter ("perhaps
    calling for a processor-dependent error should the URI fail to resolve") is
    available only by opting into a **conformance profile** — layered policy, not
    a base-spec distinction between failure causes.
  - **This is not a new argument in this repo.** `parser/parse.go`'s
    `assembly.fetch` doc block already cites the identical §4.2.3-clause-2.4 +
    §4.2.6.2 pair for identical reasoning, and already draws the same line the
    new prose does — *"Any OTHER resolver failure is real: a permission or
    transport error is not silently downgraded to 'absent'."* The change makes
    `loader` and `conformance` say out loud what `parser` already relied on.
- **Argument 2 — BY CONSTRUCTION, and this is the half that makes the fix
  durable.** `conformance/schema.go`'s `execSchemaCase` builds exactly **one**
  `loader.Dir` and threads that same instance into the closure scan, into
  `extraDocsInClosure`, and into `parser.Parse`. `dirResolver` is a value type
  holding only `root string`, with no per-call or per-instance mutable state, so
  the walk and Parse are structurally guaranteed to reach the **identical**
  resolve-or-refuse verdict for every `schemaLocation` hint — the walk cannot
  discover less than Parse composes. **The symmetry therefore holds for any suite
  shape, not because the pinned suite happens to contain no `../` hint**, which
  is precisely the difference between closing this issue and deferring it again.
- **Falsifiability — three mutants, applied and reverted in the arbiter's own
  tree, not taken on mason's report** (`git status --porcelain` confirmed clean
  after each):
  1. `conformance/schema.go`'s conjunction neutered to `if false && scan.unresolved
     && perr != nil` → `TestSchemaExecutorDeclinesConfinementRefusedDirectiveTarget`
     FAILS on all three directives at `expectValid=false`. That is the
     fabricated-rejection false-accept the test exists to catch.
  2. `loader/dir.go`'s confinement check disabled → **both** new tests FAIL, at
     `writeEscapedTree`'s own premise assertion. The anti-vacuity guard fires
     **before** the verdict assertions, which is the correct ordering: it makes
     the tests unable to silently decay into another absent-file case.
  3. `conformance/schema_closure.go`'s `compose` made to decline on any resolver
     error — the over-correction the issue weighed — →
     `TestSchemaExecutorDecidesUnbrokenConfinementRefusedDirective` FAILS on
     `<include>` and `<override>`; the `<import>` arm correctly stays green,
     since it flows through `importDirective`, not `compose`.
- Decisions:
  1. **Option (b), not (a).** A new resolver-level sentinel would have added
     exported surface to `loader` to encode a distinction **the spec refuses to
     make** — and, given argument 2, one no consumer in this repo could act on.
     The cheaper and truer fix is to write the two arguments down where the next
     reader of either file will hit them.
  2. **The regression pair mirrors #276's `noD2Trees` pair shape-for-shape**, so
     both hazards drive through identical assertions: broken polarity (the root
     names a type only the escaped document defines → DECLINED under **both**
     `expectValid` values) and unbroken polarity (nothing depends on it → DECIDED
     valid, and Fail under a flipped expectation). The second test is the one
     that forbids the over-correction: declining every confinement refusal
     outright would forfeit live conformance ground for a policy §4.2.6.2 leaves
     to the application.
  3. **Each tree asserts its own premise.** `writeEscapedTree` `os.Stat`s the
     escaped sibling AND calls `loader.Dir(caseDir).Resolve` to confirm the
     refusal is `ErrNotFound`, before any verdict is asserted. Without it a hint
     that stopped escaping, or a sibling never written, would quietly degrade
     both tests into a third copy of the absent-file case.
  4. **`escapedD2Trees` deliberately parallels `noD2Trees` and was NOT unified
     with it** (arbiter T4 ruling): the shapes differ substantively — two
     documents in sibling directories vs. one document naming a nonexistent
     target — and the parallelism is the deliverable, since it is what lets the
     refusal and the absence be compared at every polarity.
- Surprises:
  1. **The issue's own framing had already been narrowed for us, twice, on the
     thread.** #242's post-land pass confirmed labels and scope; #276's post-land
     pass re-verified the premise against `main` and recorded that *"the code it
     points at moved and the shape of fix (a) is now narrower"*. The session
     inherited a live, re-checked issue rather than a stale one — the post-land
     pass discipline paying out on an issue nobody had touched in weeks.
  2. **A "latent false-accept" issue resolved with zero production semantic
     change.** The hazard was real as stated, and the answer was that two
     properties already true of the code — a spec licence and a single shared
     resolver instance — jointly foreclose it. What was missing was not a guard
     but a *written proof*, plus tests that fail if either property is ever
     removed. Mutant 2 is the one that matters most there: it fires the moment
     anybody deletes the confinement.
- Friction:
  1. **One prose imprecision the arbiter flagged and explicitly did not charge.**
     The new `schema_closure.go` header says *"Resolve is a pure function of
     (namespace, location)"*; that is strictly true only of the **confinement
     arm** (`filepath.Rel` over root+location, no I/O) — the found-vs-absent arm
     reads the filesystem. The load-bearing claim (no hidden state, therefore
     identical verdict at both call sites) is unaffected, and the arm this issue
     is about *is* pure. The arbiter's exact replacement wording, recorded here
     so a later touch need not re-derive it: *"a function of (root, namespace,
     location) with no per-instance state"*.
  2. **Nothing else.** One round, one commit, no repair, no detour. `gofmt -l .`
     reports only the generated `xsderr/catalog.go`, untouched here and excluded
     by `.golangci.yml`'s own gofmt linter — not attributable to this diff.
  3. Gate part 2 **was** run for real this session (`golangci-lint run` → **0
     issues**, independently by the arbiter), the second consecutive session to
     manage it after the three-session streak #377's entry recorded. The pinned
     `go run` incantation is already routed to **#426**; nothing new to file.
- Next:
  1. **[post-land pass, not yet run]** #257's pass owes: the Friction 1 rewording
     — a one-line comment fix, natural to fold into **#458**, which is already
     `ready` and already carries three closure-prose corrections across
     `schema_closure.go` / `schema.go` / `schema_closure_test.go`, the exact same
     files and the exact same class — plus the usual unblock scan and branch
     sweep. **Folding it into #458 rather than filing a fourth ticket is a
     recommendation, not a decision**; the pass owns it.
  2. **[planning, still blocking]** #377's pass recorded that **band 1 is empty**
     and that a full `/backlog` re-derivation is now the blocking planning action
     rather than merely due. #257 was pulled from outside any band and does not
     change that: it removes one more `ready` issue without supplying a successor.
  3. **[carried, undischarged — the count of sessions these survive is the point
     of listing them]** #400, #430, #347, #270, #426, #336, #413, #414/#438,
     #417, #458; #346's two unfiled T4/prose advisories; #365's 20-file
     `negativeInteger`/`nonNegativeInteger`/`nonPositiveInteger`/`positiveInteger`
     harvest; and **#324's A1 (`boolAttr`, eleven call sites, no normalization)
     and A2 (eight `strings.TrimSpace` keyword-lexical sites in
     `parser/produce_complex.go`)**, whose post-land pass has still not run.
     **#256 remains parked under `needs-replan`**, awaiting the cartographer's
     supersede-and-refile.
- Thread record (do not duplicate) — <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/257>:
  the GROUNDING at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/257#issuecomment-5161209662> (clause
  2.4's `e.g.` verbatim, §4.2.6.2's "application schema component reference
  strategy", and the §C.2.5 / §4.3.2 clause 3 / conformance-profile corroboration

  neither**) and the arbiter's ACCEPT verdict at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/257#issuecomment-5161271273> (the

  and NOT re-derived: #242's post-land pass
  (<https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/257#issuecomment-5080164602>) and
  #276's (<https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/257#issuecomment-5152430717>),
  which narrowed fix (a) after `s.unresolved` landed. Prior art referenced:
  **#276** (the absent-D2 pair this one mirrors), **#242** (the walk itself), and
  `parser/parse.go`'s `assembly.fetch`, which has cited this same §4.2.3 +
  §4.2.6.2 pair all along. This entry is WORKFLOW's "Land" step, written on the
  WIP branch after the verdict and **before** the PR is opened so it rides in the
  squash; the branch's commits squash into one whose message carries `Spec:
  src-include §4.2.3 clause 2.4, src-import §4.2.6.2` and `Ratchet: unchanged`.

## 2026-08-02 — #371 — xsd: `cos-valid-simple-default` lands as ONE predicate, `checkSimpleDefault`, charged by `a-props-correct` clause 2 and `au-props-correct` clause 2 over two Phase E walks; the warden's pre-flight measured a 76-fixture `xs:anySimpleType` false-reject that never materialised, because the three undecided gates are not an approximation of the pipeline's own tests but literally the same three calls; the arbiter accepted on round 1 with the schema lane **+11, zero downward flips**, every flip attributed case by case

- Shipped on `wip/issue-371` off `origin/main` @ `4b9ca89` as three commits:
  `7857041` (mason — `wip(#371): cos-valid-simple-default via one shared
  checkSimpleDefault`), `b19e7b3` (`xsd/value: tag the item/member facet-compile
  residue GAP(value) per warden's diff review (#371)`, the one warden diff-review
  finding folded in-session), and `5310fbd` (`conformance: ratchet schema lane
  after #371's cos-valid-simple-default`, the arbiter's ratchet write). **14
  files, +874/−129**: `xsd/valuespace.go` (the interface gains its third method),
  `value/valuespace.go` (the adapter that implements it), `xsd/valueconstraintvalid.go`
  (the shared predicate and the new declaration-side walk), `xsd/resolve.go`
  (Phase E redescribed as two walks); doc-accuracy repairs across
  `xsd/attributedeclaration.go`, `xsd/attributeuse.go`, `xsd/doc.go`,
  `value/doc.go`, `xsd/schema.go`, `parser/produce.go`, `parser/parse.go`; tests
  in `value/valuespace_test.go` (+160) and `xsd/valueconstraintvalid_test.go`
  (+223); and `conformance/testdata/expectations/schema.txt` (the ratchet).
  **Exactly one new exported identifier**, `ValueSpace.ValidDefault(t *SimpleType,
  vc ValueConstraint) (valid, decided bool)` — zero new exported types, funcs,
  fields or consts. **Arbiter ACCEPT on round 1, no repair round consumed.**
- **Ratchet — the arbiter's figures, copied verbatim and not recomputed:**
  **`RATCHET: schema 9155 → 9166 pass (+11); datatypes 1131 unchanged; instance 0
  unchanged; xpath/json/ber empty (0 cases)`**. **Downward flips: 0** — the
  arbiter's own proof, `diff | grep '^<' | grep -c ' pass$'` = 0, i.e. every
  changed line is `fail` → `pass`; schema.txt's line count unchanged at 15432, so
  no case vanished. The branch **touches nothing under `conformance/`** before the
  ratchet write: `git diff origin/main...HEAD -- conformance/` was empty at the
  point of judgement, so no expectation was hand-edited in either direction.
- **Per-flip attribution — all 11, each re-run through `parser.Produce` to read
  the rule ID off the actual rejection and each cross-checked against the W3C
  `msMeta` declared validity. All 11 are declared `validity="invalid"` by the
  suite.** Both new call sites are represented, and no flip is explained by
  anything but this diff:
  - **7 via `a-props-correct` clause 2** (the declaration-side walk):
    `addB041` (`msData/additional/test72405.xsd` — `<attribute name="UTME"
    type="I6Type" default="0000000"/>` where `I6Type` is integer + pattern
    `\d{6}`; seven digits fails the pattern, and the suite's own documentation for
    the group reads *"Test invalid default value against a pattern"*), and
    `attKa003`/`004`/`005`/`007`/`008`/`009`, all global `xsd:integer`-typed
    declarations carrying `default="abc"` or `fixed="abc"`.
  - **4 via `au-props-correct` clause 2** (the use-side walk, against the RESOLVED
    declaration's type): `attO002` and `attO003` (local `<attribute
    type="xsd:integer" fixed="abc"/>` / `default="abc"` — §3.2.2.2 hands a local
    default/fixed to the sibling USE, so only the use-side walk can reach these
    at all), `attO005`, and `attZ004` (`<xs:attribute ref="_7544623984"
    default="UARHYHF"/>` charged against the ref target's `xs:decimal`).
  - Caveat the arbiter recorded rather than glossed: `attKa005`/`008`/`009` also
    carry `use="prohibited"`/`use="required"` on a GLOBAL `<attribute>`, an
    independent `src-attribute` fault. The lane compares validity verdicts, not
    rule IDs, so those three would flip on either ground — but the clause-2 ground
    holds on its own merits (`type="xsd:integer"` plus `"abc"`), so none is a
    wrong-reason pass.
  - Sample verdict text, showing rule ID, `Loc` and clause all landing correctly:
    `test72405.xsd:11:6: [a-props-correct] attribute declaration UTME has a {value
    constraint} of "0000000", which is not Datatype Valid with respect to its
    {type definition} (Datatypes §4.1.4 cvc-datatype-valid), so it is not a valid
    default (a-props-correct clause 2, cos-valid-simple-default §3.2.6.2)`.
- **The grounding, and the one finding that shaped the whole design.**
  `cos-valid-simple-default` (§3.2.6.2, `xmlschema11-1.md:981`) has two clauses:
  *"1 V.{lexical form} is ·valid· with respect to T as defined by Datatype Valid
  … 2 V.{lexical form} maps to V.{value} in the value space of T."* The oracle's
  decisive reading is that **clause 2 collapses into clause 1 for THIS codebase's
  component model**: the spec's Value Constraint record carries `{lexical form}`
  and `{value}` as two independently Required fields (`:794`), so nothing forces
  them to agree and clause 2 is what enforces it — but `xsd.ValueConstraint`
  **carries `{lexical form}` only, never a separately-stored `{value}`**, so
  `{value}` is always DERIVED through the very mapping clause 1 exercises. Clause
  2 therefore reduces to "the mapping succeeds", which `value.ValidateLexical`
  (cvc-datatype-valid §4.1.4 — lexical facets, the constructive lexical→value
  mapping, and the value-based facets, in one call) already decides for all three
  varieties. The landed `ValueSpace.ValidDefault` doc states that collapse
  explicitly, so the reasoning is not re-derived by the next reader.
  - The one place the spec leaves clause 2 genuinely separable is QName/NOTATION,
    and the evidence is **Appendix E.2 item 1** (`:6438`, INFORMATIVE — the oracle
    flagged this as medium-confidence support rather than high): *"it is
    ·implementation-dependent· whether ·namespace fixup· occurs to ensure that the
    {lexical form} maps to the {value}."* PRINCIPLES 19 territory — a value
    constraint carries no in-scope-namespace context, so this codebase cannot
    decide it, and it fails open (gate 1 below).
  - Both call sites are PRESENCE-gated — a-props-correct's *"if there is a {value
    constraint}"* and au-props-correct's *"If U.{value constraint} is not
    ·absent·"* are semantically identical — and "clause not reached" is NOT the
    same as "reached and vacuously satisfied". `TestPhaseEAPropsCorrectClause2Accepts`
    pins the distinction by asserting `defaultCalls == 0`.
  - `{type definition}` is never ·absent· for an attribute declaration (`:794`
    Required; §3.2.2.2's third tier defaults it to `xs:anySimpleType`), and
    `xs:anySimpleType`/`xs:anyAtomicType` are the ·special· datatypes
    (`xmlschema11-2.md:475`) for which Datatype Valid is **unconditionally true**.
    That sentence is the whole of pre-flight finding 1, in advance.
- **Decisions — the three warden PRE-FLIGHT findings (verdict: `revise`), and how
  each was closed.** The warden was run BEFORE code, on the design, and returned
  three blocking findings — the same pattern that paid out for #252 (eight
  findings turned into doc-contract requirements rather than repair rounds) and
  #340 (two design errors caught with no arbiter rejection at all).
  1. **[P9/ratchet — BLOCKING] The ungoverned-type false reject, measured at 76
     fixtures.** The sketched adapter was `if _, err := ValidateLexical(...); err
     != nil { return false, true }`, which reads `governingMapping`'s ok=false —
     the answer for `xs:anySimpleType` and `xs:anyAtomicType`, which
     `builtin/strict/strict.go` maps neither of — as a **decided reject**. The
     warden measured **76 self-closing typeless `<attribute>` elements carrying
     `default=`/`fixed=` in `testdata/xsdtests`**, every one of which §3.2.2.2's
     third tier types as `xs:anySimpleType`. *"This alone would drop the schema
     lane."* **Closed by gate 2**: `governingMapping(vs.b, t)` runs BEFORE the
     pipeline and answers `(false, false)`.
  2. **[P9 — BLOCKING] Construction-stage errors misattributed as verdicts.**
     Even once governed, `ValidateLexical` surfaces failures that say nothing
     about the value constraint — `compile()`'s `src-pattern-value` (a pattern
     `regex.Translate` cannot express), `src-enumeration-value`, "no backend
     mapping governs declaring type". Charging those as clause 2 rejects a schema
     for an unrelated facet, under the wrong rule ID and against the wrong
     component. **Closed by gate 3**: `compile(vs.b, t)`, also before the
     pipeline, also `(false, false)`.
  3. **[T1/robustness — BLOCKING] The newly-reachable panic surface.**
     `ValidateLexical` PANICS on a violated `cos-applicable-facets` (§4.1.5)
     precondition, and today no `xsd` finalize path enters the facet pipeline at
     all (`ValueSpace.Identical` calls `Mapping.Parse` only), so `FinalizeWith`
     cannot currently panic on component data. This design makes it reachable for
     any hand-built `*SimpleType`. The warden offered two exits — route through an
     entry point that reports undecided where ValidateLexical would panic, **or**
     state the precondition explicitly on `ValidDefault` AND on `FinalizeWith` and
     own it, *"do not leave it unstated."* **Closed by taking the second exit**: a
     consolidated `GAP(xsd)` paragraph on `checkSimpleDefault` plus a new
     precondition paragraph on `FinalizeWith`. Documented, not fixed — see Next.
- **Decisions — the rest, and why.**
  1. **Three pre-gates in front of an unchanged pipeline, not a refactor of it.**
     The warden's suggested redesign for finding 2 was to factor `validateLexical`
     into separable construction and verdict stages behind an unexported
     `value.datatypeValid(b, st, lex) (ok, decided bool)`. What landed instead is
     three gates that run before the pipeline, and **the substitution is the
     stronger form, not the cheaper one**: gate 2 is character-for-character the
     test `validateLexical` applies at `value/facets.go:143` and, via
     `unionGoverned`, the one `validateUnion` applies at `value/union.go:57`; gate
     3 is the same `compile` call made at `facets.go:119` and `union.go:61`. The
     warden's diff review put it exactly right — *"gates 2 and 3 are not a
     hand-rolled approximation of what the pipeline does, they are literally the
     same two calls the pipeline itself makes, so the pre-check and the verdict
     cannot drift."* A refactor could have drifted; a shared call cannot.
  2. **Gate 1 (`needsContext`) is recursive, and its ORDER is load-bearing.** The
     warden's answer to the design's question 3 was that the narrower
     non-recursive alternative is NOT the safer one: `resolveQNameLexical`
     (`builtin/strict/qname.go:86-89`) rejects EVERY lexical when `ctx == nil`,
     and strict **does** map QName and NOTATION — so the governed gate cannot
     catch them and a list-of-QName or union-with-QName default would be a
     guaranteed false reject. Gate 1 must therefore precede gate 2.
     `TestValidDefaultAcceptsContextDependentTypes` makes that a real ordering pin
     by using a backend that maps QName. For a union, ANY context-dependent member
     makes the whole union undecided, because dispatch takes the first member that
     accepts and a member decidable only WITH context could change which that is.
  3. **`ValidDefault` decides list and union; the two comparisons still refuse
     them.** The asymmetry is deliberate and now documented per method: the
     comparisons need a SHARED mapping across two potentially different types, this
     predicate needs one type's mapping, and Datatype Valid clauses 2.2/2.3 define
     both varieties.
  4. **A third method on `ValueSpace`, not a separate `DefaultValidator`
     interface** (warden question 5). A second interface needs either a second
     installation seam or an interface upgrade at the boundary (`if v, ok :=
     s.valueSpace.(DefaultValidator)`) — the capability-erosion type switch T2
     forbids — and makes "a value space is installed but cannot validate defaults"
     a representable state (T1). One installer, one capability bundle; the
     differing fail-open SCOPE is a per-method contract detail, not a second type.
     Recorded in the commit body: adding a method to an exported interface is a
     source break for any foreign implementer, acceptable here because
     `FinalizeWith` is the only installation point and `value.NewValueSpace` the
     only real implementation.
  5. **The two-walk split** (warden question 4). `s.attributes` is the GLOBAL
     table only; a LOCAL declaration is reachable solely through its owning use;
     a global declaration may be referenced by no use at all. Neither walk alone
     reaches every declaration, and a global one is charged **once, not once per
     referencing use** — one charge per component keeps the first reported failure
     deterministic. `s.attributes` is the document-order slice, so D1/D2 hold and
     no map is ranged.
  6. **Clause 2 now precedes clause 3 in `checkAttributeUseValueConstraint`, and
     that is a visible message change.** §3.5.6 lists 2 before 3, and "your lexical
     denotes no value at all" strictly subsumes "your value differs from the
     declaration's" — reporting the identity comparison for a lexical that denotes
     nothing would mislead. `TestPhaseEClause2PrecedesClause3` pins both halves:
     the rule/clause in the message AND `vs.calls != 0`, i.e. that clause 3 never
     consulted the value space. The second assertion is what makes it an ordering
     pin rather than a message-string pin.
  7. **The `(valid, decided bool)` shape was kept over the warden's optional
     `(reason error, decided bool)`** — and, per the warden's own condition, the
     reason for dropping the per-facet detail is now stated in the interface doc:
     the caller reports under `a-props-correct`/`au-props-correct` at the owning
     component's `Loc`, so threading the datatype-layer reason out would file a
     `cvc-*` message under a schema-component rule ID. A user who wants that detail
     gets it from instance validation, where it is charged `cvc-*`.
  8. **Seven doc-only files were in scope by necessity, not by choice.** Each had
     enumerated the value-space consumers EXHAUSTIVELY — `xsd/schema.go:205`
     (*"every {value}-identity comparison the resolution pass would make"*),
     `parser/produce.go:72` and `parser/parse.go:545` (*"the finalize-time
     constraints that COMPARE two {value}s"*), plus `xsd/doc.go`, `value/doc.go`,
     and the *"clause 2 is NOT enforced here / stays deferred to its own issue"*
     prose in `xsd/attributedeclaration.go` and `xsd/attributeuse.go`. This diff
     did not make those sentences incomplete, it made them **false**. The warden
     ruled it accuracy maintenance, not scope creep.
  9. **The anti-regression check the issue demanded.** The arbiter applied four
     surgical mutations, each reverted with `git status --porcelain` verified clean
     afterward: `checkSimpleDefault` always returning nil → 4 `xsd` tests fail;
     gate 1 disabled → `TestValidDefaultAcceptsContextDependentTypes` fails on both
     QName and NOTATION; gate 2 disabled → `TestValidDefaultAcceptsUngovernedTypes/xs:anySimpleType`
     fails; gate 3 disabled → `TestValidDefaultAcceptsConstructionStageFailures`
     fails. No new test passes with its subject reverted.
- Surprises:
  1. **The pre-flight's 76-fixture false-reject did not materialise anywhere — and
     the mutation testing showed exactly which gate is why.** With gate 2 removed,
     **only** the `xs:anySimpleType` row fails: gate 3 happens to catch the other
     four ungoverned shapes on its own. So `governingMapping` is load-bearing for
     precisely the case the pre-flight measured at 76 fixtures and for nothing
     else, and the single row that pins it is the one that matters. A design
     finding whose *scope* was over-broad but whose *core* was exactly right — and
     it would have been invisible without the mutation.
  2. **`attO005` is the subtlest of the eleven and arrives through whitespace
     collapse, not through parsing.** `default="   1  2"` collapses to `1 2`,
     which is still not an `xs:integer` — a true clause-2 reject reached one stage
     earlier than the obvious ones. (Adjacent precedent from the same day: #324's
     round-1 rejection turned on `strings.TrimSpace` trimming a WIDER class than
     §4.3.6 collapse.)
  3. **The two walks cannot double-charge on any parser-built schema, for a reason
     that lives in the producer.** `produceLocalAttribute` puts a local
     `<attribute default=…>`'s value constraint on the USE, never on the
     declaration (*"{value constraint} always absent on the declaration"*), so the
     local-declaration arm of the use-side walk is live **only for `SchemaBuilder`
     callers** — the conformance suite exercises it zero times, and
     `TestPhaseEAPropsCorrectClause2LocalDeclaration` is the only thing that pins
     it at all.
  4. **The warden's DIFF REVIEW (its second run this session) produced a landed
     change with no arbiter rejection in between** — `b19e7b3`, adding the missing
     `GAP(value):` prefix to the item/member residue paragraph. The warden's
     argument for why that one was not optional is worth keeping: gates 1–3 each
     carry `GAP(value):`, and this residue **REJECTS** rather than failing open,
     *"which is the opposite direction from every other fail-open residue in this
     file"* — so it is the one most in need of the marker. Same shape as #417,
     where the warden's diff review rather than the arbiter caught the doc
     overreach.
  5. **The residue's blast radius was measured, not asserted.** Of
     `testdata/xsdtests`: 219 schemas carry an `<attribute … default=/fixed=>`;
     only **14** of those also contain a `<list>` or `<union>` anywhere; of those
     14 only **5** contain any `<pattern>` (`msData/additional/xsd.xsd` 7,
     `test73722.xsd` 2, `test65699.xsd` 2, `addB194.xsd` 1, `common/xsts.xsd` 1).
     The union half is narrower still, since `dispatchUnion` folds a member's
     compile error into that member's rejection.
- Friction:
  1. **The D3 `clause` finding was raised three times because it was applied
     halfway.** At pre-flight the warden asked for the `clause` parameter to be
     dropped and the phrase derived from `rule`. The parameter WAS dropped and the
     doc now claims the phrase is *"DERIVED from rule rather than passed alongside
     it"* — but the code is `clause := "a-props-correct clause 2"; if rule ==
     ruleAuPropsCorrect { clause = "au-props-correct clause 2" }`, which hardcodes
     both rule names a SECOND time (they already exist as
     `ruleAPropsCorrect`/`ruleAuPropsCorrect`) and would silently mislabel any
     future third caller as a-props-correct. So the same finding came back at the
     diff review and again in the arbiter's verdict, now aimed at a doc claim that
     the code does not honour. `clause := string(rule) + " clause 2"` is
     byte-identical output. **A finding applied halfway costs more review than one
     not applied at all**, because it also plants a false comment.
  2. **Two reviewers independently re-derived the same five non-blocking
     findings.** The warden's diff review and the arbiter's verdict overlap almost
     item-for-item (the `needsContext` nil-guard justification, the D3 clause
     string, "exactly once" vs #401, the missing `effectiveWhiteSpace` panic class,
     and the T4 duplicate switch at `:244`). That is real duplicated effort, and
     only one of the five — the one the warden could point at an explicit
     acceptance criterion for — was folded in-session; the other five (four shared
     plus the arbiter's own framing) ride into the ledger below.
  3. **Nothing else.** One issue, one design round, one implementation, one
     folded-in follow-up commit, no arbiter repair round, no detour. Gate part 2
     (`golangci-lint run` → **0 issues**) was independently run by the arbiter —
     the third consecutive session to manage it after the streak #377's entry
     recorded.
- Next:
  1. **[follow-up issue — RECOMMENDED to the cartographer, the residual this
     session documented but did not fix]** `value/valuespace.go`'s `ValidDefault`
     doc now carries a `GAP(value): item/member facet compilation` paragraph. The
     `compile` gate covers **T's OWN effective facets only**: a list's ITEM type
     and a union's MEMBER types compile inside the dispatch (`listMapping`'s
     `Parse` recurses through `validateLexical`; `dispatchUnion` folds every
     member's rejection into one `cvc-datatype-valid` error), so a
     construction-stage failure down there **still reaches the caller as a decided
     REJECT** — a narrow but real residual false-reject risk of exactly the class
     the `compile` pre-check was added to close for the top-level type. Closing it
     needs package `value`'s facet pipeline to separate construction-stage from
     verdict-stage errors PER MEMBER/ITEM, which is a change to that package's own
     error model rather than to this adapter, and a bigger change than #371 — so
     it belongs to its own issue. Blast radius already measured (see Surprise 5),
     so the issue can be filed with its scope pre-costed.
  2. **[follow-up issue — file, or explicitly DEFER with reasoning, cartographer's
     call]** The `GAP(xsd)` panic-surface residual in
     `xsd/valueconstraintvalid.go`'s `checkSimpleDefault`:
     `value.ValidateLexical`/`compile` can PANIC on a violated
     `cos-applicable-facets` (§4.1.5) precondition. That precondition is
     discharged by `builtin.CheckSimpleTypeRestriction` for every type the PARSER
     builds — so the parser path, the conformance suite and the ratchet are all
     unaffected — but it is **enforced nowhere in package `xsd` itself**, so a
     `*SimpleType` assembled through this package's constructors bypasses the seam
     and the panic is newly reachable at `FinalizeWith`. Pre-existing debt, newly
     reachable through `xsd`'s own finalize phase **for the first time**, which is
     what makes it worth a decision now rather than another silent carry.
     Recovering from the panic is not an option: it marks a violated precondition,
     not a validity verdict. Note when scoping it that
     `checkVarietyApplicableFacets` (`xsd/derivation.go:911`) already enforces
     §4.1.5 for the LIST and UNION varieties — only the atomic per-primitive table
     is outside this leaf.
  3. **[cheap corrections — RECOMMENDED to fold, not necessarily to ticket]** The
     arbiter's five non-blocking findings, none of which was charged as a repair:
     - **[P2]** `value/valuespace.go` `needsContext`'s nil-guard justification is
       FALSE — it claims "a programmatically built list or union can hold a nil
       slot"; `xsd/simpletype.go:96` states the opposite and is right, because
       `checkListGraph` rejects an absent {item type definition} and
       `checkUnionGraph` rejects a nil member at `NewSimpleType`. Keep the guards,
       replace the justification ("the constructors already forbid this;
       belt-and-braces, since `governingMapping` on the next line is not nil-safe
       either").
     - **[P2]** `xsd/valueconstraintvalid.go:52`'s *"Together the two reach every
       declaration exactly once"* is contradicted by the **#401 inherited-uses note
       twenty lines later in the same file**: a local declaration owned by an
       inherited use is re-checked at every complex type that inherits it. No D1
       problem (same verdict, same `Loc`), but it should read "at least once, at a
       stable Loc" and point at the #401 note.
     - **[D3]** the `clause` string, per Friction 1 — `clause := string(rule) + "
       clause 2"`.
     - **[P3]** the `GAP(xsd)` panic paragraph is one panic class short: it names
       only `cos-applicable-facets`, but `effectiveWhiteSpace`
       (`value/whitespace.go`) ALSO panics when no whiteSpace facet is in force on
       an atomic or list type. Both new test helpers deliberately add a collapse
       whiteSpace facet, which is the tell that the class is known. Name it here
       and in the `FinalizeWith` precondition note.
     - **[T4]** `xsd/valueconstraintvalid.go:244` —
       `u.AttributeDeclaration().(LocalAttributeDeclaration)` re-derives the
       variant switch `attributeUseDeclaration` (`xsd/defaultbinding.go:130`)
       performed two lines above. Carried over unaddressed from the pre-flight;
       not a T2 erosion (defining package, sealed sum), but a sibling
       `func (s *Schema) ownedAttributeDeclaration(u AttributeUse)
       (AttributeDeclaration, bool)` would keep one switch per concern.

     All five are cheap and touch only `value/valuespace.go` and
     `xsd/valueconstraintvalid.go`. **The recommendation is to fold them into
     whatever issue next touches those two files rather than ticket them
     separately** — precedent: #445, #423 and #425 folding prose corrections into
     one commit. That is a recommendation, not a decision; the post-land pass owns
     it.
  4. **[post-land pass, not yet run]** #371's pass owes the usual unblock scan and
     branch sweep, plus the follow-up harvest of items 1–3 above. One more
     candidate it should weigh while it is here: the issue's own Notes name
     **`e-props-correct` clause 2** (via `cos-valid-default` §3.3.6.2, with its

  5. **[carried, undischarged — recorded at #257 and not touched by this session;

  warden's **PRE-FLIGHT** (`revise` — three blocking findings, the answers to the
  design's six questions including the seam, the recursive-`needsContext` and
  no-second-interface rulings, and the 76-fixture measurement); the warden's
  **DIFF REVIEW** (`ACCEPT WITH FOLLOW-UP` — both blocking findings verified
  closed by re-reading the pipeline's own call sites, the residue blast-radius
  measurement, the scope ruling on the seven doc-only files, and six non-blocking
  findings); and the **ARBITER VERDICT** (`accept` — the independently re-run
  five-leg gate, the ratchet figures with the zero-downward-flip proof, the
  case-by-case attribution of all 11 flips against `msMeta`, the four mutations,
  and the `go doc ./xsd ValueSpace` before/after surface check). Prior art
  referenced and NOT re-derived: **#236**, which created the `xsd.ValueSpace` seam
  and whose warden pre-flight (finding 8) SPLIT this issue out precisely so
  clause 2's new rejection surface would be attributable on its own; **#401**,
  whose materialised inherited uses is what makes the "exactly once" claim above
  imprecise; **#417**, the precedent for a warden diff review catching what the
  arbiter would otherwise have charged. This entry is WORKFLOW's "Land" step,
  written on the WIP branch after the verdict and **before** the PR is opened so
  it rides in the squash; the branch's commits squash into one whose message
  carries `Spec: cos-valid-simple-default, a-props-correct, au-props-correct,
  cvc-datatype-valid` and `Ratchet: schema 9155 → 9166 (+11)`.

## 2026-08-03 — #259 — parser,conformance: the byte-identical `resolveSchemaLocation` pair collapses into `internal/schemaloc.Resolve`, the module's FIRST `internal/` tree, so the sync obligation two walks depend on is discharged by the compiler instead of by a comment that says "MUST be kept in sync"; the move is verbatim and the ratchet is unchanged by measurement, and the one substantive change is a doc-comment CORRECTION the oracle forced — §4.3.2 clause 4 fixes only WHICH base URI to resolve against and mandates no algorithm, so the RFC-3986/path-join split is goxsd8's own strategy and had been mis-attributed to the spec in both copies

- Shipped on `wip/issue-259` as one mason commit — `ec802b9`
  (`parser,conformance: unify resolveSchemaLocation into internal/schemaloc
  (#259)`) — on top of `origin/main` @ `e2fb2bb` (#371's post-land pass), the
  tree the arbiter judged. **6 files, +133/−91**: `internal/schemaloc/doc.go`
  (new, 21), `internal/schemaloc/schemaloc.go` (new, 40),
  `internal/schemaloc/schemaloc_test.go` (new, 53), `parser/parse.go` (−36),
  `conformance/schema_closure.go` (−43), `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` (rewritten
  seam paragraph + a DAG line). **Arbiter ACCEPT on round 1, no repair round
  consumed**, two non-blocking notes neither charged nor filed.
- **Ratchet: unchanged.** The arbiter's figures, copied and not recomputed:
  `RATCHET: unchanged`, from a `GOXSD_RATCHET=1 go test ./conformance -run
  TestConformance -count=1` run (ok, 39.9s) after which **no expectations file
  was written** — `git status --porcelain` empty before and after. Plain
  conformance ok, 36.1s. **The silence is the result**, not the absence of one:
  a placement refactor that moved a lane in either direction would have been
  evidence the move was not verbatim.
- **"Verbatim" was verified mechanically, not asserted.** The arbiter extracted
  the three function bodies and diffed them rather than reading them:
  `origin/main:parser/parse.go`'s `resolveSchemaLocation` vs.
  `origin/main:conformance/schema_closure.go`'s → identical (the filing premise
  re-confirmed at land time, as the steward had re-confirmed it on 2026-07-26
  and again on 2026-08-02); and the parser's vs. `internal/schemaloc.Resolve`,
  modulo the rename → identical. Same four branches, same order, same
  fall-through. **There was no behaviour surface for this refactor to move**,
  which is what makes the flat ratchet a check rather than a hope.
- **The drift mode this closes is a false ACCEPT, and that is why a T4 duplicate
  earned its own issue.** `conformance/schema_closure.go`'s walk exists to gate
  every document in a schema closure, because `parser.Parse` returns components
  and not the document list. If the walk resolved a hint even slightly
  differently from the parser, it would discover a different document set;
  a document it under-discovered is one whose shape was **never gated**, and the
  expectation banked from that run is a false accept the ratchet then locks in
  permanently. The old comment's answer was a promise ("VERBATIM copy … MUST be
  kept in sync"); the new answer is that there is one definition and both
  packages import it. That paragraph was **DELETED rather than narrowed** — the
  arbiter confirmed `grep -rn "func resolveSchemaLocation" --include=*.go .`
  now returns zero hits and the name is gone from the module entirely.
- **The doc-comment correction is the session's only semantic delta, and it runs
  the opposite way from the usual.** Both retired copies read as though RFC 3986
  resolution semantics were mandated by §4.3.2 clause 4. The oracle's grounding
  — re-derived independently by the arbiter, not taken from the comment —
  found that clause 4 (`xmlschema11-1.md:4267`) says only that *"the [[base
  URI]] of the [[owner element]] must be used to resolve the relative
  references"*: it fixes **which** base URI, never **how**. `grep -c "3986"
  docs/specs/md/xmlschema11-1.md` → **0**; the bare digit string appears nowhere
  in Structures. The surviving comment now frames the split as goxsd8's own
  strategy for honoring clause 4, and says why it exists: `ResolveReference`
  would root a resolver-relative location at `/`, which no directory- or
  map-rooted resolver can serve. **A relocation that carries a false spec
  attribution into a new file's canonical comment would have made the claim
  harder to dislodge, not easier** — moving it was the moment to fix it.
- **Four call sites, where the issue text implied two.** `parser`'s `compose`
  (`parse.go:323`) and `importDocument` (`:392`); `conformance`'s `compose`
  (`schema_closure.go:256`) and `importDirective` (`:331`). The `<import>` arms
  on both sides were the ones the issue's "one call site per walk" framing
  missed. `importDocument`'s deliberate NON-use of the helper for a bare
  `<import>` with no `schemaLocation` survives with its reasoning intact and its
  reference renamed — the empty-location case returns the base, which for a bare
  import would make the importer re-read itself.
- **The algorithm now has direct tests for the first time.** Previously it was
  exercised only through its two callers, i.e. only on the inputs the suite
  happens to produce. `internal/schemaloc/schemaloc_test.go` is table-driven at
  **100.0% of statements**, covering all four branches, the `/`-rooted
  sub-branch, and both parse-error fall-throughs. Coverage being no evidence of
  assertions, the arbiter mutation-tested two branches and reverted each:
  deleting the `strings.HasPrefix(location, "/")` → `path.Clean` arm fails the
  `rooted location against relative base is cleaned, not joined` case; replacing
  `b.ResolveReference(ref).String()` with the path-join fall-through fails
  **five** absolute-base cases.
- Decisions:
  1. **`internal/…`, not an exported seam — and this landing SETS THE PRECEDENT
     #272 follows.** The steward's 2026-07-26 note on the thread made the
     landing-order point explicitly: whichever of #259/#272 lands first fixes
     the shared-package shape for the other. Exporting a resolver would add
     public surface no library consumer has asked for (STYLE T5) purely to let
     one in-module test harness reach it; `internal/` gives the sharing without
     the surface, and is unimportable outside the module by construction. The
     package is stdlib-only (`net/url`, `path`, `strings`), so it sits below
     everything and adds no edge to the dependency DAG's interesting part.
  2. **The move is verbatim, and the `%zz.xsd` mangling was PINNED rather than
     fixed.** `Resolve("http://example.com/a/main.xsd", "%zz.xsd")` returns
     `"http:/example.com/a/%zz.xsd"` — `url.Parse` fails, so the absolute and
     RFC 3986 branches are skipped and `path.Dir` collapses the authority `//`
     to `/`. Pre-existing on both retired copies. A placement refactor that also
     changed behaviour would have forfeited the flat ratchet as a check on
     itself, so the test **describes the behaviour honestly rather than blessing
     it**, and the fix question goes to a follow-up (see Next).
  3. **`docs/ARCHITECTURE.md` was reconciled in the SAME commit** (T6), not
     deferred: the seam paragraph no longer claims location resolution is
     duplicated, scopes the surviving copy to
     `assembly.discover`/`.include`/`.fetch` + `attrValue` under **#272**, and
     keeps the dated "315 → 396 lines, +26%" growth measurement, which remains
     true as a historical claim (the file is 374 lines now, *after* this
     deletion). A new `internal/…` line was added to the DAG diagram.
  4. **`src-include` / `src-override` / `src-import` stay cited at the CALL
     SITES, not moved onto the helper.** `Resolve` only resolves; whether a
     location that fails to resolve is an error is the caller's question, and
     the three rules answer it differently per composition element. `doc.go`
     says this out loud so the next reader does not "consolidate" the citations
     onto the shared function.
- Surprises:
  1. **A pure-placement issue produced a spec-accuracy fix.** The refactor was
     scoped as no-behaviour-change and delivered exactly that — yet grounding a
     comment nobody had questioned since it was written turned up a false
     attribution to the spec, duplicated in both copies, that the unification
     was about to enshrine in one canonical place. **Grounding the prose you are
     merely RELOCATING is not ceremony**; it was the only step that found
     anything this session.
  2. **The issue's own scope estimate was low in the harmless direction.** Two
     call sites became four. Mason found them; nothing was missed and no repair
     was needed. Worth noting only because the same under-count shape (a
     `<import>` arm forgotten alongside `<include>`) has now appeared in #253's
     staleness finding and #377's `<override>` gap — the import/override arms are
     where composition work reliably hides.
  3. **The first `internal/` tree in a module this old is itself the finding.**
     Nine months of "no consumer outside this package, so copy it" produced one
     duplication serious enough to earn a ticket; the answer turned out to be a
     five-minute package that the STYLE rules permitted all along. #272 is now
     unblocked on the shape question that was its stated reason to wait.
- Friction:
  1. **The GROUNDING comment points at a file that cannot survive the session.**
     It ends *"Full grounding saved to `.agent/grounding-issue-259.md` on
     `wip/issue-259`"* — but `.agent/` is in `.gitignore` (line 36), so the file
     was never committed, was never pushed, and dies with the container.
     **Nothing was lost** (the comment itself carries the three findings that
     mattered, verbatim, and the arbiter re-derived all of them from
     `docs/specs/md/` independently), so this is a dangling pointer rather than
     a data loss. But "anything not pushed does not exist" is a CLAUDE.md
     headline, and an agent-authored issue comment that tells a future session
     to go read an ignored path is a small, repeatable way to violate it. The
     cheap durable fix is one line in the oracle's prompt: **post the grounding,
     do not post a path to it.** Recorded here as a first sighting rather than
     filed — if a second session does it, it is a prompt edit at the next
     `/retro`.
  2. **Nothing else.** One round, one commit, no repair, no detour. Gate part 2
     was run for real and independently by the arbiter (`golangci-lint run` →
     **0 issues**) — the **third consecutive session** to manage it, after the
     three-session streak #377's entry recorded. That streak can now be called
     broken rather than interrupted.
- Next:
  1. **[post-land pass, not yet run — TWO arbiter notes are unfiled and this
     entry is their only record until it does]** The arbiter recorded both on
     the thread and filed neither, correctly leaving them to triage:
     (a) **a `schemaLocation` that is not a parsable URI reference arguably
     deserves a clean resolution failure**, not the mangled `path.Join` that
     collapses the authority `//` — pre-existing, now pinned by a test, a
     spec-fidelity question worth its own issue; the consequence today is a
     loader-level resolution failure and **not** a false accept, and both walks
     now fail *identically*, which is this issue's whole point;
     (b) **#272's Notes cite the deleted "VERBATIM copy" comment
     (`schema_closure.go:188-195`) as live evidence** — its Acceptance is
     unaffected and still holds verbatim after this landing, but a one-line
     housekeeping comment closes the loop. Plus the usual unblock scan and
     branch sweep. **#272 is the natural next issue to pull**: it explicitly
     deferred to this one for the `internal/` precedent, and that precedent now
     exists.
  2. **[planning, still blocking, and now worse by one]** #371's post-land note
     recorded **98 open `ready` issues** and re-affirmed #377's finding that
     **band 1 is EMPTY**. #259 was pulled from outside any band and removes one
     more `ready` issue without supplying a successor; the two notes above would
     add up to two back. A full `/backlog` re-derivation remains the blocking
     planning action, and it still inherits **#347**'s question first — a
     98-deep `ready` label is not a queue, and no band can be picked until
     "filed and unblocked" is separated from "in the working queue".
  3. **[carried, undischarged — the count of sessions these survive is the
     point of listing them]** #400, #430, #347, #270, #426, #336, #413,
     #414/#438, #417, #458; #346's two unfiled T4/prose advisories; #365's
     20-file
     `negativeInteger`/`nonNegativeInteger`/`nonPositiveInteger`/`positiveInteger`
     harvest; **#324's A1/A2, whose post-land pass has still not run**; and
     **#257's Friction 1 rewording**, routed to fold into #458. **#256 remains
     CLOSED-and-parked under `needs-replan` with its supersede-and-refile still

     still survives on `origin` — content verified absent from `main` and

## 2026-08-03 — #261 — xsd: `addAll`'s emptiability carve-out — the one line in `particleattribution.go` whose correctness rested entirely on a doc comment — gets its executable pin, a 21-line accept-direction UPA test that goes red the moment `emptiable = emptiable && e` is flipped back to the star transcription's unconditional `true`; mason DEVIATED from the shape the issue prescribed after proving that shape cannot discriminate the two treatments at all, and the arbiter upheld the deviation by building the issue's literal shape as a scratch probe and watching it reject under both

- Shipped on `wip/issue-261` as one mason commit — `74c800b` (`xsd: pin addAll's
  emptiability carve-out with an enclosing-sequence UPA test (#261)`) — on top
  of `origin/main` @ `4895d04` (#259's `internal/schemaloc` unification), the
  tree the arbiter judged. **1 file, +21/−0**: `xsd/particleattribution_test.go`
  gains `TestUPAAllGroupNonEmptiableInSequencePasses` (`:421`) and its six-line
  doc comment. No library file changed; `xsd/particleattribution.go` is
  byte-identical to `main`. **Arbiter ACCEPT on round 1, zero repair rounds, no
  rejections**, two non-blocking follow-up notes recorded and neither filed.
- **Ratchet: unchanged.** The arbiter's figures, copied and not recomputed:
  `RATCHET: unchanged`, from a `GOXSD_RATCHET=1 go test ./conformance -run
  TestConformance -count=1` run that left **zero writes to
  `conformance/testdata/expectations/`** — `git status --porcelain` empty before
  and after. Plain conformance `ok` 23.3s. `go build ./...`, `go test ./...`,
  `go vet ./...` all pass and **`golangci-lint run` → 0 issues**, run
  independently by the arbiter — the **fourth consecutive session** with a real
  gate part 2, after #259's third.
- **The pin was proved failure-capable by the arbiter, not accepted on mason's
  report.** It flipped `addAll`'s accumulation to the star form's unconditional
  `true` and ran the whole `./xsd` package: **exactly one failure, the new
  test**, reporting `[cos-nonambig]` with the two `{urn:upa}x` declarations
  competing *"at the start of"* the content model — the one state emptiability
  controls (`appendToSequence`: `if seq.emptiable { seq.first = mergePositions(…) }`).
  Line restored, `git diff` on the source file empty, `./xsd` green again.
- **Two corroborations taken while the line was flipped, both of which are the
  point of the issue.** (a) *Neither existing `<all>` test fails under the
  flip* — `TestUPAAllGroupCompetition` (`:392`) and
  `TestUPAAllGroupDistinctNamesPass` (`:403`) each build a **bare** `<all>`
  whose emptiability is discarded at the content-model root, so the new test is
  non-duplicative by measurement rather than by inspection. (b) *The full
  conformance suite still passes under the flip* (`ok`, 23.9s), independently
  reconfirming #180's finding that **none of the 15,432 W3C schema-lane cases
  can see this line**. As of this landing the new unit test is the only thing
  standing between the carve-out and a green "simplification".
- Decisions made and why:
  1. **The issue's prescribed shape was REJECTED as unwritable, and mason said
     so instead of writing a test that looked right.** The issue asked for the
     trailing particle to collide with a **member** of the `<all>`. Mason
     determined by code inspection, then confirmed by running under both
     treatments, that `sequence(all(a,b), a)` **rejects either way** — the
     verdict is identical and only the reported state moves ("after element
     declaration a" with the carve-out, "at the start of" without). Asserting
     accept on that shape yields a test that fails on `main`; asserting reject
     yields a test that passes with the change reverted, which the arbiter's
     standing rule forbids outright. Neither is a test.
  2. **The substitute routes the difference through the single consumer of the
     returned emptiability.** `sequence(x{0,1}, all(a,b), x)` — a **leading**
     optional `x?` before the group and a colliding **trailing** `x` after it.
     Emptiability's only reader is `seq.first` in `appendToSequence`, so a
     non-emptiable `<all>` stops carrying the leading `x?` past itself and the
     trailing `x` never joins the start state beside it; under the star form it
     does, and the model is falsely rejected. The arbiter re-derived the
     mechanism in the code rather than empirically — `addAll`'s loop-back
     (`:529-531`) puts every member's first-set into every member's follow-set
     and `appendToSequence` (`:555-557`) then appends the successor's first-set
     to those same follow sets, so a member-collision materialises in
     `follow[a]` **on a path that never consults emptiability at all**. Ruling:
     *"Deviation accepted as better than the issue's text."*
  3. **Assert the ACCEPT direction.** Per the issue and unchanged by the
     deviation: the hazard this pins is a false **reject**, so the test fatals
     on an error and asserts nothing about rule IDs.
- Surprises:
  1. **A well-specified acceptance criterion can name a shape that does not
     exist.** The issue was filed off a rigorous arbiter finding on #180 — a
     4000-model differential (2804 rejections with the carve-out vs 2805
     without), a removed-line re-run, a byte-identical `schema.txt` — and still
     prescribed a discriminator that discriminates nothing. The reasoning behind
     it is sound *for the interleave the spec describes*; it fails only against
     what `addAll` actually builds, which is where the over-approximation in
     Next 1 lives. **The provenance being strong is what made the shape worth
     testing rather than assuming.**
  2. **The estimate was ~12 lines and the diff is 21 — all of the overage is the
     doc comment.** The test body is essentially the predicted size. The comment
     states what the line pins and how reverting it breaks (P2), which is the
     entire reason this issue exists; a 12-line version would have re-created
     the defect class one layer up.
- Friction:
  1. **Nothing measurable.** One issue, one commit, one round, no grounding
     round, no warden pre-flight, no repair — exactly the shape the issue's
     Notes predicted ("fast, low-risk, single-session pickup — do not
     over-scope it"), and the first session in some time where the *scoping
     note in the issue body* is what kept the session small. Worth recording as
     a positive control: issues that name their own non-goals get landed at
     their stated size.
- Next:
  1. **[post-land pass, not yet run — TWO arbiter notes are unfiled and this
     entry is their only record until it does]** Both are pre-existing on
     `main`, unrelated to this diff, and were deliberately left for triage:
     (a) **a real over-approximation in `addAll`** — with the carve-out
     *present*, `sequence(all(a,b), a)` is rejected at "after a", but under true
     interleave semantics the all's `b` is still owed at that point, so the
     trailing `a` is not live there and the two `a`s arguably do not ·compete·.
     `addAll`'s doc comment claims the star transcription is "exactly
     equivalent" for deciding ·compete·; that holds for a **bare** `<all>`, but
     once an enclosing sequence appends a successor onto every member's last
     position the star has also dropped the "b is still owed" constraint,
     adding cross pairs *(member, successor)* the interleave does not have. The
     comment's exactness claim is scoped narrower than it reads; **false-reject
     direction**, so it is a correctness issue and not cosmetics.
     (b) **the shape this test pins is reachable from an ordinary document, and
     the parser may be under-charging `cos-all-limited` clause 1** —
     `parser/produce_complex.go:1039-1042` charges it only on a **literal**
     `<all>` nested in a `<choice>`/`<sequence>`; `produceGroupRefParticle`
     (`:1080`) does not, so a `<group ref>` to a named all-group inside a
     `<sequence>` yields exactly this component shape. Whether that usage site
     should charge clause 1 too is a separate pre-existing parser question.
     Note this is also what makes the pinned line **live rather than merely
     defensive** — `xsd.NewModelGroup`/`SchemaBuilder` reach the shape directly
     in any case. Plus the usual unblock scan and branch sweep.
  2. **[planning, still blocking]** #259's entry recorded **98 open `ready`
     issues** and re-affirmed that **band 1 is EMPTY**. #261 removes one more
     without supplying a successor; the two notes above would add two back. A
     full `/backlog` re-derivation remains the blocking planning action, and it
     still inherits **#347**'s question first — a ~98-deep `ready` label is not
     a queue.
  3. **[carried, undischarged — the count of sessions these survive is the
     point of listing them]** #400, #430, #347, #270, #426, #336, #413,
     #414/#438, #417, #458; #346's two unfiled T4/prose advisories; #365's
     20-file `negativeInteger`/`nonNegativeInteger`/`nonPositiveInteger`/`positiveInteger`
     harvest; **#324's A1/A2, whose post-land pass has still not run**; #257's
     Friction 1 rewording routed to fold into #458; and **#259's own two notes**
     (the unparsable-`schemaLocation` resolution question and the #272 Notes
     housekeeping), whose post-land pass has also not run. **#256 remains
     CLOSED-and-parked under `needs-replan` with its supersede-and-refile still
     owed**, and its branch `wip/issue-256` @ `c56d9f7` still survives on
     `origin` — **reported for human deletion, not acted on**.
- Thread record (do not duplicate) — <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/261>:
  the single session comment is the **ARBITER VERDICT** at
