  differential and the removed-line green run — is the entire provenance of this
  issue. This entry is WORKFLOW's "Land" step, written on the WIP branch after
  the verdict and **before** the PR is opened so it rides in the squash; the
  branch's commits squash into one carrying `Spec: cos-nonambig (§3.8.6.4),
  ·compete· (§3.8.4.2), <all> interleave semantics (§3.8.4.1.3)` and
  `Ratchet: unchanged`.

## 2026-08-03 — backlog — band 1 had been EMPTY for four consecutive landings with every post-land pass naming the re-derivation as the blocking action; this pass derives **band 2**, a 9-item dependency-ordered working queue, and writes down the ordering doctrine it used so the next pass can disagree with something specific; the `schema` lane reads 9166/15432 but the +4919 is mostly lane *widening* from narrowed decidability predicates rather than rule work — which is precisely why measurement-integrity items rank second in the new band; #470/#471/#472 filed, #411 settled by date-stamping, and #416's persona gap cost something concrete for the first time

- Doc-only reconciliation, **no commit of my own** — `docs/PLAN.md` and this LOG
  entry are the only file edits; **no Go code changed, no gate run, no
  `GOXSD_RATCHET=1`**. Branch `claude/dazzling-cerf-w8z6i5`, at `origin/main`
  tip `ecf3d79` (#261's UPA pin). The orchestrating session lands both files as
  the single `meta: backlog 2026-08-03` commit per WORKFLOW.md's "Other
  triggers". Third `/backlog` of the window, run after four landings
  (#377 / #257 / #256-park / #371) plus #259 and #261.
- **Ratchet: unchanged** (doc-only trigger). Figures below were read directly
  off `conformance/testdata/expectations/*.txt` at `ecf3d79` and independently
  re-verified for this entry — **no live conformance run was performed**, which
  is the arbiter's job and not this trigger's.

  | lane | pass / total | since the 2026-08-01 backlog |
  |---|---|---|
  | `schema` | 9166 / 15432 | 4247 → 9166, **+4919** |
  | `datatypes` | 1131 / 1153 | 1107/1129 → 1131/1153, **+24** |
  | `instance` | 0 / 26426 | unchanged |
  | `xpath` / `json` / `ber` | empty by design | unchanged |

  **The +4919 must not be read as eight landings' worth of rule work.** Most of
  it is the lane *widening* — `conformance/schema.go`'s decidability predicates
  narrowed, so cases that were previously declined at the seam now enter and
  pass. A number that large arriving mostly from the harness is the single
  strongest argument this pass had for how to order band 2.
- **Band 2 is this pass's main output, and it exists because band 1 drained.**
  Band 1 emptied at #377's landing and stayed empty through three more, with
  every intervening post-land pass flagging the full re-derivation as *the*
  blocking planning action. New dependency-ordered working queue, drawn from
  **99 open `ready` issues**:
  **#449 → #336 → #443 → #446 → #468 → #436 → #464 → #463 → #342 → #447.**
  - **#449** — claim 20 lexical fixtures; the cheapest lane movement available,
    same class as #365.
  - **#336** — extension-form decidability; the largest expected `schema`
    movement in the queue, and **both its deps closed 2026-08-02** (title
    corrected this pass, see below).
  - **#443** — pins the sibling decidability predicate's safety net; sequenced
    *alongside* #336 deliberately, not after it.
  - **#446** — `testGroup/@version` is never read, so XSD-1.0-scoped cases are
    unwinnable by any implementation change. **Measurement-integrity class, the
    same class as #277/#276**, and promoted on the strength of the widening
    above.
  - **#468** — a live false reject: `addAll`'s `last`-set claim breaks under an
    enclosing sequence. Straight out of #261's landing.
  - **#436** — `final=`/`block=` never read; **9 dead reader sites across 6
    rules, the largest single concentration of inert code in the tree**.
  - **#464 → #463** as a pair — fold #371's advisories first, then the third
    `checkSimpleDefault` caller.
  - **#342** — `substitutionGroup` type inheritance, unblocked by #281, with
    #395 and the newly-filed #471 riding behind it.
  - **#447** — list/union `<simpleType>` producer gap, cost measured against
    real fixtures.
  **Ordering doctrine, stated explicitly so the next pass can agree or
  disagree with a written rule rather than re-invent one**: measured lane
  movement first, then measurement integrity, then false rejects, then
  under-rejects, then producer completeness.
- **Issues: three filed, two edited, six commented, none closed** (the
  cartographer never closes issues as done).
  - **#470** (`ready`, `kind/bug`, `area/xsd`) — the replan of #256, which was
    closed under `needs-replan`. Scoped to fix **both** `AddIdentityConstraint`
    and `Schema.IdentityConstraints` — the pair — rather than the writer alone,
    which is exactly the half-fix that took #256 to two rejections.
  - **#471** (`ready`, `kind/gap`, `area/parser`) — the one unowned `GAP(` site
    a fresh full re-grep turned up: `parser/produce_complex.go:1235`, an
    `<element ref=… substitutionGroup=…>` silently accepted. **Under-reject,
    not a false accept.**
  - **#472** (`ready`, `kind/feature`, `area/cmd`) — `goxsd8 parse`, the first
    real subcommand. Gated on #252, which landed 2026-08-02. Folds in
    cliuser's **previously recorded** acceptance criteria (exit codes,
    greppable failure lines, the `-version` reservation) instead of a fresh
    persona pass.
  - **#16** — body rewritten to the mandatory template; the missing
    `## Depends on` section, sighted four times before without being fixed, is
    now there, and a stale sentence about `-help` corrected.
  - **#336** — stale `(blocked on #264/#265)` dropped from the title; both deps
    closed 2026-08-02.
  - Comments posted on **#256, #16, #336, #411, #416, #443**.
- **#256's park was verified as a park, not a lost merge.** Its branch
  `wip/issue-256` @ `c56d9f7` (2 commits, `3c0c918`/`c56d9f7`) was confirmed
  **not landed on `main`**, so the retirement-in-place was correct and #470 is a
  genuine replan rather than a re-file of shipped work. The branch is **flagged
  for human deletion and not acted on** — sessions never delete refs.
- **GAP ledger re-derived from scratch, not inherited**: `grep -rn "GAP("
  --include=*.go .` → **43 hits across 21 files**. One genuinely unowned site
  (filed as #471). Three stale ownership entries in the old PLAN snapshot
  corrected: `xsd/complexderivation.go:409` belongs to **#430**, not #265;
  `xsd/contentrestricts.go:514` has drifted to `:522` and is **#345's**;
  `parser/doc.go:111` is **#434's**. Two sites **reclassified from debt to
  permanent contract** — `xsd/contentrestricts.go:359`, and
  `value/valuespace.go:87-107` gates 1-3, where only the `:114` residue is real
  debt and is owned by #462. Everything else reconciles cleanly to an open
  issue. **Recorded caveat: the grep is a lower bound** — #283 owns two
  fail-open sites carrying no `GAP(` token at all.
- **#411 settled: lane counts in PLAN are date-stamped, not stripped.** Of the
  two options on the thread, (b) won. Applied to the file preamble as a standing
  convention, plus the two milestone headings that carried live counts (M3, M5).
  Left **open** for the landing session to close — the cartographer files and
  reconciles, it does not close as done.
- Decisions made and why:
  1. **Band 2 ranks measurement integrity second, above two live false
     rejects.** The +4919 is the argument: when the largest lane movement in
     the window comes from the harness deciding to look at more cases rather
     than from the processor getting more right, the harness's own correctness
     is the thing most able to silently bank a wrong-reason pass into the
     never-regress wall. #446 is promoted on that reasoning alone, and it is
     the same reasoning the 2026-08-01 pass used to promote #277/#276 after
     #238 measured 69 wrong-reason passes.
  2. **#470 re-scoped to the pair rather than the writer.** #256 died on
     exactly this: a doc-only fix that was *correct* on round 2 and still
     rejected, because fixing one of two consistently-wrong statements turns a
     consistent error into a self-contradiction. The replan's scope is the
     lesson, not the issue.
  3. **#347 was deliberately NOT decided by this pass.** Its own filing note
     says a cartographer must route changes to its own agent file through the
     queue rather than self-apply mid-sweep, and CLAUDE.md reserves
     `.claude/agents/` edits accordingly. One new datum recorded for whoever
     does take it: **~20 of the 99 `ready` issues are comment/doc-accuracy
     items — the largest coherent cohort in the label, and explicitly not a
     merge candidate.**
  4. **#472 uses recorded persona output rather than faking a persona pass.**
     A persona verdict produced by a session that has read the source is worth
     less than none; folding in criteria cliuser actually wrote is honest, and
     the entry says plainly that it **does not discharge #416**.
- Surprises:
  1. **The biggest lane number this repo has ever recorded is mostly not about
     the processor.** +4919 on `schema`, and the honest reading is "the seam
     moved". The 2026-08-01 entry recorded the mirror image — a net −37 across
     the busiest day of landings. Both readings only exist because the lane is
     reconstructed from the expectations files rather than from a feeling about
     how much work happened.
  2. **The GAP ledger did not shrink to zero and never could.** The 2026-08-01
     pass closed it at 28/28 owned; a fresh grep now finds 43 across 21 files
     with one unowned. Growth is STYLE 9 working, but the two
     **reclassifications** are new: some fail-open sites are permanent
     contracts and were being carried as debt, which inflated the ledger and
     the sense of owed work with it.
  3. **A `ready` label of 99 contains a ~20-issue cohort that nobody would put
     in a working queue.** That is a sharper form of #347's question than any
     count: the problem is not the depth, it is that one label is holding two
     populations.
- Friction:
  1. **`/backlog`'s persona half is still structurally unrunnable — fifth
     consecutive sighting, and the first one with a measurable cost.** A
     cartographer subagent has no delegation tool and cannot spawn libuser or
     cliuser, so neither ran (#416). Previous flaggings were hygiene; this one
     is not: **real CLI/API surface shipped since the last genuine persona pass
     on 2026-08-01** — #251's `-help` fix and #252's eight-accessor set — and
     no fresh persona has seen it. #472 is a stopgap built from stale persona
     output.
  2. **Sixth consecutive backlog over the 8–10 target** (99 open `ready`), and
     the sequence of overruns is now longer than any single fix could plausibly
     be blamed for. All three shrink mechanisms were exhausted again.
  3. **Three of this pass's corrections were pure drift** — one line-number
     drift in the GAP ledger, one stale title, one missing template section
     sighted four times before being fixed. The 2026-08-01 entry flagged the
     line-drift class already; a template section going unfixed across four
     sightings is the new part.
- Next:
  1. **Develop loop takes band 2 in order**: **#449 → #336 → #443 → #446 →
     #468 → #436 → #464 → #463 → #342 → #447**. #336 and #443 are sequenced
     together on purpose; #464 must precede #463.
  2. **#411 is settled but still open** — the landing session closes it.
  3. **#416 needs a broader-toolset session**, not another sighting. The
     specific question it now owes an answer to: do #251's usage contract and
     #252's eight accessors read as coherent to a persona that sees only
     godoc/README?
  4. **#347 still wants a human or a retro decision.** The ~20-issue
     doc-accuracy cohort is new input for it.
  5. **Still unowned and repeated so the leak stays visible to the follow-up
     audit**: #400, #430, #270, #426, #413, #414/#438, #417, #458; #346's two
     T4/prose advisories; #365's 20-file
     `negativeInteger`/`nonNegativeInteger`/`nonPositiveInteger`/`positiveInteger`
     harvest; **#324's A1/A2, whose post-land pass has still not run**; #257's
     Friction 1 rewording routed into #458; and **#259's two notes**, whose
     post-land pass has also not run. #261's two notes are discharged in part
     by #468. **`wip/issue-256` @ `c56d9f7` awaits human deletion**, alongside
     the previously reported stale refs.
- Thread record (do not duplicate): filings at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/470>,
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/471>,
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/472>; body/title reconciliations on

  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/336>; and sweep comments on #256
  (the not-landed verification and the #470 hand-off), #16, #336, #411 (the

## 2026-08-03 — #270 — conformance,schemaloc: the five leaked advisories are discharged three-fixed / two-dismissed, and the only one with a code artifact behind it turns out to be a real gap — `buildOwnFacets` merged EVERY same-kind facet child, so a repeated non-repeatable facet was folded into one facet and slipped past `st-props-correct` clause 4 instead of tripping it; the oracle corrected the ISSUE'S OWN text (§F.1 routes `xs:list/@itemType` through the single-QName template, not the list-aware one, and there is a SIXTH rewritten construct the "five" omitted), and item 1's wrong doc claim had already propagated into landed code via #259 — which makes the leak's predicted risk retroactively concrete rather than hypothetical

- Shipped on `wip/issue-270` as one mason commit — `cdc1d29`
  (`conformance,schemaloc: correct three doc claims and guard buildOwnFacets
  against a repeated non-repeatable facet (#270)`) — on top of `origin/main` @
  `a136838` (the `meta: backlog 2026-08-03` reconciliation), the tree the
  arbiter judged. **4 files, +78/−7**: `internal/schemaloc/schemaloc.go`
  (item 1's doc correction), `conformance/schema.go` (item 3's doc list),
  `conformance/datatypes.go` (item 5's new `mergesRepeatedChildren` helper at
  `:2307` plus the guard in `buildOwnFacets`) and `conformance/datatypes_test.go`
  (`TestBuildOwnFacetsRepeatedKinds`). **Arbiter ACCEPT on round 1, zero repair
  rounds, no rejections**, three non-blocking advisory notes, two of which were
  settled on the thread before landing.
- **Ratchet: unchanged.** The arbiter's figures, copied and not recomputed:
  `RATCHET: unchanged`, from a `GOXSD_RATCHET=1 go test ./conformance -run
  TestConformance -count=1` run (ok, 27.1s) after which **all six expectation
  files were byte-identical by md5 before and after** — confirmed by checksum
  rather than by the run's silence, and with `git status --porcelain` empty on
  both sides. Plain conformance `ok` 26.4s. `go build ./...`, `go test ./...`,
  `go vet ./...` all pass and **`golangci-lint run` → 0 issues**, run
  independently by the arbiter — the **fifth consecutive session** with a real
  gate part 2, after #261's fourth.
- **What the issue was.** #270 tracked five advisory follow-ups that had leaked
  into CLOSED-issue comments with no tracker, harvested by the 2026-07-26 retro.
  The issue itself named the root cause: **#179 was the only landed issue in
  that window with no cartographer post-land pass**, so WORKFLOW step 7(b)'s
  harvest simply never ran for it. This session discharges all five: **three
  fixed, two dismissed with the reason recorded on the thread.**
- Decisions made and why:
  1. **Item 1 — FIXED, and the leak had already cost something.**
     `schemaloc.Resolve`'s doc comment claimed `loader.Dir` was among the
     resolvers an absolute-path-shaped `schemaLocation` breaks. It is not:
     `filepath.Join` reads a leading `"/"` as no different from none, so `Dir`
     opens the same file and reports the same identity string. Only the
     **exact-key** resolvers (`loader.Map`'s bare map index, `loader.FS` via
     `fs.ValidPath`) actually break on that shape. The arbiter reproduced all
     three behaviors empirically rather than reading them. The point worth
     banking: **this wrong claim had already propagated into landed code** —
     #259 (`4895d04`) carried it into its own acceptance criterion and into the
     unified copy. A leaked advisory is not a bookkeeping defect; this one
     travelled.
  2. **Item 2 — DISMISSED: no code artifact existed to fix.** The
     under-specification (§F.1's chameleon-inclusion stylesheet rewrites
     constructs `unqualifiedRefNS` does not handle) lives **only** in the
     closed-issue comment, not in any repo file. `unqualifiedRefNS`
     (`parser/produce.go:1262-1290`) documents what it *does* and makes no
     claim, right or wrong, about what remains. Writing a new comment
     enumerating four constructs that cannot be produced yet would be
     speculative documentation of unwritten code. **What the session produced
     instead is a corrected grounding on the thread**, which supersedes item 2's
     own framing (see Surprises 1).
  3. **Item 3 — FIXED.** `conformance/schema.go`'s step-4 list of the plain
     non-`xsderr` errors `parser.Parse` can return read as exhaustive while
     omitting an `<include>`/`<override>` with no `schemaLocation` — a grammar
     fault no Schema Representation Constraint covers, which
     `closureScan.compose` declines outright. The arbiter checked the list's
     honesty against every other plain-error site in `parser` and against
     `schema_closure.go:249-252` before agreeing it is now a fair enumeration.
  4. **Item 4 — DISMISSED as already fixed, by an unrelated landing that never
     reported back.** `grep -rn SchemaSet` returns zero hits in any `.go` file;
     `parser/doc.go:56-61` now carries the *opposite* of the stale
     `*xsd.SchemaSet` prose and says so explicitly. The correction rode in the
     assembly-phase commit, **whose own LOG entry records it verbatim** — the
     fix happened, the advisory's loop just never closed. That is the same
     failure mode as the leak itself, one layer up.
  5. **Item 5 — FIXED, and it is a genuine gap, not a doc touch-up.**
     `buildOwnFacets` merged same-kind facet children unconditionally.
     Per §4.3 only **`pattern` (§4.3.4.2 case 2)** and **`enumeration`
     (§4.3.5.2 case 2)** map several children onto ONE facet; every other repeat
     is a SECOND facet of that kind, which `st-props-correct` §3.16.6.1 clause 4
     forbids ("There is not more than one member of {facets} of the same kind").
     Merging two `<minLength>` children therefore **hid the violation from
     `xsd.checkSTProps`, which iterates the merged slice** — deciding the case
     against a type the schema does not define. New unexported predicate
     `mergesRepeatedChildren`, guard placed after the `Applies` check and before
     the `seen` marking, one test.
- **The guard is proved failure-capable in BOTH directions, and its inertness is
  measured, not asserted.** The arbiter mutated it in a disposable worktree:
  `return true` (≡ pre-change behavior) fails the new test at
  `datatypes_test.go:561`; `return false` (decline every repeat) fails it at
  `:569`. On inertness — **all 28 repeated-non-repeatable-facet `.xsd` files in
  the corpus live outside the `msData/datatypes/Facets/` tree the harness's
  facet selectors are anchored to**, so zero cases change status today, and the
  arbiter confirmed this by re-running the full suite under the `return true`
  mutation against unchanged expectations. As a bonus it found the `return
  false` mutation regresses **98 datatypes cases**, which proves the
  pattern/enumeration carve-out is load-bearing on the live suite and not only
  in the unit test. **This is a guard against a currently-untested shape, not a
  fix for a currently-failing one** — and the commit message says so.
- Surprises:
  1. **The oracle's job turned out to be correcting the ISSUE, not the code.**
     Item 2 asserted that `xs:list/@itemType` and `memberTypes` both need a
     list-aware `resolveQName` wrapper. §F.1's normative stylesheet splits
     QName rewriting into **two** template rules, and the grammar settles which
     is which: `itemType = QName` (single, line 3616) is routed through the
     **non**-tokenizing template alongside `ref`/`base`/`type`/
     `defaultAttributes`/`refer`; only `memberTypes` (3621) and
     `substitutionGroup` (1116) are `List of QName`. The oracle also flagged a
     **sixth** §F.1-rewritten construct the issue's "five" list omits —
     `@notQName` on `xs:any`/`xs:anyAttribute` (2835/2843). An advisory that
     sits unread for weeks does not merely go stale; **it can have been wrong
     the whole time**, and nothing was reading it closely enough to notice.
  2. **Two of five items needed no code at all, and a third was already done.**
     Sixty percent of a tracked-advisory issue evaporated on contact. That is
     not wasted work — the dismissals are the deliverable, and each is now
     written down with its evidence — but it is a useful prior for sizing the
     next harvest issue of this shape.
  3. **The resumed-vs-started check found an expired branch whose issue was
     already dead.** `wip/issue-256` was EXPIRED and therefore resumable by the
     lease rule, but #256 itself is **closed, superseded by #470** after hitting
     the two-rejection park cap — so the branch is retired evidence, not work.
     The lease rule ordered it correctly; the *issue state* is what made it
     unresumable. Worth noting that the two signals can disagree and the issue
     wins. (This also discharges #261's carried note that #256's
     supersede-and-refile was still owed: #470 exists.)
- Friction:
  1. **Nothing measurable inside the session.** One issue, one commit, one
     round, no repair. The friction this issue documents is entirely
     *historical* — a missing post-land pass on #179 — and the fix for that
     already exists in WORKFLOW step 7(b); what was missing was it being run.
- Next:
  1. **[post-land pass, not yet run — ONE arbiter advisory is unfiled and this
     entry is its only record until it does]** The comment on
     `conformance/datatypes.go:2307` says "Only pattern and enumeration do",
     which is true of this lane but narrower than §4.3: **`assertions`
     (§4.3.9.2 / §4.3.13.2) is a third "one or more" facet**. It is structurally
     unreachable here — `facetKinds` (`:2273`) excludes it and a `<assertion>`
     child fails `facetKindOf` as unrecognized before the guard is reached — so
     **the code is right and only the sentence is wide**. Doc-only, cosmetic
     direction. Plus the usual unblock scan and branch sweep.
  2. **[surfaced this session, NOT actioned — out of scope, and it has a
     tracker]** `buildOwnFacets` concatenates `<pattern>` siblings as separate
     values rather than same-step-OR per §4.3.4.2 case 2. This is the
     **already-filed #214 hazard**, and the finding here is that it is present
     in the **conformance harness** too, not only in the parser. #214 should
     pick up that second site when it is worked.
  3. **[discharged this session, recorded so it is not re-derived]** Item 2's
     operative cross-reference ask — "`unqualifiedRefNS` must be revisited by
     each of #182, #183, #229" — is **moot, not deferred**: all three are closed
     and landed, and each was checked against the six §F.1-rewritable constructs
     and introduces **no new QName-or-QName-list-valued attribute** needing
     chameleon coercion. Item 1's cross-issue half is likewise discharged by a
     note on **#259**, whose landed acceptance criterion carried the same wrong
     "directory-rooted" claim into its own body. The standing note for future
     implementers: whoever lands `itemType`, `memberTypes`, `defaultAttributes`,
     `refer` or `notQName` routes through `p.resolveQName` (single) or
     `strings.Fields` + `p.resolveQName` (list), reusing
     `substitutionGroupAffiliations`'s shape rather than minting a second
     wrapper. **No new issue needed.**
  4. **[carried, undischarged — the count of sessions these survive is the
     point of listing them]** #400, #430, #347, #426, #336, #413, #414/#438,
     #417, #458; #346's two unfiled T4/prose advisories; #365's 20-file
     `negativeInteger`/`nonNegativeInteger`/`nonPositiveInteger`/`positiveInteger`
     harvest; **#324's A1/A2, whose post-land pass has still not run**; #257's
     Friction 1 rewording routed to fold into #458; #259's two notes (the
     unparsable-`schemaLocation` resolution question and the #272 Notes
     housekeeping); and **#261's two notes** (the `addAll` over-approximation
     and the `cos-all-limited` clause 1 under-charge on `<group ref>`), whose
     post-land pass has also not run. **#270 leaves this list**, and the branch
     `wip/issue-256` @ `c56d9f7` still survives on `origin` — **reported for
     human deletion, not acted on**.

  `2f9c0c4`) narrowing item 2 by striking `substitutionGroup`. The arbiter's

  `.agent/grounding-issue-270.md` "on `wip/issue-270`" while `.agent/` is
  gitignored — is **expected, not a defect**: the comment body is the durable

  **No warden ran**; the diff is three doc corrections plus one unexported
  predicate with an empty exported surface, and the arbiter did not charge its

## 2026-08-03 — #470 — xsd: #256's replan lands the PAIR — `AddIdentityConstraint` and `Schema.IdentityConstraints` stop saying "top-level" in the same commit, because §3.17.2's tableau sources `{identity-constraint definitions}` from items "anywhere within the [[children]]"; the replan the park predicted would be "one sentence wide" turned out to be FOUR sites once a safety grep ran, and the defect class that parked #256 — a same-file doc self-contradiction no gate leg can see — was caught this time by running the WARDEN on the exported-doc diff before the arbiter ever saw it

- Shipped on `wip/issue-470` as two mason commits on top of `origin/main` @
  `b68301e` (#270's landing), the tree the arbiter judged: `73914da`
  (`xsd: correct the schema-wide {identity-constraint definitions} scope in both
  its doc comments (#470)`) and `f1c3930` (`xsd: apply warden's exported-doc
  fixes to AddIdentityConstraint (#470)`). **3 files, +97/−14** over the base:
  `xsd/schema.go` (both doc comments plus the `namedComponent` restatement),
  `xsd/resolve_test.go` (a test comment), `parser/produce_xpath_test.go`
  (`TestProduceKeyrefOnLocalElementResolvesAcrossDeclarations`, +53).
  **Arbiter ACCEPT on round 1, zero rejections**, one non-blocking pre-land item
  (this log entry). **The two-rejection park is not inherited** — #470 is a fresh
  issue and its own round count started at zero.
- **Ratchet: unchanged.** The arbiter's figures, copied and not recomputed:
  `RATCHET: unchanged`, from `GOXSD_RATCHET=1 go test ./conformance -run
  TestConformance -count=1` → `ok 33.116s`, **wrote nothing**;
  `conformance/testdata/expectations` tree hash
  `6e3958422f317a8cf6216b05a0fffcfb0dcf225d` before the run and
  `git status --porcelain` empty after it. Plain conformance `ok 34.533s`.
  `go build ./...`, `go test ./...`, `go vet ./...` pass and **`golangci-lint
  run` → `0 issues.`**, run independently by the arbiter — the **sixth
  consecutive session** with a real gate part 2, after #270's fifth. Correct and
  expected: the arbiter confirmed **mechanically** that the diff's non-`//`
  add/remove set outside the new test is EMPTY, so no behaviour exists that could
  move a lane.
- **What the issue was.** #256 hit the two-rejection cap and was retired in place;
  #470 is its replan. Round 1 there rejected a §3.17.1 miscitation for the
  sourcing rule; round 2 rejected because fixing the writer's doc alone left
  `Schema.IdentityConstraints` — the reader of the same slice — still saying
  "top-level", turning a consistently-wrong pair into a self-contradiction 440
  lines apart on the exported surface. **The unit of work is the PAIR**, and
  #470 was scoped as such from the issue body forward.
- Decisions made and why:
  1. **Re-grounded from scratch rather than carried forward.** #256's grounding
     was available and had been arbiter-certified on round 2, but the oracle
     re-derived every citation independently against
     `docs/specs/md/xmlschema11-1.md` anyway — **because that thread's own first
     rejection WAS a citation error**, and a grounding whose first version was
     wrong is exactly the artifact not to take on trust. The re-derivation
     confirmed all of it and pinned line numbers: §3.17.2's tableau at `:3791`
     carries BOTH contrasted quotes on one line (`{identity-constraint
     definitions}` "anywhere within the [[children]]" vs `{element declarations}`
     "(top-level)"), §3.17.1 at `:3762` declares the property and nothing more,
     §3.11.2 at `:3021` is the `ref=` reuse form, `src-resolve` cl. 1.7 at
     `:3900`, `sch-props-correct` cl. 2 at `:3896`.
  2. **The `ref=` carve-out is a separate paragraph, with its consequence stated
     in both directions.** Under-register and a nested `<keyref>`'s `refer=`
     target becomes unfindable — `src-resolve` cl. 1.7 false-rejects a valid
     schema; over-register the `ref=` form and a second entry under the same name
     fabricates a `sch-props-correct` (§3.17.6.1) cl. 2 duplicate-expanded-name
     violation against the very definition it reuses. Both failure modes now sit
     in the doc that governs the call.
  3. **`Schema.IdentityConstraints`'s existing guarantees were preserved
     verbatim, not rewritten around.** Its SCHEMA-level vs
     `[ElementDeclaration.IdentityConstraints]` §3.3.1 scope contrast, the
     document-order contract (stable even though §3.17.1 words the property as an
     unordered "set"), the copy semantics — all survive; the only change is the
     scope sentence plus a cross-link to the adder for the `ref=` carve-out. This
     was the #256 verdict's explicit instruction and it held.
  4. **The new test covers the ONE shape nothing else did.** A `<keyref>` on a
     local element declaration resolving to a `<key>`/`<unique>` declared under a
     **different** local declaration — neither endpoint top-level — finalizing
     CLEANLY. The three named neighbours all miss it: `InDocumentOrder` pairs
     key+keyref on the same **top-level** element,
     `TestProduceIdentityConstraintOnLocalElement` observes registration only
     through a **reject**, and the `ref=`-form test covers `ref=` not `refer=`.
     Its second axis (`key` vs `unique`) is grounded, not decorative: §3.11.1
     `:2956` says a keyref's `{referenced key}` may be either.
- **The test is mutation-proved by the ARBITER, not on mason's report.** It
  gated the sole registration site (`produce_xpath.go:147`) on the host
  `<element>` being a direct child of `<schema>` and both subtests failed with
  **precisely the failure the new doc comment predicts** — `[src-resolve] keyref
  {urn:t}kr references identity constraint {urn:t}k, but no identity-constraint
  definition with that expanded name is present in the schema (src-resolve clause
  1.7)`. The prose is pinned by an executable fact rather than asserted.
- **The warden ran on the exported-doc diff and returned `revise` with two
  findings, both fixed BEFORE the arbiter saw the tree** (`f1c3930`, 10 lines
  swapped):
  - [T5] "Alone among the adders **above**" is false to a `go doc` reader —
    godoc sorts methods alphabetically, so `AddModelGroup`/`AddNotation`/`AddType`
    render *below*. Replaced with "Alone among `SchemaBuilder`'s adders".
  - [T5] A sentence naming `produceIdentityConstraint` leaked an unexported
    symbol from a non-imported package into exported doc prose. Dropped; the
    rationale stays where the symbol lives.
  The warden also certified the load-bearing half: a full `go doc -all ./xsd`
  walk found no other site still asserting "top-level identity-constraint
  definitions".
- **The warden's repair introduced a UNIVERSAL claim and the arbiter checked it
  exhaustively rather than waving it through.** "Alone among `SchemaBuilder`'s
  adders it is NOT a top-level-only set" quantifies over all eight; the arbiter
  enumerated `^func (b *SchemaBuilder) Add` and checked each against the §3.17.2
  tableau — `{attribute declarations}`/`{element declarations}` carry the
  explicit "(top-level)" parenthetical; `{type definitions}`, `{attribute group
  definitions}`, `{model group definitions}`, `{notation declarations}` are
  scoped "**in** the [[children]]", top-level by construction; `{annotations}` is
  `<schema>`'s own annotation plus its directive children, also top-level;
  `{identity-constraint definitions}` is the sole "**anywhere within**". The
  claim holds. It also recorded a **non-finding so a future reviewer does not
  re-flag it**: `Annotations()` carries its own "Alone among the eight §3.17.1
  properties" claim, on a different axis (sequence-vs-set, not scope) and
  independently true — the two coexist.
- Surprises:
  1. **The replan the park called "one sentence wide" was four sites.** The
     #256 verdict's forecast was exactly right about the blocking contradiction
     and exactly wrong about the size: a mandated safety grep for surviving
     "top-level" restatements found two MORE — `namedComponent`'s doc in
     `xsd/schema.go` and a test comment in `xsd/resolve_test.go`. Both were
     wrong in the same way, neither was in scope as filed, and both would have
     survived a repair scoped to the flagged lines. **The lesson #256's Friction
     recorded — "a repair round scoped to the flagged lines cannot discover a
     finding outside the flagged lines" — was actioned here by grepping the
     CLAIM, not the site.**
  2. **The defect class that parked #256 was caught this time by a persona that
     never ran on #256 at all.** Both of #470's findings are prose-accuracy
     findings on an exported doc comment; the full gate was green through every
     one of them, in both rounds of #256 and both commits here. Doc-contract
     defects are produced by an agent READING the file or not at all — and the
     warden is that agent for the exported surface. #256 spent two arbiter rounds
     doing what one warden pass did here for free.
  3. **The producer's own comment had been right the whole time.**
     `parser/produce_xpath.go:113-133` already stated the correct contract
     including the `ref=` carve-out; the exported docs had been the unhoisted,
     wrong copy. The fix is a hoist, not a discovery.
- Friction:
  1. **Nothing measurable inside the session** — one issue, two commits, one
     arbiter round, no repair round. The cost this issue represents was paid
     ALREADY, on #256: two rejections and a park for a defect one warden pass
     resolves. That is the friction, and it is now historical.
  2. The arbiter's only note was that `docs/LOG/2026-08.md` had no entry for the
     branch at judgment time. Not a defect in the change — WORKFLOW step 6 comes
     after judgment — but it is the second consecutive session where the log
     entry is the last thing standing between an accept and a land, so it is
     worth the chronicler treating the arbiter's ACCEPT as the trigger.
- Next:
  1. **[post-land pass, not yet run]** #256 must be confirmed closed as
     superseded (it was already so recorded during #270's session), the

  2. **[warden nits, non-blocking, no tracker — this entry is their only

  3. **[carried, undischarged — the count of sessions these survive is the

- Thread record (do not duplicate) — <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/470>:

## 2026-08-04 — #271 — parked (needs-replan) and closed: the warden's PRE-FLIGHT verdict — run on a planned surface before mason wrote a line — ruled the issue's own scope incoherent (it defers `SimpleType.base` to finalize while `Atomic.primitive`/`List.item`/`Union.members` stay live pointers), corrected two of mason's design premises and one of the ISSUE'S OWN acceptance bullets, and replanned it into #478/#479/#480; the whole loop cost zero code, zero commits and zero diff

- Attempted: `wip/issue-271`, claimed off `origin/main` @ `c1c3824` — **no
  commits, tip identical to `origin/main`, nothing to land and nothing to
  recover.** The only artifact the branch ever held was
  `.agent/grounding-issue-271.md`, which is gitignored and was never committed;
  the durable copy is the GROUNDING comment on the thread, which is what
  CLAUDE.md's cross-session-channel rule asks for. Issue closed
  (`state_reason: completed`) with the `needs-replan` label retained; labels now
  `needs-replan, kind/refactor, area/parser, area/xsd`. This entry lands
  separately on `main`, since a park leaves the WIP branch untouched.
- Ratchet: **unchanged on `main`, and never measured** — the arbiter did not run
  at all this session, because nothing reached judgment. #271's own acceptance
  demanded `Ratchet: unchanged` (it is a pure placement/representation change, so
  any lane movement would have meant the refactor was wrong), and that demand
  survives verbatim into #478 item 11 and #479 item 7. Ratchet integrity trivially
  clean: `conformance/` was never opened, let alone edited.
- What was attempted: #271 is the 2026-07-26 steward audit's #1 cost-of-delay
  item — every cross-component reference resolution and named-circularity
  rejection should live in one place, `xsd`'s finalize phase, instead of split
  between `parser/produce.go` (eager) and `xsd/resolve.go` (finalize). Its
  acceptance scoped two of the six reference kinds: `SimpleType.base` (a live
  `*SimpleType` pointer) and `<attributeGroup ref>` (eagerly inlined at mapping
  time). Because its Surface section was non-none — `NewSimpleType`'s exported
  signature changes and 149 call sites move with it — WORKFLOW step 4 required a
  **warden pre-flight before any code**. It returned `revise — back for replan`.
- Decisions:
  - **The oracle corrected the ISSUE'S OWN acceptance criteria, before mason
    read them.** #271's third acceptance bullet asks for the `st-props-correct`
    cl. 2 circularity rejection to move to finalize "for the simple-type-base
    **and attribute-group-ref** cases". The attributeGroup half is wrong on the
    facts: §3.6.2.1 says *"Circular reference is not disallowed... An
    `<attributeGroup>` element involved in such a reference cycle maps to a
    component whose {attribute uses} and {attribute wildcard} properties reflect
    all the `<attribute>` and `<any>` elements contained in, or referred to
    (directly or indirectly) by elements in the cycle"* — i.e. **transitive
    closure, not rejection** — and `ag-props-correct` §3.6.6 has exactly two
    clauses and no circularity clause at all. There is no rule ID to charge, so
    per PRINCIPLES 9 there is nothing to detect. The bullet describes relocating
    a rejection that must instead not exist.
  - **Mason's design pre-flight then found the bullet was a no-op in the other
    direction too**: there is no eager attributeGroup-ref circularity rejection
    in the code today to delete. `parser/produce_complex.go`'s
    `collectReferencedGroup` bounds its walk with a `visited` set, never errors,
    and its doc comment already cites §3.6.2.1 correctly. So the acceptance item
    was doubly void — wrong about the spec AND wrong about the repo.
  - **The oracle also settled that "phase" is not a spec question.** §4.1:
    *"Processors have the option to assemble ... the entire schema prior to the
    start of an ·assessment· episode, or to gather the schema lazily"*.
    Consolidating resolution into finalize is goxsd8's own architecture
    (PRINCIPLES 9), legitimate either way, and must not be cited as spec-mandated
    — a citation-overreach guard now written into #478's Spec section.
  - **The warden's blocking finding is that the issue defers HALF a
    representation.** §3.16.2.1 derives `{variety}`, `{primitive type
    definition}`, `{item type definition}` and `{member type definitions}` all
    from the base chain. Deferring `base` alone while the three variety pointers
    stay live is an inconsistent half-measure — and it is the direct cause of
    mason's own open question 2 ("once the base isn't live at mapping time, does
    `{variety}` move to a finalize-time fold, or stay producer-computed from the
    source element?"). The redesign: store the **declared derivation** as a new
    sealed sum (`RestrictionDerivation`/`ListDerivation`/`UnionDerivation`,
    mirroring `TermOrRef`) and make `Variety`/`Primitive`/`Item`/`Members`
    **derived** methods walking to the nearest ancestor that mints the property.
    That deletes the open question outright: no finalize mutation path, no
    producer re-derivation from XML (STYLE D3), and `NewPrimitiveType`'s
    self-referential `{primitive type definition}` wiring disappears because a
    primitive IS the minting ancestor. Deferring `base` alone would have migrated
    `xsd/derivation.go`'s 26 `.base` reads **twice**.
  - **Two of the warden's ten blocking findings are false-ACCEPT risks, which is
    why catching them at pre-flight rather than at judgment mattered.**
    (a) `value.CheckFacetRestriction`/`builtin.CheckSimpleTypeRestriction` are
    called from the PRODUCER, where no resolver exists and forward references are
    not yet indexed — threading a `TypeResolver` in there, as the pre-flight
    proposed, silently truncates every forward reference into a missing facet.
    The existing seam is `xsd.ValueSpace`, injected at `FinalizeWith`, which
    `parser/produce.go` already calls. (b) Anonymous inline simple types are
    never registered in `Schema.types` — `InlineTypeDefinition`'s own doc
    explains at length why registering them would be wrong — so a finalize pass
    ranging `s.types` alone lets every one of them escape `st-props-correct`
    cl. 3 and `cos-st-restricts` entirely. The walk must descend declaration
    slots the way `resolveComplexType` already does. Neither defect is visible to
    any gate leg: a false accept is green.
  - **The attributeGroup half was redesigned around ORDER, not around adding a
    field.** The pre-flight proposed a second parallel `attributeGroupRefNames
    []QName` slice; two parallel slices lose the document-order interleaving of
    `<attribute>` and `<attributeGroup ref>` children, which changes which
    duplicate `ag-props-correct` cl. 2 reports first (the error text names the
    first duplicate **by index**). The replan uses ONE ordered
    `AttributeUseOrGroupRef` sum **replacing the element type of the existing
    `attributeUses` parameter** — explicitly not a 14th positional parameter on
    `NewComplexType`. That is this log's own tripwire firing: `docs/LOG/2026-08.md`
    records the warden clearing the 13th positional parameter as *"the last
    positional slot to wave through"*, with the next one to trigger a params
    struct.
  - **The `xs:anyAtomicType` question was ruled OUT of the refactor entirely.**
    Mason flagged it as a blocker (base refs resolving through the index would
    make `xs:anyAtomicType` a real schema member, and the current rejection would
    silently disappear). The warden showed the premise false — it already
    resolves today and is already not rejected today, and neither refactor
    changes that — so it is a **pre-existing conformance gap** on its own
    timeline, filed as #480 with the third prose statement mason had not found
    (`xmlschema11-1.md:3595`, the one that speaks in `{base type definition}`
    terms and covers `xs:anySimpleType` too).
  - **Closed rather than left open**, matching the #256 → #470 precedent this
    repo established last session: `state_reason: completed`, `needs-replan`
    retained, a closing comment that names the successors, states explicitly that
    **#271's body is not the spec of record for any of them**, and enumerates
    what survives the replan (the oracle grounding and mason's call-site survey)
    versus what must not be re-inherited (the two false premises).
- Surprises:
  1. **The pre-flight warden is now measurably cheaper than the arbiter, on a
     second consecutive session.** #470 recorded that one warden pass resolved
     the doc-contract defect class that had cost #256 two arbiter rounds and a
     park. Here the same persona killed an entire mis-scoped issue for the price
     of reading a design sketch — before 149 mechanical call-site edits existed
     to unwind. The issue's own Surface section is what triggered it; the
     `WARDEN PRE-FLIGHT REQUIRED` marker earned its keep.
  2. **The design pre-flight is a real artifact with real defects.** Mason's
     pre-flight was substantively good — the `TypeDefinitionOrRef`-reuse argument
     (a bare QName cannot represent `src-simple-type` cl. 2's inline anonymous
     base) and the call-site survey both carried into #478 verbatim — and it also
     asserted two things that are simply false: that `builtin.Seed`'s contract
     changes from `len(Types)+1` to `+2`, and that `xs:anyAtomicType` is "not
     currently in `{type definitions}`, only a construction anchor". Both were
     refuted by one grep (`builtin/gen_typespec.go:777`). A design document is
     not exempt from CLAUDE.md's "grep, don't remember".
  3. **A wrong acceptance bullet survived from 2026-07-26 through two steward
     audits.** #271 was filed by the steward, re-evidenced by the steward on
     2026-08-02, and ranked #1 by cost-of-delay — and its attributeGroup-ref
     circularity bullet was wrong the whole time, in two independent ways. The
     first agent to actually grep §3.6.2.1 was the oracle, 9 days later, at
     grounding time. Long-lived `ready` issues accumulate confidence without
     accumulating verification.
  4. **A refactor issue can be rejected for shape while its DIRECTION is
     upheld.** The warden's opening line calls the direction "right and
     overdue". Nothing here was a disagreement about whether to do the work.
- Friction:
  1. **The oracle and the warden each independently discovered that the issue
     body was wrong, about different things, in the same session.** That is two
     of three specialist passes spent correcting the plan rather than executing
     it. The cost is real but it is the cheap version — the expensive version is
     the one #256 paid, discovering it after the code exists.
  2. **The develop loop's step ordering makes the design pre-flight optional and
     the warden pre-flight conditional**, and this session needed both. It got
     them only because #271's Surface section was non-none. An issue with the same
     incoherence and an understated Surface section would have gone straight to
     mason, and the first reader of the design would have been the arbiter,
     looking at a 149-call-site diff.
  3. **`gh` CLI and `gh api` are both refused in this container** (GraphQL: *"not
     enabled for this session"*; REST: *"GitHub access is not enabled for this
     session"*). Only the GitHub MCP server works. docs/ROUTINES.md lists all
     three channels as interchangeable; they are not, per-session, and finding
     out costs two failed calls each time. Worth a ROUTINES note at the next
     retro rather than a fix here.
  4. **Zero measurable implementation friction** — there was no implementation.
- Next:
  1. **The replan set is filed and ready to pick up.** #478 (`ready`,
     `kind/refactor`, M4) — the simple-type half, eleven acceptance items
     transcribing the warden's findings, `WARDEN PRE-FLIGHT REQUIRED` again.
     #479 (`ready`, `kind/refactor`, M4) — the `<attributeGroup ref>` half, seven
     acceptance items, also pre-flight-required, and the smaller/better-contained
     of the two. #480 (`blocked`, `kind/gap`, M3) — the `xs:anyAtomicType`
     pre-existing gap, held for a Phase-1 oracle pass on the rule-ID question and
     **deliberately not `ready`**, because the develop loop consumes `ready` and
     this one is not implementable until that question is answered.
  2. **Schedule #478 against #447 deliberately, not by whichever is grabbed
     first.** #447 (`<list>`/`<union>` production) is `ready` now and is exactly
     the landing #271's cost-of-delay argument was racing: written against
     today's `xsd` it adds live item/member pointers plus, most likely, its own
     producer-side circular-union rejection — the two things #478 exists to
     delete. #478 first means #447 is written directly against
     `ListDerivation`/`UnionDerivation` and no eager guard is ever written.
     Recorded as a comment on #478; not a hard dependency in either direction, so
     it is not in `## Depends on`.
  3. **#478 is large and its own body says it may need splitting again** — the
     defensible line is (i) the representation change with `checkSTGraph` still
     constructor-time against a resolved base, then (ii) the finalize migration.
     Whether (i) is even expressible without (ii) is a question for grounding. If
     scope creeps once implementation starts, split THEN rather than pushing a
     multi-concern commit at the arbiter.
  4. **[branch namespace, reported and not acted on]** `wip/issue-271` survives
     on `origin` at the same tree as `origin/main`, retired in place and never to

## 2026-08-04 — #272 — parser,conformance: the harness's second document-discovery walk is DELETED rather than kept in sync — `parser.ParseReport` reports the assembled document set AND the typed `UnfollowedDirective`s #276's fabricated-accept guard actually needs; the warden's PRE-FLIGHT ruled the bare document-set report the issue asked for unsafe to build, because it would have silently deleted that guard while satisfying `Ratchet: unchanged` on paper, and it named two lost decline branches beyond the one `docs/PLAN.md` had flagged

- Shipped on `wip/issue-272` as two commits on top of `origin/main` @ `5f1d309`
  (#271's park entry): `bf3c6d5` (`parser,conformance: report the assembled
  document set instead of re-walking it (#272)`, mason) and `5a69c0c`
  (`conformance: bank the schema lane ratchet for the assembly report (#272)`,
  the arbiter's own ratchet write). **15 files, +988/−690**: new
  `parser/report.go` (154) and `parser/report_test.go` (320, nine tests);
  `parser/parse.go` (+129/−), `parser/tree.go` (`(*Element).Attr`),
  `parser/produce*.go` + `override.go` (call-site migration), `parser/doc.go`;
  `conformance/schema.go` (`execSchemaCase` rewired),
  `conformance/schema_closure.go` **374 → 107 lines**,
  `conformance/schema_closure_test.go`, `conformance/schema_test.go`, and the
  banked lane file. **Arbiter ACCEPT on round 1, zero rejections**, three
  non-blocking notes, one of which is this entry.
- **Ratchet: schema 9166 → 9170 (+4), 0 regressed; datatypes/instance lanes
  unchanged.** The arbiter's figures, copied and not recomputed — and *verified
  by the arbiter from a clean `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` run, not taken from mason's
  report*. Exactly one lane file rewritten, four lines, **all upward**:
  `MS-Annotations2006-07-15/annotB019`, `MS-Notations2006-07-15/notatF033`,
  `MS-Schema2006-07-15/schZ010`, `MS-Schema2006-07-15/schZ014_a`, each
  `fail → pass`. Gate: `go build ./... && go test ./... && go vet ./...` green,
  **`golangci-lint run` → 0 issues** run independently by the arbiter, plain
  conformance `ok` 38s. That is the **seventh consecutive LANDING** with a real
  gate part 2 (the streak is over landings, not sessions — #271 in between
  shipped no code and ran no gate).
- **The `Ratchet: unchanged` bullet in the issue body did NOT literally hold, and
  that was known before a line was written.** The warden's pre-flight closed with
  a note *for* the arbiter: post-hoc gating over the report is a **lane-semantics
  widening**, because `closureScan` deliberately over-discovered past conditions
  on which the assembly gives up, so the report is a strict SUBSET of what the
  walk reached — "can only move Fail→Pass, never regress". All four flips are
  `<import>` elements violating **`src-import-noselfimport` (§4.2.6.2 cl. 1)**:
  `annotB019`/`notatF033` carry no `namespace` attribute in a schema with no
  `targetNamespace` (cl. 1.2), `schZ010` imports its own `targetNamespace`,
  `schZ014_a` has `namespace=""` in a no-namespace schema (cl. 1.1).
  `importDocument` runs `checkNoSelfImport` **before** the `schemaLocation`
  branch, so **no `UnfollowedDirective` is recorded** and #276's
  `len(Unfollowed()) > 0 && perr != nil` conjunction stops declining them; each
  is then decided on a genuine, implemented rejection against a genuinely
  invalid document. **Not fabricated verdicts** — the arbiter checked all four
  case by case rather than accepting the count.
- **What the issue was.** `conformance/schema_closure.go`'s `closureScan`
  independently re-implemented `parser`'s internal assembly walk
  (`discover`/`compose`/`importDocument`/`fetch`) and duplicated `parser`'s
  unexported `attrValue` as `elementAttr`. Root cause was a seam gap, not
  laziness: `Parse` returned components and could never be asked which documents
  it read, while the schema lane's decidability gate needs exactly that set.
- Decisions made and why:
  1. **The warden ran BEFORE code, on a planned surface, and returned `revise`
     with three required modifications — all three followed.** (a) `Unfollowed()
     []UnfollowedDirective` with `UnfollowedReason` as a `uint8` closed set in
     the `xsd/closedsets.go` idiom, **not** an `Unresolved bool`: a bool records
     one of three facts and drops two, and storing a bool *alongside* the slice
     would be a STYLE D3 violation (derive it). (b) `ParseReport(location,
     opts...) (*xsd.Schema, *AssemblyReport, error)` with `Parse` as a thin
     wrapper, **not** an out-param `WithAssemblyReport(*AssemblyReport)` Option —
     `parse.go:16-19` states the invariant that options only ever install
     defaults, and every existing one does; an option that returns state breaks
     it. (c) `AssembledDocument.Namespace` dropped for want of a consumer (T5);
     `Location` documented as the **resolved** location, distinct from
     `Doc.URI()`'s requested one, and pinned by
     `TestParseReportLocationIsResolvedNotRequested`.
  2. **The correctness trap was named in advance and handled.** `fetch` returns
     a nil document for **two** outcomes — genuinely not-found *and*
     already-loaded (the dedup hit). Recording the dedup hit as unfollowed would
     have marked nearly every multi-document case and declined a swathe of
     currently-passing include cases — a regression `Ratchet: unchanged` would
     only have caught after the fact. The landed `fetch` records at three sites
     (`ErrNotFound` and generic resolver failure → `UnfollowedLocationUnresolved`;
     `ReadDocument` failure → `UnfollowedUnreadable`) and **records nothing** on
     the already-loaded branch, because that directive *was* followed to a
     document already in the report. Pinned by
     `TestParseReportDedupHitIsNotUnfollowed`, which the arbiter proved
     non-vacuous by moving the record into that branch and watching it fail.
  3. **Both error-returning reasons keep their errors.** Missing `schemaLocation`
     records `UnfollowedNoSchemaLocation` **and still returns** its grammar-fault
     error; unreadable D2 records `UnfollowedUnreadable` **and still returns**
     its `xsderr` verdict. That is what makes the conjunction decline exactly
     where `closureScan` declined outright, instead of converting those into
     fabricated "invalid" verdicts.
  4. **`elementAttr`/`attrValue` collapsed into `(*parser.Element).Attr` — a
     mason deviation beyond what the pre-flight covered, disclosed as such and
     upheld.** The arbiter's reasoning: no new capability and no new dependency
     edge (`Element` and `Attributes()` are already exported, `conformance`
     already imports `parser`), presence and value returned separately because
     the distinction is load-bearing (`<import>` with no `schemaLocation` ≠
     `schemaLocation=""`; `namespace=""` is a legal `xs:anyURI`), and it puts the
     unqualified-attribute rule — subtle enough that the harness had already
     re-derived it once, which is half of what this issue exists to kill — in one
     place with one doc comment. 68 in-package call sites plus `conformance`.
  5. **`execSchemaCase` keeps its own root-read/`IsSchema` precondition intact**,
     exactly as the pre-flight required, and now calls `ParseReport` **once**,
     consuming the report post-hoc.
  6. **Determinism proved, not assumed.** `assembly.report()` builds from the
     `a.docs` slice in append order and `a.unfollowed` in encounter order; the
     `a.loaded` map is never ranged (STYLE 3).
     `TestParseReportDocumentsAreDiscoveryOrder` pins an interleaved
     import/include/import shape
     (`main, b1, lib, b3, b2`) that neither a map rebuild nor a kind-segregated
     order could produce.
- Surprises:
  1. **The oracle corrected the ISSUE'S OWN spec citation, for the second
     consecutive landing.** The body cites §3.17.3 as "the shape both walks
     traverse"; §3.17.3 (`coxr.schemas`) is literally **empty** in the local spec
     — "None as such." The real assembly rules are **§4.2.1
     `composition-concepts`, §4.2.3 `src-include`, §4.2.4 `src-redefine`, §4.2.5
     `src-override`, §4.2.6 `src-import`**. #270's session had the oracle
     correcting the issue's own text too. Issue bodies are not grounded until an
     oracle greps them.
  2. **Half the issue had already been landed by #259 — and the thread SAID so,
     nine hours earlier.** #259's post-land pass posted a housekeeping comment on
     2026-08-03 recording that `resolveSchemaLocation` was unified into
     `internal/schemaloc.Resolve`, the "MUST be kept in sync" comment deleted,
     and this issue's `## Surface` conditional (`internal/` precedent "if that
     lands first") thereby **decided rather than conditional**. Mason
     independently re-derived the same fact by reading current `main` before
     implementing. The cross-session channel worked exactly as CLAUDE.md intends
     — and the belt-and-braces re-derivation cost one grep.
  3. **`docs/PLAN.md`'s hazard note was directionally right and quantitatively
     one-third complete.** It flagged that a bare document-set report would lose
     #276's `unresolved` tracking. The warden found **two more** decline branches
     that would have become fabricated "invalid" verdicts: `parse.go:532`, a
     composed document that resolves but fails `ReadDocument` (returned as an
     `xsderr` verdict, so `errors.As(*xsderr.Error)` cannot rescue it — the read
     failure *is* an `xsderr.Error`), and `parse.go:318`, an `<include>`/
     `<override>` with no `schemaLocation`, a plain `fmt.Errorf` grammar fault.
     A written-down hazard is a starting point for the pre-flight, not a
     substitute for it.
  4. **The refactor's own `Ratchet: unchanged` acceptance bullet was falsified by
     the design, before implementation, in the safe direction.** Every prior
     session that carried a `Ratchet: unchanged` criterion satisfied it. Here the
     criterion was *structurally* unsatisfiable — the whole point of the change
     is that the harness stops over-discovering — and the pre-flight said so, so
     the +4 arrived as a prediction being confirmed rather than as a surprise the
     arbiter had to adjudicate cold.
  5. **The steward's growth evidence is now moot by deletion.** The
     2026-08-02 audit measured `schema_closure.go` at 315 → 396 lines (+26%) and
     argued the growth rate raised the cost of leaving #259/#272 unlanded. The
     file is **107 lines**.
- Friction:
  1. **Three separate post-land passes flagged this issue's staleness and none of
     them edited the body.** #276's pass (2026-08-01) recorded that Acceptance
     bullet 2 names parser-side symbols for the conformance-side triple and that
     three cited line numbers had moved; the steward (2026-08-02) appended growth
     evidence; #259's pass (2026-08-03) recorded that the Notes' load-bearing
     "VERBATIM copy" quote describes a comment that **no longer exists**. All
     three chose flag-don't-edit (#315 class 1/3, which is the standing
     convention). The consequence is real: an implementer must read the body
     **plus three housekeeping comments in order** to know what the Acceptance
     currently means, and the oracle then found a fourth defect none of them
     caught. The convention is defensible; its cost is now measurable and belongs
     in front of the next `/retro`.
  2. **Zero repair rounds and zero rejections** — the second consecutive landing
     where a pre-flight warden pass substituted for arbiter rounds. #470 recorded
     the same, #271 recorded a whole mis-scoped issue killed the same way. Three
     in a row is a pattern, not an anecdote, and the `/retro` owes it a look at
     whether `WARDEN PRE-FLIGHT REQUIRED` should stop being conditional on a
     non-empty `## Surface` section.
- Next:

## 2026-08-04 — #273 — value,builtin/strict,xsderr: the godoc claim that citing a rule the spec doesn't define is "a bug by construction" finally gets a MECHANISM — 23 unexported `xsderr.Rule` constants replace 63 bare literals plus 15 switch-arm literals, and a stdlib-only AST scan gives the orphaned `IsValidRule` its first non-test consumer; the warden's PRE-FLIGHT killed the scan the issue actually asked for as SELF-DEFEATING (once the literals become constants every `New`/`Wrap` first arg is an identifier and the check passes vacuously forever) and replaced it with a positive/negative pair; the arbiter then spent round 1 entirely on DOC HONESTY — the softened `doc.go` claim was still wider than the mechanism, and a `var` probe walked straight through both halves undetected

- Shipped on `wip/issue-273` as two mason commits on top of `origin/main` @
  `a667253` (#272's landing): `4f0fe69` (`value,builtin/strict,xsderr: converge
  onto typed Rule constants, enforce the catalog membership contract
  module-wide (#273)`) and `3643b27` (`xsderr: bound the enforcement test's doc
  claims to what it actually scans, record the clause-suffix policy (#273)`).
  **20 files, +628/−83**, of which the new
  `xsderr/rulecatalog_enforcement_test.go` is 418 lines; 14 `builtin/strict`
  files, `value/{facets,restriction,union}.go`, `xsderr/{doc,error}.go` carry
  the rest. **Arbiter REJECT on round 1, ACCEPT on round 2** — one repair round,
  prose-only. **Zero new exported identifiers**; all 23 constants are unexported
  `rule*`.
- **Ratchet: unchanged.** The arbiter's figures, copied and not recomputed:
  `RATCHET: unchanged`, `GOXSD_RATCHET=1 go test ./conformance -run
  TestConformance -count=1` **wrote nothing**, `git status --porcelain` empty
  after it, **no expectation file moved in either direction and none is touched
  by the diff**. Plain conformance `ok 35.6s`. `go build ./...`,
  `go test ./...`, `go vet ./...` clean. `golangci-lint run` reports 2
  `forbidigo` hits under `.claude/worktrees/agent-…/tools/{fetchspecs,spec2md}/
  main.go` — the arbiter refused to carry round 1's "pre-existing" finding
  forward on trust and **reproduced them from a detached worktree at
  `origin/main`**, root-causing them to `.golangci.yml:52`'s `^tools/`
  exclusion being anchored past `.claude/worktrees/…/tools/…`. Container
  artifact, not this diff; the diff touches 0 files under `.claude/`.
- **What the issue was.** `xsderr/doc.go` claimed "every Rule constructed in
  this module is either in the catalog or the sentinel … a bug by construction",
  and `xsderr.IsValidRule` — the function that would decide it — had **zero
  non-test consumers**. Meanwhile 39 typed `Rule` constants existed across
  `xsd`/`parser`/`regex` (23 files) while `value` and `builtin/strict` handed
  bare string literals straight to `New`/`Wrap`. Two halves of one defect: an
  unenforced claim, and the packages that would have violated it first.
- **Grounding takeaway (oracle): nothing new was needed anywhere.** All 7
  distinct rule IDs `value` requires and the 1 `builtin/strict` requires are
  **already live entries in the generated `xsderr/catalog.go`** — zero new
  catalog entries, no `tools/rulecat` change, so the whole issue is a naming and
  enforcement change with no data movement behind it. The oracle also pinned the
  existing convention (`const rule<PascalCaseOfID> xsderr.Rule = "<id>"`,
  unexported, per-package, doc comment citing the spec §) and flagged the trap
  that decided the test's file filter: `xsderr/error_test.go` constructs
  `New("cvc-elt.1", …)`, and `"cvc-elt.1"` is **not** in the catalog (only bare
  `cvc-elt` is), so a naive all-`.go` scan breaks a currently-passing fixture.
- Decisions made and why:
  1. **THE redesign: the scan the issue specified could not work, and the
     warden's pre-flight said so before mason wrote a line.** The plan was
     "check that the literal first argument of `New`/`Wrap` is catalog-valid" —
     an invariant **this very issue destroys**, because after the refactor every
     first argument is an identifier and the check matches nothing forever. The
     replacement is two halves that need each other: **(A) POSITIVE** — every
     `xsderr.Rule`-typed *constant* declared anywhere in the module satisfies
     `IsValidRule`; **(B) NEGATIVE** — in non-test code no `*ast.BasicLit` may
     occupy a **Rule position**: `New`/`Wrap`'s first argument, the `Rule:`
     field of an `Error` composite literal, a return from a function whose
     declared result type is `Rule`, or the operand of a `Rule(...)` conversion.
     Without (B), (A) is trivially satisfiable by reverting to bare literals,
     which declare no constant and so are scanned by nothing.
  2. **The 5 switch helpers came IN scope, against the issue's own stated
     boundary.** `boundRule`, `digitsRule`, `lengthRule`, `scaleRule`
     (`value/facets.go`) and `boundRestrictionRule` (`value/restriction.go`)
     return 15 bare literals from `case` arms feeding ~14 `New`/`Wrap` sites.
     The oracle flagged them as outside the issue's 63-site acceptance; the
     warden ruled that leaving them is leaving exactly the defect the issue
     exists to remove, **with a scan structurally blind to it**. Rule (B) then
     makes reintroduction impossible rather than merely discouraged.
  3. **No shared rule package (STYLE 8, dependency direction).** It would have
     to sit below `xsd`, which imports only `xsderr` — a new leaf in the DAG for
     zero runtime benefit, and an exported one has no external consumer. The
     cross-package duplicate consts are one-line declarations, not duplicated
     structures, and **the enforcement test is now their shared source of
     truth**.
  4. **External `xsderr_test` package, deliberately.** In-package would reach
     `ruleCatalog` directly; external forces the test through the exported
     `IsValidRule` — the same door a consumer has — which is how the orphaned
     export gets its genuine first consumer, half the point of the issue.
  5. **Vacuity floors are mandatory, because a meta-test that walks nothing goes
     green.** Each half declares a minimum it must visit. Measured at writing:
     **83 Rule constants, 287 non-test `New`/`Wrap` call sites**; floors of
     65/250 leave 22% / 13% headroom.
- **Round 1: REJECT, and all three findings were about DOC HONESTY, not code.**
  The gate was green, the exported surface was unchanged, and behaviour
  identity was already structurally proved — the arbiter still rejected:
  1. **`doc.go`'s replacement claim was narrower than the original and still
     wider than the mechanism** — and the arbiter *reproduced* it rather than
     arguing it, appending `var probeVar xsderr.Rule = "cvc-var-hole"` plus a
     `New(probeVar, …)` use to `value/union.go` and watching **both halves stay
     green** (a `var` is not a `const`, so (A) skips it; the argument is an
     identifier, so (B) skips it). In the same edit the `&Error{Rule: …}` and
     `Rule("…")` probes *were* caught, with file:line. The verdict's own
     framing: this commit exists because `doc.go` made a claim wider than what
     was enforced, and it replaced it with a claim that is still wider than what
     is enforced.
  2. **The three residual holes the pre-flight required the test to NAME were
     absent from its header** — the header enumerated only what *is* covered.
     The correct repair was naming them, not closing them; the same pre-flight
     said "do not build variable/call-site resolution".
  3. **The clause-suffix policy, flagged "decide BEFORE landing", was never
     recorded.** `IsValidRule("cvc-complex-type.2.1")` is **false** — yet that
     exact string was the canonical `Rule` example in `error.go:8` *and*
     `doc.go:12`, three lines above the catalog-membership claim. Inert prose
     until this commit; **this commit gives it teeth**, so the first `validate/`
     const spelling a clause-level rule would fail with an error message
     ("regenerate catalog.go with `go tool rulecat`") that **cannot fix it** —
     `rulecat` emits base IDs.
- **The repair (`3643b27`) is prose-only, proved mechanically rather than
  asserted.** `git diff -U0 4f0fe69..HEAD` with headers, `//`-prefixed lines and
  blanks filtered out yields **zero lines** — +46/−8 of pure comment text across
  `xsderr/{doc.go,error.go,rulecatalog_enforcement_test.go}`; even the two
  `"cvc-complex-type.2.1"` → `"cvc-complex-type"` example swaps sit inside
  comments. Substantively: `doc.go` now enumerates the four scanned positions
  and states the limit outright — **"a syntactic floor, not a dataflow proof"**,
  naming the exact hole the arbiter's probe exploited in the same paragraph that
  makes the claim; the test header names all three holes and declines to
  overclaim on the third ("tracing it back reaches a validated origin, but this
  scan does not verify that chain and does not claim to"); and `IsValidRule`'s
  doc comment records the policy as **exact match, no prefix-trimming, no dotted
  leniency**, closing the escape hatch in the right direction ("extend
  `tools/rulecat` to emit it rather than weakening this check") and classifying
  `error_test.go`'s `"cvc-elt.1"` fixtures as **incidental, not deliberate** —
  those tests exercise rendering and nil handling, where any string serves.
- **Round 2: ACCEPT, with every round-1 claim re-verified independently rather
  than carried forward.** The arbiter executed each factual assertion in the new
  `IsValidRule` prose against the real catalog (`"cvc-complex-type"` true,
  `"cvc-elt"` true, `"cvc-complex-type.2.1"` false, `"cvc-elt.1"` false);
  checked each named hole against the *implementation* (`ruleConstants()` skips
  `gd.Tok != token.CONST` at `rulecatalog_enforcement_test.go:200`; `stringLit`
  returns false for every non-`*ast.BasicLit`); re-diffed `go doc -all` across
  all 22 non-tool packages (every hunk is `xsderr` prose); and **re-proved
  behaviour identity from scratch** — harvesting all 23 consts from HEAD,
  inlining each back to its literal across all 18 non-test changed files,
  stripping comments, and diffing executable code against `origin/main`: every
  file reduces exactly to base, the sole residual line module-wide being the
  `xsderr` import that `builtin/strict/strict.go`'s new const declaration itself
  requires. No rule was silently swapped for a neighbour, so the conformance
  result set **cannot** move — which the ratchet run then confirmed empirically.
  Round 1 had done the same substitution independently, and had additionally
  checked all 22 spec citations on the new consts against `<a id=…>` anchors in
  `docs/specs/md` — 22/22 — and mutation-tested the new test five ways.
- Surprises:
  1. **A pre-flight warden pass produced a rejection anyway — and it was the
     pre-flight's own instructions that produced it.** Three of the last four
     landings recorded zero arbiter rounds off the back of a pre-flight. Here
     the pre-flight ran, its design guidance was followed in full on the code
     (the arbiter confirmed every structural decision item by item), and the
     issue still cost a round — because **two of the three findings are the
     pre-flight's explicitly-required prose obligations going unwritten**
     ("residual holes to NAME", "decide the clause-suffix policy BEFORE
     landing"). A pre-flight's *design* rulings are hard to skip; its
     *documentation* rulings are easy to, and nothing in the gate can see the
     difference.
  2. **The one non-code change most likely to be wrong was the one about the
     mechanism's own limits.** Every rule ID, every spec §, and every one of 63
     + 15 conversions was correct on the first commit. The single thing mason
     got wrong was the sentence describing what the new test *proves* — a claim
     about a proof is harder to get right than the proof.
  3. **`IsValidRule` had been sitting on `cvc-complex-type.2.1` as its
     documented example while returning false for it, in two files, for the
     whole life of the package.** Harmless while nothing consumed the function;
     the moment something did, the canonical example became a trap for the next
     package to cite a clause-level rule.
- Friction:
  1. **The issue's own acceptance criteria drew a scope boundary that was
     wrong** — the 63 direct-literal sites, explicitly excluding the switch
     helpers' 15 case arms. The oracle flagged it as a boundary to respect and
     surface rather than silently cross; the warden overruled it. Two personas
     spent effort on a line the issue should not have drawn, and the fix belongs
     at the issue-body entry point.
  2. **One repair round, all of it prose.** Cheap as rounds go, but it is the
     second consecutive session where the blocking findings were entirely about
     doc claims rather than behaviour (#470 was two [T5] prose findings). Worth
     the next `/retro` asking whether a mason self-check pass over "claims made
     in comments about what the change guarantees" belongs in WORKFLOW.
- Next:
  1. **[unfiled follow-up, named by the warden as file-it-don't-do-it-here — no
     tracking issue exists, this entry is its only record]** `xsderr.Error`'s
