## 2026-08-04 — #275 — parser: `fetch`'s single `(nil, nil)` sentinel meant "D2 does not exist" and "D2 exists and was already read" at once, so `src-import` §4.2.6.2 clause 3 was skipped on every dedup hit — a chameleon `<include>` seeds the load-once key under a namespace the document does not declare, and the later `<import>` of that same document then lands on the key and is never judged; `fetch` now returns an unexported `fetched` outcome carrying D2's own targetNamespace and whether D2 exists at all, and the arbiter accepted on round 1 with all six lanes proved flat by a read-only `Compare` probe

- Shipped on `wip/issue-275` as ONE commit on top of `origin/main` @ `fe63062`
  (#273): `9b69840` (`parser: enforce src-import clause 3 on dedup hits (#275)`,
  mason). **3 files, +227/−34**: `parser/parse.go` (+120/−34, the whole of the
  mechanism), `parser/parse_test.go` (+102, three new tests),
  `parser/doc.go` (+5, the package-doc sentence that load-once suppresses the
  second COMPOSITION only). **Arbiter ACCEPT on round 1, zero rejections, zero
  repair rounds**, no blocking findings.
- **Ratchet: unchanged — schema 9170/15432, datatypes 1131/1153, instance
  0/26426.** The arbiter's figures, copied and not recomputed. Substantiated
  rather than asserted: `TestConformance` only fails on `Regressed`, so the
  arbiter ran a **read-only `Compare` probe** over every lane via
  `go test -overlay` (no file in the tree touched, `git status` clean before and
  after) and reports `improved=0 regressed=0 new=0 vanished=0` in all six lanes,
  every `Delta` partition empty. `Ratchet()` is therefore provably the identity
  merge and `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` would rewrite the expectation files
  byte-identically, so **it was deliberately not run** — there is nothing to
  bank and the diff touches no lane file. Gate: `go build ./... && go test ./...
  && go vet ./...` green (conformance 28.3s), **`golangci-lint run` → 0
  issues** run independently by the arbiter, `gofmt -l parser/` clean. That is
  the **eighth consecutive LANDING** with a real gate part 2.
- **What the issue was.** `fetch` collapsed two different outcomes into the same
  `(nil, nil)` return: `loader.ErrNotFound` (the reference strategy failed, D2
  does not exist, clause 3 is correctly vacuous) and a hit on the load-once
  index `loaded[docKey{resolved, namespace, override}]` (D2 exists and is
  already in the assembly, only its re-composition is excused). `importDocument`
  early-returned on `d2 == nil` for both, so **clause 3 was skipped whenever a
  document was reached via dedup**. Reachable exactly as #182's warden
  pre-flight predicted: a chameleon `<include>` keys a no-`targetNamespace`
  document under the includer's namespace (§F.1), and a later
  `<import namespace="urn:b">` of that same document lands on the key with its
  clause-3.1 violation unexamined. Direction is **under-rejection only**
  (fabricated-valid) — never a false lie in a lane file, which is why the fix
  moves no lane.
- Decisions made and why:
  1. **The sentinel is split by a value, not by a second return.**
     `fetch` now returns `fetched{doc, resolved, tns, exists}`, an unexported
     struct with a **single producer** — `fetch` itself, documented as such at
     `parse.go:661-665` — so the illegal combinations (a `doc` with `exists`
     false, a `doc` that is not the one just read) cannot be constructed
     elsewhere (STYLE T1). `exists` is the PRIMARY encoding of a fact the caller
     needs and is not derivable from `doc` (a dedup hit has a nil `doc` and an
     existing D2); recovering it from `resolved != ""` would be an implicit
     encoding piggybacking on a resolver contract detail. The arbiter
     adjudicated mason's own D3/T1 self-flag and dismissed it: `resolved` and
     `tns` are SHARED across two of the three cases, so the fields are not
     mutually exclusive and T1's sum-style remedy does not bite.
  2. **`loaded` changes from `map[docKey]struct{}` to `map[docKey]string`,
     recording D2's OWN targetNamespace (·absent· as `""`) at first read.** That
     is not redundant state (STYLE D3): the key's namespace half is the
     namespace D2 was reached UNDER, which is precisely what a chameleon
     `<include>` makes differ from D2's own. Without it a dedup hit could not be
     judged against clause 3 short of re-reading the document.
  3. **`importDocument` becomes three arms, not two.** `!f.exists` → clause 2
     unsatisfied, correctly a non-error; `f.doc == nil` (dedup hit) → run
     `checkImportedNamespace` against the recorded `f.tns` and skip the
     re-composition; fresh read → the existing full path. §4.2.6.2's note
     licenses skipping the re-COMPOSITION of S2's components — that is what
     keeps repeated `<import>`s off `sch-props-correct` clause 2 (`c-nmd`) — and
     nothing more: "If D2 exists, that is, clause 2.1 or clause 2.2 above were
     satisfied" is a fact about the resolved DOCUMENT, not about which
     `<import>` element caused it to be read.
  4. **`src-include` §4.2.3 clause 2 has the structurally identical
     `d2 == nil` early return and was resolved by a documented INVARIANT rather
     than a mechanism change** (`parse.go:400-410`, in `compose`'s
     `f.doc == nil` branch). Every live entry satisfies
     `loaded[{r, ns, ov}] ∈ {"", ns}`, so an include dedup hit is already
     clause 2.1 (own == tns) or clause 2.2/2.3 (own absent) and re-running the
     test could not change the verdict. **The arbiter traced this rather than
     trusting it**: all three write sites (`newAssembly` seeds the root with its
     own tns; `fetch` writes provisionally and the caller's check gates
     continuation, via `compose` for include/override and `checkImportedNamespace`
     for import), cross-seeding both ways, the empty-`<override>` key sharing,
     and — the load-bearing caveat — that a failing check **aborts the assembly
     outright**, verified as a direct `return err` at all seven call sites
     (`parse.go:148, 306, 310, 317, 358, 388, 479`) up through `discover` to
     `ParseReport`, so a violating provisional entry is never consulted.
  5. **`a.loaded[key] = own` moved from BEFORE `ReadDocument` to after it**, since
     there is nothing to record until the document is in hand. The only way that
     could matter is a `ReadDocument` failure whose assembly continues — it never
     does, by the same propagation trace — and the key is still claimed before
     `discover` recurses, so `<include>`-cycle load-once safety is preserved.
     Documented at `parse.go:651-655`.
  6. **Over-rejection was enumerated, not hoped for.** Given the invariant the
     recorded `own ∈ {"", NS}`, so the new check rejects **iff** `hasNamespace &&
     NS != "" && own == ""` — the chameleon collision and nothing else. No valid
     schema can newly fail; independently corroborated by zero lane movement in
     either direction.
- Tests (three, all mutation-verified by the arbiter under `go test -overlay`
  against mutated copies, working tree never modified):
  `TestParseImportClause3OnDedupHit` builds the #182-derived scenario and its
  control side by side with a **byte-identical `x.xsd`** in both, asserting not
  only a `src-import` rejection positioned at `x.xsd` but
  `deduped.Error() == control.Error()` — so it cannot pass on an error raised
  elsewhere in the closure (mutation A, dedup branch restored to `return nil`:
  FAIL, and only that test); `TestParseImportUnresolvableSkipsClause3` pins the
  other direction, an unresolvable `schemaLocation` in an assembly where
  `lib.xsd` IS loaded under `urn:b`, so an implementation that treated
  not-found as "D2 exists" would find a targetNamespace to compare and wrongly
  reject (mutation B: FAIL); `TestParseImportDedupHitComposesOnce` pins that
  running clause 3 on a hit did not turn it into a second composition — two
  identical `<import>`s, `Documents()` still `[main.xsd, b.xsd]`, no `c-nmd`, no
  spurious `Unfollowed` (mutation D: FAIL with `[sch-props-correct] … repeats
  the expanded name {urn:b}shared`). `TestParseSameDocumentIncludedAndImported`
  (#182) is **byte-identical to main** — the arbiter extracted the function body
  from `git show origin/main:parser/parse_test.go` to check — and passes; it is
  unaffected because its bare `<import schemaLocation="lib.xsd"/>` keys under
  `""`, a different `docKey` from the chameleon include's, so it is a key MISS.
- Surprises:
  1. **The bug's exact shape was written down in this log ten hours earlier and
     nothing had to be rediscovered.** #272's entry, Decision 2, names
     "`fetch` returns a nil document for **two** outcomes" as a correctness trap
     and handles it for the REPORT (recording nothing on the dedup branch, since
     that directive was followed). It stopped there because the report was the
     scope. #275 is the same observation carried into the CLAUSE, filed by the
     arbiter off #182. The cross-session channel and the log did what they are
     for; the gap was one of scope, not of memory.
  2. **`GOXSD_RATCHET=1` was NOT run on an accept path, and that is the correct
     call.** The arbiter substantiated "unchanged" with a read-only probe and
     then declined the write because an identity merge banks nothing. Compare
     #272, where the accept path did run it. The distinction — probe to
     substantiate, write only when there is movement — is worth a line in
     `docs/ROUTINES.md` if it recurs.
  3. **Zero rejections and zero repair rounds with NO warden pre-flight.** The
     issue's `## Surface` was "none expected" and the landed diff added exactly
     three unexported declarations (`type fetched`, the `fetch` signature, three
     `Test*` funcs) and **zero exported identifiers**, so the pre-flight trigger
     genuinely did not fire. That breaks the three-landing run of
     pre-flight-substitutes-for-rounds (#470, #271, #272) with a fourth clean
     landing reached a different way, which is evidence the `/retro` question
     raised in #272's Friction 2 should be about the TRIGGER's accuracy, not
     about making the pre-flight unconditional.
- Friction:
  1. **The oracle's grounding comment says the full text is "saved to
     `.agent/grounding-issue-275.md` on `wip/issue-275`". It is not on the
     branch and never was — `.agent/` is gitignored (`.gitignore:36`).** The
     file exists only in this container and dies with it. Nothing is actually
     lost, because the comment itself carries the complete grounding, but the
     pointer is false and a later session following it finds nothing. Either the
     oracle stops advertising container-local paths as branch artifacts, or
     `.agent/` grounding files start riding the branch. **Cheap, recurring, and
     one sentence in the oracle prompt — for the next `/retro`.**
  2. **The follow-up ledger has now gone FIVE consecutive sessions without an
     audit.** #272 said three, #273 said four. This session adds one more entry
     to it (Friction 1). The next `/retro` owes the audit, and this is the
     second consecutive entry saying so in bold.

  1. **[post-land pass, not yet run]** the usual close/unblock scan and branch

     include `<redefine>` in `UnfollowedNoSchemaLocation`'s scope; `:67`
     correctly excludes it).
  2. **[branch namespace, reported and not acted on]** `wip/issue-271` and
     `wip/issue-256` @ `c56d9f7` both still survive on `origin`, retired in
     place, awaiting human deletion. Sessions never delete or rename refs.
  3. **[carried, undischarged — the count of sessions these survive is the point
     of listing them]** #400, #430, #347, #426, #336, #413, #414/#438, #417,

     `negativeInteger`/`nonNegativeInteger`/`nonPositiveInteger`/`positiveInteger`
     harvest; **#324's A1/A2, whose post-land pass has still not run**; #257's
     Friction 1 rewording routed to fold into #458; #259's two notes; #261's two
     notes; #270's `assertions`-is-a-third-"one or more"-facet doc note; #470's
     two warden nits; #271's replan set (#478/#479 `ready`, #480 held

     grounding-pointer defect.**
- Thread record (do not duplicate) — <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/275>:
  two comments, both this session's. **ORACLE GROUNDING** at

  FIRST `<import>` of `urn:b` anyway since the first read was an `<include>`),

## 2026-08-04 — backlog — **band 2 was published on 2026-08-03, five sessions landed work against it, and not one of them picked a band entry** — all nine entries are still open, still `ready`, untouched, because the band is prose in `docs/PLAN.md` while the develop loop selects off the flat `ready` label where all 108 issues look identical; band 3 is derived anyway (10 entries, doctrine unchanged, two ordering changes) and the pass's other measured finding is that the post-land harvest now files **≈2.6 issues per issue landed**, which turns #347's structural diagnosis from an assertion into a number and explains a seventh consecutive backlog over the 8–10 band

- Doc-only reconciliation, **no commit of my own** — `docs/PLAN.md` (283
  insertions, a pure append into the M4 section ahead of the M5 heading) and
  this LOG entry are the entire file output; **no Go code changed, no gate run,

- **Ratchet: unchanged** (doc-only trigger). Figures below were read off

  **+4 across five landings is the honest number and matches expectations** —
  no rule implementation landed in the window. It is the mirror image of the
  2026-08-03 entry's `+4919`, which was mostly lane *widening*: that pass
  recorded movement without engineering, this one records engineering without
  movement.
- **The band-2 finding is the pass's headline, and it is a process defect, not
  a planning one.** #449, #336, #443, #446, #468, #436, #464, #463, #342 and

  queue. The band has no teeth: it lives as prose in `docs/PLAN.md`, and the
  develop loop picks off the `ready` label, where all 108 ready issues carry
  equal weight. Deriving band 3 was still worth doing, but this pass's real
  output for the next `/retro` is the mechanism gap, not the ordering.
- **Band 3 derived — 10 entries, same ordering doctrine** (measured lane
  movement → measurement integrity → false rejects → under-rejects → producer
  completeness):
  **#449 → #336 → #443 → #446 → #468 → #430 → #436 → #464→#463 (pair) → #342 →
  #478→#447.** Two changes from band 2:
  - **#430 promoted into slot 6** — a live false reject of the same class as
    #468. Band 2 missed it because it drew from the recent-harvest set rather
    than a full false-reject sweep; this pass swept.
  - **#478 resequenced ahead of #447** — #447 *is* the list/union producer work
    that makes #478's representation fix more expensive once landed. This
    ordering already existed, but only as a `docs/LOG` "Next" line; it is now
    in the band itself.
- **Issues: two filed, one relabelled, six commented, none closed** (the
  cartographer never closes issues as done).
  - **#492** (`ready`, `kind/story`, `area/docs`) — README's Library section

    `UnfollowedDirective` / `UnfollowedReason`, all landed with #272 and never

  - **#493** (`ready`, `kind/process`, `area/meta`) — a `needs-replan` park
    closed with `state_reason: completed` makes **#256 and #271 indistinguishable

  - **#471** — milestone **M3 → M4**; M3 is complete and the issue is a parser
    producer item.
  - Comments on **#489** (ledger reconciled), **#347** (the harvest ratio, now
    measured), **#399** (branch table), **#295** (overlap with #433), **#411**
    (its deliverable has landed — flagged as awaiting close-as-done by the
    develop loop), **#416** (sixth consecutive sighting).
- **Queue arithmetic, and the first *measured* explanation of it**: **120 open
  (108 `ready`, 11 `blocked`, 1 unlabelled #411)**, up from 110 (99/10/1) at
  the 2026-08-03 pass — **+9 ready in a single day**, seventh consecutive pass
  over the 8–10 band. The five landings closed five `ready` issues; their
  post-land harvest filed **thirteen** (#476–#491) plus this pass's two.
  **Harvest ratio ≈2.6 filed per issue landed.** #347 has argued the structural

- **Consolidation remains exhausted — third consecutive confirmation.** Every
  candidate stale/duplicate pair was checked and confirmed **not** a duplicate:

  that #271's own body carried**. The only defect the check found was the
  `state_reason` mislabelling, which became #493.
- **GAP ledger unchanged**: 43 hits / 21 files, same count, same files, same
  owners as the 2026-08-03 re-derivation. **No new unowned site.** Line numbers

- **Branch namespace: two stale refs, nothing under `parked/`.**
  `wip/issue-256` carries 2 commits not in `main` (correctly retired as a park).
  `wip/issue-271` carries **zero** commits not in `main` — its tip *is* #470's
  already-landed commit, which is the branch-level fingerprint of an attempt
  that died at warden pre-flight before any commit existed. Both **flagged for
  human deletion, not acted on** — sessions never delete refs.
- Decisions made and why:
  1. **Band 3 was derived even after discovering the band mechanism does not
     work.** The alternative — refusing to publish an ordering until it has
     teeth — would leave the develop loop with nothing at all. The band is
     published *and* the mechanism failure is recorded as the retro's input,
     rather than one substituting for the other.
  2. **#430 was found by a full false-reject sweep, and that method choice is
     the lesson.** Band 2 drew its false-reject entries from the recent-harvest
     set, which is why a live false reject of the same class as #468 sat outside
     the queue for a day. Bands get swept from the label, not from what happened
     to land recently.
  3. **#478 → #447 was promoted from a LOG "Next" line into the band.** An
     ordering constraint that only exists in a log entry is invisible to the
     session that needs it; #447 landing first makes #478 strictly more
     expensive.
  4. **#411 was commented, not closed.** Its deliverable has landed and was
     verified applied this pass, but the cartographer files and reconciles — the
     develop loop closes as done.
- Surprises:
  1. **A published, dependency-ordered, doctrine-justified working queue
     changed nothing.** Five sessions, zero band entries picked. The queue was
     not disputed, disagreed with, or superseded; it was simply not visible at
     the point of selection.
  2. **The harvest ratio is ≈2.6.** The backlog is not growing because filing is
     careless — it is growing because *landing an issue is itself a filing
     event*, ~2.6 times over. No shrink mechanism available to a `/backlog` pass
     touches that.
  3. **`wip/issue-271` has zero commits of its own.** Its tip is another issue's
     landed commit. A pre-flight rejection leaves a branch that is
     indistinguishable from a fresh one, which is a second way (after #493's
     `state_reason`) that a park erases its own evidence.
  4. **#417 was found closed but still carried as undischarged** on #489's
     ledger — flagged on the thread.
- Friction:
  1. **`/backlog`'s persona half is still structurally unrunnable — sixth
     consecutive sighting (#416).** A cartographer subagent cannot spawn the
     libuser/cliuser subagents its own procedure calls for. Substitute performed

     the `xsd`/`parser` exported surface directly. It found a real gap and it
     became #492 — but a session that has read the source is not a persona, and

  2. **The band has no enforcement surface.** Ordering lives in `docs/PLAN.md`
     prose; selection happens against a flat `ready` label. Every band this
     project has published has had this defect; band 2 is the first one with
     five landings' worth of evidence that it matters.
  3. **Parks are indistinguishable from completions in two independent places**

     found this pass, neither by looking for it.
  4. **Seventh consecutive backlog over the 8–10 band**, and for the first time

## 2026-08-04 — #279 — parser: a QName reference stops resolving into any namespace the ASSEMBLY happened to contribute and must name one the CONTAINING DOCUMENT itself licensed — `resolveQName` splits into `bindQName` (§3.3.18 prefix mapping, unchanged) plus a new `licensedNamespace` charging src-resolve clause 4, with `notQName` deliberately left OUT of the gate because §3.10.2 takes its items as QName VALUES that never ·resolve· to components; arbiter ACCEPT on round 1, schema lane **+6**

- Shipped on `wip/issue-279` as TWO commits on top of `origin/main` @ `cc9bf23`
  (`meta: backlog 2026-08-04`): `ec4fee6` (`parser: gate QName resolution on the
  containing document's own imports (#279)`, mason) and `5bc9bba` (`conformance:
  ratchet the schema lane on src-resolve clause 4 (#279)`, the arbiter's own
  ratchet write). Mason's commit is **6 files, +407/−29**: `parser/produce.go`
  (+127/−17, the whole of the mechanism),
  `parser/produce_srcresolve_internal_test.go` (+139, new file),
  `parser/parse_test.go` (+102, three assembly-level tests), `parser/doc.go`
  (+26/−9, one GAP marker retired and one restated), `parser/produce_complex.go`
  (+8/−2, the `notQName` exemption and its rationale), `parser/parse.go` (+5/−1,
  the doc sentence naming `<import>`'s second duty). The ratchet commit is
  `conformance/testdata/expectations/schema.txt` alone, +6/−6.
  **Arbiter ACCEPT on round 1, zero rejections, zero repair rounds**, no
  blocking findings.
- **Ratchet: schema +6 (`fail -> pass`); datatypes unchanged; instance
  unchanged.** The arbiter's figures, copied and not recomputed. The six upward
  flips: `MS-Element2006-07-15/elemZ006`, `.../elemZ007`,
  `MS-IdentityConstraint2006-07-15/idC019`, `MS-Schema2006-07-15/schG9`,
  `.../schZ004`, `.../schZ005`. **No downward flip and no vanished case**;
  `go test ./conformance -run TestConformance -count=1` ran **41858 cases across
  6 lanes, 0 regressed**. The committed `schema.txt` now reads 9176/15432 (that
  is a count of the banked FILE, not a run — 9170 before). Gate: `go build ./...`,
  `go test ./...`, `go vet ./...` all ok, **`golangci-lint run` → 0 issues**,
  working tree clean at judgment. That is the **ninth consecutive LANDING** with
  a real gate part 2.
- **Attribution was proved, not asserted.** The arbiter stubbed
  `licensedNamespace` to always-nil — reverting clause 4 alone while leaving the
  `bindQName` split and the `containingSchema`/`declares` rewrite in place — and
  watched **exactly those six** regress and nothing else. `elemZ006` is the
  textbook shape: `elemZ006.xsd` names `b:b` while importing only namespace `a`,
  and namespace `b` reaches the assembly solely through `elemZ006_a.xsd`'s own
  `<import>`. That is precisely the "a sibling document contributed the
  namespace" bug the issue exists to close.
- **What the issue was.** `resolveQName` implemented src-resolve clauses 1-3 and
  simply never asked clause 4 (`cl.qnr.nsdeclared`, §3.17.6.2). A QName landed on
  whatever namespace the prefix bindings named, and finalize then found the
  component if ANY document of the assembly had contributed that namespace — so a
  document could reach into a namespace only a sibling had `<xs:import>`ed.
  §4.2.6.1 is explicit that this is a src-resolve verdict **at the reference**
  and not a §5.3 missing sub-component ("references … not imported by that
  schema document … are *not* handled as if they referred to missing
  components"), which is why the charge belongs in the producer and not in
  `Finalize`. Direction was **under-rejection only**, which is why all six flips
  are `fail -> pass` and nothing moved the other way.
- Decisions made and why:
  1. **`resolveQName` splits rather than grows.** `bindQName` keeps the old
     §3.3.18 prefix mapping and the `unqualifiedRefNS` default-namespace logic
     verbatim; `licensedNamespace` is the new, separately-callable clause-4
     charge. The split is what makes decision 2 expressible at all — there is now
     a caller that wants binding WITHOUT licensing.
  2. **`notQName` was deliberately kept OUT of the gate, and this was mason's
     own judgment call, not the issue's or the oracle's.**
     `disallowedNames` (`parser/produce_complex.go`) now calls `bindQName`
     directly. §3.10.2's `{disallowed names}` mapping takes its items as QName
     **values** — "If the item is a QName value (i.e. an expanded name), then
     that QName value is a member of the set" — with no `·resolved·` link to
     `src-resolve` anywhere, unlike every `type=`/`base=`/`ref=` slot. The
     arbiter verified this independently against `xmlschema11-1.md:2863` and
     corroborated it from the other end: `cvc-wildcard-name` clauses 2.1/2.2
     route `{disallowed names}` through **`cvc-resolve-instance`**, the
     un-gated instance-side rule. Gating it would have false-rejected
     `<any notQName="foo:bar"/>` naming an unimported namespace — a legal schema.
  3. **The license is read per DOCUMENT, from that document's own `<import>`
     children only** (`importsNamespace`), never the assembly-wide or transitive
     closure. 4.2.2's own words scope it to "some `<import>` … contained in the
     `<schema>` … of THAT schema document". This is the entire bug.
  4. **Clause 4.2.1 tests the EFFECTIVE (post-§F.1) target namespace `p.target`,
     while 4.1.1 reads the raw `targetNamespace` attribute.** §F.1 task (a) has
     already made the including namespace a chameleon document's own by the time
     clause 4 runs, and the coercion is constant across an `<include>`/
     `<override>` cascade — so `p.target` IS the containing document's coerced
     target namespace, and a chameleon document's references are checked under
     4.2.1, not 4.1.
  5. **`containingSchema(elem)`, not `p.schemaElem`, answers "which document
     contains this reference".** A declaration substituted into a top level by an
     `<override>` in a DIFFERENT document (§F.2 clause 1) is licensed by the
     OVERRIDING document's imports. `declares` was then rewritten in terms of
     `containingSchema` so the parent walk exists once rather than twice (STYLE
     T4 — the diff REMOVES a duplicated walk).
  6. **`namespace=""` folds into the ·absent· namespace rather than tracking
     attribute PRESENCE separately**, the same fold `checkNoSelfImport` already
     documents — keeping them apart would be a second encoding of
     namespace-absence (STYLE D3).
  7. **The clause-4 `GAP(xsd)` marker is retired; the §5.3 one is RESTATED, not
     dismissed.** `parser/doc.go` now explains why clause 4 cannot close it —
     clause 4 judges whether the document ASKED for the namespace, §5.3 what
     follows when a namespace it did ask for supplies no such component — and
     leaves it owned by **#434**, which is open (STYLE P3). Marker count in
     `parser/doc.go` 5 → 4; module-wide GAP ledger **43 → 42 hits, still 21
     files**. The arbiter judged this a better discharge of acceptance bullet 5
     than the bullet itself asked for.
- **Exported surface: zero movement.** `go doc -all ./parser` diffed `origin/main`
  vs the branch is **prose only** — no identifier added, changed or removed.
  Matches the issue's "none expected", and no warden pre-flight fired.
- Tests (six new functions, all mutation-verified by the arbiter):
  three assembly-level in `parser/parse_test.go` —
  `TestParseImportLicensesOnlyItsOwnDocument` (a sibling's import licenses
  nothing here), `TestParseUnqualifiedReferenceNeedsBareImport` (the 4.1
  absent-namespace direction), `TestParseChameleonReferenceLicensedByCoercedNamespace`
  (4.2.1 against the post-§F.1 namespace) — plus a new internal file
  `parser/produce_srcresolve_internal_test.go` holding a six-row table on
  `resolveQName` (`4.2.1`, `4.2.2`, `4.2.3`, `4.2.4`, unimported, absent-without-
  bare-import), `TestResolveQNameBareImportLicensesAbsentNamespace` and
  `TestResolveQNameNotQNameIsNotLicensed`. **It is package-internal on purpose**:
  from outside the package every case would also have to make the named component
  EXIST, which would confuse "unlicensed namespace" (rejected here, at the
  reference) with "namespace supplied nothing" (rejected at finalize) — both are
  src-resolve, and only the reference-time call tells them apart. Five mutations,
  all caught: `licensedNamespace` → always nil (4 parser tests); the XSI arm
  dropped (the `XSI_namespace,_unimported_(4.2.4)` subtest ALONE); `notQName`
  back through `resolveQName` (`TestResolveQNameNotQNameIsNotLicensed` alone);
  4.2.1 reading the raw attribute instead of `p.target` (8 tests).
- Surprises:
  1. **The fifth mutation is caught by NO Go test — only by conformance
     `Override/over029`.** Replacing `containingSchema(elem)` with the
     obvious-looking `p.schemaElem` at the `licensedNamespace` call site
     (`parser/produce.go:1310`) leaves the ENTIRE `./parser` unit suite green
     while false-rejecting a spec-legal schema: an `<override>` child referencing
     a namespace the overriding document imports but the overridden one does not.
     The arbiter probed it directly and confirmed both that the branch is correct
     and that the guard lives in the SUITE, not in `parser`. It filed this as a
     note rather than a finding — correctly, since nothing is unguarded — but a
     Go-level regression test pinning that specific invariant is **a real
     follow-up for the post-land pass to file** (see Next 1). Recorded here so
     the cartographer can pick it up; this session did not file it.
  2. **The oracle's grounding, mason's implementation and the arbiter's
     independent spec re-derivation all landed on the same reading, and the one
     place mason went off-script — `notQName` — was the one place it mattered
     most.** The issue text says "wire it into `resolveQName`"; taken literally
     that gates `notQName` too and false-rejects. The correct call came from
     reading §3.10.2's mapping rather than the issue.
  3. **The issue's own body has now been corrected twice in-thread for stale
     pointers, and BOTH corrections had themselves gone stale by the time the
     work ran.** The 2026-08-01 comment repointed `parser/doc.go:81/:87` to
     `:103/:109`; at `cc9bf23` the two markers were actually at `:123` and
     `:129`. Nothing was lost — `resolveQName` is still the single choke point
     and the markers are greppable — but a line-number correction has a shorter
     half-life than the interval between filing and landing.
- Friction:
  1. **Line-number pointers in issue bodies and issue comments do not survive
     the backlog latency.** Twice-corrected, twice stale (Surprise 3). The cheap
     durable fix is to cite the marker TEXT or the enclosing identifier rather
     than the line — one sentence in the cartographer prompt. **For the next
     `/retro`.**
  2. **The follow-up ledger has now gone SIX consecutive sessions without an
     audit.** #272 said three, #273 four, #275 five (in bold), this makes six.
     This session adds two more entries to it (the `containingSchema` Go-test
     gap and the 4.1.1/4.2.1 under-rejection below). **The next `/retro` owes the
     audit, and this is the third consecutive entry saying so in bold.**
  3. **Band 3 was published by the 2026-08-04 backlog pass and this session did
     not pick from it either** — #279 was already in flight against the flat
     `ready` label. That is a seventh data point for the backlog pass's own
     headline finding (the band has no enforcement surface), not a new one, but
     it is the first one recorded from the develop side rather than the planning
     side.
- Next:
  1. **[post-land pass, not yet run]** the usual close/unblock scan and branch
     sweep, plus **file the `containingSchema`-invariant follow-up**: a
     `./parser` regression test for the `<override>`-licensing case that today
     only `Override/over029` guards (arbiter's note, Surprise 1). This entry does
     NOT file it — the cartographer does.
  2. **[carried, undischarged — new this session]** the deliberate
     under-rejection the arbiter noted and did not charge: for an `<override>`
     child, clause 4.1.1 reads the containing document's RAW `targetNamespace`
     while 4.2.1 uses the effective `p.target`. The two coincide on every
     reachable non-`<override>` path and diverge only in the ACCEPTING direction,
     so it is a note, not a gap — but it is unwritten anywhere but the verdict
     thread until now.
  3. **#286 (`<xs:redefine>`) is the one composition path that can still widen
     the set of documents minting components**, and per this issue's own Notes
     bullet (as corrected in-thread from #183 → #286) the per-document import
     scope this session landed should be re-read when it does.
  4. **#434 owns the §5.3 gap** the restated marker now points at, and it is open.
  5. **[branch namespace, reported and not acted on]** `wip/issue-271` and
     `wip/issue-256` both still survive on `origin`, retired in place, awaiting
     human deletion. Sessions never delete or rename refs.
  6. **[carried, undischarged — the count of sessions these survive is the point
     of listing them]** #400, #430, #347, #426, #336, #413, #414/#438, #417,
     #458; #346's two unfiled T4/prose advisories; #365's 20-file
     `negativeInteger`/`nonNegativeInteger`/`nonPositiveInteger`/`positiveInteger`
     harvest; **#324's A1/A2, whose post-land pass has still not run**; #257's
     Friction 1 rewording routed to fold into #458; #259's two notes; #261's two
     notes; #270's `assertions`-is-a-third-"one or more"-facet doc note; #470's
     two warden nits; #271's replan set (#478/#479 `ready`, #480 held `blocked`)
     and the #478-before-#447 ordering note; #272's two cartographer items;
     #273's three notes; #275's `.agent/`-is-gitignored grounding-pointer defect;

- Thread record (do not duplicate) — <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/279>:
  four comments, two of them this session's. **ORACLE GROUNDING** at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/279#issuecomment-5181780913> — clause
  4 verbatim with all six sub-clause labels intact, §4.2.6.1's licensing text and
  its explicit §5.3 carve-out, the src-resolve / `cvc-resolve-instance` boundary
  ("do NOT gate `xsi:type`/instance QName lookup by it"), §F.1's two coercion
  tasks and the ruling that §F.1 synthesizes no imports into D2, plus the
  instruction to reuse `ruleSrcResolve` rather than mint a rule string
  (`xmlschema11-1.md` §3.17.6.2, §3.17.6.3, §4.2.6.1, §5.3, §F.1).
  **ARBITER VERDICT round 1 (`accept`)** at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/279#issuecomment-5182178557> — the
  five-row gate table, the six-line flip list with the always-nil attribution
  stub, the independent spec re-derivation of all six sub-clauses, the
  `notQName` exemption checked against `xmlschema11-1.md:2863` and the
  `cvc-wildcard-name` → `cvc-resolve-instance` path rather than against mason's
  summary, the acceptance-bullet walk (bullet 5 judged met in a BETTER form than
  asked), the `go doc -all` prose-only surface diff, a clause-by-clause STYLE
  sweep (S1, E1/E2, D2, D3/D4, T4, T5), the five-mutation table, and the two
  informational notes this entry carries forward as Next 1 and Next 2. The two
  earlier comments are the cartographer's: a **#183 → #286 stale-pointer
  correction** (<…#issuecomment-5151579981>) and the **GAP-marker line-number
  correction** (<…#issuecomment-5151799037>), both filed 2026-08-01 and both
  already drifted by the time the work ran (Surprise 3). This entry is written on
  the WIP branch after the verdict and **before** the PR is opened so it rides in
  the squash. The squash commit carries `Spec: src-resolve cl. 4
  (cl.qnr.nsdeclared, §3.17.6.2), §4.2.6.1, §F.1, §3.10.2` and `Ratchet: schema
  +6 (elemZ006, elemZ007, idC019, schG9, schZ004, schZ005); datatypes and
  instance unmoved`.

## 2026-08-04 — #282 — xsd: the `maxProductStates = 4096` ceiling in `contentModelRestricts` is MEASURED rather than argued — 0 ceiling hits and a high-water mark of 15 product states over 41858 conformance cases (688 walk entries), so the issue takes its own zero-cases branch and closes as documentation; the substantive change is that the `GAP(xsd)` marker stops presenting §3.4.6.3's implementation-defined (a)/(b)/(c) sentence as an unconditional licence, because the oracle found it textually anchored to `{compositor} = all` — a condition this branch never sees; arbiter ACCEPT on round 1, ratchet unchanged

- Shipped on `wip/issue-282` as ONE substantive commit on top of `origin/main`
  @ `8490beb` (`parser: gate QName resolution on the containing document's own
  imports (#279)`): `c850d11` (`xsd: record the measured headroom behind
  maxProductStates and scope its licence honestly (#282)`, mason). The earlier
  `7f4b0d1` was a checkpoint of the temporary measurement instrumentation
  (`xsd/zz_diag_measure.go`), fully removed again before the verdict. Net diff
  against `origin/main` is **1 file, +60/−18, `xsd/contentrestricts.go` alone**,
  and the arbiter verified comment-only MECHANICALLY, not by eye: in `-U0`,
  every added and removed line that is not a `//` line or blank is the empty
  set. No behavior change, no test touched, no expectations file touched.
  **Arbiter ACCEPT on round 1, zero rejections, zero repair rounds**, no
  blocking findings and four advisories, all optional.
- **Ratchet: unchanged** — the arbiter's figure, copied and not recomputed.
  `GOXSD_RATCHET=1 go test ./conformance -run TestConformance -count=1` passed
  and `conformance/testdata/expectations/` was **byte-identical afterwards**
  (`git status` empty), so no lane moved in either direction. Gate: `go build
  ./...`, `go test ./...` (all 15 packages), `go vet ./...` all ok,
  **`golangci-lint run` → 0 issues**, `go test ./conformance -run
  TestConformance -count=1` pass, working tree clean at start and at end of
  judgment. That is the **tenth consecutive landing with a real gate part 2**.
- **The measurement, and the arbiter's independent reproduction of it.** Mason
  instrumented three points in `contentModelRestricts` — entry into the walk,
  the `len(visited) >= maxProductStates` giveup branch, and every insertion into
  the visited set (so the high-water mark comes from walks that FINISH, not only
  from ones that give up) — and ran the full suite over the initialized
  `testdata/xsdtests` submodule (`7bc3365`):

  | quantity | value |
  | --- | --- |
  | suite cases discovered | 41858, across 6 lanes |
  | entries into the product walk | 688 |
  | times the ceiling branch was reached | **0** |
  | largest `len(visited)` observed | **15** |
  | ceiling constant | 4096 |

  `go test ./... -count=1` (conformance plus every unit-test package) produced
  the same figures. The arbiter did **not** take these on report — because they
  become permanent assertions in a code comment, it applied its own separate
  counters at the same three points, re-ran, then removed them and re-confirmed
  a clean tree: `walks=688 maxVisited=15 ceilingHits=0`, exact agreement on all
  three quantities. The suite framing checks out from the conformance test's own
  output (`discovered 41858 cases across 6 lanes`: datatypes 1153, schema 15432,
  instance 26426, xpath/json/ber 0). The 688 is the load-bearing number: it
  proves the branch sits on a **live path reached by real schemas**, so the zero
  is headroom, not an unreachable arm.
- Decisions made and why:
  1. **Documentation-only close, per the issue's own zero-cases branch.** The
     constant was NOT raised — the issue forbids that explicitly, and the
     marker's retirement condition is carried through verbatim: *"retired by a
     construction that decides containment without materializing the product,
     never by raising the constant."* The `GAP(xsd)` marker stays; the
     incompleteness is real, merely latent.
  2. **`maxProductStates`' doc comment now states the measurement instead of an
     unbacked claim.** The old text asserted "every content model the W3C suite
     and real schemas contain settles in a handful of states"; it now carries the
     figures above and says plainly that the bound is kept for the worst case the
     powerset admits — which nothing measures — not because any known schema
     approaches it. The numbers are inline, so no reader has to follow the issue
     link to get the substance.
  3. **The licence is scoped honestly, and this is the session's real content.**
     The oracle's grounding found §3.4.6.3's leniency textually anchored to
     condition (1), `T.{content type}.{particle}.{term}.{compositor} = all`, with
     the (a)/(b)/(c) sentence introducing no condition of its own — and the
     all-compositor condition appearing **exactly once** in the document. A
     genuine `all` compositor never reaches this branch anyway:
     `contentTypeRestricts` takes the narrow, correctly scoped allowance through
     `usesAllCompositor` before an automaton is built. The ceiling applies
     uniformly to sequence and choice models, so for those the marker now reads
     (c) as a **residual catch-all detached from condition (1) — defensible,
     since nothing in the local specs forecloses it, but not textually
     guaranteed** — named as a stretch rather than asserted as a licence.
  4. **"Provisionally accept" is no longer allowed to read as a safety
     guarantee.** The marker quotes §3.4.6.3's consequence sentence verbatim —
     *"the schema does not conform to this specification, and no ·assessment· can
     be performed using that schema"* — and states that (b)/(c) as worded
     describe processors that do runtime cross-checking, whereas this ceiling
     gives up permanently with no runtime fallback. What IS guaranteed is
     direction: the whole walk is abandoned, never truncated into a verdict, so
     it is fail-open — a missed rejection, never a false one.
  5. **The file header's "DIRECTION OF EVERY APPROXIMATION" paragraph gained one
     clause** pointing at the giveup site for how narrowly the licence is
     conditioned. Mason flagged this as a possible scope question; the arbiter
     ruled it in scope and the minimum edit — without it the header would assert
     the retired unconditional reading 220 lines above its own correction, to the
     reader most likely to be misled.
- Surprises:
  1. **The zero-hit result is not a near miss.** 15 against 4096 is ~273x
     headroom. The comment says "two orders of magnitude", which the arbiter
     noted is ~2.4 — conservative and true as a floor, not an overclaim, so it
     was left as written (advisory 3, no action).
  2. **Mason quoted MORE of the spec than either the issue body or the
     grounding did**, carrying (c) in full as "sometimes detects such violations
     *by examination of the schema in isolation and sometimes not*" where both
     upstream sources truncated at "such violations". The arbiter called this the
     right call and verified all three quotes verbatim against
     `docs/specs/md/xmlschema11-1.md` (lines 2041 and 2043) — the tail is exactly
     the clause that makes (c) a residual reading rather than a blank cheque.
- Friction:
  1. **The follow-up ledger has now gone SEVEN consecutive sessions without an
     audit.** #272 said three, #273 four, #275 five, #279 six (in bold), this
     makes seven. **The next `/retro` owes the audit, and this is the fourth
     consecutive entry saying so in bold.**
  2. **Two unreachable `contentAutomatonOf` error arms in the same file
     (`:246-255` and `:257-259`) still carry the retired unconditional framing**
     — `:251-252` reads "provisionally accepting is the fail-open direction
     §3.4.6.3's implementation-defined licence covers", 50 lines above the
     correction. They were left untouched deliberately. The arbiter recorded the
     inconsistency and did **not** charge it: the arms are unreachable by
     construction (their own comment establishes why), the header's new clause is
     a file-wide caveat that covers them, and widening a comment-only diff to
     chase an unreachable branch is the worse trade. Noted so it is on the
     record rather than rediscovered. It is a loose end, not a defect.
  3. **The marker is spelled `GAP(xsd) #282:`**, with the issue number outside
     the `<area>` parens but before the colon, where STYLE P3's shape is
     `GAP(<area>): <text>` with the issue named IN the text. It is the only marker in the
     repo in that form (1 of 36, the arbiter's count). The debt sweep `grep -rn "GAP("`
     still finds it and `<area>` is still `xsd`, so nothing is defeated —

     sweep's own ledger is **unchanged by this session at 42 hits across 21
     files**, as a comment-only diff that adds and retires no marker must leave
     it.
- Next:
  1. **[post-land pass, not yet run — the one item this session deliberately

## 2026-08-04 — #283 — xsd: a COMMENTS-ONLY disclosure issue surfaced a live conformance-correctness bug — mason went to write the "established empirically, fail-open" sentence the issue asked for, actually gathered the evidence, and found the `unfoldCopies` 2/2 copy cap diverges in BOTH directions on a bare element particle: `R = e{3,6}` under `B = e{0,100}` is a conforming restriction the walk FALSE-REJECTS, `R = e{5,5}` under `B = e{3,3}` an invalid one it accepts; the arbiter reproduced both independently through the `cRestricts` harness and widened the class to five witnesses; the marker landed says what is true rather than what the acceptance criteria literally demanded, deviation approved on the record; arbiter ACCEPT on round 1, ratchet unchanged

- Shipped on `wip/issue-283` as ONE commit on top of `origin/main` @ `089b76a`
  (`xsd: record the measured headroom behind maxProductStates and scope its
  licence honestly (#282)`): `a564bfd` (`xsd: complete contentrestricts.go's
  fail-open disclosure (#283)`, mason). Net diff against `origin/main` is
  **1 file, +37/−4, `xsd/contentrestricts.go` alone**. Comments only — no
  algorithm change, no test touched, no expectations file touched, and
  `go doc -all ./xsd` **byte-identical** to `origin/main` (the arbiter
  re-verified this independently in a `git worktree` of the base, not from
  mason's report). **Arbiter ACCEPT on round 1, zero rejections, zero repair
  rounds**, no blocking findings and four advisories, all out of scope.
- **Ratchet: unchanged (verified, not merely un-lowered)** — the arbiter's
  figure, copied and not recomputed. `GOXSD_RATCHET=1 go test ./conformance
  -run TestConformance -count=1` passed (`ok 38.056s`) and
  `git status --porcelain` was **empty both before and after** that run, so no
  expectations file was rewritten and no lane moved in either direction. Gate:
  `go build ./...`, `go test ./...`, `go vet ./...` all ok, **`golangci-lint
  run` → `0 issues.`**, `go test ./conformance -run TestConformance -count=1`
  → `ok 35.202s`. That is the **eleventh consecutive landing with a real gate
  part 2**.
- The four acceptance items, all landed:
  1. **(1)** `someBindingSubsumes` now carries a `GAP(xsd)` marker
     (`xsd/contentrestricts.go:571`); the pre-existing prose was kept, not
     rewritten, as the issue required. The marker states why the existential is
     this file's and not the spec's, and enumerates the consumer per STYLE P3a:
     `contentModelRestricts` is the sole non-comment caller and does nothing
     with a `false` except charge clause 2 and stop, so every acceptance the
     quantifier adds costs a rejection and none can fabricate one.
  2. **(2)** the copy cap is disclosed in the file header's `DIRECTION OF EVERY
     APPROXIMATION` block as its own marker at `:77` — but **not** as the issue
     worded it; see the discovery below.
  3. **(3)** the first unreachable `contentAutomatonOf` arm in
     `contentTypeRestricts` stops restating §3.4.6.3's licence unconditionally
     and defers to `contentModelRestricts`' giveup site for the actual, narrower
     scope. The second arm already read *"same unreachable error slot, same
     direction"* and needed nothing — the issue anticipated two edits and one
     was already correct.
  4. **(4)** the giveup marker is respelled from `GAP(xsd) #282:` to
     `GAP(xsd):` with the issue named in prose per STYLE P3, and repointed from
     the closed #282 to the open **#499** which owns the ceiling's retirement.
     The inline measurement figures #282 landed (688 walks, 15 high-water, 4096)
     live on `maxProductStates`' doc and are untouched.
  Post-condition met: `grep -rn "GAP(" --include=*.go xsd/contentrestricts.go`
  returns **six** sites (`:77`, `:249`, `:319`, `:423`, `:571`, `:597`), and the
  only issue named in any of them is #499, open.
- **THE DISCOVERY — the issue's own premise for item (2) is FALSE, and the
  refutation is a live false-reject.** The body directed mason to write that the
  cap's fail-open direction is *"established empirically, not proved"*, citing
  the arbiter's #263 round-1 observation that *"the direction works out
  fail-open for the shapes I could construct"*. Mason went to gather that
  evidence before writing the sentence and found the opposite. The cap
  (`maxMandatoryCopies`/`maxOptionalCopies` = 2/2 in
  `xsd/particleattribution.go`) rewrites an occurrence range —
  `e{3,6}` → `e{2,4}`, `e{0,100}` → `e{0,2}` — and that rewrite is **monotone in
  neither direction**. `{3,4,5,6} → {2,3,4}` is incomparable to its source: it
  adds 2 and drops 5 and 6. Both divergences are reachable on a **bare single
  element particle, no group nesting**:

  | R | B | today's verdict | truth |
  | --- | --- | --- | --- |
  | `e{3,6}` | `e{0,100}` | `derivation-ok-restriction` | valid — **FALSE REJECT** |
  | `e{3,6}` | `e{0,6}` | `derivation-ok-restriction` | valid — **FALSE REJECT** |
  | `e{5,5}` | `e{3,3}` | accept | invalid — fail-open |
  | `e{4,4}` | `e{3,3}` | accept | invalid — fail-open |
  | `e{3,3}` | `e{2,2}` | accept | invalid — fail-open |

  The mechanism, hand-traced and confirmed: R unfolds to `p0..p3` (mandatory
  `p0,p1`), B to `q0,q1` (both skippable, no loop-back since `{0,100}` is
  bounded); at product state `{r:p1, b:{q1}}`, `follow(q1)` is empty,
  `matchPositions(p2, live={})` returns nil, `contentModelRestricts` takes
  `return false // clause 1: R can continue where B cannot`, and
  `checkRestrictionContentType` (`xsd/complexderivation.go:261`) charges
  `derivation-ok-restriction` **against a conforming schema**. Raising the
  constants only moves the two thresholds — at 5/5, `e{3,6}` unfolds exactly but
  `e{0,100}` still collapses to `e{0,5}` and `e{6}` is still false-rejected. The
  real retirement is deciding containment over the declared
  `{min occurs}`/`{max occurs}` rather than over a truncated unfolding.
- **The arbiter did not take the discovery on report.** It re-derived the
  arithmetic from `unfoldCopies` (`xsd/particleattribution.go:383`), hand-traced
  `contentModelRestricts`, and then **reproduced both divergences end-to-end
  through the existing `cRestricts` harness** in a throwaway test — written,
  run, and deleted before the verdict, tree clean. It then **widened the class**
  past mason's two witnesses to the five in the table above; `e{3,6}` under
  `e{0,6}` is the sharpest, since R's range is a literal subset of B's *with the
  same maximum* and it is still rejected. It audited every checkable assertion
  in the new marker (the two rewrites, "monotone in neither direction",
  "reachable on a single element particle", "raising the constants only moves
  the two thresholds", and the green-gate claim) and found no overclaim and no
  underclaim — in particular the marker says outright that the bound *"does NOT
  obey that direction"* and correctly **declines to fold itself into the file's
  fail-open ledger**.
- Decisions made and why:
  1. **Item (2) shipped the true finding, not the literal acceptance-criteria
     language.** Writing "established empirically, fail-open" would have been a
     claim the evidence refutes. The arbiter approved the deviation explicitly
     and on the record, reasoning that the instruction's binding half was *"do
     not write a stronger claim than the evidence supports"* — and that the
     literal wording would have been exactly such a claim. Recorded here so no
     later session reads the diff as non-compliant with the issue body.
  2. **No behavior was changed.** #283 authorized comments only and
     `Ratchet: unchanged` was an acceptance criterion; a fix here would have
     overstepped the issue. The divergence is documented honestly at `:77` with
     concrete repro shapes and left unfixed, and the fix is owed as a separate
     `kind/bug` issue (Next 1).
  3. **No test landed pinning the reproductions.** Correct, and the arbiter said
     so: a test asserting today's behavior on those shapes would **pin a bug**.
     They must land as tests on the fix issue, which is where they will assert
     the corrected verdict.
  4. **The unreachable arms were fixed by POINTING, not restating.** The issue
     was explicit that a third copy of the licence-scoping argument is the wrong
     shape; the arm now defers to the giveup site, matching what the file header
     already does at `:74-76`.
- Surprises:
  1. **A documentation-hardening issue found a conformance-correctness bug.**
     #283 was filed as comment hygiene — a missing `GAP(` token and a marker
     spelling. The substantive half turned into the discovery that this
     processor **rejects valid schemas**. The mechanism that produced it is worth
     naming: the issue told mason to write down an empirical claim, and mason
     went and checked the empirics first. The claim had been carried forward
     three issues deep (#263 observation → #283 body → this session) without
     anyone gathering the evidence, because every gate leg and every ratchet lane
     is green with the bound in place. **That is exactly how the divergence
     stayed latent**: the W3C suite contains no schema of these shapes.
  2. **A false-reject is a different severity class from the gaps this file
     otherwise tracks**, and the arbiter said so directly: a fail-open costs
     conformance score, a false-reject makes valid user schemas unusable and
     **cannot be worked around by the user**. That inverts the usual priority
     ordering for `xsd/contentrestricts.go`'s debt.
  3. **One of item (3)'s two expected edits was already correct.** The second
     arm's comment (`"same unreachable error slot, same direction"`) needed no
     change — the issue body, refreshed against `089b76ae` only hours earlier,
     still over-counted the work by one site.
- Friction:
  1. **The follow-up ledger has now gone EIGHT consecutive sessions without an
     audit.** #272 said three, #273 four, #275 five, #279 six, #282 seven (in
     bold, and noting it was the fourth consecutive entry saying so). **The next
     `/retro` owes the audit, and this is the fifth consecutive entry saying so
     in bold.**
  2. **`xsd/particleattribution.go` now contains two demonstrably FALSE
     comments** — `:59` *"The bound is verdict-preserving, not a heuristic"* and
     `:381-382` *"why the copy count is bounded without changing any verdict"*.
     Both are unqualified; both are disproved by this session's discovery for
     this file's consumer. They are **pre-existing and were NOT touched on this
     branch** — correcting them would widen a comment-only diff across a package
     boundary, the same trade #282's landing refused. The arbiter recorded them
     as advisory 1 and ruled that **the follow-up fix issue must own them**:
     they are the unenumerated-consumer form STYLE P3a exists to forbid, and
     they are the reason the bound survived unexamined this long.
  3. **The two new markers name no owning issue.** Legal — P3's issue
     requirement is conditional on an issue owning the retirement, and none does
     yet — but the `:77` marker documents a live false-reject and **should be
     repointed the moment the follow-up is filed** (arbiter advisory 2).
  4. **The issue body's premise was wrong in a way no reviewer could have caught
     from the body.** It was not sloppy: it faithfully quoted the arbiter's own
     #263 observation, hedged correctly (*"established empirically, not
     proved"*), and told mason not to overclaim. The defect is that an
     unverified empirical claim was propagated as a planning premise across
     three issues. Worth the `/retro`'s attention as a pattern (entry point:
     issue body / cartographer), not just this instance.

  2. **[carried from #282, now DISCHARGED]** the two unreachable
     `contentAutomatonOf` arms' stale framing and the `GAP(xsd) #282:` spelling
     drift — both folded into this touch as items (3) and (4), exactly as that

  3. **#282's Next 3 stands undischarged**: of the three `GAP(xsd)` sites #263's
     entry flagged as naming no open issue, only one has been filed. The other

## 2026-08-05 — #286 — parser: `<xs:redefine>` (§4.2.4) stops being skipped and is carried as DATA through discovery, mirroring `override.go`'s two-producers-meeting-over-a-set architecture; the session's sharpest correctness question was whether src-expredef clause 2's self-reference RESOLUTION inherits src-redefine clause 6.1's `<element>`-ancestor exclusion (it does not — the exclusion is scoped to the COUNTING rule alone, and the arbiter re-derived that from the spec rather than trusting it), and a redefining `<complexType>` is declined ENTIRELY on an architectural ground — `xsd.ComplexType`'s base is a pre-resolution QName that cannot represent an anonymous already-resolved base without a library-surface change; arbiter ACCEPT on round 1, schema lane **9176→9241 (+65)**

- Shipped on `wip/issue-286` as TWO commits on top of `origin/main` @ `4b1bd6a`
  (`xsd: complete contentrestricts.go's fail-open disclosure (#283)`):
  `b1c26d6` (`parser: implement <xs:redefine> composition (#286)`, mason) and
  `148fa89` (`conformance: bank the ratchet for <xs:redefine> composition
  (#286)`, arbiter — the baseline bank). Net diff against `origin/main` is
  **17 files, +1881/−224**, of which `parser/redefine.go` is **+788 new** and
  `parser/redefine_test.go` **+531 new**; the remaining thirteen source files
  are the discovery/producer wiring (`parse.go`, `produce.go`,
  `produce_complex.go`, `override.go`, `doc.go`, `report.go`), the conformance
  decidability narrowing (`schema.go`, `schema_test.go`), the expectations
  bank, and three doc/prose touches (`README.md`, `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md`,
  `parser/parse_test.go` plus three renamed-helper test call sites).
  **Arbiter ACCEPT on round 1, zero rejections, zero repair rounds**, no
  blocking findings and three advisories, all non-blocking and all carried
  below rather than fixed on this branch.
- **Ratchet: schema 9176 → 9241 pass, +65 (arbiter-measured, copied and not
  recomputed)** — **65 upward flips, 0 regressions, 0 vanished cases**, and the
  case-ID set held at **15432**, so the lane WIDENED by rule work, not by a
  decidability predicate admitting new IDs. The expectations diff is 65 changed
  lines, all Fail→pass. `datatypes` and `instance` unmoved. Gate: `go build
  ./...`, `go test ./...`, `go vet ./...` all ok, **`golangci-lint run` clean**,
  `go test ./conformance -run TestConformance -count=1` pass. That is the
  **twelfth consecutive landing with a real gate part 2**.
- **The architecture: two producers meeting over a `redefineSet`, deliberately
  not a new mechanism.** `<xs:redefine>` is carried through discovery as data
  beside the effective namespace — exactly the shape `parser/override.go`
  already established for §F.2 — rather than by pre-transforming the redefined
  document. The redefined document (S2) and the redefining document (S1) are
  both produced; the `redefineSet` is where S2's originals and S1's redefining
  children meet, so a redefining child becomes a top-level definition of the
  REDEFINING document (src-expredef clause 1.2 / clause 2, §4.2.4 clause
  4.1.1) with the original still reachable for the self-reference. No third
  code path, no document rewriting, and the existing chameleon coercion (§F.1)
  applies to a redefined no-`targetNamespace` document without new plumbing.
- **Clauses charged, and the one that is an ERROR where `<include>` is silent.**
  `src-redefine` clause **1** — a non-empty `<redefine>` whose `schemaLocation`
  does not resolve is an **error**, NOT the silent skip §4.2.3 grants
  `<include>`; clause **2**; clause **5** (simpleType/complexType
  self-derivation, checked at both spec depths — a
  `<complexContent>`/`<simpleContent>` child holding the derivation, so the
  implicit-content form states no derivation and fails the clause as it
  should); clauses **6.1.1** and **6.1.2** (group self-reference count and
  `minOccurs`/`maxOccurs` = 1); clause **7.1** (attributeGroup self-reference
  count). `src-expredef`'s closing requirement — a top-level definition of the
  appropriate name and kind must exist in the redefined document — is charged
  at `parser/redefine.go:507`.
- **THE SUBTLE ONE — src-expredef clause 2's resolution does NOT inherit
  clause 6.1's `<element>`-ancestor exclusion.** Self-reference RESOLUTION is
  implemented at three sites: `resolveBase` (`produce.go`),
  `produceGroupRefParticle` (`produce_complex.go`), and
  `collectReferencedGroup`. The question that decided their scope is whether
  the `<element>`-ancestor carve-out — which stops a group self-reference
  buried under an `<element>` from counting toward src-redefine clause 6.1's
  "exactly one" — also stops that reference from RESOLVING to the original.
  It does not: the exclusion is textually scoped to clause 6.1's counting rule
  alone, and clause 2's resolution is unconditional. This was **not taken on
  mason's report** — the arbiter ran its own spec check and confirmed the
  distinction independently, which is the only reason it is recorded here as
  settled rather than as a risk. `TestParseRedefineGroupSelfReferenceUnderElementNotCounted`
  is the executable pin.
- Decisions made and why:
  1. **A redefining `<complexType>` is DECLINED entirely, and the ground is
     architectural, not a shortcut.** src-expredef clause 1.1 pairs the
     redefining type with an ·absent·-`{name}` base that is *already resolved*;
     `xsd.ComplexType`'s base is a **pre-resolution QName**, which cannot
     represent an anonymous already-resolved base at all. Producing one would
     require a library-surface change, so the producer refuses the shape as
     not-yet-produced rather than emitting a wrong component — a decline is a
     limitation, never a verdict. This is the biggest deferral of the session
     and it owns its own follow-up (Next 1), which needs a **warden pre-flight**
     precisely because the fix is a surface change.
  2. **`docKey` gains a `redefine` component — document identity, NOT a cycle
     guard.** The load-once index was already keyed by resolved location, the
     namespace the document was reached under, and the override applied to it;
     `redefine` joins that tuple for the same reason the others are there. Two
     distinct `<redefine>` elements pointing at one document produce two
     genuinely different documents. Recorded explicitly because STYLE §5 forbids
     cycle checks and a reader could misread the widening as one —
     `TestParseRedefineSameDocumentTwiceDiffers` and
     `TestParseRedefineAndIncludeOfOneDocumentCollide` pin both halves.
  3. **`parser/override.go`'s §F.2 clause 1 comment is CORRECTED, not
     extended.** It reasoned that the "or `<redefine>`" arm of clause 1's scope
     was *"empty by construction"* — true only while `<redefine>` was skipped.
     An `<override>` in force over a document now substitutes for that
     document's `<redefine>` children too, restricted to the four element types
     `<redefine>`'s own content model admits. Leaving the old comment would have
     left a false statement in a file this change made false.
  4. **Four fail-open sites, every one marked and every one naming #286.**
     Clause **6.2.2** (group language-containment — needs #263's engine, which
     does not exist yet); clause **7.2.2** (attributeGroup restriction against
     the original); the declined `<complexType>` (Decision 1); and an
     **override-substituted `<redefine>` child's self-reference walk**, which
     loses its self-reference resolution when clause 1 replaces the declaration.
     All four are `GAP(...)` per STYLE P3 with the issue named in prose. The
     repo-wide debt sweep `grep -rn "GAP("` moves **42 → 50 hits across 21
     files** — the largest single-session addition to that ledger so far, and
     every one of the eight is a disclosure this issue could not have avoided
     without landing #263 or a surface change first.
  5. **`schemaShapeDecidable` narrowed rather than widened.** A new
     `redefineDecidable` gates each `<redefine>` child by the same predicate a
     top-level child of any document is gated by, minus `<complexType>`. That
     is why the +65 arrives with the case-ID set **unchanged at 15432**: the
     lane admitted no new IDs, it just started passing cases it already
     counted. Contrast the backlog pass of 2026-08-03, which measured most of
     the schema lane's growth as predicate widening rather than rule work.
  6. **Zero exported identifiers added — no warden pre-flight for this
     commit.** `parser/redefine.go` is entirely unexported; the only published
     surface change is README prose (the `<xs:redefine>` limitation narrows
     from "skipped rather than followed" to "a `complexType` redefinition is
     declined"). The pre-flight debt lands on the follow-up instead, where the
     surface actually moves.
- Surprises:
  1. **The clause-6.1 exclusion very nearly leaked into the resolution path.**
     It is the natural over-generalization — one carve-out, three call sites,
     and no test in the W3C suite that discriminates them — and it would have
     produced a false-reject class no ratchet lane would have shown. It was
     caught by re-reading the spec's scoping rather than by any gate leg.
  2. **`<redefine>`'s clause 1 is stricter than `<include>`'s §4.2.3**, which
     is easy to get backwards from a reading of `<include>` alone. An
     unresolvable non-empty `<redefine>` must error; an unresolvable
     `<include>` is silently skipped. `TestParseRedefineUnresolvableIsAnError`
     exists because the asymmetry is counter-intuitive, not because it was
     hard.
  3. **`override.go`'s "empty by construction" comment was true when written.**
     It is worth naming the class: a comment whose correctness depends on
     ANOTHER feature being unimplemented, with nothing linking the two. No gate
     leg can see it go stale. This is the second consecutive session to land a
     correction of a comment made false elsewhere (#283's Friction 2 named two
     of them in `particleattribution.go`, still uncorrected).
- Friction:
  1. **The follow-up ledger has now gone NINE consecutive sessions without an
     audit.** #272 said three, #273 four, #275 five, #279 six, #282 seven, #283
     eight (in bold, and noting it was the fifth consecutive entry saying so).
     **The next `/retro` owes the audit, and this is the sixth consecutive
     entry saying so in bold.**
  2. **A factually-misleading `src-expredef` error message at
     `parser/redefine.go:507`**, found by the arbiter and NOT fixed on this
     branch. In a double-discovery edge case the message asserts the redefined
     document declares no top-level definition of that name when the real cause
     is the discovery path, not the document. It is **unreachable for valid
     schemas** and is a mirror of an existing GAP note in `parser/doc.go`, so
     the arbiter recorded it and did not charge it. A wrong-but-unreachable
     error string is exactly the shape that survives unexamined; noted so it is
     on the record rather than rediscovered.
  3. **Two cosmetic drifts, both left in place deliberately.**
     `docs/ARCHITECTURE.md:252`'s docKey prose is slightly stale against
     Decision 2 (it names location, namespace and override but not redefine in
     one of its two passes), and `parser/redefine.go:597`
     (`checkRedefinedComplexType`) walks children without skipping
     `<annotation>` subtrees the way `selfReferences` does. Neither changes a
     verdict — the complexType path is declined anyway — and both are one-line
     edits with no natural owner. Candidates to fold into any future touch of
     these files, per the fold-don't-file routing #283 proved out.
- Next:
  1. **[post-land pass, NOT YET FILED — the one hard requirement this session
     leaves open]** The declined redefining `<complexType>` (Decision 1) needs
     its own issue, and it is **not** a small one: it requires a
     `xsd.ComplexType` surface change so a base can be an anonymous

  2. **[carried, new this session]** src-redefine clause 6.2.2's group

  3. **[carried, new this session]** the misleading error message

  4. **[branch namespace, reported and not acted on]** `wip/issue-271` and

  5. **[carried, undischarged — the count of sessions these survive is the

## 2026-08-05 — #287 — parked (needs-replan): §F.2 clause-1 first-match-wins was implemented, is spec-correct, and CANNOT LAND — the issue's own Acceptance predicted "no W3C `Override/*` case is known to exercise a duplicated (element type, name) pair"; `Override/over021` ("Override the same thing twice", `accepted`) does exactly that and pins the CURRENT rejection, so honouring the normative stylesheet moves a recorded `pass` DOWN

- Attempted: `wip/issue-287` @ `6d79251` — **not merged, retired in place**,
  and parked WITHOUT an arbiter round (zero rejections; the blocker is a policy
  question no verdict could have licensed). Issue relabeled `needs-replan` (was
  `ready`); labels now `needs-replan, kind/gap, area/parser`. This entry lands
  separately off `origin/main` @ `90562ff` (`parser: implement <xs:redefine>
  composition (#286)`), since a park leaves the WIP branch untouched.
- **Ratchet: unchanged on `main` — nothing landed.** The branch was measured
  read-only (`GOXSD_RATCHET` **not** set, no expectations file touched) and
  would have moved `schema` **9241 → 9240 pass, −1**: zero improvements, one
  regression, `Override/over021/schema/over021.bad.xsd`, `datatypes` and
  `instance` unmoved. Ratchet integrity clean — the diff never touched
  `conformance/`, and the downward move was detected by the gate rather than
  absorbed by an edit.
- What was attempted: retire the `GAP(xsd)` over-rejection at
  `parser/override.go:129` so two children of ONE `<xs:override>` sharing an
  (element type, name) pair resolve to the FIRST in document order instead of
  being reported under `src-override`. Selected as the oldest `ready` issue with
  closed dependencies (#183, closed). Five files, +176/−41:
  `parser/override.go` (`newOverrideSet` keeps the first child per
  `componentKey`, drops later ones out of `entries` and hence out of both
  `index` and `id`; the `xsderr.New` rejection and the GAP marker deleted; the
  diagnostic survives as a `slog` debug line on the injected logger, STYLE 10;
  the function can no longer fail, so it returns no `error`), `parser/parse.go`
  (`assembly.override` loses the dead error branch), `parser/doc.go` (GAP bullet
  deleted, composition prose states first-match-wins),
  `parser/override_test.go` (`TestParseOverrideDuplicateChildren` **inverted**,
  plus `…MatchNothing` and `…NestedMerge`) and a new
  `parser/override_internal_test.go` (`TestNewOverrideSetShadowedChildIsInert`).
  Gate green on `go build`, `go test` (unit), `go vet` and `golangci-lint run`
  (0 issues); red on conformance only.
- **The branch surveyed first, per WORKFLOW's WIP index.** `wip/issue-256` and
  `wip/issue-271` both still on `origin` with tips from 2026-08-03 — stale by
  the 2h lease, but **both issues are CLOSED** (`needs-replan`, closed directly
  by the human owner), so neither is resumable and both were ignored rather
  than picked up. #287's is now the third branch retired in place awaiting human
  deletion.
- Grounding held up completely and does not need re-asking. The oracle
  confirmed from §F.2's **normative** stylesheet — "the normative description of
  the transformation is given by the stylesheet below; the transformation can
  also be described (non-normatively) in prose" — that clause 1's template
  selects `<xsl:copy-of select="($replacement, $original)[1]"/>` over
  `$overrideElement/*`, a child-axis selection XPath 2.0 returns in document
  order: **FIRST wins**, and the later duplicate is copied nowhere at all rather
  than merely shadowed. §4.2.5's match-nothing rule ("It is not an error for an
  `<override>` element to contain a source declaration which matches nothing in
  the target set, but it will be ignored") applies per-child with no provision
  for duplicates, so where the duplicated pair matches nothing BOTH copies drop
  identically and neither sub-case is an error.
- Decisions:
  - **The issue's open design question is SETTLED — the losing child is
    EXCLUDED from `overrideSet`'s identity string, and hence from `docKey`.**
    Not spec-mandated; the oracle reasoned from §4.2.5's non-normative note
    ("applications are allowed, indeed encouraged, to avoid overriding the same
    schema document in the same way more than once to forestall the necessity of
    establishing identity component by component"), whose notion of override
    identity is output-equivalence (`fn:deep-equal` on the transformation
    result), not literal syntax. Since `[1]` guarantees a losing duplicate
    contributes nothing to *D*old′, two `<override>` elements differing only in
    an inert duplicate are deep-equal-equivalent and must read *D*old ONCE.
    Same treatment as `index`, STYLE D3-consistent, and it moves in the same
    direction the sibling GAP #379 would eventually move — which is what the
    issue body asked be checked. Confidence recorded as medium (the note
    discusses a different literal scenario), flagged "spec-informed, not
    spec-mandated". **This survives the replan** and is the one durable product
    of the session.
  - **The park is the ONE RULE applied, not a judgement call.** CLAUDE.md:
    "Never regress the ratchet… Never edit an expectations file downward to
    make CI green", and that rule "changes only via a human-filed issue".
    An issue's Acceptance section cannot license a downward move, however
    correct its spec reading, and a session that discovers the conflict has no
    authority to resolve it. Implementation checkpointed and pushed, branch
    retired, analysis and recommendation posted to the thread, label moved.
  - **Who is right, spec or suite, is recorded rather than decided.** Under the
    letter of the REC over021 is VALID and the fixture is wrong. The suite's
    reading is reconstructable and not stupid: take clause 1's **prose** ("*O1*
    has a child *E1* … then *D*2′ has a copy of *E1*") distributively and *D*2′
    gets two `<xs:element name="doc">` children of `<xs:schema>`, violating the
    `element` `xs:key` in the schema for schema documents (§A, `<xs:selector
    xpath="xs:element"/>` / `<xs:field xpath="@name"/>`), so *D*old′ is
    non-conforming and `src-override` clause 3.1.1 fails. That is a coherent
    derivation of "invalid" from spec text; it loses to the stylesheet only on
    §F.2's own precedence sentence.
- Surprises:
  1. **This is a spec-vs-fixture disagreement in the OPPOSITE direction from
     every precedent.** `anyURI_a004_1339.i` (#190) and `pdecimal006.n2` both
     moved a recorded `fail` to `pass` — honouring the spec cost nothing the
     ratchet protects. Here honouring the spec moves a recorded `pass` to
     `fail`, which is precisely the movement the one rule exists to forbid. The
     precedents do not transfer, and treating them as if they did would have
     been the easy mistake.
  2. **The issue predicted the conformance answer and predicted it wrong, in
     writing.** Acceptance said "Ratchet: expected **unchanged**… Movement, if
     any, can only be upward (this only *stops* rejecting)", and Notes said "no
     conformance case is affected". A GAP marker that describes a rejection as
     a costless over-rejection is not the same claim as one that has been
     checked against the suite; nobody had run the check until mason did, at the
     end of a complete implementation.
  3. **`over019.xsd` is byte-for-byte `over021.bad.xsd` minus the second
     child**, and is declared valid. The duplicated pair is the suite's *sole*
     differentiator, so the fixture is not incidentally about this case — it
     exists to pin exactly it.
- Friction:
  1. **The whole implementation cost was spent before the cheapest possible
     check ran.** Grounding, design settlement, five files, four tests and a
     mutation check all preceded `go test ./conformance` — yet the issue itself
     named the check ("check and report whether one does") as an Acceptance
     bullet. A one-command fixture grep against `testdata/xsdtests` for the
     construct under change is available at issue-triage time and would have
     re-scoped #287 before a single line was written. **This is the process
     finding of the session and belongs to the cartographer's entry point, not
     to mason's.**
  2. **The follow-up ledger has now gone TEN consecutive sessions without an
     audit.** #272 said three, #273 four, #275 five, #279 six, #282 seven, #283
     eight, #286 nine (in bold, noting it was the sixth consecutive entry saying
