## 2026-08-05 — #290 — conformance: the #190 anyURI cohort's two over-promising doc comments are brought down to what the code checks, the conflict check NARROWED in words rather than widened in code — `collectAnyURIElems` compares `type=` ATTRIBUTE STRINGS, so two same-named declarations both carrying an inline `complexType` have `Type == ""`, compare equal, and the second silently overwrites the first; the comment that called this "a verified fact rather than an assumption" now names the residual last-wins hole and records that `anyURIShapeCase` is a FAMILY regexp admitting new fixtures with no review gate — a warning the arbiter verified is not hypothetical, since a003/a005…a016 schemas are already sitting in that directory with no matching instance; comments only, `go doc -all ./conformance` byte-identical, arbiter ACCEPT on round 1, ratchet unchanged

- Shipped on `wip/issue-290` as ONE commit on top of `origin/main` @ `7c39ee5`
  (`docs/log: 2026-08-05 session — #287 parked, ratchet conflict with over021`):
  `61e94ff` (`conformance: narrow anyURI-cohort doc claims to what the code
  checks (#290)`, mason). Net diff against `origin/main` is **1 file,
  +25/−6, `conformance/datatypes.go` alone**. Comments only — no check widened,
  no validation loop touched, no field added, no test touched, no expectations
  file touched. **Arbiter ACCEPT on round 1, zero rejections, zero repair
  rounds**, no blocking findings and two advisories, both explicitly marked
  "do not repair" and carried below.
- **Ratchet: unchanged (proved twice, arbiter's figures copied and not
  recomputed).** (1) `git diff origin/main...HEAD -U0` filtered to changed lines
  that are NOT `//` comment lines yields **0** — every one of the 25 insertions
  and 6 deletions is a comment line, so the diff cannot move a verdict by
  construction. (2) No file under `conformance/testdata/expectations/` appears in
  the diff (`--name-only` grep for `expectations`: 0 hits). Gate: `go build
  ./...`, `go test ./...`, `go vet ./...` all ok, **`golangci-lint run` →
  0 issues**, `go test ./conformance -run TestConformance -count=1` → ok 25.1s,
  `gofmt -l conformance/` clean, `git status --porcelain` empty. That is the
  **thirteenth consecutive landing with a real gate part 2**.
  `TestDatatypesAnyURIShapeCohort` and its `specValid` pins (including a004's
  deliberate `false`) are untouched — the test file is not in the diff.
- **The arbiter deliberately did NOT run `GOXSD_RATCHET=1`, and said why.** The
  issue's Acceptance says "unchanged"; with a comments-only diff there is no
  upward movement to bank, so a write run could only have banked drift belonging
  to something other than this change. The read-only `TestConformance` pass plus
  the two structural checks above are conclusive without it. Recorded here
  because it is the right default for any comments-only landing and the
  reasoning should not have to be re-derived next time.
- **Surface: `go doc -all ./conformance` byte-identical**, diffed by the arbiter
  between a clean `origin/main` worktree and the branch rather than taken on
  report. Both edited doc comments attach to unexported identifiers
  (`indexAnyURIElements`, `collectAnyURIElems`), and the cohort lane doc block is
  a free-floating comment separated from `synthNS`'s doc by a blank line, so it
  is not package doc and never surfaces. The #190 arbiter's byte-identical-godoc
  property therefore holds across this landing as the issue's Surface section
  required.
- The three acceptance items, all landed:
  1. **Defect 1 — the overclaim is gone.** "the check makes that a verified fact
     rather than an assumption" is deleted; the claim is now scoped to
     declarations that "name DIFFERENT types in their `type=` attribute". The
     arbiter verified the narrowed words against the code:
     `anyURIElemDecl.Type` is `xml:"type,attr"` and the check is
     `prior.Type != el.Type` — a string comparison of the `type=` attribute,
     exactly as the words now say.
  2. **Residual condition + family-pattern selector stated.** The residual is
     spelled out concretely (both-inline-`complexType` ⇒ `Type == ""` ⇒ compare
     equal ⇒ second silently overwrites, last-wins, unflagged), and
     `collectAnyURIElems` carries the addendum at the comparison site itself.
  3. **Defect 2 — content-model blindness stated ONCE in the lane doc**, noting
     it is shared with `execFacetsCase`/`execD34Case` rather than specific to
     this cohort.
- Decisions made and why:
  1. **NARROW the words, not widen the check.** Comparing decoded inline
     `complexType` shapes is real structural-equality complexity for a case no
     current fixture exercises; CLAUDE.md's "don't add validation for scenarios
     nothing exercises" applies directly and the issue itself declared either
     resolution acceptable. What makes the narrowing honest rather than a
     dodge is that the comment now records the TRIGGER for revisiting: the
     selector is a family pattern, so a future fixture can reach the hole
     without anyone deciding to widen the cohort, and widening the comparison is
     the fix at that point.
  2. **The Defect-2 sentence was attached to the existing leaf-AND paragraph,
     not given a standalone subsection** — mason flagged this as an open
     question and the arbiter ruled it the BETTER reading of "stated once in the
     lane doc", not a shortfall: the limitation is a property of that model and
     belongs in the same breath as the sentence that names it. A subsection
     would have divorced the caveat from the claim it qualifies.
  3. **No back-references at `execFacetsCase`/`execD34Case`.** The criterion
     says "stated **once**"; cross-notes at those two sites would be three
     statements of one fact, which STYLE D3's spirit and P2 both push against.
  4. **No `GAP(conformance)` marker for either hole.** There is no
     `GAP(conformance)` anywhere in the module — markers exist only for
     `xsd`/`value`/`parser`/`xml`/`xpath` — because P3's greppable debt sweep
     tracks PROCESSOR incompleteness that could produce a wrong verdict for a
     user, not harness reader shortcuts. The arbiter considered and dismissed
     this explicitly; recorded so the question is not reopened from scratch.
  5. **No oracle round.** Spec `n/a` by the issue's own text — this is harness
     doc accuracy, verified directly against `conformance/datatypes.go`, and
     the cohort's `cvc-enumeration-valid` / `anyURI-lexical-representation`
     anchors are untouched.
- Surprises:
  1. **The family-regexp warning is not hypothetical, and the arbiter proved
     it.** `msData/datatypes/Facets/anyURI/` currently holds 22 `.xml` files of
     which exactly 8 match `anyURI_[ab][0-9]+\.xml$` — but it ALSO holds
     `anyURI_a003.xsd`, `a005.xsd`…`a016.xsd` with no matching instance. The
     schemas for fixtures that would silently join this cohort are already on
     disk; only the instance documents are missing. The comment's warning is
     therefore about a door that is ajar, not one that might be built.
  2. **A doc-accuracy fix got its new prose fact-checked, not just its deletions
     audited.** The arbiter independently re-derived the `maxOccurs` figures
     (100 for b001/b002/b004/b005, 1000 for a004, unbounded for b006, absent for
     a001/a002) and the tested-leaf counts (3 for b001/b002/b005; 6 `<bar>` + 1
     attribute = 7 for a004; 7 for b004; 26 for b006) rather than trusting the
     issue body — the right standard for an issue whose entire subject is
     comments claiming more than the code checks.
- Friction:
  1. **The issue body's line numbers were stale by ~500 lines** (`:3014-3020`,
     `:3037`; the real sites are `:3511-3550` and `:3554`), and the issue
     ITSELF flagged that the #190 LOG entry it inherited was stale by ~2400

     if anyone wants it: cite identifiers, never line numbers, in issue bodies

  2. **The follow-up ledger has now gone ELEVEN consecutive sessions without an

## 2026-08-05 — #294 — parser: the five absent-name / empty-ref rejections #202 minted stop being pinned by a one-off arbiter hand-run and become CI, one table-driven test driving each shape from a schema DOCUMENT end to end — and the issue's own Acceptance turned out to be WRONG about the suite, which the commit body corrects on the record rather than repeating: two W3C cases (`MS-Element elemA012`, `MS-Attribute attE007`) already reach two of the five leaves and are already recorded `pass`, so "no W3C suite case reaches these leaves" was inaccurate in a way that strengthens rather than weakens the case for the tests; arbiter ACCEPT on round 1, ratchet unchanged, proved by a clean tree after a `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` write run

- Shipped on `wip/issue-294` as ONE commit on top of `origin/main` @ `cebe9f2`
  (`conformance: narrow anyURI-cohort doc claims to what the code checks
  (#290)`): `50a0f89` (`parser: pin the five absent-name / empty-ref rejections
  end to end (#294)`, mason). Net diff against `origin/main` is **1 file,
  +108/−0, `parser/produce_absentname_test.go` alone** — the arbiter's
  `--numstat` reads exactly `108 0 parser/produce_absentname_test.go`. Test
  only: no production file, no `conformance/testdata/expectations/*` file, no
  generated table. **Arbiter ACCEPT on round 1, zero rejections, zero repair
  rounds**, no blocking findings and no advisories.
- **Ratchet: unchanged (arbiter's figures copied, not recomputed).** The
  arbiter ran `GOXSD_RATCHET=1 go test ./conformance -run TestConformance
  -count=1` **despite** test-only scope and found the tree still clean —
  **zero expectation lines rewritten, in either direction**. The reasoning
  recorded on the verdict is worth keeping: a clean tree after a WRITE run is
  the only *proof* nothing flipped, where reasoning about scope is only an
  argument. (Note this is the opposite call from #290's, and both were right:
  #290 was comments-only against production code where a write run could bank
  drift belonging to something else; here the diff is a single new `_test.go`
  that the conformance lanes never load, so a write run has nothing to
  contaminate and everything to prove.) Gate: `go build ./...`, `go test
  ./...` (all packages, `conformance` 26.8s), `go vet ./...` all ok,
  **`golangci-lint run` → `0 issues.`**, `go test ./conformance -run
  TestConformance -count=1` ok, `git status --porcelain` empty before and
  after every run. That is the **fourteenth consecutive landing with a real
  gate part 2**.
- **Surface: unchanged, and unchangeable by construction** — the only changed
  file is a `_test.go` in the external `parser_test` package, which cannot
  contribute to any package's godoc. Matches the issue's `## Surface: none —
  test-only`.
- What landed: `TestProduceAbsentNameAndEmptyRefRejected`, one table-driven
  test with five subtests, one per shape — nameless global `<element>`
  (`produce.go` `produceElement`), nameless local `<element>`
  (`produce_complex.go` `produceLocalElement`), `<group ref="">`
  (`ModelGroupRef`), `<element ref="">` (`ElementDeclarationRef`) and
  `<attribute ref="">` (`AttributeDeclarationRef`). Each asserts three things
  and only three: the document is **rejected**, `xsderr.RuleOf` reports the
  expected rule, and the error is positioned at `URI:line:col` on the
  offending element's own line (E3). It reuses `produce`, `wrap`, `assertRule`
  and `produceURI` from `produce_test.go` rather than minting parallel
  helpers, and takes its own file rather than growing the 65KB
  `produce_test.go`.
- Grounding (oracle, before implementation): two questions, both answered
  cleanly. (1) **e-props-correct clause 1 is not local-only.** §3.3.1's
  tableau types `{name}` as a Required `xs:NCName` with no variety carve-out,
  and §3.3.2.1 `dcl.elt.common` maps it identically for both `{scope}`
  varieties *before* the global/local supplements (§3.3.2.2 / §3.3.2.3) apply
  — so the same check fires on both paths, and the test names say so.
  (2) **`RuleComponentInvariant` is a genuine non-spec sentinel**:
  `component-invariant` appears nowhere in `docs/specs/md/`, and the five
  clauses it deliberately does not borrow are all real and all distinct —
  `au-props-correct` §3.5.6, `p-props-correct` §3.9.6.1, `ref.att.local`
  §3.2.2.3 and `ref.elt.global` §3.3.2.4 (mapping-rule sections, not fireable
  constraints), `src-resolve` §3.17.6.2 (which *presupposes* a non-absent
  QName rather than covering an absent one). None states a "ref must not be
  empty" clause, so borrowing any of them would be a fabricated verdict (E2).
  Posted as a GROUNDING comment on the thread.
- Decisions made and why:
  1. **Pin behavior, never message text.** Three of the five messages are
     under active revision by **#296** (the message-quality follow-up filed
     alongside this issue: `§3.8.2` should be `§3.7.2`, and internal Go type
     names leak to schema authors). Asserting wording here would have made
     #296 break CI for improving a message — the exact coupling that turns
     diagnostics into frozen strings. The three assertions chosen are the ones
     #296 cannot invalidate.
  2. **The rejection stays in the `xsd` constructor, not in `resolveQName`**
     — one fact, one encoding (D3/D4). `resolveQName` maps `ref=""` to a zero
     QName without error, so what these tests pin is that the parser
     **propagates** the constructor's verdict positioned on the offending
     element; they do not ask the parser to re-check the same fact. The issue
     asked for this to be stated in one line in the test file rather than
     re-litigated later, and it is (P2).
  3. **Cases are a slice, not a map**, with the reason in a comment: subtest
     order is output (D2).
  4. **The five shapes were re-derived from source, not transcribed from the
     probe trace.** The issue's verbatim trace showed two `e-props-correct`
     lines at the same position and the oracle flagged (at medium confidence)
     that it could not tell from spec text alone whether that was one shape
     mapping twice or two distinct call sites. It is two call sites —
     `produceElement` and `produceLocalElement` — which is why there are two
     `<element>` cases and not one.
  5. **`src-element` clause 2.1 (name-XOR-ref) is deliberately NOT asserted**
     on the nameless-local case; the oracle called it out as a separate
     representation-level constraint, and the test comment says so to stop the
     two being conflated later.
- Surprises:
  1. **The issue's Acceptance was factually wrong about the suite, and the
     mistake was found by doing the work rather than by reading the issue.**
     "no W3C suite case reaches these leaves" is false: `MS-Element
     elemA012` (`<xsd:element name=""/>`, charged `e-props-correct`) sits at
     `conformance/testdata/expectations/schema.txt:4352` and `MS-Attribute
     attE007` (`<xsd:attribute ref=""/>`, charged `component-invariant`) at
     `:1173`, **both already `pass`**. The arbiter verified both rows himself
     rather than taking the correction on report. No lane moves — both were
     passing before this branch — and the correction *strengthens* the case
     for the file, since the suite reaches two leaves incidentally and none of
     the other three: `<group ref="">` and `<element ref="">` are covered by
     this test and nothing else. Recording the correction in the commit body
     instead of quietly landing against stale text is the behavior the arbiter
     said he wants to see.
  2. **The arbiter re-verified failure-capability independently instead of
     trusting mason's report** — a detached worktree at `50a0f89` outside the
     repo tree, all four #202 guards disabled by flipping the condition to a
     never-matching literal (so compilation stays honest), all five subtests
     failing with `got nil`. `got nil` is the right failure mode: without the
     guards each shape is *accepted*, which is exactly the false-accept the
     file fences.
  3. **He then went one step further and mutated the test.** A test that pins
     a line number can pass by luck if every candidate line is the same, so he
     restored the guards and changed one case's `wantLine` from 3 to 2; it
     failed as it should (`position = mem://produce.xsd:3:1, want the
     offending element at mem://produce.xsd:2 with a column`). The layout
     earns the assertion: cases 2–5 put the container on line 2 and the
     offending element on line 3, so an implementation charging the
     *container's* position would fail. **Verifying that a new test's
     assertions discriminate — not merely that the test currently fails
     without the fix — is the stronger standard, and it is cheap.**
- Friction:
  1. **A stale premise in an Acceptance section survived from 2026-07-28 to
     today unchallenged**, and would have shipped as a false sentence in the
     commit body if mason had taken the issue at its word. This is the second

     conformance fixtures — #287 was parked outright because its Acceptance
     predicted no `Override/*` case exercised a duplicated pair and `over021`
     does. **Two sessions, same wall: issue bodies assert what the W3C suite
     does or does not cover, from memory, with no grep at filing time.** That

     mason who trips over it. #287's Next already carries "the

     the audit, and this is the ninth consecutive entry saying so in bold.**
     This session adds nothing new to the pile (no advisories) but does not
     shrink it either.
- Next:
  1. **[the coupling #296 must know about]** **#296** — the message-quality
     pass on these same rejections — is now downstream of a test file that
     deliberately does NOT assert message text. Whoever takes #296 should read

  2. **[the sibling #202 gap, still open]** **#295** ("the sibling always-named
     M4 constructors still accept a zero `{name}` — finish what #202 did for
     `NewElementDeclaration`") was filed alongside #294 and #296 on 2026-07-28
     and remains open and untouched. This session closes the *coverage* half of
     the #202 aftermath; #295 is the *completeness* half, and landing it will
     mint more shapes that want the same end-to-end treatment this file gives.
  3. **[carried, undischarged]** everything on #290's Next — the two #290
     arbiter advisories (the "decides LEXICAL validity only" understatement and

     `needs-replan`, still needing a human or cartographer decision), the #379

     `wip/` branches retired in place on `origin` awaiting human deletion, and

- Thread record (do not duplicate) —
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/294>: **GROUNDING** at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/294#issuecomment-5191761419>
  (e-props-correct clause 1 applies to both `{scope}` varieties via
  §3.3.2.1 `dcl.elt.common`; `RuleComponentInvariant` confirmed non-spec and
  the five near-miss clause IDs confirmed real, distinct and inapplicable);
  **ARBITER VERDICT** at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/294#issuecomment-5191886492>
  (`accept` / ratchet `unchanged` / no blocking findings, the clean-tree
  `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` proof, the independent detached-worktree failure-capability

  STYLE pass across S1/S2/D1/D2/E2/E3/T4/T5/P2, and the verification of both
  corrected `schema.txt` rows).

## 2026-08-05 — #287 — parser: the duplicate-`<xs:override>`-children rejection is KEPT and its `GAP(xsd)` marker re-grounded on W3C Bugzilla 17617 — the 2026-08-04 park asked whether spec or fixture was right and the answer turned out to be neither-as-framed: the test's own author conceded the strict reading, the WG resolved an erratum was needed to make it an error, and that erratum was never filed

- Shipped on `wip/issue-287-doc` (branched off `de58a9b`; `wip/issue-287` holds
  the retired implementation and was NOT resumed, per CLAUDE.md's
  "abandoned attempts are retired in place, never resumed"). Comments only:
  `parser/override.go` (the `GAP(xsd)` block in `newOverrideSet` plus the
  `xsderr` message text), `parser/doc.go` (the composition-gaps bullet),
  `parser/override_test.go` (`TestParseOverrideDuplicateChildren`'s doc
  comment). No behaviour change, no exported-surface change.
- **Ratchet: unchanged.** `Override/over021/schema/over021.bad.xsd` stays
  `pass`; the rejection this issue was filed to retire is deliberately left
  standing.
- **The park's open question is CLOSED by external evidence, not by a ruling.**
  The 2026-08-04 park framed this as spec-vs-fixture with no authority in the
  session to choose. The missing fact was upstream: **W3C Bugzilla 17617**,
  "should 'over021.bad.xsd' be valid?", filed 2012-06-27 by Altova asking
  exactly which clause makes it invalid. Michael Kay — who WROTE the test —
  conceded on 2012-06-29 that on a strict reading it is not an error, argued
  from the explicitly NON-normative prose that it ought to be, and concluded
  "we should make this an error". David Ezell recorded the same day: "On
  discussion the WG believes that an erratum is needed to make this situation
  an error."
- **The erratum was never filed, approved or integrated — verified, not
  inferred.** The Part 1 errata document says "None integrated into this
  document to date" and points at Bugzilla as the authority for
  approved-but-unintegrated changes. The complete archive for product
  `XML Schema` — 1164 bugs across all six components, ids 1849–30262, i.e.
  through decommissioning in 2018 — was downloaded and parsed directly rather
  than read through a summarizer. Every override-related Structures bug is
  accounted for; the last touching the transformation prose is 14416
  (2011-10-10, CLOSED FIXED), which PREDATES 17617. Only nine Structures bugs
  were opened at all after 2012-06-27 and none concerns this. A product-wide
  search for `override` + `twice|duplicate|same|repeat` returns zero rows.
- **So the disagreement is not spec-vs-fixture, it is published-REC-vs-recorded-WG-intent.**
  goxsd8 implements the intent. That is a defensible target and it is what the
  suite scores, but it had been documented as the opposite: the marker
  #183 landed called the rejection "a deliberate over-rejection of
  spec-defined behaviour" and a "deliberate simplification", which is why a
  later session read it as free to retire and spent a whole session
  discovering it was not. The marker now names 17617, the WG resolution, the
  never-filed erratum, and `over021`'s dependence on all three.
- Surprise worth keeping: **`w3c/xsdtests` has a first-class vocabulary for
  exactly this** — `common/xsts.xsd` enumerates `queried`, `disputed-test`,
  `disputed-spec` alongside `accepted`, plus a `bugzilla` attribute, and it is
  used elsewhere (`nistMeta/…:39430`). `over021` carries plain `accepted` with
  no bug link. The suite's own git history is a dead end by construction: the
  fixture arrives in a single 2018 bulk import (`74c70275`, "Add Saxonica
  tests", Michael Kay), eight years after its `date="2010-05-05"` metadata.
- Friction: the W3C Bugzilla archive silently DROPS all but one `component`
  parameter and ignores `bug_status` filters entirely, so a filtered query
  returns a full single-component list that looks like a filtered result. Two
  earlier read-throughs were wrong because of it. Downloading the HTML and
  parsing it locally is the only reliable way to make a negative claim
  ("no such bug exists") against that archive.
- Next: (1) the upstream fix is `status="disputed-spec"` +
  `bugzilla=…17617` on `over021` in `w3c/xsdtests`, not deleting the test —
  unfiled, needs a human to open it; (2) the declared-divergence
  expectations status floated at park time is still worth building, but this
  case is NO LONGER its motivating example and should not be cited as one;
  (3) #379 is unaffected — `over022` is invalid under both readings.

## 2026-08-05 — backlog — **`/backlog` step 4 ran for the first time in the project's history**, which decides #416 by executing option (a) rather than by ruling on it: the orchestrating session ran libuser and cliuser itself and handed their reports to the cartographer, producing #513 (libuser) and #514 (cliuser) plus a "not a defect" verdict on a question open across two API-changing days; the pass's other finding is a **near-miss on the project's durable memory** — the GitHub MCP tool strips `<...>` tokens on read and replaces the whole body on write, so this pass silently damaged #492's body before noticing and filed **#515**

- Doc-only reconciliation, **no commit of my own** — four files modified in the
  working tree and left uncommitted for the orchestrating session:
  `docs/PLAN.md` (**+254**, a pure append into M4 ahead of the M5 heading),
  `docs/WORKFLOW.md`, `.claude/agents/cartographer.md`,
  `.claude/commands/backlog.md` (**276 insertions / 11 deletions** across the
  four). Branched from `origin/main` @ `6b99b2f` (#287's doc-only resolution).
  **No Go code changed, no gate run, no `GOXSD_RATCHET=1`.** The orchestrator
  lands this entry and the four files as the single `meta: backlog 2026-08-05`
  commit, PR + squash-merge, **with `Closes #416` in the body** — #416 is
  deliberately still open here, because its fix is not on `main` yet.
- **Ratchet: unchanged** (doc-only trigger). Figures read off
  `conformance/testdata/expectations/*.txt` at `6b99b2f`; **read values, not a
  measurement** — a live run is the arbiter's job and not this trigger's.

  | lane | pass / total | since the 2026-08-04 backlog |
  |---|---|---|
  | `schema` | 9241 / 15432 | 9170 → 9241, **+71** |
  | `datatypes` | 1131 / 1153 | unchanged |
  | `instance` | 0 / 26426 | unchanged |
  | `xpath` / `json` / `ber` | empty by design | unchanged |

  **The +71 is real processor movement, not lane widening** — #286
  (`<xs:redefine>` composition), #279 (QName resolution gated on the containing
  document's imports), #294 (the five absent-name / empty-ref rejections). The
  mirror image of 2026-08-04's `+4` (engineering, no movement) and
  2026-08-03's `+4919` (movement, mostly widening).
- **#416 is decided — option (a) — and the decision is a transcript, not a
  ruling.** Seven consecutive passes recorded that a cartographer subagent has
  no tool with which to spawn a persona. This pass ran the personas from the
  orchestrating session and handed the cartographer their output, which *is*
  option (a). Three files carry it and **exactly one states the rule** (#195's
  precedent: an under-settled scope that propagated into four documents is what
  parked #195): `docs/WORKFLOW.md`'s `/backlog` bullet states it;
  `.claude/agents/cartographer.md` step 4 states the receiving half only (fold
  what you are handed, **never role-play a persona yourself**, say so when
  handed nothing); `.claude/commands/backlog.md` assigns the delegation to the
  session that reads it. **Both `.claude/` diffs are agent configuration and
  are flagged for human review**; neither touches CLAUDE.md's one rule nor the
  arbiter's ratchet-integrity section.
- **The third file is the whole lesson.** #416's own Acceptance named two files.
  `.claude/commands/backlog.md` — the trigger prompt, the most load-bearing
  statement of the rule — still said *"have the cartographer consult libuser
  and cliuser"*, and editing only the two named files **would have re-created
  the defect on the next `/backlog` run**. It was found by
  `grep -rn "libuser\|cliuser" --include=*.md .` and by nothing else. #512 now
  carries that grep forward as an instruction to whoever fixes `/story`.
- **What step 4 produced, now that it runs.** libuser (README + `go doc -all`
  only) answered the `Finalize`/`FinalizeWith` coherence question #416 had been
  carrying: **coherent as documented, not a defect** — a persona result, not a
  null — with one discoverability story filed as **#513**; corroborated **#492**
  independently and sharpened its framing (`Parse` is documented as
  *"ParseReport without the report"*, so `ParseReport` is the primitive); and
  **declined** to judge #407's annotation removal because #407 has not landed
  and the surface still exists, leaving its criterion on #407's thread instead
  of fabricating a verdict. cliuser (README + built binary `-help` only)
  **disbelieved its own briefing** — which wrongly said #472 had landed —
  tested the binary, confirmed the current stub is correct-for-unimplemented,
  and filed **#514**: `run` never inspects `args[0]`, so `goxsd8 frobnicate`
  and `goxsd8 validate` are byte-identical to the user.
- **#515 — the pass's real hazard, found by causing it.** The GitHub MCP read
  path returns issue bodies with `<...>`-shaped tokens **deleted** (quotes come
  back merely escaped, which is visible; tag shapes come back gone, which is
  not), and the write path replaces the **whole body** with no append and no
  diff. Read-modify-write therefore destroys every XML element name in a body —
  on a repo whose bodies are made of `<xs:redefine>`, `<simpleType>`,
  `<element ref=…>`. This pass updated **#492**'s body before noticing and
  restored six tokens by inference from surrounding prose; #472, #16, #407,
  #396 and #347 were consequently amended **by comment only**, because #472's
  Notes carry a bare autolink that comes back as `()` and is
  **unrecoverable** — the token-stripping deletes the target with no trace.
  `gh` is 403-blocked on this session type, so there is no lossless fallback
  channel today. **Until #515 lands, amend bodies by comment.**
- **#347 was re-examined and deliberately NOT decided.** Its own body requires
  the shape be settled on its thread first, and one pass's opinion is not a
  settlement. What did change is that the standing reason for holding it —
  "the fix edits an agent definition and so needs a human filing" — is now
  demonstrably wrong, since #416 was decided and its agent definition edited on
  the orchestrator's direction this pass. The thread carries the new evidence
  plus a concrete recommendation (**option (b)** — retire the numeric band,
  formalize the ordering — plus widening `blocked` to *"waiting on a named
  dependency, issue **or** trigger"*) so the next pass can ratify rather than
  re-derive.
- **Queue arithmetic: 128 open (115 `ready`, 12 `blocked`, 1 unlabelled
  #411)**, against 120 (108/11/1) on 2026-08-04. Seven landings closed seven
  `ready`; their post-land harvests filed eight and this pass filed four
  (#512/#513/#514/#515). **Harvest ratio ≈1.7 this window against ≈2.6 last** —
  materially lower, and the queue still grew by seven. **Eighth consecutive
  overrun of the 8–10 band**, and the twelfth consecutive landing that ignored
  the published band.
- **Nothing closed as stale, obsolete or duplicate — fourth consecutive
  confirmation.** Four near-miss pairs were checked rather than assumed and
  each is genuinely distinct: **#472/#514**, **#398/#514** (#398 is fenced as
  changing no behaviour; #514 changes behaviour), **#396/#513**, **#512/#484**.
- **Branch namespace: three refs, all retired in place, nothing under
  `parked/`.** `wip/issue-256` (`c56d9f7`, #256 closed `needs-replan`, two
  commits genuinely not in `main` — the correct outcome for a park),
  `wip/issue-271` (`c1c3824`, zero commits of its own), `wip/issue-287`
  (`6d79251`, the retired implementation attempt — #287 landed instead as a
  doc-only resolution on `wip/issue-287-doc`). All three correspond to **closed**
  issues; nothing new to reconcile. **Listed for human deletion**, as they have
  been for four passes. Sessions never delete or rename refs.
- Decisions made and why:
  1. **#416 was implemented, not merely recommended.** Seven passes of "carry
     it" produced nothing; one pass of "just run it" produced two issues, one
     answered question and three file edits. The write-up follows the act.
  2. **`.claude/commands/backlog.md` was edited even though #416 did not name
     it.** A rule stated everywhere except the prompt that fires the trigger is
     not stated at all.
  3. **Bodies stopped being edited mid-pass.** After #492's damage the pass
     switched to comment-only amendments rather than finishing the planned
     body edits. Losing an amendment is recoverable; losing an autolink target
     is not.
  4. **#347 was left undecided on purpose** — see above. Recommending is not
     ratifying.
  5. **#472's milestone is M3 and should be M4**, by the rule applied to #471
     on 2026-08-04. **Not corrected**: this session's tooling exposes no
     milestone listing, so the numeric id could only be guessed, and a wrong
     milestone is worse than a stale one.
- Surprises:
  1. **A persona caught the orchestrator lying to it.** cliuser was briefed
     that #472 had landed. It had not. cliuser tested the binary anyway and
     re-derived the actual contract. The isolation that makes a persona useful
     also makes it the only reader in the loop with no reason to trust the
     briefing.
  2. **The cross-session channel is lossy in the one direction nobody checks.**
     CLAUDE.md calls GitHub issues *the* cross-session channel; the tool this
     session type must use to write them silently deletes their content. Caught
     by damage, self-repaired, and filed — but it is a genuine near-miss on the
     project's durable memory.
  3. **#396's Acceptance item 5 cannot be met as written** — it requires the
     tree-wide grep to show "no marker naming no issue", and **seven** markers
     name no issue while a filed issue owns each. The GAP ledger moved 43/21 →
     **47 hits / 20 files**, decomposed exactly (`redefine.go` +4 from #286,
     `contentrestricts.go` 4→6 from #283, two retirements verified rather than
     assumed). Recommendation posted on #396: widen the diff, don't narrow the
     postcondition. **This is #510's class, found again within hours of #510.**
  4. **Four `GAP(` markers name no issue and correctly should not** — they
     argue at the site that they are permanent licensed approximations. STYLE
     P3 has no vocabulary for that category; **#499** is the issue that must
     settle it, and its ruling becomes the precedent for the other three.
- Friction:
  1. **The issue-body channel (see #515)** — one damaged body, six tokens
     restored by inference, five planned body edits downgraded to comments, and
     no lossless fallback available. This is the pass's largest cost and the
     only one that risked permanent loss.
  2. **No milestone listing in this session's tooling** blocked a known-correct
     one-field fix on #472 for the second pass running.
  3. **The follow-up-ledger audit is now THIRTEEN consecutive sessions
     overdue** — the tenth consecutive entry to say so in bold. This pass adds
     four filings and one recommendation to the pile and discharges none of it.
- Next:
  1. **The landing PR must carry `Closes #416`** — the decision is only real
     once the three files are on `main`.
  2. **#515 first among the new four.** Every trigger that edits a body is
     exposed until it lands, and the interim rule (amend by comment) lives
     nowhere durable yet.
  3. **#512** carries `/story`'s identical persona-ownership defect plus the
     unowned *"is the published contract **current**?"* check — the latter is a
     landing-time mechanism question, not an interview one, and #484 is the
     adjacent candidate home.
  4. **#347 is ready to ratify, not re-derive** — the thread now carries the
     evidence and a named option (b).
  5. **#472's milestone** awaits a session whose GitHub tooling can list
     milestones.
  6. **Carried, undischarged**: everything on #294's and #287's Next lines —
     the cartographer-side pre-implementation fixture grep (now three data
     points), the #287 upstream `disputed-spec` filing against `w3c/xsdtests`,
     the six-way `*Schema` decoder duplication filed for the steward, #295, the
     #379 identity-decision record, and the three `wip/` refs awaiting human
     deletion.
- Thread record (do not duplicate) — filings at
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/512>,
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/513>,
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/514> and
  <https://github.com/kud360/goxsd8/issues/515>; **#492 amended in its body**
  (and spot-checked against the six restored tokens — #515 item 4 asks a later

## 2026-08-05 — #295 — xsd: the five sibling always-named M4 constructors stop accepting a zero `{name}`, each citing its OWN `*-props-correct` clause 1 rather than a borrowed rule or `RuleComponentInvariant`, finishing what #202 did for `NewElementDeclaration` alone; the issue's own Acceptance predicted `Ratchet: unchanged` and it was **wrong in the good direction** — the arbiter measured **schema 9241 → 9251 (+10)**, ten W3C cases the non-ratchet harness is structurally silent about, every one attributed to a named construct; arbiter ACCEPT on round 1

- Shipped on `wip/issue-295` as TWO commits on top of `origin/main` @ `bfdb920`
  (`meta: backlog 2026-08-05`): `cdfa832` (`xsd: reject empty {name} in five M4
  constructors (#295)`, mason — 10 files, +323/−45) and `5b48915`
  (`conformance: ratchet the schema lane for the five empty-{name} rejections
  (#295)`, **the arbiter's own machine-written ratchet**, 1 file, +10/−10).
  Branch total against `origin/main`: **11 files, +333/−55** —
  `xsd/attributedeclaration.go`, `xsd/attributegroupdefinition.go`,
  `xsd/identityconstraint.go`, `xsd/modelgroupdefinition.go`,
  `xsd/notation.go`, the five matching `_test.go` files, and
  `conformance/testdata/expectations/schema.txt`. **Arbiter ACCEPT on round 1,
  zero rejections, zero repair rounds**, no blocking findings, two
  non-blocking notes (one nit, one process — both discharged, see below).
- **Ratchet: schema lane 9241 → 9251 pass of 15432 (+10, all `fail -> pass`);
  `datatypes`, `instance`, `xpath`, `ber` and `json` unchanged.** The
  arbiter's figures, copied not recomputed. The ten upward flips, each
  verified individually against its source `.xsd` rather than inferred from
  the lane delta:

  | Case | Construct | Source |
  |---|---|---|
  | `addB010` | `<notation>` with no `name` | `msData/additional/test66531.xsd` (testGroup doc: *"xsd: notation without a name attribute should give error"*) |
  | `notatB011` | `<notation name="">` | `msData/notations/notatB011.xsd` |
  | `Notation/name00201m1` | `<notation public="image/png"/>`, no `name` | `sunData/Notation/name/name00201m/` |
  | `attC004` | local `<attribute name="">` | `msData/attribute/attC004.xsd` |
  | `attQ005` | top-level `<attribute/>`, no `name` | `msData/attribute/attQ005.xsd` |
  | `attP003` | top-level `<attribute ref="att1"/>` (no `name`) | `msData/attribute/attP003.xsd` |
  | `attgB007` | `<attributeGroup name="">` | `msData/attributeGroup/attgB007.xsd` |
  | `idA031` / `idB031` / `idC031` | `<unique name="">` / `<key name="">` / `<keyref name="">` | `msData/identityConstraint/` |

  All ten are `expected validity="invalid"` cases this processor previously
  **accepted**. No downward flip anywhere. Gate: `go build ./...`, `go vet
  ./...`, `go test ./...` (all packages) all ok, **`golangci-lint run` → 0
  issues**, `go test ./conformance -run TestConformance -count=1` ok (25s),
  and green again after the ratcheted expectations file. `git status` empty
  before review.
- **Surface: no new exported identifier — the diff adds zero.** The arbiter's
  T5 check (`git diff | grep '^+\(func\|type\|const\|var\) '`) is empty; every
  non-test added line is either an unexported guard or a doc comment on
  already-exported surface. Matches the issue's `## Surface: none expected`.
  The four named non-goals — `NewComplexType`, `NewSimpleType`,
  `NewPrimitiveType`, `NewLocalScope` — are untouched.
- What landed: one `if name.Local == "" { return zero, xsderr.New(rule…, loc,
  …) }` at the head of `NewAttributeDeclaration` (`a-props-correct`),
  `NewAttributeGroupDefinition` (`ag-props-correct`),
  `NewModelGroupDefinition` (`mgd-props-correct`), `NewNotation`
  (`n-props-correct`) and `NewIdentityConstraint` (`c-props-correct`), each
  message naming that component's own tableau section and the NCName
  value-space argument; the godoc of each constructor rewritten to state the
  clause it now enforces, the §5.3 exclusion, and the "no anonymous form"
  basis; and five failure-capable table tests, `TestNew*RejectsAbsentName`,
  four sub-cases each — `QName{}` and `QName{Space: "urn:ns"}` reject,
  `QName{Local: "n"}` and `QName{Space: "urn:ns", Local: "n"}` accept.
- **Clause granularity is not uniform across the five, and the messages say
  so.** The arbiter verified against `docs/specs/md/xmlschema11-1.md` that
  `a-props-correct` (`:977`), `ag-props-correct` (`:2212`) and
  `c-props-correct` (`:3154`) are **All**-of lists whose clause 1 is the
  tableau clause — those three messages say "clause 1" — while
  `mgd-props-correct` (`:2302`) and `n-props-correct` (`:3433`) are single
  unnumbered constraints, and those two messages correctly omit a clause
  number. All five tableaux (`:794`, `:2163`, `:2254`, `:2955`, `:3359`) read
  `{name} An xs:NCName value. Required.`
- Grounding (oracle, before implementation, posted on the thread): each of the
  five confirmed component by component — `{name}` Required `xs:NCName` in its
  own §3 tableau; the enforcing clause is that component's own
  `*-props-correct`, not a borrowed one and not `RuleComponentInvariant`; §5.3
  "Missing Sub-components" does **not** cover `{name}` (it is scoped to
  properties resolved *by* QName reference, and `{name}` is the identity others
  resolve *against*); `xs:NCName`'s value space (`\i\c*`, Datatypes §3.4.7)
  excludes the empty string. It also **corrected the issue's own anchor for
  `a-props-correct`** — the issue cited `:975`, which is the section-heading
  anchor `sec-a-props-correct`; the rule anchor is `:977`. And it fenced the
  reasoning: **"no anonymous form" must not be generalized to the
  type-definition constructors**, which have a genuine one.
- Decisions made and why:
  1. **Five near-identical guards, no shared helper** — the issue asked for
     this explicitly and the reason survives review: each cites a *different*
     rule ID, and #202's root-cause finding was a single misattributed rule ID
     cascading into four review findings. A helper would re-create exactly the
     coupling that defect came from.
  2. **`name.Local == ""`, never `name == QName{}`.** The two are different
     predicates: `QName{Space: "urn:x", Local: ""}` is not the zero QName but
     is still not a legal `xs:NCName`. The #295 thread had flagged this in
     2026-07-29 (do not "align" the constructor guards with `indexByName`'s
     `n == (QName{})` — the two answer different questions), and the tests
     make the distinction executable: the `namespace with empty local`
     sub-case is the one that discriminates the shipped guard from the weaker
     one.
  3. **The `{name}` guard goes FIRST in `NewAttributeDeclaration`, ahead of
     `checkTypeDefinitionOrRef`.** The arbiter reviewed the ordering rather
     than waving it through: `checkTypeDefinitionOrRef` raises only
     `RuleComponentInvariant`, a representation invariant this package owns
     and not a spec clause a schema author can violate, so when both faults are
     present the spec-rule error is the more actionable one — and the
     alternative would have interpolated `name.String()` as `""` into the
     message. It also matches `NewElementDeclaration` exactly (name guard at
     `:541`, `checkTypeDefinitionOrRef` at `:545`). No caller depended on the
     old ordering.
  4. **The exclusion of `NewComplexType`/`NewSimpleType` is stated in every
     one of the five doc comments, not once.** Each says the "no anonymous
     form" reasoning is deliberately NOT generalized to the type-definition
     constructors, which carry `{name}` Optional. The issue asked for a code
     comment so a later session does not "finish the job" and break #210;
     putting it at all five sites is the version that survives someone reading
     only one file.
- Surprises:
  1. **The issue predicted no lane movement, mason's report repeated it, and
     both were wrong — because neither could see otherwise.** The non-ratchet
     conformance harness is silent on `fail -> pass` improvements by
     construction: it fails only on regressions. A `Ratchet: unchanged`
     prediction about *upward* movement is therefore unverifiable by anyone
     except the arbiter, and mason's honest report of "unchanged" was the best
     claim available to a role that may not run `GOXSD_RATCHET=1`. The arbiter
     caught it, measured +10, attributed all ten case by case, and flagged the
     stale `Ratchet: unchanged` line in `cdfa832`'s body as a **process
     finding** — the squash body at land time must carry the real movement.
     **That correction is an obligation on the landing commit, not on a repair
     round.**
  2. **`NewModelGroupDefinition`'s guard is currently unreachable from the
     parser and was still the right thing to add.** `produceModelGroupDefinition`
     (`parser/produce_complex.go:1152`) already rejects a nameless top-level
     `<group>` before construction — deliberately, with its own reasoning about
     the schema for schema documents — so this constructor contributed exactly
     zero of the ten flips. It is the one of the five whose value is purely the
     programmatic-construction-time T1 guarantee, and that is a legitimate
     reason to ship a guard.
  3. **`attP003` flips for a *coarser* reason than it deserves.** It is a
     top-level `<attribute ref="att1"/>`, forbidden by the schema for schema
     documents (top-level attributes take `name`, not `ref`). goxsd8 now
     rejects it — correct outcome, legitimate flip — but the producer derives
     an empty `{name}` for it, so it is charged `a-props-correct` rather than
     the representation constraint that actually forbids the shape. The
     arbiter recorded this as **a note for a future issue, explicitly not a
     defect in this change and not worth blocking**. See Next 1.
  4. **Failure-capability was verified by mutation, in a throwaway worktree,

## 2026-08-05 — #296 — xsd: a message-quality pass on the three absent-QName rejections turns up a PRE-EXISTING WRONG CITATION — `<xs:group ref=…>`'s Particle mapping lives in §3.7.2 `declare-namedModelGroup`, not §3.8.2 `declare-contentModel`, which §3.9.2's particle-mapping table (`xmlschema11-1.md:2694-2695`) separates in as many words — and the three messages stop opening with the internal Go variant name (`ModelGroupRef`, `ElementDeclarationRef`, `AttributeDeclarationRef`) and lead with the construct the schema author actually wrote (`<group ref>`, `<element ref>`, `<attribute ref>`), STYLE E1; the arbiter's sharpest finding was that the FIRST commit alone would have been a REJECT — the whole wording change reverted cleanly with every committed test still green — so a second commit added `assertMsgLeadsWith` and made the change load-bearing; arbiter ACCEPT, ratchet unchanged, proved by a clean tree after a `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` write run

- Shipped on `wip/issue-296` as TWO commits on top of `origin/main` @ `1bc607d`
  (`xsd: reject empty {name} in five M4 constructors (#295)`):
  `35ffdee` (`xsd: lead absent-QName rejections with the construct, cite §3.7.2
  for <group ref> (#296)`, mason) and `142edf3` (`xsd: pin that the three
  empty-ref rejections lead with the construct (#296)`, mason). Net diff against
  `origin/main` is **6 files, +45/−16**: `xsd/particle.go`,
  `xsd/attributeuse.go`, `xsd/term.go`, `xsd/particle_test.go`,
  `xsd/attributeuse_test.go`, `xsd/occurs_test.go`. Strings, doc comments and
  tests only — no rule reclassified, no field added, no `Loc` threading changed,
  no expectations file touched. The squash-merge lands both commits as one.
- **Ratchet: unchanged (arbiter's figures, copied not recomputed).**
  `git diff origin/main...HEAD --name-only -- conformance/` → **empty**, and
  `GOXSD_RATCHET=1 go test ./conformance -run TestConformance -count=1` → ok
  25.960s with `git status` AND `git diff` over
  `conformance/testdata/expectations/` **both empty afterward** — zero
  expectation files written, no lane moved in either direction. Gate at tip
  `142edf3` on a clean tree: `go build ./...` OK, `go vet ./...` OK,
  **`golangci-lint run` → 0 issues**, `go test ./...` all ok (conformance
  27.594s, xsd 0.040s, xsderr 0.112s), `go test ./conformance -run
  TestConformance -count=1` → ok 25.131s. As in #295 this entry deliberately
  claims no consecutive-gate-part-2 number: the gate part 2 was really run and
  really returned `0 issues`, which is worth more than a streak nobody can
  audit.
- The four landed changes:
  1. **The citation fix.** `xsd/particle.go`'s `ModelGroupRef` message and its
     `Particle` doc comment, and `xsd/term.go`'s `ModelGroupRef` doc, said
     §3.8.2. §3.8.2 (`declare-contentModel`) defines only the
     `<all>`/`<choice>`/`<sequence>` mappings; `<group>` appears there merely as
     a possible child contributing to `{particles}` and `ref=` is never defined
     there at all. §3.7.2 (`declare-namedModelGroup`) is where `ref = QName`
     lives and where the `ref`-bearing `<group>` → particle mapping (`xr.mgd3`)
     is written. The oracle grounded it; the **arbiter independently
     re-derived it** from `xmlschema11-1.md:2694-2695` rather than taking the
     grounding on report.
  2. **Three messages reworded to lead with the construct (E1).** Was
     *"particle {term} is a ModelGroupRef carrying the absent (zero) QName…"*;
     is *"`<group ref>` carries an empty reference: group/@ref is typed
     xs:QName (§3.7.2 declare-namedModelGroup), whose local part is an NCName
     and so is never empty (particle {term} variant ModelGroupRef)"*. The
     internal variant name is kept, demoted to trailing detail — a reader who
     needs it still has it; a schema author who wrote `<group ref="">` no
     longer needs a debugger to find their own tag.
  3. **`assertMsgLeadsWith`** (`xsd/occurs_test.go`, beside the existing
     `assertRule`) wired into `TestNewParticleRejectsAbsentRefName` and
     `TestNewAttributeUseRejectsAbsentRefName`. It reads `*xsderr.Error.Msg`
     rather than `err.Error()`, so the loc/rule prefix Error() adds is out of
     the comparison, and it pins the LEAD only — the explanatory remainder
     stays free to change.
  4. **Doc comments in `xsd/term.go` and `xsd/particle.go` follow the
     citation**, including §3.7.2's `xr.mgd1` parenthetical (the named branch
     requires a `<schema>`/`<redefine>`/`<override>` parent), which the arbiter
     checked against `xr.mgd1` and its `<override>` Note.
- Decisions made and why:
  1. **Rule stays `xsderr.RuleComponentInvariant`, `Loc` stays threaded.** A
     wording pass is not a reclassification; E2 is satisfied by one rule ID per
     error and E3 by `loc` still reaching every `xsderr.New` rather than being
     reconstructed. Nothing about the outcome of any construction changed.
  2. **Pin the lead, not the sentence.** Asserting the full message would make
     every future clarification a CI break; asserting nothing is what made
     `35ffdee` unverifiable. `HasPrefix` over `Msg` is the narrowest predicate
     that makes the E1 property executable.
  3. **`parser/produce_absentname_test.go` left alone, on #294's explicit
     instruction.** The post-land pass for #294 posted on this thread that its
     three-assertion shape (rejected / `RuleOf` / `URI:line:col`, never message
     text) is decoupling BY DESIGN, and that pinning wording there would
     destroy the very property letting #296 improve a diagnostic without
     breaking CI. The new pin therefore lives where the message is minted, in
     `xsd/`.
  4. **Consistency criterion checked rather than asserted.** The arbiter
     confirmed the only §3.8.2 citations left in `xsd/` are correct ones —
     `xsd/particle.go:22` (naming §3.7.2/§3.8.2/§3.9.2 as the three mapping
     kinds) and `xsd/wildcardadmit.go:100` (containment definitions).
- Surprises:
  1. **A message-quality issue found a spec-citation defect that had been
     sitting in landed code since #202.** The issue was filed about author-facing
     wording; §3.8.2 vs §3.7.2 came along as the first bullet and turned out to
     be the more durable of the two fixes. Nothing in the gate can see a wrong
     `§` in a string literal — the same blind spot class as #290's
     over-promising comments and #470's "top-level" pair, now three sightings.
  2. **`35ffdee` alone would have been a REJECT, and the arbiter said so on the
     record.** Its only test-file change was a doc comment; the arbiter reverted
     the full message rewording in an isolated worktree and watched every
     committed test still pass. A wording change nothing asserts is a wording
     change that can silently regress. `142edf3` fixes exactly that, and the
     revert probe now fails all six subtests with the E1 message.
- Friction:
  1. **The orchestrator committed mason's in-progress diff prematurely**,
     reacting to a stop-hook uncommitted-changes warning while mason's
     background agent was still adding the test coverage. No work was lost —
     `142edf3` landed cleanly on top — but **the arbiter was handed a moving
     branch mid-review**: it started at `35ffdee` with a clean tree, watched the
     tree go dirty across four files, and saw transient `vet`/`lint` typecheck
     failures (`undefined: assertMsgLeadsWith`, `"strings" imported and not
     used`) that belonged to no commit at all. It re-gated from isolated
     worktrees and its verdict says: *"don't hand the arbiter a branch another
     agent is still writing to. Checkpoint and settle the tip first."* Related
     in spirit to **#350** though not identical — #350 was a mutation-check
     race, this is a premature-commit race — with the same root cause:
     **orchestrator and subagent share one working tree with no serialization
     on "is the subagent actually done"**. That is now two data points, which
     makes it `/retro`'s call rather than this entry's.
  2. **The follow-up ledger has now gone FIFTEEN consecutive sessions without an
     audit** (#294 said twelve, the 2026-08-05 `/backlog` thirteen, #295
     fourteen). **The next `/retro` owes it, and this is the twelfth consecutive
     entry saying so in bold.** This session adds two Next items and discharges
     none of the pile.
- Next:

## 2026-08-06 — #449 — conformance: the twenty `nonPositiveInteger`/`negativeInteger`/`nonNegativeInteger`/`positiveInteger` `001–005` lexical fixtures — the harvest #365's arbiter left enumerated in the code and unfiled — are claimed by widening ONE regexp alternation and touching no engine code; the route's first HALF-bounded arm and its first TWO-HOP base chains, the suffix-relation trap (`negativeInteger` inside `nonNegativeInteger`) settled empirically rather than argued, and the arbiter accepted on round 1 after re-deriving every figure independently

- Shipped on `wip/issue-449` as two commits on top of `origin/main` @ `b6e7127`
  (`xsd: message-quality pass on the absent-QName rejections (#296)`) —
  `3e23c9e` (`conformance: claim the twenty half-bounded integer lexical
  fixtures (#449)`, mason, 2 files, +239/−19) and `63f5225` (`conformance: bank
  the twenty half-bounded integer wins (#449)`, the arbiter's own
  machine-written ratchet write, 1 file, +20/−0) — plus this LOG commit, which
  also carries the arbiter's one comment-only fix (below). Branch total before
  the LOG commit: **3 files, +259/−19**, all under `conformance/` —
  `conformance/datatypes.go`, `conformance/datatypes_test.go`,
  `conformance/testdata/expectations/datatypes.txt`. **Arbiter ACCEPT on the
  first round, no repair round consumed**; two findings, both non-blocking, one
  of them fixed in place in this commit at the arbiter's explicit direction.
- **Ratchet: datatypes 1131/1153 → 1151/1173 — +20, all `New@pass`, 0
  `New@fail`, 0 Regressed, 0 Vanished; `schema` 9251/15432 and `instance`
  0/26426 unchanged.** The arbiter's figures, copied not recomputed.
  `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` produced 20 insertions and 0 deletions, touching only
  `expectations/datatypes.txt`; no expectation was hand-edited, and no case
  moved between lanes. Mason's `Ratchet:` trailer on `3e23c9e` forecast exactly
  this from a read-only diagnostic run and the banking run confirmed it
  **figure for figure, qualifier included** — the fourth consecutive honest
  trailer after #235, #344 (low-by-one, explained) and #331.
- **The change is one regexp alternation, and everything interesting about it is
  what the alternation is NOT allowed to do.** `datatypesCase` gained the four
  sub-family names between the literal `msData/datatypes/` and the trailing
  `[0-9]+\.xml$`. No engine code: each of the twenty rides the route #331 built
  and #365 last widened — `execLexicalCase` → `decideLexicalByFacets` →
  `value.ValidateLexical`, with `governingMapping` walking the base chain to
  `xs:decimal`'s strict mapping (`st-restrict-facets` §3.16.6.4) and the type's
  **own** effective facets deciding the literal.
- **The suffix-relation trap was settled by measurement, not by argument.**
  `negativeInteger` is a proper suffix of `nonNegativeInteger`, and
  `positiveInteger` of `nonPositiveInteger` — the shape that makes alternation
  order look load-bearing. It is not: RE2 has no leftmost-FIRST alternation, and
  the alternatives are anchored so that a match must consume the file name from
  the character immediately after the `msData/datatypes/` literal, which
  disambiguates the pair outright. Mason **proved rather than reasoned** it, with
  a whole-tree regex match diff of the old pattern against the new one over
  `testdata/xsdtests`: **323 → 343 matches, +20/−0**. The arbiter rebuilt the
  alternation standalone and walked the suite itself rather than trusting the
  number, and got the same 323 → 343. The alternation still lists the `non*`
  forms first, and the code now says in as many words that the ordering is for
  the reader only.
- **Spec** — `cvc-datatype-valid` §4.1.4 as a conjunction with clause 1
  (`dv_pattern`) gating before clause 3 (`dv_vfacets`), so the four empty
  literals are `cvc-pattern-valid` §4.3.4.4 rejections (no value V is ever
  established) and every other rejection is a well-formed integer failing only
  its single bound: `cvc-maxInclusive-valid` §4.3.7.3,
  `cvc-minInclusive-valid` §4.3.10.3, `cvc-fractionDigits-valid` §4.3.12.3,
  `st-restrict-facets` §3.16.6.4, and §3.4.14/§3.4.15/§3.4.20/§3.4.25 for the
  four derivations.
- **This cohort is the first to drive two shapes no earlier cohort could, and
  that is why it is a sibling test rather than four more rows in an existing
  one.** #331's forty-eight (`byte`/`long`/`short`/`unsigned*`) carry BOTH
  `minInclusive` and `maxInclusive`; #365's `xs:integer` carries NEITHER. These
  four carry **exactly one bound each** (`maxInclusive` 0 and −1,
  `minInclusive` 0 and 1) — the route's first half-bounded arm, where a
  single-sided value-facet check is the only thing between a well-formed
  integer literal and acceptance. And `negativeInteger` restricts
  `nonPositiveInteger` while `positiveInteger` restricts `nonNegativeInteger`,
  so those two are the route's first **TWO-HOP** base chains
  (`negativeInteger → nonPositiveInteger → integer → decimal`); the overlay walk
  has to cross two restriction steps before it reaches `xs:decimal`'s mapping.
  An overlay that stopped a hop short would pick up `nonPositiveInteger`'s bound
  of 0 and false-ACCEPT `"0"` as an `xs:negativeInteger` —
  `negativeInteger003.xml` and `positiveInteger003.xml` exist precisely to catch
  that, and both are asserted from the rejecting side with their rule ID.
- **The grounding caught a real citation error in the issue body — and it is the
  SAME digit that #331's grounding caught, from the same entry point.** The
  issue text cited `cvc-fractionDigits-valid` as §4.3.12.4; it is **§4.3.12.3**
  (§4.3.12.4 is the Schema Component Constraint section, not the Validation
  Rule). #331's entry records the identical correction against #331's own issue
  body, and #365's grounding re-confirmed it. **Three sightings of the same
  wrong digit, all originating in the issue body**, i.e. upstream of the oracle
  — see Next 4. The oracle also corrected two derivation premises the issue body
  had flattened: `negativeInteger`'s declared base is `nonPositiveInteger`, not
  `integer`, and `positiveInteger`'s is `nonNegativeInteger` — the two-hop fact
  the whole test design then hung on. Mason used the corrected §4.3.12.3
  throughout; the arbiter re-verified every citation against `docs/specs/md`
  rather than against the grounding.
- **The stale paragraph was rewritten to describe the post-landing world, under
  STYLE P3a — the rule #365's own repair round created.** `conformance/datatypes.go`
  carried a paragraph describing these twenty files as unclaimed. It now
  enumerates what genuinely remains undecided in the integer family — the
  `Facets/int/test111092.xml` multi-element reader-shape limit — rather than
  either leaving the stale claim or replacing it with a summary sentence. This is
  the third consecutive datatypes-selector issue to touch that paragraph and the
  first where the prose was right on the first pass: #331 undercounted, #365
  overclaimed and burned a repair round for it, #449 enumerated.
- **Both polarities and both REASONS are pinned, and the pins were proved
  failure-capable.** `TestDatatypesSelectorClaimsOnlyCohort` gained one positive
  row per family plus two negative `.xsd` pins
  (`negativeInteger.xsd`/`nonNegativeInteger.xsd`), keeping #365's
  `Facets/int/test111092.xml` and `int.xsd` pins. New
  `TestDatatypesLexicalHalfBoundedIntegerFamily` drives all twenty through the
  real executor in both polarities — each fixture is asserted to Pass against
  its spec-correct validity **and to Fail against the opposite claim**, the
  load-bearing half — pins the routing premises per family (seeded, NOT directly
  strict-mapped, `strictGoverns` true, `fixesTimezone` false, declared base) and
  carries a rejection-rule-ID table so a right answer for a wrong reason cannot
  pass (#149). Mason mutation-proved the table with four mutations, each
  confirmed to fail and then reverted; **the arbiter reproduced all four
  independently in an isolated worktree** rather than reading mason's transcript.
- **The arbiter re-derived rather than reviewed, and the cross-check is the part
  worth recording.** It ran the full gate independently; rebuilt the alternation
  and re-walked the suite (323 → 343, +20/−0); and extracted the expected
  validity of all twenty fixtures **directly from `msMeta/DataTypes_w3c.xml`**,
  cross-checking against mason's table — **0 mismatches**, so no goxsd8-vs-suite
  disagreement exists anywhere in the twenty and no #149 dismissal is owed
  (recorded explicitly, as #331's entry did, so a later reader does not go
  hunting for a missing dismissal note).
- **Exported surface: unchanged** — `go doc -all ./conformance` byte-identical
  before and after, confirmed by the arbiter. Package `conformance` is a
