## 2026-08-06 — #305 — parser: the five top-level named kinds stop each minting their own `{name}` and take it from one `producer.topLevelName`, so a nameless `<attributeGroup>`/`<element>`/`<attribute>` is a CONTENT-INDEPENDENT grammar fault instead of a `QName{tns, ""}` carried on — but the arbiter's round-1 reject was not about the helper at all, it was about the TWO GUARDS THE CHANGE LEFT BEHIND: one retained guard's freshly-written justification named a path `xsd.NewParticle` had already closed, and BOTH retained guards turned out provably deletable with the whole suite still green, because the new helper shadows them on every path the existing rows exercise

- Shipped on `wip/issue-305` as two commits on top of `origin/main` @ `8b3aa8d`
  (`conformance: claim the twenty half-bounded integer lexical fixtures (#449)`)
  — `17768e1` (`parser: route all five top-level kinds through one topLevelName
  helper (#305)`, mason, 4 files, +172/−68) and `9eb4f22` (`parser: repair round
  for #305 — correct retained-guard docs, add coverage, fix GAP ref`, mason,
  4 files, +83/−4) — plus this LOG commit. Branch total before the LOG commit:
  **5 files, +251/−68**, all under `parser/` — `parser/produce.go`,
  `parser/produce_complex.go`, `parser/produce_absentname_test.go`,
  `parser/produce_groups_test.go`, `parser/redefine_test.go`. **Arbiter REJECT on
  round 1, ACCEPT on round 2 — one repair round of the two-round cap consumed**,
  three blocking findings, all three fixed at the root rather than papered over.
- **Ratchet: unchanged, on BOTH arbiter runs. schema 9251 pass / 6181 fail
  (15432), datatypes 1151 / 22 (1173), instance 0 / 26426 — identical to
  `origin/main` before and after.** The arbiter's figures, copied not recomputed;
  it read them straight out of the expectations files rather than off mason's
  report, on each round. `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` at `9eb4f22` **wrote nothing** and
  `git status --porcelain` was empty afterwards, so there is no flip in either
  direction to explain and nothing regressed. This is the outcome the #295
  post-land cartographer comment predicted for a *knowable* reason rather than a
  hopeful one: #295 already banked the +10 that every nameless/empty-named
  construct in the suite was worth (`attQ005`, `attC004`, `attgB007`,
  `notatB011`, `addB010`, `Notation/name00201m1`, `idA031/B031/C031`), so #305's
  flips were **already taken** and this issue converts rejections into
  better-cited rejections rather than moving a lane. It was still MEASURED, per
  the issue body's own warning about #206's wrong "unchanged" prediction.
- **The change is one helper and five call sites, and the whole of its value is
  WHERE the rejection happens rather than THAT it happens.** `producer.topLevelName`
  (`parser/produce.go:736`) expands the `name` attribute of a top-level
  `<complexType>`, `<group>`, `<attributeGroup>`, `<element>` or `<attribute>`
  into the document's target namespace (§3.17.2) and rejects an unusable one
  before any child is walked; `run`'s switch takes all five names from it
  (`:602`, `:612`, `:622`, `:637`, `:647`). Three of the five previously minted
  `QName{Space: tns, Local: ""}` and carried on — `attributeGroup` via
  `buildAttributeGroup`, plus `element` and `attribute`; the other two had
  guarded it inside their own producer since #206. **After #295 all five were
  already rejected one layer down** with a real rule ID, so the improvement is
  not the outcome but the diagnostic: the schema author now gets a grammar
  message about the document they wrote instead of a component-invariant about a
  component they never saw, and gets it **content-independently** — the #206
  defect was that a nameless container's verdict depended on whether its body
  happened to hold a local element, whose `xsd.NewLocalScope` would charge
  `e-props-correct`, an unrelated rule, and only sometimes.
- **The fault carries NO rule ID, uniformly across all five, and that abstention
  is the grounding's central holding.** `name` is `use="required"` typed
  `xs:NCName` on `xs:topLevelComplexType`, `xs:namedGroup`,
  `xs:namedAttributeGroup`, `xs:topLevelElement` and `xs:topLevelAttribute` in
  the schema for schema documents, and no numbered Schema Representation
  Constraint states a clause of its own for it — §3.4.3 `src-ct` has exactly five
  clauses, none about `name`, and §3.6.3 `src-attribute_group` is literally "None
  as such". A plain `fmt.Errorf`, the footing `<include>` with no `schemaLocation`
  already stands on. **For the two DECLARATION kinds the oracle ruled the same
  way for a different reason**: `e-props-correct` cl.1 (§3.3.6.1) and
  `a-props-correct` cl.1 (§3.2.6.1) already exist at the xsd constructor layer,
  both riding the generic "properties as tableau'd" clause (there is no
  `e-name-correct`/`a-name-correct`), so attaching either at the grammar layer
  would **duplicate and compete with** a citation already in use rather than
  close a gap — legitimate defense in depth, not new enforcement. STYLE E2 was
  held on both halves, and the new tests pin the abstention by asserting
  `errors.As(err, &xe)` does **not** match — the right shape for pinning an
  abstention rather than merely a message.
- **The grounding corrected the issue body's own citation, and the correction is
  a one-digit section slip of exactly the class this log has been counting.** The
  body cited §3.7.3 for attribute-group's "None as such"; §3.7.3 is the
  **model-group-definition** section (`xmlschema11-1.md:2286`, also "None as
  such"), and the attribute-group anchor is **§3.6.3** `src-attribute_group`
  (`:2198`). Both are "None as such", so the body's *claim* was true and its
  *citation* was wrong — the failure mode nothing in the gate can see. The
  corrected §3.6.3 is used in `topLevelName`'s doc and the test doc; the arbiter
  separately verified that `produceModelGroupDefinition`'s pre-existing §3.7.3
  citation for `<group>` is correct and stays.
- **Finding 1 — the retained `<group>` guard's justification named a path that
  cannot reach it, which is the P3a failure mode in its purest form.** `17768e1`
  kept both #206 guards and argued at length for keeping them; for the `<group>`
  half it named `resolveModelGroup`'s on-demand build via a `<group ref>` lexical.
  That path **cannot deliver an empty local part**: `resolveModelGroup`'s only
  non-test caller is `allGroupOf`, the only non-test construction site of
  `xsd.ModelGroupRef` is `produceGroupRefParticle` (`produce_complex.go:1124`)
  feeding straight into `xsd.NewParticle`, and `xsd/particle.go:89-93` rejects an
  empty `ModelGroupRef` local part as `RuleComponentInvariant` unconditionally —
  **#294's guard fires first**. The arbiter reproduced that, then did the thing
  that made the finding useful rather than merely correct: **it went and found a
  path that IS live.** `produceRedefinition` (`parser/redefine.go:505,533`) mints
  `qn` from a `<redefine>` child's own `name` attribute and calls
  `buildModelGroupDefinition` directly, never touching `run`'s switch or
  `topLevelName`. So the conclusion ("keep the guard") was right and every word
  of the reasoning was wrong — and a comment asserting a false invariant is worse
  than no comment (P2), because the next session that trusts it deletes the guard
  when #294's coverage is noticed and silently reopens the #206 shape on the
  redefine path. The complexType half's account (`resolveBaseType`'s on-demand
  build off a `base=` lexical resolved against the prescan index, which indexes a
  `name=""` declaration under `QName{tns, ""}`) was checked and was accurate
  throughout.
- **Finding 2 is the one with teeth, and it names a defect class this project has
  no automated defence against: an upstream guard SILENTLY UN-TESTS the
  downstream guard it shadows.** Before this branch the seven rows of
  `TestProduceNamelessTopLevelContainerRejected` were the only thing pinning the
  two #206 guards. `topLevelName` now intercepts on exactly the path those rows
  drive, so after `17768e1` all sixteen rows pinned `topLevelName` **and nothing
  else** — the arbiter stubbed each retained guard with `if false` and `go test
  ./...` stayed **fully green both times**. The branch retained two guards,
  argued for retaining them, and left both provably deletable. That reproduces
  the issue's own thesis one layer down: its `## Notes` say *"'harmless today' is
  not 'leave it'"* because the empty local part "sat latent until a new consumer
  read it", and a retained guard with a wrong rationale and no test is the same
  latency. Nothing in build, vet, lint or conformance can see this — the suite
  going green is precisely the symptom.
- **The repair added one end-to-end test per retained guard, each reaching it by
  its OWN real live path, and both were mutation-proved twice — by mason and
  independently by the arbiter.**
  `TestProduceNamelessBaseTypeRejectedOffDispatch`
  (`parser/produce_groups_test.go:427`) drives the complexType guard through
  `resolveBaseType`: a `<complexType name="d">` restricting `base=""` binds to
  the `QName{"", ""}` key the prescan indexed a later `<complexType name="">`
  under, so the on-demand build pulls the nameless type in **before `run` ever
  dispatches on it** — and DOCUMENT ORDER IS LOAD-BEARING and documented as such,
  because with the declarations swapped `topLevelName` raises the fault first and
  the guard goes untested again. Under the mutation it fails by **panic**, in
  `complexTypeIdentity.scopeParent`'s zero-identity assertion, with the arbiter's
  stack trace showing `resolveBaseType` entered with the zero QName — the panic
  being exactly why the fault must be charged before anything is built. Because a
  panic aborts the binary, the arbiter could not read "only this test fails" off
  that run and **did the exclusion run properly**: enumerated the package with
  `go test ./parser -list '.*'`, dropped the new test, ran the remaining **265**
  under the same mutation — `ok`. `TestParseRedefineNamelessGroupRejected`
  (`parser/redefine_test.go:460`) drives the group guard through the redefine
  path, two documents both declaring `<group name="">`; under the mutation it
  fails with the **#206 defect verbatim** — `[e-props-correct]` charged against
  the `{scope}.{parent}` because the body happens to hold a local `<element>`.
  The seven pre-existing rows stay green under both mutations.
- **The redefine test only works because THREE separate filters let `name=""`
  through, and the arbiter re-walked all three rather than trusting the trace.**
  `newRedefineSet` (`redefine.go:179-183`) does `name, ok := c.Attr("name")` and
  rejects only `!ok`, so an **absent** name dies there and an **empty** one
  survives; the prescan's `recordOriginal` branch (`produce.go:465-472`) records a
  `name=""` original after the same `ok`-only check, which is what carries the
  case past `produceRedefinition`'s `src-expredef` closing-requirement check
  (`redefine.go:506-510`); and only then does `redefine.go:533` reach the guard.
  Miss any one and the test would pass for the wrong reason.
- **Finding 3 — the new `GAP(parser)` marker landed pointing at the issue the
  commit closes**, i.e. a dead end on arrival, which STYLE P3 forbids in as many
  words. The gap is real and the arbiter reproduced it: a top-level
  `<xs:simpleType>` with `name` absent **or** `name=""` produces end to end
  **without error** — `run` expands its name inline and `xsd.NewSimpleType` has
  no `{name}` guard, unlike `NewElementDeclaration`, `NewAttributeDeclaration` and
  `NewNotation`, so the type is registered under `QName{target, ""}` and both
  layers stay silent. Filed as **#523** (`kind/gap`, open) and the marker at
  `parser/produce.go:735` repointed at it. The marker claims **no error
  direction** — what a schema so registered goes on to do downstream is not
  established — which the arbiter called textbook P3a rather than a hedge.
- **Exported surface: unchanged** — `go doc -all ./parser` byte-identical against
  a throwaway worktree at `origin/main`, verified by the arbiter on both rounds.
  `topLevelName` is unexported and no new exported identifier appears, so no T5
  question arises. `produceElement` and `produceAttribute` gained a `qname`
  parameter; `grep` confirms `run` is each one's only caller, so the signature
  change is total and no path is left reading `name` itself for these five kinds.
- **Gate at the tip of this LOG commit, on a clean tree:** `go build ./...` OK,
  `go vet ./...` OK, `go test ./...` all ok, **`golangci-lint run` → 0 issues**.
  The arbiter additionally ran `go test ./conformance -run TestConformance
  -count=1` (ok, 28s) and the ratchet write run (ok, 30s, wrote nothing) at
  `9eb4f22`; this LOG commit is docs-only and moves no expectation.
- Decisions made and why:
  1. **Both #206 guards are KEPT, not deleted, even though `run` can no longer
     reach either.** Each has a live non-`run` entry path whose name never passes
     through `topLevelName` — `resolveBaseType`'s on-demand build for the
     complexType half, `produceRedefinition` for the group half — and deleting
     them would restore the #206 content-dependent verdict on exactly those
     paths. The issue's Acceptance left "unreachable-from-`run` or folded into
     the helper" open; this answers it as *retained and re-documented*, and the
     repair round is what made the answer defensible rather than merely stated.
  2. **The fault stays a plain `fmt.Errorf` for the two declaration kinds too**,
     rather than borrowing `e-props-correct`/`a-props-correct`. Per the
     grounding: those verdicts belong to the constructor layer, still fire on the
     LOCAL paths, and duplicating them at the grammar layer would put two
     citations in competition for one condition.
  3. **`TestProduceNamelessTopLevelContainerRejected` → `TestProduceNamelessTopLevelRejected`.**
     All seven original rows survive verbatim and nine were added for the three
     newly-guarded kinds (each with `name` absent AND `name=""` pinned identical,
     each with a content-bearing body as the content-independence guard). The
     test's subject genuinely widened past "container"; the arbiter accepted the
     rename explicitly as the more honest choice rather than charging it as a
     deviation.
  4. **The new tests went in `produce_groups_test.go`/`redefine_test.go`, not in
     `produce_absentname_test.go`.** That file's table is `wantRule`/`wantLine`-
     shaped and these rows assert a plain-error shape; forcing them in would have
     meant two tables in one file for no gain. Cross-references were written in
     both directions and the arbiter verified both.
  5. **`TestProduceAbsentNameAndEmptyRefRejected` lost its `nameless global
     <element>` row** — that shape now fails at the grammar layer before reaching
     `xsd.NewElementDeclaration`, so it moved to the table above; the
     constructor's own `e-props-correct` verdict stays pinned by the surviving
     LOCAL row, whose `produceLocalElement` path is its only remaining
     parser-visible call site for a missing `{name}`. Verified as leaving no gap.
- Surprises:
  1. **A correct conclusion reached through an entirely false trace, written in
     the same commit that was CORRECTING other people's false traces.** `17768e1`
     kept a guard for a good reason and documented a reason that could not
     happen; the sentence survived because only the consumers in view were
     checked. #449's entry recorded the same shape as a citation defect; this is
     the same defect class in a *reachability* claim, where the gate is equally
     blind and the reader has even less chance of noticing.
  2. **The suite going green was the SYMPTOM, not the evidence.** Both retained
     guards were stubbable with `if false` and nothing turned red, and that is a
     direct consequence of the change being *good* — the new helper intercepts
     earlier on every path the tests drive. Any change that adds an upstream
     guard has this property, and there is no lint, vet or coverage signal for
     it. It cost this issue its one repair round, and it will recur: #518, #433
     and #523 are all upstream-guard issues over the same producers.
  3. **`resolveModelGroup` looked live to two readers and was closed by #294 all
     along.** The path that actually still matters — `<redefine>` — only exists
     because #286 landed `<xs:redefine>` five sessions ago, so the guard's real
     justification is younger than the guard.
- Friction:
  1. **The GitHub thread was READABLE this session, and the difference is worth
     recording against #449's Friction 1.** That entry could cite no comment
     permalinks because `gh` returned 403 for the chronicler; this session read
     all four comments through the GitHub MCP tool, so the Thread record below is
     complete. The gap is therefore **session-scoped and intermittent**, not a
     standing loss — which is the more annoying diagnosis, because a convention
     every entry in this file honours holds or breaks according to which toolset
     a session happens to get. Still worth the retro's attention as
     tooling-variance rather than as an outage.

## 2026-08-06 — #299 — docs: the `PRINCIPLES N` citations that were really indexing CLAUDE.md's "Style headlines" SUMMARY are corrected at 37 sites and a module-root `citations_test.go` makes the mistake fail CI — but the session's two sharpest findings are that the ISSUE'S OWN CENSUS UNDERCOUNTED EVERY BUCKET and had stale line numbers essentially everywhere, and that the trap the 2026-08-01 sequencing comment predicted — #299 landing first and enshrining the phantom `STYLE T2/T7` as its exemplar of a correct citation, inside `docs/STYLE.md`, where it is hardest to unpick — was AVOIDED rather than merely survived; comment-and-doc only, zero compiled delta, arbiter ACCEPT on round 1

- Shipped on `wip/issue-299` as two commits on top of `origin/main` @ `b768bcb`
  (`parser: route all five top-level kinds through one topLevelName helper
  (#305)`) — `dd86803` (`wip #299: partial PRINCIPLES citation fixes (in
  progress, mason still running)`, an orchestrator checkpoint, 16 files,
  +24/−24, the `5`→`9` and `9`→`20` renumbers) and `1139a92` (`docs: fix
  PRINCIPLES citations that indexed CLAUDE.md's headline list (#299)`, mason
  final, 13 files, +340/−36, the sealed-sum clause deletions, the `4`→`5`
  bucket, both doc edits and the guard) — plus this LOG commit. Branch total
  before the LOG commit: **29 files, +364/−60**. **Arbiter ACCEPT on round 1,
  no blocking findings, four non-blocking advisories (A1–A4).**
- **Ratchet: unchanged.** The arbiter's words, copied not recomputed:
  *"`conformance/testdata/expectations/` is untouched by the diff
  (`git diff --stat origin/main...HEAD -- conformance/` is empty) and the
  ratchet run produced no writes. **No lane moved in either direction.**
  Correct for a change with no compiled delta."* It published **no per-lane
  table this round because there was nothing to move**; the standing figures at
  this branch's base are therefore the ones the #305 entry above recorded at
  `b768bcb` — schema 9251/6181, datatypes 1151/22, instance 0/26426 — carried
  here as unmoved context, not as a fresh measurement. `GOXSD_RATCHET=1` at
  `1139a92` wrote nothing and `git status` was clean afterwards.
- **The change is 37 sites in four buckets, and the arbiter reconciled every
  one of them against the grounding's 48-row inventory by its own re-grep
  rather than off mason's report.** **21 sites** `PRINCIPLES 5` → `9`
  (`parser/produce.go:255,498,786,796`, `value/valuespace.go:293`,
  `xsd/attributeusefold.go:57`, `attributewildcardfold.go:81`,
  `complexderivation.go:64`, `complexextension.go:575`,
  `contentrestricts.go:58,628`, `effectivetotalrange.go:26`,
  `example_test.go:392`, `modelgroup.go:11`, `resolve.go:83,521,584,668`,
  `substitutiongroup.go:23`, `valueconstraintvalid.go:61`,
  `wildcardadmit.go:16`) — every one argues "no visited set, licensed by a
  prior phase", which is PRINCIPLES.md item **9** *"Phased construction beats
  cycle checks"* (`docs/PRINCIPLES.md:53`), not item 5. **3 sites**
  `PRINCIPLES 9` → `20`, the INVERSE collision (`xsd/assertionprefix.go:75`,
  `valueconstraintvalid.go:335`, `valuespace.go:34` — all fail-open arguments,
  item 20 at `:119`). **8 sites** where the whole clause
  `, the PRINCIPLES 7 sealed-sum exception` is **deleted** rather than
  renumbered (`xsd/term.go:8`, `typedefinition.go:12,51`,
  `complextype.go:70,207`, `attributeuse.go:39`, `defaultbinding.go:30`,
  `elementdeclaration.go:97`): PRINCIPLES.md 7 is *"No concurrency"*, the
  sealed-sum licence lives in `docs/STYLE.md:97`/`:102`, and the retained
  `STYLE T2/T7` text already carries it, so the number was pure noise.
  **5 sites** `PRINCIPLES 4` → `5` (`builtin/strict/datetime.go:39` — the
  `4/5` hedge collapsed to `5` — plus `datetime.go:298`, `duration.go:36`,
  `gregorian.go:24`, `strict.go:172`).
- **The issue's census undercounted EVERY bucket and its line numbers were
  stale essentially everywhere, and both errors are of exactly the class the
  issue exists to fix.** It predicted "~30 citations", "~11 sites, all in
  `xsd/`" for the dominant cluster and 6 sealed-sum sites. Actual: **37**, with
  the dominant cluster at **21** and reaching **outside `xsd/`** into
  `parser/produce.go` (4 sites) and `value/valuespace.go`; the sealed-sum
  cluster at **8**, including `xsd/elementdeclaration.go:97`, which the issue
  omitted entirely and the grounding recovered by grep. Of the roughly twenty
  `file:line` pairs the issue names, the oracle found the line number wrong at
  nearly all of the multi-line-comment sites (`example_test.go` 251→392,
  `resolve.go` 60/410/466/550→83/521/584/668, `contentrestricts.go` 546→628
  with no citation at 546 at all, `strict.go` 162→172, and more) — files have
  drifted since 2026-07-29. A session that had trusted the body's coordinates
  and sed'd them would have edited the wrong lines; the grounding's instruction
  to re-grep first is what made the sweep safe.
- **The 2026-08-01 sequencing warning is DISCHARGED, and this is the part worth
  reading before touching #382.** That comment argued #382 should land FIRST,
  because #299's own `## Acceptance` offers **`STYLE T2/T7`** as its exemplar
  of a correct citation and `T7` **is not a rule `docs/STYLE.md` defines**
  (T1–T6 is the full series) — landing #299 first would write a phantom ID into
  the one document a future session consults to settle exactly this question.
  #299 landed first anyway, and the trap was avoided rather than sprung: the new
  `docs/STYLE.md` "Citing rules" paragraph exemplifies with **`STYLE D4`** and
  **`STYLE T2`**, both real, and the `CLAUDE.md` pointer does the same. The
  arbiter checked this specifically (§4) and ruled the warning discharged. The
  guidance now published is therefore compatible with either resolution #382
  picks, because it names no ID #382 might delete.
- **Scope discipline against #382 was measured, not asserted: the `T7`
  population in `*.go` is 25 on `origin/main` and 25 on the branch** —
  byte-identical. `STYLE T2/T7` survives verbatim at all seven sites,
  `duration.go:36` keeps its bare `STYLE T7`, `typedefinition.go:11` keeps its
  bare `(STYLE T7)`. Mason changed only the `PRINCIPLES` token in each
  parenthetical and never the adjacent `STYLE` text, per the orchestrator's
  §5 scope ruling on the grounding.
- **The one deliberate exception is bare `STYLE 5` → `STYLE D4` at three sites**
  — `xsd/attributeusefold.go:57`, `xsd/attributewildcardfold.go:81`,
  `parser/produce.go:786` — and it is narrowly right for a reason worth
  recording: `STYLE 5` is not a mis-scoped ID, it is a POSITIONAL index into
  CLAUDE.md's headline list wearing STYLE's prefix, i.e. **this issue's own
  defect in the other document**, and headline 5's real ID is `D4`
  (`docs/STYLE.md:82`, "No cycle checks — build in phases"), which sits in the
  same parenthetical as the `PRINCIPLES 5`→`9` fix. `grep -rn "STYLE 5"
  --include=*.go .` now returns nothing. **Note for #382: its census grep
  (`STYLE [A-Z][0-9]`) structurally cannot match `STYLE 5`, so these three were
  never in its 23-site table and nothing has been taken off its plate.**
- **The guard is `citations_test.go` at the module root (292 lines,
  `package goxsd8_test`), and the arbiter verified it NON-VACUOUSLY rather than
  reading it.** It dropped a probe file into `xsd/` citing `PRINCIPLES 7` and
  `PRINCIPLES 99`, confirmed **both halves** fail with actionable messages, then
  removed it and confirmed the tree clean. Two halves: **BOUND** parses `1..N`
  out of `docs/PRINCIPLES.md` and **checks for gaps** (`highestPrincipleItem`),
  so "in range" genuinely means "names that item"; **ALLOW-LIST** pins the four
  collision-prone numbers (4, 5, 7, 9) per file *with counts*, catching both a
  new unlisted citation and a stale entry whose count no longer matches. **4 and
  7 are absent deliberately** — no site legitimately cites either today, so any
  reappearance is a hard failure. Three anti-vacuity floors with `t.Fatalf`
  (`minGoFilesScanned` 200, `minCitationsFound` 70, `minPrincipleItems` 30)
  stop a walk that silently stops finding files from passing, which the arbiter
  named as the failure mode meta-tests of this shape actually die of. It scans
  `*.go` only and skips dot-directories, `testdata` and itself — so LOG entries
  such as this one, which necessarily spell the numbers, cannot trip it.
- **Exported surface: unchanged.** Pure comment diff confirmed mechanically —
  filtering the `*.go` diff (excluding the new test) for added/removed lines
  that are not `//` comments returns **zero lines**. No production logic
  changed; the only new file is an external test package with no exports, so no
  T5 question arises.
- **Gate at the tip of this LOG commit, on a clean tree:** `go build ./...` OK,
  `go vet ./...` OK, `go test ./...` all ok, **`golangci-lint run` → 0 issues**.
  The arbiter additionally ran `go test ./conformance -run TestConformance
  -count=1` (ok, 30.1s) and the ratchet write run (ok, wrote nothing) at
  `1139a92`; this LOG commit is docs-only and moves no expectation.
- Decisions made and why:
  1. **The sealed-sum sites lose the number rather than gaining a corrected
     one.** There is no PRINCIPLES.md item that says "sealed sums are the
     exception"; the licence is a STYLE rule, and the site already cites it.
     Renumbering `PRINCIPLES 7` to something would have manufactured a citation
     where the honest edit is a deletion.
  2. **`STYLE T7` stays, everywhere, even in the eight parentheticals being
     edited.** It is #382's decision to make — that issue must first rule
     between (a) STYLE.md grows the rules the code cites and (b) the 23 sites
     are renumbered — and pre-empting it from inside #299 would have decided a
     question this issue was not authorized to answer. The measured 25/25
     occurrence count is the evidence that the line held.
  3. **`gregorian.go:24` and `datetime.go:298` took `PRINCIPLES 5` for cluster
     consistency**, over the grounding's own medium-confidence alternative of
     `STYLE T4`. Both comments argue that a shared rule is *written once*, which
     item 5 covers; the arbiter read both and endorsed as-is (A3).
  4. **The guard's collision set is the four numbers where a miscitation was
     actually FOUND (4/5/7/9), not all ten where the two lists disagree.**
     Deliberate and documented in the file; see Next 3 for the edge it leaves.
  5. **`CLAUDE.md` got three inserted lines and nothing else.** "The one rule
     that outranks everything" and the ratchet-integrity text are untouched, as
     the issue's own caveat and this repo's governance rule require; the arbiter
     verified the hunk in isolation.
  6. **The A1 miscount is left in the landed comment and handed forward rather
     than fixed in a post-verdict edit.** See Next 2 — the fix belongs to the
     squash message the orchestrator composes, not to a chronicler amending an
     accepted diff without re-review.
- Surprises:
  1. **The grounding's own prose miscounted its own table, and the miscount
     propagated into the landed comment — in the issue whose entire subject is
     the reliability of numbers written into comments.** §3's summary line says
     "34 sites" for the `5`→`9` bucket and "6" for `4`→`5`; the per-site table
     immediately below it enumerates **21** and **5**. Mason applied the table
     (correct) and then wrote the prose's total into `citations_test.go:7`
     ("#299 corrected 38 such sites") and the commit body ("6
     one-fact-one-encoding sites"). The arbiter caught it, and did the thing
     that made it settled rather than merely flagged: it proved the **table**
     was the complete set by exhaustive re-grep — **zero `PRINCIPLES 4` and zero
     `PRINCIPLES 7` remain anywhere in the Go tree**, all 6 surviving
     `PRINCIPLES 5` are one-fact-one-encoding, all 21 surviving `PRINCIPLES 9`
     are cycle-checks-by-phasing, and the remaining 1/2/8/10 citations each
     re-read and correct. **The true total is 37 = 21+3+8+5.**
  2. **The defect was never confined to `xsd/` and `builtin/strict/`, which is
     what the issue title says.** `parser/produce.go` alone carries four of the
     21, and `value/valuespace.go` one. The title's scope was inherited from the
     #203 verdict's sighting, and nobody re-grepped before filing.
  3. **A guard for this class turned out to need an ALLOW-LIST, not a rule.**
     The tempting shape — "every cited N is in range" — passes on 100% of the
     37 sites, since every wrong number was a real PRINCIPLES.md item. The
     bound half is nearly worthless alone and only earns its place because it
     also proves the list is gap-free; all the enforcement is in the per-file
     counts.
- Friction:
  1. **`gh` was 403 for the chronicler AGAIN, and the MCP tool worked AGAIN —
     third consecutive witness for the same session-scoped variance.** #449's
     Friction 1 recorded permalinks lost to a 403; #305's Friction 1 recorded
     the thread readable via MCP and called the gap *"session-scoped and
     intermittent, not a standing loss"*. This session got both signals in one
     place: `gh api` returned *"GitHub access is not enabled for this session"*
     and the GraphQL path returned *"only the pinned set of PR-review operations
     is served"*, while `mcp__github__issue_read` read the issue and all three
     comments without trouble. **The Thread record below is therefore
     complete.** Two data points would be noise; three consecutive is a pattern
     for `/retro` — the fallback order (`gh` → MCP) should probably be written
     into `docs/ROUTINES.md` so a session that hits the 403 does not conclude
     the thread is unreadable, as #449's did.
  2. **#515's lossy-channel defect has TWO fresh witnesses, both hit while
     reading rather than writing.** #299's own body, as served through MCP,
     ends its Provenance paragraph with *"Non-blocking observation 2 in the
     arbiter's accept verdict on #203: ."* — **the permalink is gone**; and
     #382's body opens *"Every `STYLE ` citation in the tree"* with its `<ID>`
     placeholder stripped, then repeats the loss in its guard paragraph. So the
     stripping is not confined to write-back round-trips (the 2026-08-05
     `/backlog` witness) or to filings (#305's Friction 2): **reads lose tokens
     too**, which means no session can quote an issue body verbatim through this
     channel and be sure of it. Anything angle-bracketed in an issue must be
     re-derived from the repo.
  3. **The arbiter had to raise the missing LOG entry itself for the SECOND
     CONSECUTIVE ISSUE** — advisory A4, *"it must be added before the PR is
     squash-merged"*, listing the same obligation #305's verdict listed as
     landing-step 2. Twice in a row from the wrong persona is no longer a
     one-off: the chronicler is being invoked after the final verdict, so the
     arbiter is structurally the first agent positioned to notice. `/retro`
     should decide whether the develop loop's step ordering or the arbiter's
     checklist is the right home for it; the entry point is the WORKFLOW step
     order, not the arbiter.
  4. **The follow-up ledger has now gone EIGHTEEN consecutive sessions without
     an audit** (#294 said twelve, the 2026-08-05 `/backlog` thirteen, #295
     fourteen, #296 fifteen, #449 sixteen, #305 seventeen). **The next `/retro`

     This session discharges one item from the pile (the #203 verdict's

     and the miscount in the landed comment survives as a `file-or-dismiss` nit.
     Cosmetic — it is prose, not a citation, and it misleads no reader to a
     wrong rule — but this issue's whole subject is numbers in comments being
     trustworthy.
  3. **[file-or-dismiss, arbiter non-blocking A2]** **The guard's collision set
     is 4/5/7/9 only; the two lists also disagree at 1, 2, 3, 6, 8 and 10.**
     PRINCIPLES.md 1 is "pure leaf" while headline 1 is "happy path on the
     left", and roughly ten sites cite those numbers today — the arbiter
     verified all ten are correct, but a FUTURE miscitation of 1/2/8/10 passes
     the guard silently. Widening it costs allow-listing ~10 more files. The
     narrowing is deliberate and documented (`citations_test.go:37–43`); this is
     recorded so the guard's edge is known rather than rediscovered by a session
     that trusts it more than it deserves.
  4. **[the process observation, for `/retro` rather than a filing]** **Two
     defect classes now share one home: a claim in a comment that the gate
     cannot see.** #449's Next 4 (the `§`-citation blind spot), #305's Next 5
     (an upstream guard silently un-testing the one it shadows) and this issue
     (positional citations of the wrong list) are the same family, and #299 is
     the first of the three to land a MECHANICAL check for its own slice of it.
     What that check demonstrates — the allow-list-with-counts shape (Surprise

## 2026-08-06 — backlog — **#347 is RATIFIED in shape and deliberately NOT APPLIED**, and the reason it was not applied is new: the cartographer declined because its own operating rule bars an agent message from authorizing a change to the agent's own configuration, and *this orchestrating session declined too* — a scheduled unattended trigger scoped to "no code changes" is the wrong actor to rewrite agent governance with no human able to weigh in, so the fully-specified three-part edit is parked on #347's thread for explicit sign-off rather than auto-applied under the #416 precedent; the pass's other two findings are that **the priority band was consumed for the first time** (#449, after a twelve-landing ignore streak) and that the **post-land follow-up harvest came back totally clean** — twelve owed items, seven filings, four fold-by-comment, one N/A, zero leaks, the first such audit in a long while

- Doc-only reconciliation, landed as ONE commit on `claude/dazzling-cerf-j2c06o`
  on top of `origin/main` @ `779d61e` (#299): `675a8e8`
  (`meta: backlog 2026-08-06`), **`docs/PLAN.md` only, +240 / −0**, a pure
  append into M4 ahead of the `## M5` heading. **No Go code changed, no
  `.claude/` file changed, no gate run, no `GOXSD_RATCHET=1`.** The
  orchestrating session squash-merges it via PR immediately after this entry
  lands. This was a **scheduled trigger**, not a live develop-loop session:
  the orchestrating session ran the cartographer's full `/backlog` procedure
  per `docs/WORKFLOW.md`'s "Other triggers".
- **Ratchet: unchanged** (doc-only trigger). Figures read off
  `conformance/testdata/expectations/*.txt` at `779d61e` — **read values, not a
  measurement**; a live run is the arbiter's job and not this trigger's.

  | lane | pass / total | since the 2026-08-05 backlog (`bfdb920`) |
  |---|---|---|
  | `schema` | 9251 / 15432 | 9241 → 9251, **+10** |
  | `datatypes` | 1151 / 1173 | 1131 / 1153 → 1151 / 1173, **+20 pass, +20 total** |
  | `instance` | 0 / 26426 | unchanged |
  | `xpath` / `json` / `ber` | empty by design | unchanged |

  **The two moved lanes moved for opposite reasons and the distinction is the
  point.** `schema` **+10** with the case-ID set **held at 15432** is rule work
  — #295's five constructors rejecting an empty `{name}`, ten W3C cases the
  non-ratchet harness is structurally silent about. `datatypes` **+20 pass on
  +20 total** is a **widening**: #449 claimed twenty half-bounded integer
  `001–005` fixtures by extending one regexp alternation, so the lane began
  *counting* twenty cases it had never counted, and all twenty pass. A reader
  comparing only the pass column would score them identically. Compare
  2026-08-05's `+71` (first kind) and 2026-08-03's `+4919` (second kind).
- **#347 — the governance finding, and the one thing a later reader must not
  misread.** The 2026-08-05 pass left a recommendation on the thread *"for the
  next pass to ratify rather than re-derive"*; this trigger's launching prompt
  delegated that judgment explicitly ("ratify if you judge it ready"), and the
  cartographer **ratified option (b)**: retire the numeric 8–10 ready-queue
  band from the cartographer's own procedure, formalize the `docs/PLAN.md`
  priority band as the real deliverable in its place, and widen `blocked` to
  *"waiting on a named dependency — an issue **or** a trigger."* **The exact
  three-part edit is specified verbatim on #347's newest comment**
  (`.claude/agents/cartographer.md` step 3, its Labels line, and
  `docs/WORKFLOW.md` if it restates the 8–10 number), so no later session owes
  a re-derivation — only an application.
  **Neither agent in this run would make that edit, for two DIFFERENT
  reasons, and both refusals are correct:**
  1. **The cartographer declined** per its own operating rule — no agent
     message, including an orchestrator's, can authorize it to change its own
     configuration file, and `.claude/agents/cartographer.md` is exactly that.
     It pointed at the **#416 precedent**, where a previous orchestrating
     session made an equivalent edit directly.
  2. **This orchestrating session also declined**, on a narrower and
     independent ground worth recording exactly: **this trigger's launching
     prompt scoped the work to "no code changes" and the standard
     reconcile-and-file-`docs/PLAN.md`-edits procedure.** Unilaterally
     rewriting agent governance/process configuration during an **unattended
     scheduled run**, with no live human able to weigh in, is a real escalation
     beyond that scope — even though the decision itself is well-reasoned and
     the #416 precedent exists. **The distinction from #416** is actor
     standing, not correctness: #416's edit was made by an orchestrating
     session that, per the historical record, appears to have had more direct
     standing and context for the call. This session takes the conservative
     path and surfaces a fully-specified edit for explicit human sign-off
     instead of auto-applying it.
  **#347 therefore stays open and stays `ready`** — it is still real work, and
  it is deliberately not relabelled — carrying **ratified-but-pending** status.
  It has now been open across **nine consecutive passes**. The blocker is no
  longer a decision, and no longer "no prompt has named an option" (2026-08-05
  discharged that); it is **an actor with standing to apply a settled edit**.
- ***The post-land follow-up harvest is CLEAN — twelve owed items, twelve
  accounted for, zero leaks — and this is the first pass where that is true.***
  Every `Next:` line from the window's landings was audited against reality:
  **seven filed as new issues, four folded by comment into issues that already
  owned the thing being harvested (#338, #433, #382, #525), one N/A.** The
  fold-don't-file routing carried a third of the work, and each of those four
  filings would have been a duplicate this project would later have had to
  merge. Recorded in bold because the ledger's whole value is that every owed
  item maps to an issue: **#489 exists precisely because the ledger had gone
  unaudited for long stretches**, and the running "N consecutive sessions
  overdue" counter that ran through fifteen consecutive entries is, for this
  window, discharged rather than incremented.
- **Queue arithmetic: 129 open (116 `ready`, 12 `blocked`, 1 unlabelled
  #411)**, against 128 (115/12/1) on 2026-08-05. **Five issues landed in the
  window** (#295, #296, #449, #305, #299) — but the arithmetic only reconciles
  against **six** closures, because **#416 closed two seconds after `bfdb920`**
  and therefore fell outside that pass's own count: 115 + 7 − 6 = 116.
  **Harvest ratio 1.4**, continuing a clean downward trend — **2.6 → 1.7 →
  1.4**. A ratio converging on 1.0 holds the queue steady **at 116**, not at
  10, which is the arithmetic behind #347's ratified shape.
- **Step 4 (libuser/cliuser) was SKIPPED this pass, and the reason is
  surface-unchanged — this is NOT a recurrence of #416's structural inability,
  which is decided and landed.** `git diff --stat bfdb920..origin/main --
  README.md cmd/` is **empty**: nothing in the window touched the published
  library or CLI surface. The five landings are internal to `xsd/`, `parser/`
  and `conformance/`. A persona pass would have re-read an unchanged surface
  and reproduced 2026-08-05's verdict, so the orchestrating session — which
  owns the step per `docs/WORKFLOW.md` — handed the cartographer nothing, and
  the cartographer said so rather than silently omitting it, exactly as #416
  rewrote step 4 to require. **Said plainly here so no future reader conflates
  a because-nothing-moved skip with the seven-pass structural gap #416
  closed.** The next window that touches `README.md` or `cmd/` owes a real
  pass; #472 and #514 will both trigger one.
- **#472's milestone CORRECTED, M3 → M4** — the same rule applied to #471

  tooling exposed no milestone listing; it is readable through the
  issue-**search** path rather than the issue-**list** path. **No body field

- **`GAP(` ledger: 47 → 48 sites, and the one new site is already owned.** The

  **Every one of the 48 maps to an issue; the ledger stays fully owned and
  shows no drift.**
- **Priority band re-derived — and consumed for the first time in its
  history.** **#449 was slot 1 for three consecutive passes and it LANDED**,
  breaking a twelve-landing streak of the band being ignored and supplying the
  first evidence that publishing an ordering does anything at all. Head slot
  retired; **#336 promoted to the head** (its acceptance is literally a lane
  number); **#478 → #447 promoted into the band from on deck**, as a pair and
  in that order. **#430 was checked against the window's landings rather than
  assumed** — #295 and #296 touched absent-`{name}` guards and message wording
  in `xsd/`, the same package but neither the same file nor the same rule, so
  #430's wildcard-subset defect is untouched and no re-scoping is owed. **New
  on deck: #499, #442, #506.**
- **Branch namespace: unchanged, three refs, nothing new.** `wip/issue-256`
  (park — #256 closed `needs-replan`, two commits genuinely not in `main`, the
  correct outcome for a park), `wip/issue-271` (carries no work at all, dead),
  `wip/issue-287` (superseded by #511's doc-only landing). All three still

  delete or rename refs.
- Decisions made and why:
  1. **#347 was ratified but not applied, and the two refusals were recorded
     separately rather than collapsed into one.** They rest on different
     grounds — the cartographer's is a standing rule about self-configuration;
     the orchestrator's is about **scope and unattendedness in this particular
     run**. Collapsing them would let a future reader conclude the edit is
     blocked forever, when in fact it is one human sign-off away.
  2. **Step 4's skip was written down as a skip with its reason**, not omitted.
     An omission is indistinguishable from the #416 defect; a stated skip is
     falsifiable against `git diff --stat`.
  3. **The clean harvest was audited item-by-item rather than asserted.** Each
     of the twelve was traced to a filing, a comment, or an explicit N/A. Four
     were folded rather than filed because the receiving issue already owned
     the fact.
  4. **#411 left unlabelled again** — its deliverable is on `main` and a
     `/backlog` does not close issues as done. Sixth pass recording this;
     #347's ratified `blocked` widening does not touch it, because #411 waits
     on a *commit*, not a dependency.
- Surprises:
  1. **The band worked, and it worked without any label enforcing it.** #449
     was consumed on the strength of published prose in `docs/PLAN.md` alone.
     That cuts *toward* #347's option (b) — formalize the ordering — and away
     from the numeric-band relabelling it replaces, which is a pleasant
     coincidence rather than an argument the pass went looking for.
  2. **The two lanes moved by opposite mechanisms in a single window.** A
     +10 rule-work move and a +20 widening in the same eight-line table is the
     clearest side-by-side the log has recorded, and it is exactly why the
     measurement-integrity items rank second in the band's doctrine.
  3. **The follow-up ledger's first clean audit arrived without anyone
     scheduling it.** The counter that had been climbing in bold for fifteen
     entries stopped because a window's worth of `Next:` lists happened to be
     small enough to discharge in one pass — which is evidence that the
     harvest ratio dropping (2.6 → 1.7 → 1.4) is doing real work, not just
     looking tidier.
- Friction:
  1. **A settled, fully-specified, well-reasoned edit could not be applied by
     any actor present in the run.** The cartographer is barred by its own
     rule; the orchestrating session judged itself out of scope for an
     unattended trigger. This is the pass's only real cost, and it is
     structural rather than accidental — **a scheduled `/backlog` contains no
     actor with standing to change agent configuration.** `/retro` or a human
     filing should decide whether that is a bug or the intended safety
     property.
  2. **The #416 precedent is ambiguous as precedent.** It shows an
     orchestrating session making exactly this class of edit, which invites a
     later session to treat it as blanket authorization. The distinction this
     pass drew (actor standing, attended vs. unattended) is nowhere durable
     except in this entry.

  2. **The #416-precedent ambiguity (Friction 2) belongs somewhere durable** —

     sessions may edit `.claude/` configuration and under what conditions.
     File-or-dismiss at the next retro.
  3. **The next window touching `README.md` or `cmd/` owes a real step-4
